Below is a full **MMO Class, Skill, Power, Ability, and Progression System**.

Core rule:

```text
A class system should give players identity.
A skill system should give players growth.
An ability system should give players meaningful choices.
A power system should make combat and world interaction feel different depending on how the player built their character.
```

The system should support:

```text
Classes
Subclasses / specializations
Skills
Abilities
Powers
Passives
Talents
Weapon mastery
Magic schools
Profession skills
Social skills
Exploration skills
Class quests
Skill trainers
Skill books
Respecs
Loadouts
NPC classes
Balance rules
Server validation
```

---

# 1. Class System Overview

A **class** defines the player’s core identity.

A class should answer:

```text
What is this character good at?
What weapons do they use?
What armor do they wear?
What resources do they spend?
What abilities do they unlock?
What role do they fill in groups?
How do NPCs react to them?
What unique world interactions do they get?
```

Classes should affect:

```text
Combat style
Starting attributes
Ability unlocks
Weapon access
Armor access
Dialogue options
Quest options
Faction access
Training paths
Role in parties/raids
NPC recognition
Profession synergies
```

---

# 2. Recommended Core Classes

Use a clear set of classes with strong identities.

```text
Warrior
Rogue
Ranger
Mage
Priest
Paladin
Necromancer
Druid
Bard
Monk
Engineer
Summoner
```

You do not need all of these at launch, but the system should support them.

---

# 3. Class Roles

Each class can support one or more roles.

```text
Tank
Healer
Damage Dealer
Support
Controller
Summoner
Scout
Crafter/Utility
Hybrid
```

Important rule:

```text
A class can have multiple possible roles, but each specialization should have a clear primary role.
```

Example:

```text
Paladin:
- Protection Paladin: Tank
- Devotion Paladin: Healer/Support
- Wrath Paladin: Damage Dealer

Druid:
- Guardian: Tank
- Restoration: Healer
- Wildshape: Melee Damage
- Naturecaller: Support/Control
```

---

# 4. Class Examples

## Warrior

```text
Role:
Tank or melee damage.

Primary Attributes:
Strength, Constitution, Willpower.

Armor:
Medium and heavy armor.

Weapons:
Swords, axes, maces, shields, spears, great weapons.

Resource:
Rage, stamina, or momentum.

Combat Style:
Direct physical combat, blocking, taunting, stunning, cleaving, charging.

World Interactions:
Break weak doors, lift heavy objects, intimidate NPCs, train militia, lead charges.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Shield Bash
Power Strike
Battle Cry
Taunt
Cleave
Charge
Last Stand
Guard Ally
```

---

## Rogue

```text
Role:
Melee damage, stealth, control, infiltration.

Primary Attributes:
Dexterity, Perception, Luck.

Armor:
Light armor.

Weapons:
Daggers, short swords, bows, throwing knives, poisons.

Resource:
Energy, combo points, focus.

Combat Style:
Stealth, critical strikes, poison, evasion, backstab, traps, escapes.

World Interactions:
Lockpick doors, disarm traps, pickpocket, sneak through restricted areas, detect hidden caches.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Backstab
Vanish
Poison Blade
Pick Lock
Disarm Trap
Shadowstep
Smoke Bomb
Cheap Shot
```

---

## Ranger

```text
Role:
Ranged damage, tracking, survival, animal companion.

Primary Attributes:
Dexterity, Perception, Wisdom.

Armor:
Light and medium armor.

Weapons:
Bows, crossbows, spears, knives, traps.

Resource:
Focus or stamina.

Combat Style:
Ranged attacks, traps, tracking, mobility, animal support.

World Interactions:
Track footprints, tame animals, forage food, detect ambushes, navigate wilderness.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Aimed Shot
Multi-Shot
Bear Trap
Track Beast
Tame Animal
Camouflage
Hunter’s Mark
Eagle Eye
```

---

## Mage

```text
Role:
Spell damage, control, utility.

Primary Attributes:
Intelligence, Willpower, Perception.

Armor:
Cloth armor.

Weapons:
Staff, wand, orb, spell focus.

Resource:
Mana.

Combat Style:
Elemental spells, shields, teleportation, crowd control, area damage.

World Interactions:
Read magical runes, identify artifacts, open sealed doors, stabilize magic, solve arcane puzzles.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Fireball
Frost Barrier
Lightning Bolt
Teleport
Arcane Lock
Mana Shield
Meteor
Polymorph
```

```text
Role:
Healer, support, holy damage.

Primary Attributes:
Wisdom, Charisma, Willpower.

Armor:
Cloth or light armor.

Weapons:
Staff, mace, holy symbol, tome.

Resource:
Mana, faith, devotion.

Combat Style:
Healing, shields, blessings, cleansing, anti-undead magic.

World Interactions:
Bless NPCs, heal sickness, calm spirits, perform rituals, detect corruption.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Heal
Blessing
Cleanse
Holy Light
Resurrection
Sanctuary
Prayer of Protection
Turn Undead
```

---

## Paladin

```text
Role:
Tank, healer, support, lawful warrior.

Primary Attributes:
Strength, Wisdom, Charisma, Constitution.

Armor:
Heavy armor.

Weapons:
Sword, mace, shield, holy weapon.

Resource:
Faith, conviction, mana, holy power.

Combat Style:
Defensive melee, holy magic, protection, justice, group support.

World Interactions:
Invoke legal authority, protect civilians, judge criminals, bless locations, resist corruption.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Smite
Shield of Faith
Judgment
Lay on Hands
Holy Shield
Consecrate
Oath Strike
Divine Challenge
```

---

## Necromancer

```text
Role:
Summoner, damage over time, control, dark magic.

Primary Attributes:
Intelligence, Willpower, Constitution.

Armor:
Cloth or light armor.

Weapons:
Staff, dagger, skull focus, tome.

Resource:
Mana, souls, bone charges, corruption.

Combat Style:
Curses, undead summons, life drain, corpse magic, fear.

World Interactions:
Speak with dead, raise temporary servants, detect death magic, corrupt objects, interact with spirits.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Raise Skeleton
Life Drain
Curse of Weakness
Bone Armor
Corpse Explosion
Soul Harvest
Fear
Speak With Dead
```

---

## Druid

```text
Role:
Healer, shapeshifter, nature damage, support.

Primary Attributes:
Wisdom, Constitution, Dexterity.

Armor:
Leather or natural armor.

Weapons:
Staff, sickle, spear, claw forms.

Resource:
Mana, nature energy, spirit energy.

Combat Style:
Healing, roots, shapeshifting, animal forms, nature spells.

World Interactions:
Speak with animals, grow plants, cleanse corruption, calm beasts, restore damaged nature.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Rejuvenation
Entangling Roots
Bear Form
Wolf Form
Nature’s Grasp
Cleanse Land
Thorn Shield
Call Storm
```

---

## Bard

```text
Role:
Support, social manipulation, buffs, debuffs, utility.

Primary Attributes:
Charisma, Dexterity, Wisdom.

Armor:
Light armor.

Weapons:
Rapier, dagger, bow, instrument.

Resource:
Inspiration, rhythm, mana.

Combat Style:
Songs, buffs, debuffs, charm, morale, illusions.

World Interactions:
Perform for crowds, gather rumors, persuade nobles, calm mobs, inspire workers, unlock social quests.
```

Example abilities:

```text
Song of Courage
Mocking Verse
Charm
Inspiring Tune
Discordant Note
Calm Crowd
Rumor Song
Heroic Ballad
```

---

# 5. Subclass / Specialization System

A class should branch into specializations.

Specializations allow different builds without needing entirely new classes.

Example:

```text
Warrior:
- Guardian: Tank
- Berserker: Melee damage
- Warlord: Support commander

Mage:
- Pyromancer: Fire damage
- Cryomancer: Ice control
- Arcanist: Utility and burst magic

Rogue:
- Assassin: Burst damage
- Trickster: Control and evasion
- Shadowblade: Stealth magic

Priest:
- Life Priest: Healing
- Light Priest: Damage/support
- Oracle: Utility and foresight
```

Rules:

```text
Specialization should unlock around level 10 to 20.
Player should understand what each specialization does.
Specialization should change abilities, passives, and role.
Players should be able to respec, but not abuse instant switching in combat.
```

---

# 6. Skill System Overview

A **skill** is something the player improves over time.

Skill categories:

```text
Combat Skills
Weapon Skills
Armor Skills
Magic Skills
Profession Skills
Gathering Skills
Crafting Skills
Social Skills
Exploration Skills
Survival Skills
Movement Skills
Stealth Skills
Leadership Skills
```

A skill should answer:

```text
What does this skill do?
How does it improve?
What does it unlock?
What actions give progress?
What actions do not give progress?
What abilities or bonuses depend on it?
Can it be trained?
Can it decay?
Can it be respecced?
```

---

# 7. Combat Skills

Combat skills improve fighting.

Examples:

```text
Melee Combat
Ranged Combat
Shield Use
Dual Wielding
Heavy Weapons
Light Weapons
Critical Strikes
Blocking
Dodging
Parrying
Tactical Awareness
Battle Focus
```

Effects:

```text
More accuracy
More damage
Better block chance
Better parry timing
Reduced stamina cost
New combat abilities
Weapon mastery bonuses
```

---

# 8. Weapon Skills

Each weapon type can have its own skill.

```text
Sword Mastery
Axe Mastery
Mace Mastery
Spear Mastery
Dagger Mastery
Bow Mastery
Crossbow Mastery
Staff Mastery
Wand Mastery
Shield Mastery
Unarmed Mastery
Great Weapon Mastery
```

Weapon skill improves:

```text
Accuracy with that weapon
Damage with that weapon
Attack speed
Critical chance
Special weapon abilities
Reduced durability loss
Better combo options
```

Example:

```text
Sword Mastery 1:
Can equip basic swords.

Sword Mastery 25:
Unlocks Riposte.

Sword Mastery 50:
+5% sword damage.

Sword Mastery 75:
Unlocks Whirlwind Slash.

Sword Mastery 100:
Master passive: Sword attacks can reset parry cooldown.
```

---

# 9. Armor Skills

Armor skills define defense style.

```text
Cloth Armor
Light Armor
Medium Armor
Heavy Armor
Shield Use
Evasion
Endurance
```

Armor skill effects:

```text
Reduced movement penalty
Improved defense
Improved resistance
Reduced stamina cost
Better dodge/block/parry
Unlocks armor passives
```

Example:

```text
Heavy Armor 1:
Can equip basic heavy armor.

Heavy Armor 30:
-10% movement penalty from heavy armor.

Heavy Armor 60:
+5% physical damage reduction.

Heavy Armor 100:
Cannot be critically hit by enemies below your level unless special mechanic.
```

---

# 10. Magic Skill System

Magic should have schools.

Magic schools:

```text
Fire
Ice
Lightning
Earth
Water
Wind
Arcane
Holy
Dark
Nature
Poison
Blood
Illusion
Necromancy
Summoning
Time
Space
Spirit
```

Each magic school improves:

```text
Spell power in that school
Mana efficiency
Spell accuracy
Critical chance
Spell unlocks
Resistance penetration
Special passive effects
Ritual access
World interactions
```

Example:

```text
Fire Magic 1:
Spark, Ignite.

Fire Magic 25:
Fireball.

Fire Magic 50:
Flame Shield.

Fire Magic 75:
Meteor Strike.

Fire Magic 100:
Mastery: Burning enemies take increased fire damage from you.
```

# 13. Social Skills

Social skills should matter in an MMO, especially with NPCs.

```text
Persuasion
Intimidation
Deception
Diplomacy
Bartering
Leadership
Performance
Etiquette
Streetwise
Interrogation
Teaching
```

Social skill effects:

```text
Better dialogue options
Better vendor prices
Better quest outcomes
Recruitment options
Crowd control in social events
Reduced bribe costs
Access to nobles/criminals/factions
More peaceful solutions
```

Example:

```text
Persuasion 30:
Can convince minor guards.

Persuasion 60:
Can negotiate better quest rewards.

Persuasion 90:
Can prevent some fights through dialogue.
```

```text
Perception
```

Effects:

```text
Find hidden doors
Detect traps
Track enemies
Read maps
Discover secret areas
Open locked containers
Find rare resources
Survive harsh environments
```

---

# 15. Skill Progression Methods

Skills can improve through multiple methods.

```text
Use-based progression
XP-based progression
Trainer-based progression
Book-based learning
Quest-based learning
Mentor-based learning
Crafting/gathering practice
Class milestone unlocks
Reputation unlocks
Guild training
Achievement unlocks
```

Recommended:

```text
Use hybrid progression.

Core class abilities unlock by level.
Skill mastery improves by use.
Advanced abilities require trainers, quests, books, or milestones.
```

---

# 16. Use-Based Skill Progression

Players improve by doing the thing.

Examples:

```text
Using swords increases Sword Mastery.
Casting fire spells increases Fire Magic.
Mining ore increases Mining.
Persuading NPCs increases Persuasion.
Picking locks increases Lockpicking.
Crafting potions increases Alchemy.
```

Rules:

```text
Only meaningful actions give progress.
Fighting training dummies should give reduced progress after a point.
Very low-level enemies should not give full combat skill progress.
Repeated trivial crafting should give reduced progress.
AFK actions should not give progress.
```

Bad:

```text
Player swings at a wall for 12 hours and maxes Sword Mastery.
```

Good:

```text
Player gains Sword Mastery by fighting real enemies near their level, completing warrior training, and using sword abilities in combat.
```

---

# 17. Skill XP Formula

Example:

```text
Skill XP Gained =
Base Action XP
× Difficulty Modifier
× Relevance Modifier
× Success Modifier
× Anti-Abuse Modifier
```

Difficulty modifier:

```text
Trivial action: x0.1
Easy action: x0.5
Normal action: x1.0
Hard action: x1.5
Elite action: x2.0
```

Success modifier:

```text
Failed but meaningful attempt: x0.25
Partial success: x0.5
Success: x1.0
Exceptional success: x1.25
```

---

# 18. Skill Level Ranges

Use clear skill levels.

Example:

```text
0: Untrained
1–24: Novice
25–49: Apprentice
50–74: Adept
75–99: Expert
100–124: Master
125+: Grandmaster / Legendary
```

Or simpler:

```text
Skill Level 1–100
```

Recommended:

```text
Use 1–100 for readability.
Use titles for flavor.
```

---

# 19. Skill Milestones

Skills should unlock bonuses at milestones.

Example:

```text
Skill 10:
Basic passive.

Skill 25:
New ability or recipe.

Skill 50:
Major passive or advanced action.

Skill 75:
Special technique.

Skill 100:
Mastery bonus or signature ability.
```

Example Sword Mastery:

```text
10: +2% sword accuracy
25: Unlock Riposte
50: +5% sword damage
75: Unlock Whirlwind Slash
100: Sword Mastery passive
```

---

# 20. Ability System Overview

Abilities are actions the player can use.

Ability types:

```text
Basic attack
Active ability
Passive ability
Toggle ability
Channeled ability
Cast-time spell
Instant ability
Ultimate ability
Reaction ability
Combo ability
Movement ability
Defensive ability
Utility ability
Profession ability
Social ability
World interaction ability
```

Each ability should define:

```text
Ability ID
Name
Class requirement
Skill requirement
Level requirement
Resource cost
Cooldown
Cast time
Range
Target type
Effect
Scaling
Animation
Sound
Visual effect
Interrupt rules
PvP rules
Upgrade path
Tooltip
Server validation rules
```

---

# 21. Ability Data Structure

```json
{
  "ability_id": "warrior_shield_bash",
  "name": "Shield Bash",
  "class": "Warrior",
  "required_level": 5,
  "required_skills": {
    "shield_mastery": 10
  },
  "resource_type": "stamina",
  "resource_cost": 20,
  "cooldown_seconds": 12,
  "cast_time_seconds": 0,
  "range": 2,
  "target_type": "enemy",
  "effects": [
    {
      "type": "damage",
      "scaling": "attack_points",
      "coefficient": 0.75
    },
    {
      "type": "interrupt",
      "duration_seconds": 0
    },
    {
      "type": "stun",
      "duration_seconds": 2
    }
  ],
  "pvp_rules": {
    "stun_duration_multiplier": 0.5,
    "affected_by_diminishing_returns": true
  }
}
```

---

# 22. Ability Unlock Rules

Abilities can unlock from:

```text
Leveling up
Class trainer
Skill milestone
Specialization selection
Talent tree
Quest reward
Spell scroll
Skill book
Faction vendor
Boss drop
Guild training
Hidden teacher
World discovery
Achievement
```

Recommended:

```text
Core abilities unlock automatically by level.
Advanced abilities require trainers, quests, books, or specialization.
Rare abilities come from exploration, bosses, factions, or class quests.
```

---

# 23. Ability Ranks

Abilities can improve through ranks.

Example:

```text
Fireball Rank 1:
120 fire damage.

Fireball Rank 2:
180 fire damage.

Fireball Rank 3:
240 fire damage and small burn.

Fireball Rank 4:
Higher damage, larger radius.

Fireball Rank 5:
Can be modified by fire runes.
```

Rules:

```text
Ranks should not require excessive trainer visits.
Higher ranks should improve the ability clearly.
Avoid bloating the spellbook with too many duplicate ranks.
Use automatic scaling if simpler.
```

Recommended:

```text
Use ability scaling plus occasional meaningful upgrades instead of 20 tiny ranks.
```

---

# 24. Talent Tree System

Talents let players customize within a class or specialization.

Talent categories:

```text
Damage talents
Defense talents
Healing talents
Utility talents
Mobility talents
Control talents
Resource talents
Crafting talents
Social talents
Exploration talents
```

Talent rules:

```text
Talents should create meaningful choices.
Avoid obvious mandatory picks in every row.
Avoid trap talents that are always bad.
Let players preview builds.
Allow respecs.
Do not allow talent switching during combat.
```

Example talent row:

```text
Warrior Level 15 Talent Choice:

1. Iron Guard:
Shield Block lasts 2 seconds longer.

2. Crushing Blows:
Heavy attacks reduce enemy armor.

3. Rallying Cry:
Battle Cry also grants allies temporary HP.
```

---

# 25. Passive Ability Rules

Passive abilities should change playstyle.

Examples:

```text
+5% sword damage
Fire spells apply burn
Healing spells leave a small heal-over-time effect
Dodging restores energy
Blocking generates rage
Critical hits reduce cooldown
Animals are less likely to attack
Vendors offer small discount
```

Rules:

```text
Passives should be visible in character sheet.
Passive effects should be understandable.
Passive stacking must be capped.
Passives should not create infinite loops.
```

Bad passive:

```text
Critical hits increase critical chance, which creates infinite scaling with no cap.
```

Good passive:

```text
Critical hits have a 20% chance to restore 5 energy. This can occur once every 5 seconds.
```

---

# 26. Ultimate Abilities

Ultimate abilities are powerful, limited abilities.

Rules:

```text
Long cooldown.
High resource cost or charge requirement.
Strong visual/audio effect.
Cannot be spammed.
Should feel class-defining.
PvP version may be reduced.
Bosses may resist some effects.
```

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Avatar of Battle — huge defense and threat for 12 seconds.

Mage:
Meteor Storm — large fire area damage.

Priest:
Divine Intervention — prevents party wipe once.

Rogue:
Shadow Execution — massive strike against low-HP target.

Druid:
Nature’s Renewal — restores allies and clears poison.

Necromancer:
Army of Bones — summons temporary undead army.
```

---

# 27. Resource Systems

Classes should use different resources.

Common resources:

```text
Mana
Stamina
Energy
Rage
Focus
Faith
Combo Points
Souls
Heat
Ammo
Inspiration
Nature Energy
Runes
Shield Charge
```

Resource rules:

```text
Resource should match class fantasy.
Resource should create meaningful pacing.
Resource should prevent ability spam.
Resource should be readable in UI.
Resource should recover in a clear way.
```

Examples:

```text
Warrior Rage:
Generated by taking/dealing damage.
Spent on powerful attacks.

Rogue Energy:
Regenerates quickly over time.
Spent on attacks.

Mage Mana:
Large pool, slower recovery.
Spent on spells.

Priest Faith:
Generated by healing, prayer, or holy actions.
Spent on miracles.

Necromancer Souls:
Generated when enemies die or from life drain.
Spent on summons/curses.

Bard Inspiration:
Generated by songs, combos, and ally actions.
Spent on powerful support abilities.
```

---

# 28. Class-Specific Resource Examples

## Warrior Rage

```text
Starts at 0.
Gained by attacking, blocking, and taking damage.
Decays out of combat.
Spent on heavy strikes, taunts, defensive bursts.
```

## Rogue Energy + Combo Points

```text
Energy regenerates over time.
Basic attacks spend energy.
Certain attacks generate combo points.
Finishers spend combo points.
```

## Mage Mana

```text
Mana regenerates slowly.
Spells cost mana.
Mana potions and rest restore mana.
High-cost spells need planning.
```

## Paladin Conviction

```text
Generated by protecting allies, blocking, healing, and lawful combat.
Spent on judgments, shields, and holy bursts.
```

## Necromancer Souls

```text
Generated from nearby deaths or specific spells.
Spent on undead summons, curses, and dark rituals.
Cannot be generated from harmless civilians without legal/moral consequences.
```

---

# 29. Ability Scaling

Abilities should scale from:

```text
Player level
Relevant attribute
Weapon damage
Spell power
Skill level
Ability rank
Gear bonuses
Buffs/debuffs
Target resistance
Level difference
```

Example melee formula:

```text
Final Damage =
Base Ability Damage
+ Weapon Damage
+ Strength Scaling
+ Skill Scaling
+ Buffs
- Target Mitigation
```

Example spell formula:

```text
Final Spell Damage =
Base Spell Damage
+ Intelligence × Coefficient
+ Spell Power × Coefficient
+ Magic School Skill Bonus
- Target Resistance
```

---

# 30. Skill and Ability Scaling Rules

Good scaling:

```text
Level gives baseline power.
Attributes shape build.
Skills improve specialization.
Gear improves performance.
Abilities create playstyle.
Player skill matters through timing, positioning, and choices.
```

Bad scaling:

```text
Only level matters.
Only gear matters.
Only one attribute matters.
One ability becomes best forever.
Low-level skills become useless.
```

---

# 31. Ability Loadouts

Players should not equip every ability at once.

Recommended:

```text
Players may know many abilities.
Players equip a limited number to action bars.
Players can save loadouts.
Changing loadouts is allowed in safe areas, towns, or out of combat.
Changing loadouts during combat is blocked.
```

Example:

```text
Known abilities: 80
Active combat slots: 10
Utility slots: 4
Passive slots: 3
Ultimate slot: 1
Profession slots: 3
```

This creates meaningful build choices.

---

# 32. Loadout Types

```text
Solo PvE
Dungeon Tank
Raid Damage
PvP
Gathering
Crafting
Stealth
Healing
Support
Exploration
```

Rules:

```text
Loadouts should save abilities, talents, gear sets, and possibly hotbars.
Switching should validate gear, class, specialization, and cooldown rules.
```

---

# 33. Class Quests

Classes should have unique quests.

Class quests can unlock:

```text
Abilities
Specializations
Class mounts
Class cosmetics
Class titles
Class trainers
Class weapons
Advanced mechanics
Class storylines
```

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Prove yourself in a trial of endurance.

Mage:
Stabilize a broken leyline.

Priest:
Cleanse a haunted shrine.

Rogue:
Infiltrate a noble estate.

Ranger:
Track and tame a rare beast.

Necromancer:
Bind a restless spirit.
```

Rules:

```text
Class quests should teach class identity.
Class quests should introduce important mechanics.
Class quests should not block basic progression too harshly.
```

---

# 34. Trainers and Teachers

Players can learn from trainers.

Trainer types:

```text
Class trainer
Weapon trainer
Magic school trainer
Profession trainer
Faction trainer
Secret master
Guild trainer
NPC mentor
```

Trainer rules:

```text
Trainers teach abilities if requirements are met.
Some trainers require reputation.
Secret trainers require discovery.
Illegal trainers may teach forbidden skills.
Training may cost gold, currency, quest completion, or materials.
```

Example:

```text
Fire Mage Trainer:
Teaches fire spells up to Expert rank.

Forbidden Pyromancer:
Teaches dangerous flame rituals.
Requires low legal standing or secret quest.
```

---

# 35. Skill Books and Scrolls

Books and scrolls can teach skills or abilities.

Types:

```text
Skill book
Spell scroll
Combat manual
Crafting recipe
Ancient tablet
Forbidden tome
Guild manual
Profession blueprint
```

Rules:

```text
Book may be consumed on use.
Book may require class/skill/level.
Book may be tradeable or bound.
If player already knows it, show warning.
Rare books should have known sources or clues.
```

---

# 36. Respec Rules

Players need to fix mistakes.

Respec types:

```text
Attribute respec
Talent respec
Specialization respec
Ability loadout change
Profession respec
Class change, optional and rare
```

Rules:

```text
Allow talent/attribute respec through trainer, shrine, item, or town service.
Charge gold or currency, but do not make it brutal.
Do not allow respec during combat.
Do not allow respec to bypass cooldowns.
Class change should be rare, expensive, or not allowed depending on design.
```

Recommended:

```text
Talents:
Easy to respec in towns.

Attributes:
Moderate cost.

Specialization:
Moderate cost, out of combat.

Class:
Not allowed or very limited.
```

---

# 37. Multi-Classing

Optional system.

Multi-classing allows players to combine classes.

Benefits:

```text
More build freedom.
More roleplay.
More unique combinations.
```

Risks:

```text
Harder to balance.
Players find broken combos.
Class identity becomes weaker.
New players get confused.
```

Recommended approach:

```text
Use subclasses/specializations first.
If multi-classing exists, limit it carefully.
```

Possible rule:

```text
Player chooses one primary class and one secondary discipline.
Primary class gives full abilities.
Secondary gives limited utility/passives.
Ultimate abilities remain primary-class only.
```

Example:

```text
Warrior + Mage:
Spellblade.

Rogue + Ranger:
Stalker.

Priest + Warrior:
Paladin-like build.

Mage + Necromancer:
Death Arcanist.
```

---

# 38. Hybrid Classes

Hybrid classes should be useful but not best at everything.

Rule:

```text
A hybrid can perform multiple roles, but must choose build/loadout to specialize.
```

Bad hybrid:

```text
Paladin deals top damage, tanks best, heals best, and has best utility.
```

Good hybrid:

```text
Paladin can tank or heal depending on specialization, but cannot be the best at all roles at once.
```

---

# 39. NPC Class System

NPCs should also have classes or archetypes.

NPC classes:

```text
Guard
Bandit
Archer
Mage
Healer
Priest
Necromancer
Beastmaster
Assassin
Knight
Berserker
Shaman
Merchant
Civilian
Crafter
Boss
Elite
```

NPC class affects:

```text
HP
Attack
Defense
Abilities
AI behavior
Loot
Dialogue
Faction role
Combat tactics
```

Example:

```text
Bandit Rogue:
Uses stealth, backstab, poison, flee.

Town Guard:
Uses shield bash, arrest, call reinforcements.

Cult Mage:
Uses fireball, fear, summon imp.

Priest NPC:
Heals allies, cleanses debuffs, avoids frontline.
```

---

# 40. NPC Ability Rules

NPCs should use abilities intelligently.

Rules:

```text
NPCs should not spam every ability instantly.
NPCs need cooldowns.
NPC abilities should be telegraphed if dangerous.
Boss abilities need clear warnings.
NPC healers should prioritize wounded allies.
NPC tanks should protect important NPCs.
NPC rogues should flank or ambush.
```

---

# 41. Skill Checks in the World

Skills and abilities should affect the world.

Examples:

```text
Lockpicking:
Open locked doors/chests.

Fire Magic:
Light braziers, burn webs, melt ice.

Ice Magic:
Freeze water, slow mechanisms.

Strength:
Move boulders, break doors.

Persuasion:
Convince NPCs.

Medicine:
Treat wounded NPCs.

Carpentry:
Repair bridge.

Mining:
Clear collapsed tunnel.

Necromancy:
Question corpse.

Druidism:
Grow vines, calm beasts.
```

Rules:

```text
World skill checks should have alternate paths.
Do not block main story behind one class unless class-specific quest.
Reward different builds with different solutions.
```

---

# 42. Power Progression

Powers are major character growth paths.

Power sources:

```text
Class power
Magic power
Divine power
Corruption power
Nature power
Technology power
Weapon mastery
Bloodline power
Artifact power
Faction power
Guild power
Reputation power
```

Power progression can unlock:

```text
New abilities
Passive bonuses
Transformations
Special dialogue
Visual effects
Faction reactions
World interactions
Titles
Risks/consequences
```

Example:

```text
Corruption Power:
Grants dark abilities.
Makes holy NPCs distrust player.
May unlock forbidden spells.
May cause guards or priests to react.
```

---

# 43. Artifact Power

Special items can grow with the player.

Artifact rules:

```text
Artifact has its own XP or milestones.
Artifact unlocks powers through quests, kills, achievements, or sacrifices.
Artifact should not create infinite power gaps.
Artifact may have moral or faction consequences.
```

Example:

```text
Blade of the Dawn:
Level 1: Holy damage.
Level 2: Bonus vs undead.
Level 3: Grants Radiant Slash.
Level 4: Can cleanse minor corruption.
Level 5: Unlocks class quest.
```

---

# 44. Ability Upgrade Choices

Instead of only increasing numbers, upgrades can change behavior.

Example Fireball upgrades:

```text
Bigger explosion
Lower mana cost
Faster cast
Adds burn
Splits into smaller fireballs
Leaves fire on ground
Deals less damage but knocks back
Becomes blue flame that pierces resistance
```

Rule:

```text
Ability upgrades should support playstyle differences, not just bigger damage.
```

---

# 45. Ability Synergy

Abilities can synergize.

Examples:

```text
Rogue applies poison.
Ranger fires marked shot for bonus damage.

Mage freezes enemy.
Warrior shatters frozen target.

Priest shields ally.
Paladin converts shield into holy burst.

Necromancer curses enemy.
Warlock/dark mage drains more life from cursed target.

Druid roots enemies.
Mage area spell hits rooted group.
```

Rules:

```text
Synergies should reward teamwork.
Synergies should be readable.
Synergies should have limits.
Avoid infinite loops.
Avoid mandatory one-combo meta for all content.
```

---

# 46. Cooldown Rules

Cooldowns control ability pacing.

Cooldown types:

```text
Global cooldown
Ability cooldown
Shared category cooldown
Charge-based cooldown
Daily cooldown
Encounter cooldown
Out-of-combat cooldown
PvP-only cooldown
```

Rules:

```text
Powerful abilities need longer cooldowns.
Basic abilities need short or no cooldown.
Interrupts and crowd control need careful cooldowns.
Cooldowns should be visible.
Cooldowns should persist through relog if needed.
```

---

# 47. Interrupt and Silence Rules

Abilities can be interrupted.

Interrupt rules:

```text
Cast-time abilities can be interrupted.
Channeled abilities can be interrupted.
Instant abilities usually cannot be interrupted.
Some bosses are immune to certain interrupts.
Interrupts may lock a spell school temporarily.
```

PvP rule:

```text
Interrupts should matter but not completely prevent a player from playing for too long.
```

---

# 48. Crowd Control Rules

Crowd control includes:

```text
Stun
Root
Slow
Fear
Charm
Sleep
Silence
Disarm
Knockback
Taunt
Blind
Polymorph
Freeze
```

Rules:

```text
Normal enemies can be controlled.
Elites may resist.
Bosses are immune or partially affected.
PvP needs diminishing returns.
No player should be unable to act for too long.
```

---

# 49. Diminishing Returns

For PvP and some PvE:

```text
First control: 100% duration
Second within window: 50%
Third within window: 25%
Fourth: temporary immunity
```

Apply to:

```text
Stuns
Fears
Charms
Roots
Silences
Sleeps
Knockbacks
```

---

# 50. Class Balance Rules

Balance does not mean every class is identical.

Good balance means:

```text
Every class has strengths.
Every class has weaknesses.
Every class has useful group value.
Every class has viable solo play.
Every class has counters.
No class dominates every mode.
No class is unwanted in all content.
```

Balance should consider:

```text
Solo PvE
Dungeons
Raids
PvP
World events
Economy
Crafting
Exploration
Social gameplay
```

---

# 51. Class Weakness Rules

Each class should have weaknesses.

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Strong defense, limited ranged options.

Mage:
High damage/control, fragile if caught.

Rogue:
High burst/stealth, weaker in sustained frontal fights.

Priest:
Great healing/support, limited mobility or direct burst.

Ranger:
Strong range/tracking, weaker when trapped in melee.

Necromancer:
Strong summons/curses, vulnerable if interrupted or without corpses/souls.
```

Weaknesses create teamwork and counterplay.

---

# 52. Avoiding Trap Builds

Players should be able to make creative builds without ruining their character.

Rules:

```text
No attribute should be completely useless.
Every class should have recommended builds.
The UI should warn when a choice is unusual.
Players should be able to respec.
New players should get guidance.
```

Example warning:

```text
You are investing heavily in Intelligence as a Warrior.
This may support rare spellblade builds, but it will not improve most Warrior abilities.
Continue?
```

---

# 53. Build Recommendations

The game should help players.

Recommended builds:

```text
Beginner Tank
Beginner Healer
Beginner Damage
Solo Explorer
PvP Duelist
Raid Support
Crafter Build
Gatherer Build
Stealth Build
```

Rules:

```text
Recommended builds should be optional.
Do not force players into one meta.
Allow advanced players to customize.
```

---

# 54. Skill Cap Rules

Decide whether players can master everything.

Options:

## Unlimited Mastery

```text
Players can eventually max all skills.
Good for casual freedom.
Harder for economy specialization.
```

## Skill Cap

```text
Players have limited total skill points.
Encourages specialization.
Creates player economy.
```

## Soft Cap

Recommended.

```text
Players can learn many skills.
Mastering many takes much longer.
Specialization gives bonuses.
Respec is possible but not instant.
```

---

# 55. Profession Specialization

Professions should have specialization paths.

Example Blacksmithing:

```text
Weaponsmith
Armorsmith
Toolsmith
Siege Smith
Runesmith
Repair Master
```

Example Alchemy:

```text
Healing Potions
Poisons
Elixirs
Explosives
Transmutation
Magical Reagents
```

Rules:

```text
Players can specialize to become valuable.
No one profession path should be mandatory for all players.
Guilds benefit from diverse specialists.
```

---

# 56. Ability Use Restrictions

Abilities can be restricted by:

```text
Class
Specialization
Level
Skill
Weapon equipped
Armor type
Resource
Cooldown
Zone
PvP mode
Silence/stun/death
Target type
Line of sight
Range
Quest state
Moral/corruption state
```

Examples:

```text
Shield Bash requires shield.
Backstab requires dagger and behind target.
Fireball requires line of sight.
Resurrection cannot be used in some PvP modes.
Pick Lock requires lockpicking tool.
Raise Skeleton requires corpse or soul resource.
```

---

# 57. Ability Targeting Rules

Target types:

```text
Self
Ally
Enemy
Ground area
Cone
Line
Circle
Chain
Corpse
Object
Building
Pet/summon
Party
Raid
NPC only
Player only
```

Rules:

```text
Target must be valid.
Target must be in range.
Line of sight must be checked if required.
Ground targeting must avoid invalid terrain.
Building abilities must obey building/PvP rules.
```

---

# 58. Ability and Class UI

The UI should show:

```text
Class
Specialization
Level
Skill levels
Known abilities
Locked abilities
Requirements
Resource costs
Cooldowns
Talent choices
Loadouts
Recommended builds
Respec options
Weapon/armor restrictions
World interaction abilities
```

Ability tooltip should show:

```text
Name
Type
Cost
Cooldown
Range
Cast time
Effect
Scaling
Requirements
PvP changes
Upgrade path
Combo effects
```

Example:

```text
Shield Bash
Warrior Ability
Requires: Shield equipped
Cost: 20 Stamina
Cooldown: 12 sec
Range: Melee

Deals moderate damage, interrupts casting, and stuns normal enemies for 2 seconds.
PvP: Stun duration reduced by 50%.
```

---

# 59. Skill Journal

Players need a skill journal.

Skill journal should show:

```text
Skill name
Current level
Progress to next level
How to improve it
Unlock milestones
Current bonuses
Trainers
Related abilities
Related professions
Known recipes
Recommended activities
```

Example:

```text
Mining Level 34
Progress: 620 / 900

How to improve:
- Mine Iron Veins
- Complete mining contracts
- Gather dense stone
- Train with a mining instructor

Next unlock:
Mining 40: Track Silver Veins
```

---

# 60. Progression Notifications

When a skill improves:

```text
Skill increased.
New milestone unlocked.
New ability learned.
New recipe learned.
New dialogue/world interaction unlocked.
```

Example:

```text
Sword Mastery increased to 50.
New bonus unlocked: +5% sword damage.
```

Do not spam too much. Combine repeated gains when needed.

---

# 61. Class Progression Example

Example Warrior progression:

```text
Level 1:
Basic Attack, Power Strike

Level 2:
Block

Level 3:
Charge

Level 5:
Shield Bash

Level 8:
Battle Cry

Level 10:
Choose specialization: Guardian, Berserker, Warlord

Level 15:
First talent row

Level 20:
Class quest unlocks signature ability

Level 30:
Advanced specialization ability

Level 40:
Ultimate ability

Level 50:
Master class passive
```

---

# 62. Skill Progression Example

Example Lockpicking:

```text
Level 1:
Open simple locks.

Level 10:
Detect basic lock traps.

Level 25:
Open iron locks.

Level 40:
Open noble house locks.

Level 60:
Disarm lock alarms.

Level 80:
Open magical locks with special tools.

Level 100:
Master Locksmith: chance to open locks without consuming tools.
```

World effects:

```text
Unlock hidden rooms.
Access alternate quest paths.
Open treasure chests.
Break into buildings illegally.
Increase risk of legal penalties if caught.
```

---

# 63. Power Progression Example

Example Fire Magic:

```text
Fire Magic 1:
Spark

Fire Magic 10:
Ignite

Fire Magic 25:
Fireball

Fire Magic 40:
Flame Wall

Fire Magic 60:
Burning Dash

Fire Magic 75:
Meteor

Fire Magic 100:
Inferno Mastery
```

World effects:

```text
Light torches.
Burn webs.
Melt ice.
Intimidate certain enemies.
Accidentally cause fire damage if used carelessly.
Trigger legal penalty if used in town unlawfully.
```

---

# 64. Class and Reputation Interaction

Classes should affect how NPCs react.

Examples:

```text
Priests are welcomed in temples.
Necromancers may be feared or banned in holy towns.
Paladins may be trusted by guards.
Rogues may be watched by merchants.
Bards may be welcomed in taverns.
Druids may be respected in forest villages.
Mages may be feared in anti-magic regions.
Warriors may be recruited by militias.
```

Rules:

```text
Class reactions should add flavor and options.
Do not make a class unplayable in major areas.
Let reputation override stereotypes over time.
```

Example:

```text
A Necromancer with high Likeability may still be distrusted at first, but NPCs may say:
"I do not like your magic, but you saved this town."
```

---

# 65. Class and Legal System

Some powers may be illegal.

Illegal or restricted actions:

```text
Necromancy in holy towns
Mind control
Forbidden blood magic
Summoning demons
Theft abilities
Lockpicking private doors
Poisoning
Unlicensed magic duels
Destructive spells in public
```

Rules:

```text
Warn players before illegal ability use in lawful areas.
Witnesses can report crimes.
Guards react.
Illegal skills can still exist for outlaw gameplay.
```

Example:

```text
Warning:
Casting Raise Dead inside Harthmere is illegal.
Guards may respond if witnessed.
```

---

# 66. Class and Guild Interaction

Guilds can support class roles.

Guild features:

```text
Class mentors
Role channels
Class trainers
Guild build recommendations
Raid role assignments
Crafting specialists
Class-specific storage tabs
Training events
```

Example:

```text
Guild has a Master Blacksmith.
Warriors can request crafted shields.
Guild has a Mage Library.
Mages can learn guild-approved spells.
```

---

# 67. Class and Party/Raid Roles

Party finder should understand class roles.

Example:

```text
Warrior Guardian: Tank
Warrior Berserker: Damage
Priest Life: Healer
Mage Pyromancer: Damage
Bard Maestro: Support
Druid Restoration: Healer
```

Rules:

```text
Player must queue as a role supported by their class/specialization/loadout.
A damage build should not queue as healer without healing tools.
Role mismatch should be prevented or warned.
```

---

# 68. Skill-Based World Access

Skills can unlock world access.

Examples:

```text
Climbing 40:
Reach cliff path.

Swimming 30:
Cross river safely.

Arcane 50:
Open magical gate.

Lockpicking 60:
Enter noble vault.

Diplomacy 70:
Attend council meeting.

Mining 50:
Clear collapsed cave.

Druidism 40:
Open vine-covered path.
```

Rules:

```text
Main story should offer alternate paths.
Optional treasure/secrets can require specific skills.
Group play can allow one skilled player to help others.
```

---

# 69. Group Skill Checks

Parties can solve problems together.

Examples:

```text
One player picks lock for party.
One mage opens portal.
One warrior moves boulder.
One priest blesses party through cursed gate.
One ranger tracks target for everyone.
```

Rules:

```text
Group skill checks should reward party diversity.
Do not require every player to have the same skill.
Quest credit should update for eligible party members.
```

---

# 70. Skill Decay

Generally avoid skill decay.

If used:

```text
Use only for hardcore/survival systems.
Decay should be slow.
Decay should not remove unlocked abilities abruptly.
Decay should never punish casual players harshly.
```

Recommended:

```text
No skill decay for normal MMO.
Use soft caps, specialization, and respec costs instead.
```

---

# 71. Training Dummies and Practice

Practice should help but not be exploitable.

Rules:

```text
Training dummies can teach basics.
Training dummies give reduced skill XP.
After a skill threshold, dummies stop giving progress.
Special training courses can grant limited XP daily.
```

Example:

```text
Training dummy gives Sword Mastery XP until Sword Mastery 20.
After that, real combat is required.
```

---

# 72. Skill Catch-Up

New players or alts may need catch-up.

Catch-up options:

```text
Rested skill XP
Mentor training
Account-wide knowledge bonus
Guild training bonus
Class tutorial quests
Profession work orders
Low-level skill books
```

Rules:

```text
Catch-up should reduce boring grind.
Catch-up should not instantly max important economic skills.
```

---

# 73. Ability Balance in PvP

PvP needs separate tuning when necessary.

PvP modifiers can change:

```text
Damage
Healing
Shield strength
Crowd control duration
Cooldowns
Resource generation
Summon durability
Stealth duration
Movement effects
```

Rules:

```text
Do not ruin PvE balance just to fix PvP if separate PvP tuning is cleaner.
Tooltips should show PvP changes.
```

Example:

```text
Fireball:
PvE: 300 damage.
PvP: 240 damage.

Shield Bash:
PvE stun: 2 seconds.
PvP stun: 1 second.
```

---

# 74. Summon and Pet Ability Rules

Summoner, ranger, necromancer, and pet classes need rules.

Pet/summon stats scale from:

```text
Player level
Relevant skill
Relevant attribute
Pet level
Ability rank
Gear bonuses
Talent bonuses
```

Rules:

```text
Summons should not fully replace players.
Pets need commands.
Pet death should be recoverable.
Summons have duration or resource cost.
Pet AI must avoid pulling unwanted enemies.
Pets should not block doors or NPCs.
```

Commands:

```text
Attack
Follow
Stay
Defend
Passive
Aggressive
Assist
Return
Use special ability
```

---

# 75. Transformation Rules

Some classes may transform.

Examples:

```text
Druid bear form
Druid wolf form
Werebeast form
Lich form
Elemental form
Battle avatar
Shadow form
```

Transformation rules:

```text
Changes abilities.
Changes stats.
May change movement.
May restrict items/spells.
May change NPC reactions.
May be illegal/frightening in towns.
Cannot be abused to bypass doors/collisions.
```

Example:

```text
Bear Form:
+HP, +armor, tank abilities.
Cannot use normal weapons.
Cannot talk to most NPCs unless shifted back.
May scare civilians.
```

---

# 76. Class Starting Experience

Each class should start with a tutorial that teaches its fantasy.

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Block, strike, taunt, protect NPC.

Rogue:
Stealth, pick lock, avoid guard, backstab enemy.

Mage:
Cast spell, manage mana, interrupt ritual.

Priest:
Heal wounded NPC, cleanse poison, repel undead.

Ranger:
Track beast, use bow, set trap.

Bard:
Perform, persuade, buff ally.
```

Rules:

```text
Tutorial should teach class basics quickly.
Do not overload new players with every system at once.
```

---

# 77. Ability Acquisition Pacing

Avoid giving too many abilities too quickly.

Good pacing:

```text
Level 1:
2 to 3 basic abilities.

Levels 2–10:
One new thing every 1 to 2 levels.

Levels 10–30:
Specialization and talent choices.

Levels 30–50:
Advanced abilities, upgrades, mastery.

Endgame:
Refinements, alternate abilities, artifacts, cosmetics.
```

Bad pacing:

```text
Player starts with 25 abilities and no idea what matters.
```

---

# 78. Class Identity Outside Combat

Classes should matter outside combat.

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Train guards, intimidate bandits, break obstacles.

Rogue:
Infiltrate, steal, disarm traps, gather secrets.

Mage:
Solve arcane puzzles, teleport, identify magic.

Priest:
Heal sickness, bless towns, calm spirits.

Ranger:
Track enemies, find paths, tame animals.

Bard:
Perform, gather rumors, influence crowds.

Druid:
Restore nature, speak with animals, grow plants.

Necromancer:
Speak with dead, manipulate spirits, understand curses.
```

This makes class choice feel meaningful.

---

# 79. Class Restrictions

Restrictions can support identity.

Possible restrictions:

```text
Armor type
Weapon type
Magic school access
Faction access
Legal restrictions
Resource type
Role access
Trainer access
Ability access
```

Rules:

```text
Restrictions should make classes distinct.
Restrictions should not make players feel punished constantly.
Restrictions should be explained.
```

---

# 80. Advanced Class Evolution

At high levels, classes can evolve.

Examples:

```text
Warrior → Champion / Warlord / Juggernaut
Mage → Archmage / Elementalist / Spellblade
Rogue → Assassin / Shadowmaster / Trickster
Priest → High Priest / Oracle / Exorcist
Ranger → Beastlord / Marksman / Warden
Necromancer → Lichbinder / Bonecaller / Soulweaver
```

Rules:

```text
Class evolution should require a major quest or milestone.
It should unlock identity, not invalidate previous build.
It should be reversible only carefully, if at all.
```

---

# 81. Class Titles

Players can earn class titles.

Examples:

```text
Warrior:
Shieldbearer, Champion, Warlord.

Mage:
Adept, Arcanist, Archmage.

Rogue:
Cutpurse, Shadow, Master Thief.

Priest:
Acolyte, Cleric, High Priest.

Ranger:
Tracker, Warden, Beastmaster.

Necromancer:
Gravetouched, Bonecaller, Deathspeaker.
```

Titles can affect dialogue and reputation.

---

# 82. Ability and Skill Edge Cases

## Player Uses Ability Without Required Weapon

Rule:

```text
Block ability.
Show clear message: "Requires shield equipped."
```

## Player Changes Gear Mid-Cast

Rule:

```text
Cancel cast or block gear change.
Server validates final ability requirements.
```

## Player Learns Same Ability Twice

Rule:

```text
Block duplicate learning.
Offer compensation if scroll/book is consumed accidentally.
```

## Player Respecs While Ability on Cooldown

Rule:

```text
Cooldown persists or equivalent cooldown applies.
No cooldown reset abuse.
```

## Player Switches Loadout in Combat

Rule:

```text
Block loadout switching during combat.
```

## Player Skill Level Drops Below Requirement

Rule:

```text
Normally skill should not drop.
If temporary debuff lowers skill, ability may be disabled temporarily.
Show reason.
```

## Player Uses Illegal Ability in Town

Rule:

```text
Warn before use if possible.
If used, apply legal/witness rules.
```

## Pet Blocks Door

Rule:

```text
Pet collision should not block important doors.
Pet should move aside or become non-blocking in crowded areas.
```

## Ability Hits Through Wall

Rule:

```text
Server line-of-sight validation prevents it unless ability explicitly ignores walls.
```

## Ability Double-Casts From Lag

Rule:

```text
Use server action IDs and cooldown locks.
Process once.
```

## Player Disconnects During Cast

Rule:

```text
Cancel cast unless ability completed server-side.
```

## Ability Kills Target After Player Dies

Rule:

```text
Credit can still apply if effect was valid and contribution rules allow it.
```

---

# 83. Server Authority Rules

The server must validate all skill and ability actions.

Server validates:

```text
Player class
Player level
Specialization
Skill level
Known ability
Equipped ability
Weapon requirement
Armor requirement
Resource cost
Cooldown
Range
Line of sight
Target validity
Player state
Zone rules
PvP rules
Legal rules
Damage/healing result
Buff/debuff application
Skill XP gain
Ability unlock
Talent selection
Respec
```

The client cannot decide:

```text
Ability hit success
Damage amount
Healing amount
Skill XP gain
Ability unlocks
Cooldown reset
Resource cost
Talent ownership
Class state
Pet/summon damage
PvP modifier
```

---

# 84. Ability Use Pipeline

When player uses ability:

```text
1. Client sends ability request.
2. Server checks player is alive and able to act.
3. Server checks ability is known/equipped.
4. Server checks class/specialization/skill requirements.
5. Server checks weapon/armor/resource/cooldown.
6. Server checks target/range/line of sight.
7. Server checks PvP/legal/zone rules.
8. Server applies cost and starts cooldown.
9. Server resolves hit/crit/resistance.
10. Server applies damage/healing/effects.
11. Server grants valid skill XP if applicable.
12. Server sends result to clients.
13. Server logs important events.
```

---

# 85. Skill Gain Pipeline

When player performs a skill action:

```text
1. Server receives action result.
2. Server checks action is valid.
3. Server checks action difficulty.
4. Server checks anti-abuse rules.
5. Server calculates skill XP.
6. Server adds skill XP.
7. If threshold reached, skill level increases.
8. Server unlocks milestones if any.
9. Client shows progress notification.
10. Server logs significant unlocks.
```

---

# 86. Data Structure: Class

```json
{
  "class_id": "warrior",
  "name": "Warrior",
  "roles": ["tank", "damage"],
  "primary_attributes": ["strength", "constitution"],
  "secondary_attributes": ["willpower"],
  "allowed_armor": ["medium", "heavy"],
  "allowed_weapons": ["sword", "axe", "mace", "shield", "spear", "great_weapon"],
  "resource": "rage",
  "specializations": ["guardian", "berserker", "warlord"],
  "starting_abilities": ["basic_attack", "power_strike"],
  "world_interactions": ["break_weak_doors", "move_heavy_objects", "intimidate"]
}
```

---

# 87. Data Structure: Player Class State

```json
{
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "class_id": "warrior",
  "specialization": "guardian",
  "level": 24,
  "resource": {
    "type": "rage",
    "current": 35,
    "max": 100
  },

    "power_strike",
    "shield_bash",
    "taunt",
    "battle_cry"
  ],
  "equipped_abilities": {
    "slot_1": "power_strike",
    "slot_2": "shield_bash",
    "slot_3": "taunt",
    "slot_4": "battle_cry"
  },
  "talents": [
    "iron_guard",
    "rallying_cry"
  ]
}
```

```json

  "name": "Sword Mastery",
  "category": "weapon",
  "max_level": 100,
  "progression_type": "use_based",
  "milestones": [
    {
      "level": 10,
      "bonus": "+2% sword accuracy"
    },
    {
      "level": 25,
      "unlock_ability": "riposte"
    },
    {
      "level": 50,
      "bonus": "+5% sword damage"
    },
    {
      "level": 75,
      "unlock_ability": "whirlwind_slash"
    },
    {
      "level": 100,
      "unlock_passive": "sword_mastery_passive"
    }
  ]
```

```json
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "skills": {
    "sword_mastery": {
      "level": 52,
      "xp_current": 340,
      "xp_required_next": 700
    },
    "shield_mastery": {
      "level": 41,
      "xp_current": 120,
      "xp_required_next": 620
    },
    "mining": {
      "level": 18,
      "xp_current": 90,
      "xp_required_next": 300
    }
  }
}
```

---

# 90. Data Structure: Ability

```json
{
  "ability_id": "fireball",
  "name": "Fireball",
  "type": "active_spell",
  "class_requirements": ["mage"],
  "skill_requirements": {
    "fire_magic": 25
  },
  "level_requirement": 8,
  "resource_type": "mana",
  "resource_cost": 35,
  "cooldown_seconds": 6,
  "cast_time_seconds": 2,
  "range": 30,
  "target_type": "enemy",
  "requires_line_of_sight": true,
  "effects": [
    {
      "type": "damage",
      "damage_type": "fire",
      "base": 120,
      "scaling_attribute": "intelligence",
      "scaling_ratio": 0.8
    },
    {
      "type": "burn",
      "duration_seconds": 6,
      "tick_damage": 12
    }
  ],
  "pvp_modifiers": {
    "damage_multiplier": 0.85
  }
}
```

---

# 91. Checklist for Classes, Skills, and Abilities

Before adding a class:

```text
Does it have a clear identity?
Does it have strengths?
Does it have weaknesses?
Does it support at least one group role?
Does it have solo tools?
Does it have unique world interactions?
Does it have class quests?
Does it scale with attributes and skills?
Does NPC dialogue react to it?
```

Before adding a skill:

```text
What improves it?
What does it unlock?
Can it be abused?
Does it affect the world?
Does it affect combat, crafting, exploration, or social gameplay?
Is progress clear to the player?
```

Before adding an ability:

```text
Who can use it?
What does it cost?
What is the cooldown?
What does it target?
How does it scale?
Can it be interrupted?
Does it work in PvP?
Can it be exploited?
Does the tooltip explain it clearly?
```

---

# 92. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design and implement a complete class, skill, power, ability, and progression system for this MMO.

The system must support:
- Classes
- Subclasses / specializations
- Skills
- Weapon mastery
- Armor mastery
- Magic schools
- Profession skills
- Gathering skills
- Crafting skills
- Social skills
- Exploration skills
- Active abilities
- Passive abilities
- Ultimate abilities
- Talent trees
- Ability loadouts
- Class quests
- Trainers
- Skill books
- Spell scrolls
- Respecs
- NPC classes
- PvP balance rules
- Server-authoritative validation

Every class must define:
- Class ID
- Name
- Role options
- Primary attributes
- Secondary attributes
- Armor access
- Weapon access
- Resource type
- Starting abilities
- Specializations
- Class quests
- World interactions
- NPC reaction rules

Every class should have:
- Clear identity
- Strengths
- Weaknesses
- Useful solo play
- Useful group play
- Unique dialogue/world interactions
- Progression milestones

Recommended classes include:
- Warrior
- Rogue
- Ranger
- Mage
- Priest
- Paladin
- Necromancer
- Druid
- Bard
- Monk
- Engineer
- Summoner

Every skill must define:
- Skill ID
- Name
- Category
- Max level
- Progression method
- What actions improve it
- What actions do not improve it
- Unlock milestones
- Passive bonuses
- Related abilities
- Related world interactions
- Anti-abuse rules

Skills should include:
- Combat skills
- Weapon skills
- Armor skills
- Magic skills
- Profession skills
- Gathering skills
- Crafting skills
- Social skills
- Exploration skills
- Survival skills

Skill progression should be hybrid:
- Core abilities unlock by level
- Skill mastery improves by use
- Advanced abilities require trainers, quests, books, reputation, or milestones
- Trivial repeated actions give reduced progress
- AFK or exploit actions give no progress

Every ability must define:
- Ability ID
- Name
- Type
- Class requirement
- Specialization requirement
- Skill requirement
- Level requirement
- Resource cost
- Cooldown
- Cast time
- Range
- Target type
- Scaling
- Effects
- PvP modifiers
- Interrupt rules
- Tooltip
- Upgrade path

Abilities can be:
- Basic attacks
- Active abilities
- Passive abilities
- Toggle abilities
- Channeled abilities
- Cast-time spells
- Instant abilities
- Ultimate abilities
- Reaction abilities
- Combo abilities
- Movement abilities
- Defensive abilities
- Utility abilities
- Profession abilities
- Social abilities
- World interaction abilities

Resource systems must match class fantasy:
- Warriors may use Rage or Stamina
- Rogues may use Energy and Combo Points
- Mages may use Mana
- Priests may use Faith or Mana
- Paladins may use Conviction or Holy Power
- Necromancers may use Souls or Corruption
- Bards may use Inspiration
- Rangers may use Focus

Players may know many abilities but should equip a limited number through loadouts.
Loadouts can be changed out of combat or in safe areas.
Loadout changes during combat are blocked.

Talent trees must:
- Offer meaningful choices
- Avoid mandatory obvious picks
- Avoid trap talents
- Support multiple playstyles
- Allow respecs
- Prevent combat swapping abuse

Class and skill choices should affect the world:
- Warriors can move heavy objects or intimidate
- Rogues can lockpick, sneak, and disarm traps
- Mages can identify magic and open arcane seals
- Priests can heal sickness and cleanse corruption
- Rangers can track and tame animals
- Druids can speak with animals and restore nature
- Necromancers can speak with the dead
- Bards can persuade crowds and gather rumors

The system must handle edge cases:
- Player uses ability without required weapon
- Player changes gear mid-cast
- Player learns same ability twice
- Player respecs while ability is on cooldown
- Player switches loadout in combat
- Player uses illegal ability in town
- Pet blocks door
- Ability hits through wall
- Ability double-casts from lag
- Player disconnects during cast
- Ability kills target after player dies
- Skill XP exploit through trivial actions
- Training dummy abuse
- PvP crowd control chains
- Summon/pet AI griefing
- Class change abuse

The server must be authoritative:
- Server validates class
- Server validates specialization
- Server validates skill level
- Server validates known abilities
- Server validates equipped abilities
- Server validates resources
- Server validates cooldowns
- Server validates target, range, and line of sight
- Server validates PvP/legal/zone rules
- Server calculates damage, healing, buffs, debuffs, and skill XP
- Client only displays UI and sends action requests

The system should make class choice meaningful, skill growth rewarding, abilities readable, and builds flexible without becoming impossible to balance.
```

---

# Final Class / Skill / Ability Rule

Every class should answer:

```text
Who am I?
What am I good at?
What am I bad at?
How do I fight?
How do I help groups?
How do NPCs see me?
What can I do in the world that others cannot?
```

Every skill should answer:

```text
How do I improve this?
What does it unlock?
Where does it matter?
Can it be abused?
Does it affect combat, crafting, social play, or exploration?
```

Every ability should answer:

```text
Who can use it?
What does it cost?
What does it do?
What does it scale with?
What counters it?
What happens in PvP?
Can the server validate it?
Can the player understand it from the tooltip?
```

That is how you make progression feel like real character growth instead of just random buttons and numbers.



Below is a full **MMO Guild System Rule Set**.

Core rule:

```text
Guilds should give players a reason to organize, cooperate, build identity, share resources, run events, raid together, fight wars, own property, complete projects, and form long-term communities.

A guild should not just be a chat room with a name.
```

---

# 1. Guild System Overview

A guild should support:

```text
Guild creation
Guild names and identity
Guild ranks
Guild permissions
Guild chat
Guild bank
Guild treasury
Guild taxes
Guild quests
Guild achievements
Guild reputation
Guild halls
Guild housing
Guild crafting projects
Guild raids
Guild PvP
Guild wars
Guild alliances
Guild recruitment
Guild events
Guild calendar
Guild logs
Guild leadership transfer
Guild moderation
Guild disbanding
```

Every guild should answer:

```text
Who leads it?
Who belongs to it?
What is its purpose?
What permissions do members have?
What resources does it own?
What buildings does it own?
What content does it participate in?
What reputation does it have?
What happens if leadership disappears?
How does the system prevent theft, abuse, and griefing?
```

---

# 2. Guild Creation Rules

Players should be able to create guilds if they meet requirements.

Common requirements:

```text
Minimum player level
Gold fee
Guild charter
Minimum signatures
No active ban/restriction
No recent guild creation cooldown
Valid guild name
Valid guild tag
Legal standing, if world requires lawful registration
Faction compatibility, if faction-based
```

Example:

```text
Create Guild Requirements:
- Player Level 10
- 100 gold registration fee
- 5 player signatures
- Valid guild name
- Not currently in another guild
- No active guild creation cooldown
```

---

## Guild Creation Flow

```text
1. Player visits Guild Registrar or opens guild creation UI.
2. Player enters guild name, tag, description, and type.
3. Server validates name/tag.
4. Player pays creation fee or starts charter.
5. Required signatures are collected, if used.
6. Server creates guild.
7. Creator becomes Guild Leader.
8. Guild chat, roster, bank access, and basic permissions unlock.
9. Guild appears in guild directory if public.
```

---

# 3. Guild Name Rules

Guild names should be controlled.

Rules:

```text
Guild name must be unique.
Guild name must respect length limits.
Guild name cannot impersonate staff, NPC factions, or official groups.
Guild name cannot contain banned words.
Guild name cannot use confusing symbols to fake another guild.
Guild tag must be unique or at least not misleading.
```

Example limits:

```text
Guild Name: 3 to 32 characters
Guild Tag: 2 to 6 characters
Description: 0 to 500 characters
Message of the Day: 0 to 500 characters
```

---

# 4. Guild Types

Guilds can declare a focus.

Guild types:

```text
Casual
Social
Roleplay
PvE
Dungeon
Raid
PvP
Faction War
Crafting
Trading
Gathering
Mercenary
Bounty Hunter
Criminal / Outlaw
Hardcore
New Player Friendly
Endgame
Housing / Building
Exploration
```

Guild type affects:

```text
Recruitment filters
Guild recommendations
Guild quests
Guild achievements
Guild perks
Guild events
Guild reputation
Guild hall upgrades
```

Example:

```text
Crafting Guild:
Gets better access to work orders, crafting projects, material storage, and town construction contracts.

Raid Guild:
Gets raid planning tools, boss progression logs, raid banners, and raid achievements.

PvP Guild:
Gets war tools, battleground groups, siege planning, and territory rankings.
```

---

# 5. Guild Membership Rules

Players may join guilds through:

```text
Direct invite
Application
Open recruitment
Guild finder
NPC recruitment board
Faction recommendation
Friend invite
Officer invite
Merged guild invite
```

Membership rules:

```text
A player can usually belong to one main guild at a time.
Optional secondary groups can exist as companies, clubs, or trade associations.
Guild membership is character-based or account-based depending on game design.
Guild membership should have clear join and leave rules.
```

Recommended:

```text
Use one main guild per character.
Allow account-wide guild visibility if desired.
Allow separate non-guild social groups for communities.
```

---

# 6. Guild Ranks

Guilds need ranks with permissions.

Default ranks:

```text
Guild Leader
Co-Leader
Officer
Raid Leader
Veteran
Member
Recruit
Trial Member
Alt Character
Muted / Restricted
```

Optional ranks:

```text
Treasurer
Quartermaster
Builder
Crafter
PvP Commander
Event Organizer
Diplomat
Recruiter
Bank Manager
Guild Hall Manager
```

---

## Rank Rules

Each rank controls permissions.

Permissions should be customizable.

```text
Invite members
Kick members
Promote members
Demote members
Edit guild info
Edit message of the day
Manage recruitment
Start guild events
Create guild calendar events
Access guild bank
Withdraw from guild bank
Deposit to guild bank
Withdraw gold
Spend guild treasury
Set guild tax
Start guild projects
Contribute to projects
Manage guild hall
Place decorations
Manage NPC workers
Declare guild war
Accept alliances
Manage raid groups
Use guild repair funds
Disband guild
Transfer leadership
```

Important:

```text
Only the Guild Leader should be able to disband the guild or transfer ownership.
High-risk permissions should require confirmation and logs.
```

---

# 7. Guild Permission System

Permissions should be granular.

Example permission categories:

```text
Roster Permissions
Bank Permissions
Treasury Permissions
Chat Permissions
Event Permissions
Raid Permissions
PvP Permissions
Guild Hall Permissions
Shop Permissions
Diplomacy Permissions
Administrative Permissions
```

---

## Permission Example

```json
{
  "rank": "Officer",
  "permissions": {
    "invite_members": true,
    "kick_recruits": true,
    "promote_to_member": true,
    "edit_message_of_day": true,
    "create_events": true,
    "start_ready_check": true,
    "withdraw_bank_items_per_day": 10,
    "withdraw_gold_per_day": 100,
    "declare_war": false,
    "disband_guild": false
  }
}
```

---

# 8. Guild Chat System

Guilds need communication tools.

Channels:

```text
Guild Chat
Officer Chat
Raid Chat
Party Chat
Event Chat
Guild Voice, optional
Guild Announcement Channel
Guild Trade Channel
Guild Recruitment Channel
```

Rules:

```text
Guild chat should be available to all members unless muted.
Officer chat should be permission-based.
Announcements should be restricted.
Muted members can read but not speak, depending on settings.
Guild chat should have moderation tools.
```

Moderation tools:

```text
Mute
Warn
Kick
Temporary chat restriction
Report
Delete message, if supported
Chat logs for officers, if allowed
```

---

# 9. Guild Message of the Day

Guilds should have a message of the day.

Uses:

```text
Raid reminders
Rules
Event announcements
Recruitment needs
Crafting requests
War alerts
Guild project goals
```

Example:

```text
Guild Message:
"Raid tonight at 8 PM. Bring fire resistance potions. We still need 200 Iron Bars for the guild hall forge upgrade."
```

---

# 10. Guild Roster

The guild roster should show:

```text
Member name
Class
Level
Role
Rank
Profession
Online/offline status
Last online
Current zone
Join date
Contribution score
Raid role
PvP rank
Main/alt status
Notes, officer-only if needed
```

Filters:

```text
Online members
Role
Class
Rank
Profession
Raid availability
PvP availability
New recruits
Inactive members
```

---

# 11. Guild Recruitment System

Guild recruitment should be structured.

Guild profile should include:

```text
Guild name
Guild tag
Guild type
Faction
Language
Region/time zone
Playstyle
Activity level
Required level
Voice requirement
Raid schedule
PvP focus
New player friendly status
Application questions
Rules
Description
Contact officers
```

Player can search by:

```text
Guild type
Faction
Language
Time zone
Activity level
Raid schedule
PvP focus
Casual/hardcore
New player friendly
Role needed
```

---

## Guild Application Rules

Application can include:

```text
Player message
Preferred role
Play schedule
Experience
Class/spec
Why they want to join
Friend referral
```

Officers can:

```text
Accept
Reject
Request interview
Put on trial
Invite to event
Message applicant
```

---

# 12. Guild Bank

A guild bank is shared storage.

Guild bank should support:

```text
Item tabs
Gold treasury
Material storage
Raid consumables
Crafting materials
Gear donations
Guild project materials
Withdrawal limits
Deposit logs
Withdrawal logs
Rank permissions
Item locking
Tab names
Tab descriptions
```

---

## Guild Bank Rules

```text
Members can deposit allowed items.
Withdrawals depend on rank permissions.
Gold withdrawals depend on rank limits.
Every deposit and withdrawal is logged.
High-value withdrawals may require officer approval.
Locked tabs can be restricted.
Guild bank cannot store quest-bound personal items.
```

Recommended:

```text
Use daily withdrawal limits per rank.
Use separate tabs for materials, raid supplies, gear, and projects.
Log everything.
```

---

## Guild Bank Tabs

Example:

```text
Tab 1: General Supplies
Tab 2: Raid Consumables
Tab 3: Crafting Materials
Tab 4: Gear
Tab 5: Guild Projects
Tab 6: Officer Vault
```

---

# 13. Guild Treasury

The treasury holds guild currency.

Guild treasury sources:

```text
Member donations
Guild tax
Guild quest rewards
Guild event rewards
Guild shop sales
Territory income
Guild-owned property revenue
Guild contract payments
```

Treasury uses:

```text
Guild hall upgrades
Guild repairs
Guild bank tabs
Guild vendors
Guild events
Raid repairs
Siege equipment
Town projects
Guild taxes/fees
Worker wages
```

---

# 14. Guild Tax

Guild tax is optional.

Rules:

```text
Guild tax must be visible to members.
Guild leader/officers can set tax only if permitted.
Tax applies only to selected income sources.
Tax should have a reasonable cap.
Players should see how much was contributed.
```

Recommended cap:

```text
Guild tax range: 0% to 10%
Default: 0%
```

Taxable sources may include:

```text
Gold from enemy loot
Quest gold, optional
Guild contract rewards
Trade income, optional
```

Avoid:

```text
Secret taxes.
High taxes that trap players.
Taxing every player action aggressively.
```

---

# 15. Guild Contributions

Players should receive contribution credit.

Contribution sources:

```text
Donating gold
Donating materials
Crafting guild items
Completing guild quests
Joining raids
Joining PvP events
Defending guild territory
Repairing guild structures
Recruiting members
Helping new players
Completing guild contracts
```

Contribution can unlock:

```text
Guild rank eligibility
Cosmetic rewards
Guild titles
Access to guild perks
Priority in crafting requests
Recognition on leaderboard
```

Important:

```text
Contribution should not become a toxic score used to shame casual players.
Use it for recognition and permissions, not harassment.
```

---

# 16. Guild Leveling

Guilds can gain guild XP.

Guild XP sources:

```text
Members completing quests
Dungeon completions
Raid completions
PvP victories
Guild projects
Crafting contributions
Gathering contributions
Town project contributions
World event participation
Guild achievements
```

Guild level unlocks:

```text
Guild bank tabs
Guild hall upgrades
Guild banners
Guild perks
Guild vendors
Guild quests
Guild cosmetics
Guild mounts
Guild titles
Guild project slots
Guild event tools
```

---

## Guild XP Anti-Abuse

Rules:

```text
Only meaningful member activity counts.
AFK members do not generate guild XP.
Repeated low-level farming gives reduced guild XP.
Alt-account abuse should be detected.
Guild XP from repeatable actions should have caps.
```

---

# 17. Guild Perks

Guild perks should help but not make guildless players useless.

Perk examples:

```text
Slight repair discount
Guild hearthstone cooldown reduction
Small crafting station bonus
Guild banner buff during guild events
Guild bank access expansion
Extra rested XP in guild hall
Small gathering bonus during guild missions
Cheaper guild travel
Guild cosmetic unlocks
Raid planning tools
Guild vendor access
```

Avoid overpowered perks:

```text
Massive damage bonuses
Mandatory PvP advantages
Huge XP bonuses that force every player into guilds
Exclusive power required for basic progression
```

Good rule:

```text
Guild perks should reward community, convenience, identity, and coordination more than raw power.
```

---

# 18. Guild Quests and Missions

Guilds should have group objectives.

Guild quest types:

```text
Gather resources
Craft supplies
Complete dungeon as guild group
Defeat raid boss
Win battleground
Defend town
Escort caravan
Build guild structure
Repair public building
Hunt world boss
Complete faction mission
Host social event
Train new players
```

Guild quest rewards:

```text
Guild XP
Guild currency
Guild reputation
Guild materials
Guild hall progress
Guild cosmetics
Town reputation
Faction reputation
```

---

## Guild Quest Rules

```text
Guild quests should require multiple member contributions.
Rewards should scale with contribution.
Repeatable guild quests need daily/weekly limits.
Guild quests should match guild type.
Guild quests should not be impossible for small guilds unless marked large-scale.
```

Example:

```text
Guild Mission: Rebuild the East Watchtower

Objectives:
- Contribute 500 Stone Blocks
- Contribute 200 Wood Beams
- Defeat bandits near the road
- Escort builder NPC to site

Rewards:
+2,000 Guild XP
+Harthmere Likeability
Unlocks guard patrol on east road
```

---

# 19. Guild Achievements

Guild achievements track major accomplishments.

Examples:

```text
First dungeon clear as guild
First raid boss defeated
Guild hall built
1,000 resources donated
Won 10 guild wars
Controlled territory for 7 days
Crafted legendary guild banner
Protected town during invasion
Hosted festival
Reached guild level 10
```

Rewards:

```text
Titles
Banners
Decorations
Guild hall trophies
Mount skins
Guild tabard designs
Cosmetic armor
Guild emblems
```

---

# 20. Guild Identity

Guilds need visual identity.

Identity options:

```text
Guild name
Guild tag
Guild emblem
Guild colors
Guild banner
Guild tabard
Guild cape
Guild mount banner
Guild hall decorations
Guild title prefix
Guild crest on shields
Guild war flag
```

Rules:

```text
Guild symbols must follow content rules.
Guild colors/emblems should be customizable.
Guild identity should appear in guild halls, PvP, raids, and events.
```

---

# 21. Guild Hall System

Guilds should be able to own or build a guild hall.

Guild hall features:

```text
Guild bank
Guild treasury
Meeting room

Training dummies
Raid planning table
War room
Trophy room
Portal room
Guild shop
Stables

Decorations
```

## Guild Hall Upgrade Paths

Upgrade categories:

```text
Storage
Crafting
Raid tools
PvP tools
Defense
Travel
Vendors
Decorations
Training
Worker capacity
Guild project slots
```

Example upgrades:

```text
Tier 1 Guild Hall:
Basic meeting room, guild bank, notice board.

Tier 2 Guild Hall:
Crafting stations, storage expansion, training dummies.

Tier 3 Guild Hall:
War room, portal room, guild vendors, trophy displays.

Tier 4 Guild Hall:
Defensive structures, advanced crafting, raid strategy tools.

Tier 5 Guild Hall:
Grand hall, unique cosmetics, special guild events.
```

---

# 22. Guild Hall Permissions

Guild hall permissions should control:

```text
Enter hall
Invite guests
Use guild bank
Use crafting stations
Use portals
Place decorations
Move decorations
Manage NPC workers
Start guild projects
Use treasury funds
Lock rooms
Access officer room
Access war room
Access trophy room
```

# 23. Guild Building and Repairs

Guild buildings connect to the building system.

Guilds can build:

```text
Guild halls
Walls
Towers
Crafting wings
Barracks
Stables
Warehouses
Shops
Outposts
Siege workshops
Farms
Mines
Docks
```

Rules:

```text
Requires guild permissions.
Requires materials and treasury funds.
Members can contribute.
Progress is logged.
Guild buildings can require upkeep.
Guild buildings can be damaged only under valid rules.
```

---

# 24. Guild Crafting System

Guilds can organize crafting.

Features:

```text
Guild crafting orders
Shared material storage
Guild recipe unlocks
Guild crafting stations
Crafter roles
Commission board
Guild project crafting
Crafting contribution logs
```

Rules:

```text
Members can request items.
Crafters can accept work orders.
Guild bank materials can be used only with permission.
High-value materials may require officer approval.
Crafted guild items are logged.
```

---

# 25. Guild Shop System

Guilds may run shops.

Guild shop can sell:

```text
Crafted goods
Raid consumables
Materials
Guild cosmetics
Food/drink
Repair kits
Siege supplies
Decorations
```

Shop rules:

```text
Guild officers set prices.
Revenue goes to guild treasury.
Permissions control who lists/removes items.
Taxes may apply.
Public visibility can be enabled/disabled.
```

---

# 26. Guild Raids

Guilds should support organized raiding.

Guild raid tools:

```text
Raid calendar
Role signup
Roster management
Raid leader assignment
Ready check
Pull timer
Boss progression tracker
Loot history
Attendance tracker
Strategy notes
Voice/chat integration
Raid logs
Guild achievements
```

Rules:

```text
Guild raids should recognize guild participation.
Raid rewards go to individuals, but guild achievements can unlock cosmetics or trophies.
Guild should not be able to steal individual loot unfairly.
```

---

# 27. Guild Raid Progression

Guild raid progression can track:

```text
Bosses defeated
Difficulty cleared
Fastest clear
No-death clear

Server ranking
Composition
Attendance
Wipes
Mechanic performance
```

Use this for:

```text
Trophies
Guild achievements
Recruitment proof
Raid planning
Progression history
```

Guilds can participate in PvP.

Guild PvP types:

```text
Guild wars
Territory battles
Sieges
Arena teams
Battleground premades

Outpost control
```
