```text
Faction support
```

Rewards:

```text
Guild PvP rating
Territory control
Guild currency
Resources
Titles
Banners
Cosmetics
Guild hall trophies
Guild ranking
```

---

# 29. Guild Wars

Guild wars should be structured and fair.

Guild war requirements:

```text
Guild leader/officer permission
War declaration cost
Valid target guild
War duration
War rules
War objectives
Safe-zone protections
Cooldowns
Consent rules, depending on server type
```

War types:

```text
Mutual War
Faction War
Territory War
Scheduled Siege
Rivalry War
Bounty War
Hardcore War
```

---

## Guild War Rules

```text
Guild members must know they are war-flagged.
War objectives must be clear.
Starter areas remain protected.
Safe zones remain protected unless special siege rules apply.
War cannot be used to grief non-consenting low-level players.
War rewards require meaningful participation.
War declarations should have cooldowns to prevent spam.
```

---

# 30. Guild Alliances

Guilds can ally with other guilds.

Alliance features:

```text
Alliance chat
Shared events
Shared war support
Shared raid planning
Trade agreements
Shared territory defense
Non-aggression pact
Resource sharing
```

Rules:

```text
Alliance must be accepted by both guilds.
Alliance permissions are separate from guild permissions.
Alliance members should not access guild bank unless explicitly allowed.
Alliance can be ended with cooldown.
```

---

# 31. Guild Rivalries

Guilds can mark rivals.

Rivalry can affect:

```text
PvP scoring
Leaderboard
Guild achievements
Public notices
War declarations
Territory conflict
Bounty bonuses
```

Rules:

```text
Rivalry should not enable griefing by itself.
Combat legality still depends on PvP rules.
```

---

# 32. Guild Territory

Advanced system for open-world control.

Guilds may control:

```text
Outposts
Mines
Farms
Fortresses
Watchtowers
Trade posts
Docks
Resource camps
PvP zones
Neighborhoods, if allowed
```

Territory control can grant:

```text
Resource bonuses
Tax income
Guild reputation
Map visibility
Fast travel
Crafting bonuses
NPC guards
Trade route control
PvP ranking
```

---

## Territory Rules

```text
Territory must have capture rules.
Territory must have vulnerability windows.
Territory must have defense options.
Territory rewards must not make one guild permanently unbeatable.
Territory should be contestable.
Small guilds need alternate content.
```

---

# 33. Guild Diplomacy

Guilds can have relationships with other guilds.

Relationship states:

```text
Neutral
Friendly
Allied
Rival
At War
Truce
Trade Partner
Non-Aggression Pact
Hostile
```

Diplomacy actions:

```text
Send alliance request
Declare rivalry
Declare war
Offer truce
Set trade agreement
Share event invitation
Coordinate siege
```

Permissions:

```text
Only leader/diplomat/officer can manage diplomacy.
All diplomacy changes are logged.
Major actions require confirmation.
```

---

# 34. Guild Reputation

Guilds should have reputation separate from individual players.

Guild reputation can exist with:

```text
Towns
Factions
NPC organizations
Other guilds
Player community
Criminal groups
Trade companies
Religious orders
Military groups
```

Guild reputation changes from:

```text
Completing guild quests
Defending towns
Winning wars honorably
Committing crimes as guild
Raiding caravans
Helping NPC factions
Building town projects
Crafting public supplies
Killing civilians in guild activities
```

---

## Guild Reputation Effects

High guild reputation:

```text
Discounts
Guild contracts
Land grants
Guild hall permits
Faction vendors
Town project priority
Special titles
NPC support
```

Low guild reputation:

```text
Higher taxes
Denied permits
Guard suspicion
Vendor refusal
Bounties
Faction hostility
Guild hall restrictions
```

---

# 35. Guild and Legal System

Guild actions can affect legal standing.

Examples:

```text
Guild attacks lawful town:
Guild legal reputation decreases.

Guild defends caravan:
Guild legal reputation increases.

Guild shelters criminals:
Guild becomes suspicious.

Guild pays fines:
Some restrictions lifted.

Guild repeatedly raids civilians:
Guild may be outlawed in region.
```

Rules:

```text
Individual crimes should not automatically punish entire guild unless done in guild war/event context.
Guild-sanctioned actions can affect guild reputation.
Guild leader/officer declarations can create guild consequences.
```

---

# 36. Guild Bounties

Bounties can target guilds or guild members.

Types:

```text
Individual bounty
Guild bounty
Faction bounty
War bounty
Territory bounty
Criminal guild bounty
```

Rules:

```text
Bounty must have valid cause.
Repeated bounty farming is blocked.
Guild bounty rewards require meaningful action.
Capture may be more valuable than killing.
```

Example:

```text
Guild repeatedly attacks trade routes.
Merchant Guild posts bounty:
- Destroy their road camp.
- Capture their war leader.
- Recover stolen cargo.
```

---

# 37. Guild Events and Calendar

Guilds need scheduling.

Calendar features:

```text
Create event
Set date/time
Set event type
Set required roles
Sign up
Tentative/confirmed status
Waitlist
Reminder notifications
Recurring events
Event notes
Voice requirement
Loot rules
Location marker
```

Event types:

```text
Raid
Dungeon night
PvP night

World boss
Town project
Recruitment event
Roleplay event
Training event
Siege
```

---

# 38. Guild Notifications

Guild notifications should include:

```text
Member joined
Member left
Member promoted
Member kicked
Guild level up
Guild project completed
Guild bank withdrawal
Guild war declared
Guild alliance request
Guild event reminder
Guild hall damaged
Guild tax changed
Guild achievement earned
```

Important notifications should not spam everyone.

Use:

```text
Critical notifications
Officer notifications
Personal notifications
Event reminders
Optional social notifications
```

---

# 39. Guild Logs

Guild logs are mandatory to prevent drama and theft.

Logs should include:

```text
Member joined
Member left
Invite sent
Kick
Promotion
Demotion
Permission change
Guild bank deposit
Guild bank withdrawal
Treasury deposit
Treasury withdrawal
Guild tax change
Guild project contribution
Guild project spending
Guild hall upgrade
Guild property sale
Guild war declaration
Alliance change
Guild shop sale
Guild disband attempt
Leadership transfer
```

Logs should show:

```text
Who did it
What changed
When it happened
What item/gold was involved
Before/after value if relevant
```

---

# 40. Guild Bank Theft Prevention

Guild theft is a major MMO issue.

Rules:

```text
Use withdrawal limits.
Use rank permissions.
Log all withdrawals.
High-value tabs require officer approval.
New members have limited access.
Recently promoted members may have temporary withdrawal limits.
Guild leader can lock bank during emergency.
Mass withdrawal triggers alert.
```

Example:

```text
Recruit:
Can deposit items.
Cannot withdraw.

Member:
Can withdraw 3 items/day from basic tab.

Veteran:
Can withdraw 10 items/day.

Officer:
Can withdraw more, but high-value items are logged and announced.

Treasurer:
Can manage treasury but not disband guild.
```

---

# 41. Leadership Inactivity Rules

Guilds need protection if leader disappears.

Rules:

```text
If Guild Leader is inactive for X days, leadership transfer can begin.
Eligible officers can request transfer.
Members are notified.
Leader gets grace period.
If no response, leadership transfers to highest eligible active rank.
```

Recommended:

```text
Casual guild: 45 to 90 days inactivity.
Competitive guild: 30 to 60 days inactivity.
```

Eligibility:

```text
Active officer
Long membership duration
No recent disciplinary restriction
Majority officer vote, optional
```

---

# 42. Leadership Transfer

Leadership can be transferred manually.

Rules:

```text
Only Guild Leader can transfer leadership voluntarily.
Transfer requires confirmation.
New leader must be a guild member.
Transfer is logged.
Some high-risk changes may have short cooldown.
```

Warning:

```text
Transfer guild leadership to Arlen?
This gives them full control of guild ranks, bank, hall, treasury, and disband rights.
Type TRANSFER to confirm.
```

---

# 43. Guild Disbanding

Disbanding should be protected.

Rules:

```text
Only Guild Leader can disband.
Requires strong confirmation.
Cannot disband during active war/siege unless rules allow.
Guild bank items must be handled.
Treasury must be distributed or moved to recovery.
Guild property must be sold, transferred, or locked.
Members must be notified.
Disband is logged.
```

Safer option:

```text
Disband enters pending state for 72 hours.
Leader can cancel during timer.
Officers/members are notified.
```

---

# 44. Guild Merging

Guilds may merge.

Merge rules:

```text
Both guild leaders must agree.
Members are notified.
Bank/property/treasury transfer rules are shown.
Guild achievements may merge partially or remain historical.
Guild name may be kept or changed.
Ranks must be mapped.
```

This is optional, but useful for long-term MMO health.

---

# 45. Guild Leaving Rules

Players can leave a guild.

Rules:

```text
Player can leave voluntarily.
If player owns guild property, handle separately.
If player has borrowed guild items, rules depend on item ownership.
Leaving does not erase contribution history immediately.
Guild chat access ends.
Guild bank access ends.
Guild buffs end.
```

Cooldowns:

```text
Optional cooldown before joining another guild.
Short cooldown prevents guild-hopping abuse.
Avoid punishing casual players too harshly.
```

Recommended:

```text
No cooldown or 24-hour cooldown for competitive guild rewards.
```

---

# 46. Guild Kicking Rules

Officers can kick members if permitted.

Rules:

```text
Cannot kick equal/higher rank unless permitted.
Kick is logged.
Kicked player receives notice.
Kicked player loses guild access.
Items personally owned remain theirs.
Guild-owned items remain with guild.
```

Protection:

```text
Cannot kick player from raid after boss kill to deny earned personal loot.
Cannot remove earned individual rewards.
```

---

# 47. Guild Reward Rules

Guild rewards should be fair.

Reward types:

```text
Guild XP
Guild currency
Guild cosmetics
Guild titles
Guild hall trophies
Guild project progress
Guild reputation
Individual contribution rewards
```

Rules:

```text
Guild rewards require meaningful participation.
Individual rewards cannot be stolen by guild leaders.
Guild rewards should not force players into guilds for basic progression.
Guild cosmetics are safer than raw power rewards.
```

---

# 48. Guild Storage and Personal Ownership

Clear ownership rules prevent disputes.

Item ownership types:

```text
Personal item
Guild-owned item
Guild project material
Temporary loan item
Bound item
Guild-bound item
```

Rules:

```text
Items deposited into guild bank become guild-accessible.
Warn players before depositing valuable personal items.
Guild-bound items cannot be withdrawn permanently by individuals unless allowed.
Loaned guild items may return automatically after time/event.
```

Example warning:

```text
Deposit Rare Dragon Scale into Guild Bank?
Officers with permission may withdraw this item.
```

---

# 49. Guild Loan System

Optional but useful.

Guild can loan:

```text
Raid consumables
Gear
Mounts
Tools
Crafting equipment
Siege items
```

Loan rules:

```text
Loan has duration.
Item returns automatically if possible.
Loaned item cannot be sold/traded.
Loaned item may be repaired by guild funds.
If player leaves guild, item returns.
```

---

# 50. Guild Repair Funds

Guild treasury can pay repair costs.

Rules:

```text
Only allowed ranks can use guild repairs.
Daily gold cap per member/rank.
Only applies to equipped gear, optionally.
Can be limited to guild raids/events.
Every repair withdrawal is logged.
```

Example:

```text
Guild Repair:
Members: 20 gold/day
Raiders: 100 gold/day during raid events
Officers: 200 gold/day
```

---

# 51. Guild Raid Loot Rules

Guilds need transparent loot rules.

Possible systems:

```text
Personal loot
Need/greed
Loot council
DKP
Guild points
Roll with priority
Token-based loot
Crafting material bank
```

Recommended for modern MMO:

```text
Personal loot for individual gear.
Guild-controlled loot only for guild project materials or explicitly guild-bound items.
```

If loot council exists:

```text
Rules must be shown before raid.
Loot decisions must be logged.
Player-earned personal loot cannot be taken.
```

---

# 52. Guild New Player Support

Guilds should be able to support new players.

Systems:

```text
Mentor rank
New player channel
Training events
Beginner gear tab
Quest helper groups
Dungeon teaching runs
Guild guides
Role/class advice
```

Rewards:

```text
Mentor contribution
Guild XP
New player friendly badge
Recruitment visibility
Social achievements
```

---

# 53. Guild Mentoring

Mentoring helps members play together.

Rules:

```text
High-level members can level-sync to help lower-level members.
Mentor receives scaled rewards.
New player receives normal quest/XP rewards.
Mentor contribution counts for guild.
Power-leveling abuse is limited.
```

---

# 54. Guild Contracts

Guilds can accept contracts from NPCs or players.

Contract types:

```text
Gathering contract
Crafting contract
Escort contract
Bounty contract
Defense contract
Raid contract
PvP contract
Construction contract
Trade contract
Mercenary contract
```

Contract rules:

```text
Guild officers accept contract.
Members contribute.
Rewards go to guild treasury and contributors.
Failure conditions are clear.
Contracts have time limits if needed.
```

Example:

```text
Merchant Guild Contract:
Protect three caravans this week.

Rewards:
- 2,000 guild XP
- 500 gold treasury
- Merchant Guild reputation
- Discount on trade goods
```

---

# 55. Guild Economy

Guilds should participate in the economy.

Guild economy includes:

```text
Treasury
Guild tax
Guild bank
Guild shop
Guild hall upkeep
Guild projects
Guild repairs
Guild contracts
Guild trade agreements
Guild property
Guild crafting
Territory income
```

Rules:

```text
Guild funds must be logged.
Guild leaders should not be able to secretly drain everything without record.
Members should see tax settings.
Guild projects should show material/gold needs.
```

---

# 56. Guild Rank Progression

Guilds can create rank advancement rules.

Rank advancement can depend on:

```text
Time in guild
Contribution
Raid participation
PvP participation
Officer approval
Trust
Crafting support
Helping members
Trial completion
```

Recommended:

```text
Do not make rank progression automatic only by contribution score.
Officer trust and behavior matter.
```

---

# 57. Guild Titles

Guilds can grant internal titles.

Examples:

```text
Raid Captain
Master Smith
Quartermaster
Champion
Recruiter
Architect
War Caller
Lorekeeper
Mentor
Diplomat
Treasurer
```

Titles may be cosmetic or permission-linked.

Rules:

```text
Guild titles should not override system ranks unless configured.
Titles can appear in guild roster.
```

---

# 58. Guild and Player Reputation Interaction

Guild reputation and player reputation should interact carefully.

Rules:

```text
Player actions can affect guild reputation only if done in guild context.
Guild actions can affect player reputation only if player participated.
Do not punish offline members for every guild action.
Major guild-sanctioned actions may affect all active participants.
```

Example:

```text
A guild group raids a lawful caravan.
Participants lose Legal Standing.
Guild loses reputation with Merchant Guild.
Offline members do not personally lose Legal Standing.
```

---

# 59. Guild Criminal System

Criminal guilds can exist if the MMO supports outlaw gameplay.

Criminal guild activities:

```text
Smuggling
Black market trading
Illegal contracts
Bounty evasion
Hideout ownership
Robbery events
Assassination contracts
Contraband storage
```

Rules:

```text
Criminal guilds have risk.
Lawful towns may deny services.
Guards may raid hideouts.
Black market vendors may offer benefits.
Members may be flagged if participating in crimes.
Guild can gain criminal notoriety.
```

---

# 60. Guild Hideouts

Criminal or secret guilds may have hideouts.

Hideout features:

```text
Hidden entrance
Black market vendor
Stolen goods storage
Forgery table
Smuggling routes
Bribe contacts
Secret meeting room
Escape tunnel
```

Rules:

```text
Hideouts are not public map markers.
Can be discovered by quests, investigation, betrayal, or guards.
May be raided under legal events.
```

---

# 61. Guild War Crimes and Consequences

Guild warfare should not become consequence-free griefing.

Possible guild-level crimes:

```text
Attacking civilian towns
Destroying protected buildings
Killing non-combatants
Raiding neutral caravans
Breaking truce
Using forbidden magic
Sheltering wanted criminals
```

Consequences:

```text
Faction hostility
Town bans
Higher taxes
Bounties
Guild outlaw status
Guard raids
Loss of legal property permits
Diplomatic penalties
```

---

# 62. Guild Territory Upkeep

Territory should require upkeep.

Upkeep can include:

```text
Gold
Food supplies
Building materials
Guard wages
Repair materials
Faction taxes
Guild activity
```

If upkeep fails:

```text
Services disabled
Guards leave
Walls decay
Territory becomes vulnerable
NPCs complain
Rival guilds can challenge
Territory returns to neutral
```

Use grace periods and warnings.

---

# 63. Guild Voting System

Optional governance system.

Votes can be used for:

```text
Electing leader
Approving war
Approving alliance
Spending treasury
Starting major project
Changing guild tax
Changing guild name
Kicking high-rank member
```

Rules:

```text
Voting settings are chosen by guild governance type.
Votes have start/end times.
Eligible voters are based on rank/membership duration.
Results are logged.
```

Governance types:

```text
Leader-controlled
Officer council
Democratic
Rank-weighted
Hybrid
```

---

# 64. Guild Governance Types

## Leader-Controlled

```text
Leader has final authority.
Fast decision-making.
Risk of abuse.
```

## Officer Council

```text
Major decisions require officer approval.
Good for organized guilds.
```

## Democratic

```text
Members vote on major issues.
Good for social guilds.
Slower decision-making.
```

## Hybrid

```text
Leader manages daily operations.
Major decisions require vote.
```

---

# 65. Guild Calendar and Time Zones

Guild events must support time zones.

Rules:

```text
Show event time in player local time.
Show server time if relevant.
Allow recurring events.
Allow reminders.
Allow RSVP.
Allow role signup.
```

Event RSVP states:

```text
Accepted
Declined
Tentative
Waitlisted
No response
```

---

# 66. Guild Finder Recommendations

The game can recommend guilds.

Recommendation factors:

```text
Player level
Player class/role
Player activity time
Language
Region
PvE/PvP preference
Raid interest
Crafting interest
New player status
Friends in guild
Guild recruitment status
Guild toxicity/report history
```

Do not recommend inactive guilds.

---

# 67. Guild Inactivity

Inactive guilds can clutter the world.

Rules:

```text
Guild marked inactive if no members log in for a long period.
Recruitment listing hidden after inactivity.
Guild hall services may pause.
Open-world property may enter grace period.
Guild is not deleted quickly.
Members receive recovery options if they return.
```

---

# 68. Guild Disputes and Support

The game should log enough to resolve disputes.

Support should be able to review:

```text
Bank withdrawals
Treasury spending
Rank changes
Guild disband
Property sale
Leadership transfer
War declarations
Guild shop sales
Member kicks
```

Without logs, guild drama becomes impossible to resolve fairly.

---

# 69. Guild Security Tools

Guilds should have security features.

Security features:

```text
Two-step confirmation for high-risk actions
Withdrawal limits
Promotion cooldowns
New member restrictions
Officer approval for high-value withdrawals
Emergency bank lock
Treasury spending limits
Guild leader inactivity transfer
Disband delay
Guild logs
Permission templates
```

---

# 70. Guild API/Data Structure

Guild definition example:

```json
{
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "name": "Iron Lanterns",
  "tag": "IRON",
  "type": "PvE/Raid",
  "faction": "Harthmere Alliance",
  "leader_id": "player_001",
  "level": 8,
  "xp": 245000,
  "created_at": "2026-05-13T05:00:00Z",
  "description": "Casual raid and crafting guild focused on helping members progress.",
  "message_of_the_day": "Raid Friday at 8 PM. Bring fire potions.",
  "recruitment_open": true,
  "member_count": 86,
  "max_members": 250,
  "treasury_gold": 125000,
  "guild_reputation": {
    "harthmere": 1200,
    "merchant_guild": 400,
    "thieves_guild": -300
  }
}
```

---

# 71. Guild Member Data Structure

```json
{
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "player_id": "player_045",
  "rank": "Veteran",
  "joined_at": "2026-05-13T05:30:00Z",
  "last_online": "2026-05-13T10:15:00Z",
  "main_character": true,
  "roles": ["Healer", "Crafter"],
  "professions": ["Alchemy", "Herbalism"],
  "contribution": {
    "lifetime": 15400,
    "weekly": 800
  },
  "notes": {
    "public": "Raid healer",
    "officer": "Reliable for weekend raids"
  }
}
```

---

# 72. Guild Rank Data Structure

```json
{
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "rank_name": "Officer",
  "rank_order": 2,
  "permissions": {
    "invite_members": true,
    "kick_members_below_rank": true,
    "promote_members_below_rank": true,
    "edit_message_of_day": true,
    "create_events": true,
    "manage_recruitment": true,
    "withdraw_bank_items_per_day": 20,
    "withdraw_gold_per_day": 250,
    "spend_treasury": false,
    "declare_war": false,
    "manage_guild_hall": true,
    "disband_guild": false
  }
}
```

---

# 73. Guild Bank Log Data Structure

```json
{
  "log_id": "guild_bank_log_901",
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "player_id": "player_045",
  "action": "withdraw_item",
  "item_id": "greater_healing_potion",
  "quantity": 20,
  "bank_tab": "Raid Consumables",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-13T11:00:00Z"
}
```

---

# 74. Guild Event Data Structure

```json
{
  "event_id": "guild_event_777",
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "title": "Ashen Drake Raid",
  "type": "raid",
  "start_time": "2026-05-15T20:00:00-05:00",
  "created_by": "player_001",
  "required_roles": {
    "tank": 2,
    "healer": 4,
    "damage": 14
  },
  "location": "Ashen Peak",
  "notes": "Bring fire resistance potions. Watch the wing buffet mechanic.",
  "signups": [
    {
      "player_id": "player_045",
      "status": "accepted",
      "role": "healer"
    }
  ]
}
```

---

# 75. Guild Project Data Structure

```json
{
  "project_id": "guild_project_forge_t2",
  "guild_id": "guild_123",
  "name": "Upgrade Guild Forge to Tier 2",
  "status": "in_progress",
  "required_materials": {
    "iron_bar": 500,
    "stone_block": 300,
    "oak_plank": 250,
    "arcane_crystal": 20
  },
  "contributed_materials": {
    "iron_bar": 320,
    "stone_block": 280,
    "oak_plank": 100,
    "arcane_crystal": 5
  },
  "required_gold": 10000,
  "contributed_gold": 6500,
  "rewards": {
    "guild_xp": 5000,
    "unlock": "tier_2_forge"
  }
}
```

---

# 76. Guild Server Authority Rules

The server must validate all guild actions.

Server validates:

```text
Guild membership
Rank permissions
Bank withdrawal limits
Treasury balance
Guild tax settings
Guild project requirements
Guild hall ownership
War declaration rights
Alliance permissions
Recruitment application state
Leadership transfer rules
Disband rules
Guild XP rewards
Guild achievement completion
```

The client cannot decide:

```text
Guild rank
Guild permissions
Guild bank contents
Guild treasury amount
Guild XP
Guild war status
Guild ownership
Guild hall access
Guild project completion
Guild rewards
```

---

# 77. Guild Transaction Pipeline

For high-risk guild actions:

```text
1. Player sends guild action request.
2. Server checks player is in guild.
3. Server checks rank permission.
4. Server checks cooldowns and restrictions.
5. Server validates target member/item/gold/project.
6. Server applies action atomically.
7. Server writes guild log.
8. Server notifies affected players.
9. Client updates UI.
```

High-risk actions include:

```text
Withdraw gold
Withdraw rare item
Change permissions
Promote officer
Kick member
Spend treasury
Declare war
Sell guild property
Transfer leadership
Disband guild
```

---

# 78. Guild Edge Cases

## Guild Leader Deletes Character

Rule:

```text
Leadership transfers to eligible co-leader/officer.
If no eligible member exists, guild enters leaderless recovery state.
```

## Guild Leader Is Inactive

Rule:

```text
After inactivity threshold, eligible officers can trigger leadership transfer.
Members are notified.
Leader has grace period.
```

## Officer Steals Guild Bank

Rule:

```text
Withdrawal limits, logs, and approval systems reduce risk.
Support can review logs.
Guild can lock bank.
```

## Member Is Kicked During Raid

Rule:

```text
Kicked player keeps personal loot eligibility already earned.
Guild chat and future guild access end.
```

## Guild Disbands With Items in Bank

Rule:

```text
Items go to recovery system based on guild rules.
Treasury distribution follows configured or default rules.
Disband has delay and warnings.
```

## Two Officers Change Same Permission

Rule:

```text
Server processes changes in timestamp/order.
Permission versioning prevents overwrite confusion.
Log both changes.
```

## Guild War Declared Accidentally

Rule:

```text
Requires confirmation.
Optional cooldown/cancel window.
Notify all members.
```

## Guild Name Violates Rules Later

Rule:

```text
Moderators can force rename.
Guild leader prompted to choose new valid name.
```

## Guild Hall Destroyed

Rule:

```text
Guild hall enters ruined state.
Stored items are protected.
Repair/rebuild project begins.
Services disabled until repaired.
```

## Player Leaves With Guild Loan Item

Rule:

```text
Loaned item returns automatically.
If impossible, item is disabled and recovered.
```

## Guild Tax Changed Secretly

Rule:

```text
Tax changes are logged and announced.
Members can view current tax.
```

## New Recruit Withdraws Valuable Items

Rule:

```text
Recruit rank has no withdrawal access by default.
New member withdrawal cooldown applies if configured.
```

---

# 79. Guild Abuse Prevention

Prevent:

```text
Guild bank theft
Fake guild recruitment scams
Mass invite spam
Guild name impersonation
War declaration spam
Guild tax abuse
Leadership hostage situations
Kicking players to deny earned loot
Using guilds to launder stolen goods
Bot guilds farming resources
Alt guilds dodging taxes or lockouts
Harassment through guild chat
```

Protection systems:

```text
Permission limits
Logs
Cooldowns
Confirmation prompts
Withdrawal caps
Recruitment rate limits
Report tools
Guild finder moderation
Disband delay
Leadership inactivity transfer
Guild bank approval
Suspicious transaction detection
```

---

# 80. Guild UI

Guild UI should include:

```text
Overview
Roster
Ranks
Permissions
Recruitment
Applications
Guild chat
Guild bank
Treasury
Guild projects
Guild hall
Guild events
Raid planner
PvP/war tab
Alliances
Guild achievements
Guild logs
Settings
```

Important UI rule:

```text
High-risk actions should show warnings and require confirmation.
```

---

# 81. Guild System Checklist

Before finalizing the guild system, check:

```text
Can players create guilds?
Are names validated?
Are ranks customizable?
Are permissions granular?
Is the guild bank logged?
Are withdrawals limited?
Can guilds recruit?
Can players apply?
Can guilds schedule events?
Can guilds manage raids?
Can guilds own halls/property?
Can guilds complete projects?
Can guilds participate in PvP/wars?
Can guilds ally with others?
Are guild actions logged?
Can inactive leadership be resolved?
Can guilds be disbanded safely?
Are stored items protected?
Are abuse cases handled?
Does the server validate everything?
```

```text
The guild system should create long-term community, cooperation, identity, economy, raids, PvP, construction, crafting, and social gameplay while preventing theft, griefing, and abuse.
```

# Final Guild System Rule

Every guild should answer:

```text
Who leads it?
Who can invite people?
Who can remove people?
Who can control money?
Who can access the bank?
Who can start projects?
Who can manage property?
Who can declare war?
Who can represent the guild?
What does the guild own?
What has the guild accomplished?
What prevents abuse?
What happens if the leader disappears?
```

Every guild action should answer:

```text
Does this player have permission?
Is the action logged?
Can it harm the guild?
Does it need confirmation?
Can it be reversed?
Can it be exploited?
What server validation is required?
```

That is how you make guilds feel like real organizations instead of simple player lists.

Below is a full **MMO Player Building, Repairing, Buying, and Property Ownership System**.

Core rule:

```text id="building-core-rule"
Players should be able to buy, build, repair, upgrade, decorate, rent, sell, and manage buildings in ways that feel physical, fair, useful, and connected to the world economy.

Buildings must never float, block important paths, clip into terrain, trap players, or break town navigation.
```

---

# 1. Building System Overview

The system should support:

```text id="building-supports"
Buying buildings
Buying land
Renting property
Building new structures
Repairing damaged buildings
Upgrading buildings
Decorating interiors
Managing permissions
Selling property
Guild-owned buildings
Town projects
Player housing
Shops
Workshops
Farms
Storage buildings
Defensive structures
Siege-damaged structures
Public buildings
NPC-assisted construction
```

Buildings can include:

```text id="building-types"
Houses
Cottages
Mansions
Shops
Market stalls
Guild halls
Workshops
Blacksmiths
Alchemy labs
Stables
Warehouses
Farms
Barns
Mills
Taverns
Inns
Watchtowers
Walls
Gates
Bridges
Docks
Shrines
Crafting halls
Player apartments
Castles
Fortresses
Outposts
```

# 3. Buying Buildings

Players can buy existing buildings if the world allows it.

## Buying Requirements

A player may need:

```text id="buying-requirements"
Enough gold
Required level
Required reputation
Required legal standing
Faction permission
Town citizenship
Property license
Guild rank
Noble title
No active bounty
No unpaid taxes
No ownership limit violation
```

Example:

```text id="buy-house-example"
Small Harthmere Cottage

Cost: 250 gold
Requires:
- Level 10
- Harthmere Likeability +500
- Legal Standing above -500
- No active bounty in Harthmere
```

---

## Building Purchase Flow

```text id="purchase-flow"
1. Player inspects property.
2. UI shows price, upkeep, features, permissions, taxes, and restrictions.
3. Server checks eligibility.
4. Player confirms purchase.
5. Server transfers gold.
6. Server assigns ownership.
7. Building becomes accessible to player.
8. Property deed is created.
9. Map marker updates.
10. NPCs react if relevant.
```

---

## Property Deed

Every owned building should have a deed.

A deed tracks:

```text id="deed-tracks"
Owner ID
Property ID
Location
Purchase price
Tax rate
Upkeep status
Permissions
Building condition
Upgrade level
Decorations
Storage access
Sale status
Guild/account status
Legal restrictions
```

Example:

```json id="deed-example"
{
  "deed_id": "deed_harthmere_cottage_01",
  "property_id": "harthmere_cottage_01",
  "owner_type": "player",
  "owner_id": "player_123",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "purchase_price": 25000,
  "tax_due_days": 14,
  "condition": 92,
  "access_permissions": {
    "friends": "visit",
    "guild": "visit",
    "public": "none"
  },
  "upgrades": ["small_storage", "hearth", "basic_garden"],
  "created_at": "2026-05-13T04:00:00Z"
}
```

---

# 4. Renting Buildings

Not every player needs to buy.

Rental property can include:

```text id="rental-types"
Inn rooms
Apartments
Market stalls
Workshops
Storage units
Farm plots
Guild offices
Temporary event booths
```

Rental rules:

```text id="rental-rules"
Player pays rent for a fixed duration.
If rent expires, access may pause.
Stored items must not be deleted unfairly.
Player receives warnings before expiration.
Rental can be renewed.
Some rentals can be upgraded to ownership.
```

Good rental design:

```text id="good-rental"
Cheap entry point for new players.
Limited storage and decoration.
No permanent loss if player misses a payment.
```

Bad rental design:

```text id="bad-rental"
Player loses all stored items because they missed one payment.
```

---

# 5. Buying Land

Players may buy land plots.

## Land Plot Rules

A land plot defines:

```text id="land-plot-defines"
Buildable area
Allowed building types
Maximum building size
Height limits
Road access
Terrain constraints
Ownership rules
Tax rate
Resource rights
Decoration limits
NPC path restrictions
PvP/siege status
```

Example:

```json id="land-plot-example"
{
  "plot_id": "harthmere_plot_farm_03",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "plot_type": "farm",
  "size": "medium",
  "allowed_buildings": ["farmhouse", "barn", "well", "fence", "garden"],
  "max_structures": 6,
  "requires_road_access": true,
  "tax_rate": 0.04,
  "pvp_damage_enabled": false,
  "build_zone_bounds": {
    "x_min": 100,
    "x_max": 140,
    "z_min": -80,
    "z_max": -40
  }
}
```

---

# 6. Land Claim Rules

If open-world building exists, players must claim land carefully.

## Valid Land Claim

Land claim must:

```text id="valid-land-claim"
Be inside a buildable zone
Not overlap roads
Not overlap towns unless allowed
Not overlap dungeon entrances
Not block quest objectives
Not block NPC patrols
Not block resource nodes unless intended
Not block player spawns
Not block water routes
Not be inside another player's claim
Not be inside protected/faction land without permission
Have valid terrain support
Have access path
```

---

## Invalid Land Claim

Invalid if:

```text id="invalid-land-claim"
Too steep
Underwater, unless dock/water building
Inside wall or terrain
Too close to town gate
Too close to major road
Too close to boss arena
Inside safe-zone public service area
Blocking bridge/path
Overlapping another property
Too close to enemy territory
Too close to rare resource spawn
```

---

# 7. Building Placement Rules

Building placement must be physically believable.

Before placing any building, validate:

```text id="building-placement-checks"
1. Is the terrain valid?
2. Is the slope acceptable?
3. Is the foundation touching ground?
4. Is the building not floating?
5. Is the building not sinking into terrain?
6. Is the building not clipping into another structure?
7. Is the entrance accessible?
8. Is there a path to the entrance?
9. Does it avoid roads, stairs, bridges, quest areas, and NPC routes?
10. Does it fit inside the plot boundary?
11. Does it respect height and size limits?
12. Does it leave enough space for players, NPCs, mounts, and carts?
```

---

## Foundation Rule

Every building must have a valid foundation.

```text id="foundation-rule"
A building must rest on ground, pillars, platform, stilts, foundation blocks, dock supports, tree supports, magical supports, or other clearly defined structural support.
```

Examples:

```text id="foundation-examples"
House on flat ground:
Uses stone/wood foundation.

House on slope:
Requires raised foundation or terracing.

Dock building:
Requires posts into waterbed.

Treehouse:
Requires tree supports and platforms.

Floating magic tower:
Allowed only if marked magical and has visual magical support.
```

---

# 8. Building Accessibility Rules

A building is not valid if players cannot use it.

Every usable building must have:

```text id="accessibility-building"
At least one accessible entrance
Clear path to entrance
Door not blocked
Interior spawn point, if enterable
Exit point
Enough headroom
Enough floor space
Valid collision
Valid navigation
Map marker if important
```

Bad:

```text id="bad-building-access"
Door faces a cliff.
Door opens into a wall.
Building sits in a fenced area with no gate.
Shop counter blocks the only entrance.
NPC shopkeeper is inside but player cannot reach them.
```

Good:

```text id="good-building-access"
Path from road to front door.
Door opens to valid interior.
Counter leaves customer space.
NPC has valid standing anchor.
Stairs connect floors.
```

---

# 10. Building Blueprint System

Players should build from blueprints.

Blueprints define:

```text id="blueprint-defines"
Building type
Size
Allowed plots
Material requirements
Gold cost
Labor required
Construction stages
Required skill/profession
Required permits
Upgrade paths
Interior layout
NPC anchor points
Collision bounds
Navmesh requirements
Map icon
```

Example:

```json id="blueprint-example"
{
  "blueprint_id": "small_cottage_tier_1",
  "name": "Small Cottage",
  "building_type": "residential",
  "allowed_plots": ["village_residential", "farm", "forest_homestead"],
  "required_materials": {
    "wood_plank": 80,
    "stone_block": 40,
    "iron_nail": 30,
    "cloth_bundle": 10
  },
  "gold_cost": 5000,
  "construction_stages": ["foundation", "frame", "walls", "roof", "interior"],
  "required_profession": null,
  "required_permit": "basic_building_permit",
  "max_occupants": 5,
  "storage_slots": 40,
  "map_icon": "house"
}
```

# 11. Building Construction Stages

Buildings should not appear instantly unless the game intentionally simplifies construction.

Recommended stages:

```text id="construction-stages"
1. Site Preparation
2. Foundation
3. Frame
4. Walls
5. Roof
6. Interior
7. Utility Setup
8. Final Inspection
9. Completed Building
```

---

## Stage Example

### Stage 1: Site Preparation

Requires:

```text id="site-prep"
Clear land
Remove rocks/trees
Level terrain
Mark build area
```

### Stage 2: Foundation

Requires:

```text id="foundation-stage"
Stone blocks
Wood beams
Clay or mortar
Labor
```

### Stage 3: Frame

Requires:

```text id="frame-stage"
Wood beams
Iron nails
Rope
Builder labor
```

### Stage 4: Walls

Requires:

```text id="walls-stage"
Planks
Stone
Brick
Clay
```

### Stage 5: Roof

Requires:

```text id="roof-stage"
Thatch
Tiles
Wood
Slate
Metal, for advanced buildings
```

### Stage 6: Interior

Requires:

```text id="interior-stage"
Doors
Windows
Floors
Furniture
Hearth
Lighting
```

---

# 12. Construction Time

Construction can take time.

Options:

```text id="construction-time-options"
Instant construction after materials
Short timer
Real-time staged construction
NPC labor-based construction
Player labor actions
Guild contribution construction
```

Recommended:

```text id="construction-time-recommended"
Small personal buildings:
Minutes to hours.

Guild halls:
Hours to days.

Town projects:
Days, with community contribution.

Major fortifications:
Long-term project with stages.
```

Avoid making basic housing take real-life weeks unless the MMO is built around long-term settlement gameplay.

---

# 13. Construction Labor

Labor can come from:

```text id="labor-sources"
Player actions
NPC workers
Hired builders
Guild members
Crafting professions
Town workers
Companions
Special construction tools
```

Labor can be represented as:

```text id="labor-represented"
Construction points
Work hours
Build progress percentage
Stage completion
NPC worker assignments
```

Example:

```text id="labor-example"
Small Cottage requires:
- 200 Construction Points
- 80 Wood Planks
- 40 Stone Blocks
- 30 Iron Nails

Player can contribute materials.
Carpenter NPC contributes 20 Construction Points per hour.
Guild members can speed progress.
```

---

# 14. Builder Profession

Optional profession: **Construction / Masonry / Carpentry**.

Profession affects:

```text id="builder-profession-effects"
Build speed
Material efficiency
Repair quality
Upgrade access
Structural durability
Decoration crafting
Blueprint unlocking
Bridge/wall construction
Guild project efficiency
```

Builder profession roles:

```text id="builder-profession-roles"
Carpenter
Mason
Architect
Engineer
Decorator
Siege engineer
Road builder
Shipwright
```

Common materials:

```text id="building-materials"
Wood logs
Wood planks
Stone blocks
Bricks
Clay
Iron nails
Iron bars
Rope
Cloth
Glass
Roof tiles
Mortar
Leather straps
Metal fittings
Paint/dye
Magic crystals
Enchanted supports
Decorative stone
```

Material rules:

```text id="material-rules-building"
Large buildings require large quantities.
Basic buildings use common materials.
Advanced buildings use refined materials.
Magical buildings use magical materials.
Defensive buildings require stronger materials.
Luxury buildings require rare decorations.
```

Building costs should include:

```text id="building-costs"
Land cost
Blueprint cost
Permit cost
Material cost
Labor cost
Tax/upkeep
Upgrade cost
Repair cost
Decoration cost
Service installation cost
```

Example:

```text id="cost-example"
Small Cottage:
Land: 250 gold
Permit: 25 gold
Materials: 80 planks, 40 stone blocks, 30 nails
Labor: 50 gold or player-built
Upkeep: 5 gold/week
```

# 17. Permits and Legal Rules

Towns should not let players build anything anywhere.

Permits can include:

```text id="permit-types"
Residential permit
Commercial permit
Crafting permit
Guild hall permit
Defensive structure permit
Farm permit
Dock permit
Noble estate permit
Magic tower permit
Black market permit, illegal
```

Permit requirements:

```text id="permit-requirements"
Gold fee
Legal standing
Town reputation
Faction rank
No active bounty
Zoning match
Building plan approval
```

Illegal building:

```text id="illegal-building"
Building without permit may cause fines.
Illegal structures may be demolished.
Guards may evict player.
Criminal factions may protect illegal buildings.
```

---

# 18. Zoning Rules

Cities and towns should have zoning.

Zones:

```text id="zoning-types"
Residential
Commercial
Crafting
Industrial
Noble
Slum
Farm
Dock
Military
Religious
Guild
Wilderness
Protected Nature
No-Build
PvP Territory
```

Rules:

```text id="zoning-rules"
Residential buildings go in residential zones.
Shops go in commercial zones.
Forges and workshops go in crafting/industrial zones.
Farms go outside town or in farm zones.
Defensive structures require military/faction permission.
Sacred/protected areas block normal building.
No-build zones protect roads, dungeons, quest areas, and landmarks.
```

```text id="ownership-controls"
Who can enter

Who can repair
Who can upgrade
Who can sell
Who can rent rooms
Who can set prices
Who can place NPC workers
Who can demolish
```

Permission roles:

```text id="permission-roles"
Owner

Guild officer
Tenant
Friend
Guest
Customer
Worker
Public
Blocked
```

Permission actions:

```text id="permission-actions"
Enter
Use storage
Deposit items
Withdraw items
Use crafting stations
Place decorations
Move decorations
Repair
Upgrade
Manage NPCs
Sell goods
Collect revenue
Invite guests
Kick visitors
Lock/unlock doors
Sell property
Demolish property
```

Example:

```json id="permissions-example"
{
  "owner": ["all"],
  "friend": ["enter", "use_crafting", "deposit_storage"],
  "guild": ["enter", "use_crafting"],
  "public": ["enter_shop_area", "buy_goods"],
  "blocked": []
}
```

# 22. Buying Shops and Running Businesses

Players can own shops.

Shop features:

```text id="shop-features"
Public sales area
Private storage
Shopkeeper NPC
Display shelves
Price settings
Open/closed hours
Tax rate
Revenue log
Customer permissions
Map icon
Reputation rating
```

Shop rules:

```text id="shop-rules"
Player lists items for sale.
Items are held in shop storage.
Buyers purchase from shopkeeper/interface.
Taxes are collected.
Unsold items remain in storage.
Shop can be robbed only if theft system allows it.
```

Map icon:

```text id="shop-map-icon"
Player shops should appear on the map only if public and open.
Shop category icon should match goods sold.
```

Examples:

```text id="shop-examples"
Player Blacksmith Shop:
Sells crafted weapons and repairs.

Player Alchemy Shop:
Sells potions and reagents.

Player Furniture Store:
Sells housing decorations.

Player Tavern:
Sells food, drink, rooms, rumors, and social buffs.
```

# 23. Building Upgrades

Buildings can be upgraded.

Upgrade categories:

```text id="upgrade-categories"
Size
Storage
Crafting stations
Defense
Comfort
NPC workers
Decorations
Shop capacity
Security
Resource production
Fast travel
Guild features
Magical features
```

Examples:

```text id="upgrade-examples"
Small Cottage → Large Cottage → Townhouse
Basic Forge → Improved Forge → Master Forge
Wooden Wall → Stone Wall → Reinforced Wall
Small Warehouse → Large Warehouse → Trade Depot
```

Upgrade rules:

```text id="upgrade-rules-building"
Requires materials.
Requires gold.
Requires building condition above threshold.
Requires blueprint or skill.
May require permit.
May take time.
Should not block entrances during construction unless intended.
```

---

# 24. Building Condition

Every damageable building has a condition score.

```text id="condition-score"
Condition: 0% to 100%
```

Condition states:

```text id="condition-states"
100%: Perfect
75% to 99%: Good
50% to 74%: Worn
25% to 49%: Damaged
1% to 24%: Critical
0%: Destroyed / Ruined / Disabled
```

Effects:

```text id="condition-effects"
Lower condition may reduce services.
Damaged buildings may leak, smoke, look broken, or lose function.
Critical buildings may become unsafe.
Destroyed buildings cannot function until rebuilt.
```

---

# 25. Building HP and Damage

Combat-relevant buildings can have HP.

Building stats:

```text id="building-stats"
HP
Max HP
Armor
Fire resistance
Siege resistance
Magic resistance
Structural integrity
Condition
Repair difficulty
Vulnerability type
```

Damage types:

```text id="building-damage-types"
Fire
Siege
Explosion
Earthquake
Decay
Monster attack
Player attack, if allowed
Weather
Flood
Corruption
Magic
War event
```

Rules:

```text id="building-damage-rules"
Normal players cannot damage buildings unless PvP/siege rules allow it.
Siege weapons deal more building damage.
Fire spreads only if fire system exists and is controlled.
Safe-zone buildings cannot be destroyed by random players.
Quest/event buildings may use scripted damage.
```

---

# 26. Repair System

Players can repair buildings.

Repair requires:

```text id="repair-requires"
Correct materials
Gold or labor
Repair tool
Repair skill/profession, optional
Permission
Safe access
Building not under active enemy attack, unless combat repair allowed
```

Repair materials depend on damage type:

```text id="repair-materials"
Wood damage:
Wood planks, nails, rope.

Stone damage:
Stone blocks, mortar, iron braces.

Fire damage:
Wood, stone, water, ash cleanup, cloth.

Magic damage:
Crystals, runes, essence, specialist NPC.

Siege damage:
Stone, beams, iron fittings, labor.
```

---

## Repair Flow

```text id="repair-flow"
1. Player inspects damaged building.
2. UI shows condition and required repair materials.
3. Server checks permission.
4. Server checks materials.
5. Player starts repair action or contributes materials.
6. Repair progress increases.
7. Building condition improves.
8. Services reactivate when threshold is met.
9. Visual damage updates.
```

---

## Repair Types

```text id="repair-types"
Quick Repair:
Fast, restores small condition.

Full Repair:
Restores to 100%, costs more.

Emergency Combat Repair:
Allowed during siege, limited and slower.

NPC Repair:
Hire workers to repair over time.

Guild Repair:
Members contribute materials and labor.

Town Repair:
Community project repairs public structures.
```

---

# 27. Combat Repair Rules

During sieges or attacks:

```text id="combat-repair"
Repairing under attack is allowed only if rules permit.
Repair speed is reduced.
Taking damage can interrupt repair.
Siege damage may apply repair lockout for a few seconds.
Repair materials are consumed gradually.
```

Example:

```text id="combat-repair-example"
Gate is at 35% HP.
Players repair with wood beams and iron braces.
If gate takes siege hit, repair pauses for 5 seconds.
```

---

# 28. Building Decay and Upkeep

Buildings may require upkeep.

Upkeep can include:

```text id="upkeep-includes"
Property tax
Repair maintenance
Food for workers
Fuel for crafting stations
Guild hall upkeep
Rent
Land lease
Town fees
```

Decay rules:

```text id="decay-rules"
Buildings should not decay too aggressively.
Players should receive warnings before penalties.
Personal homes should not vanish quickly.
Guild/territory structures may decay if abandoned.
Public-world land claims may decay to free space.
```

Recommended:

```text id="decay-recommended"
Instanced homes:
No decay or very gentle upkeep.

Open-world claims:
Upkeep required to prevent abandoned clutter.

Guild territory:
Upkeep required, with warnings and grace periods.
```

---

## Upkeep Failure

If upkeep is unpaid:

```text id="upkeep-failure"
Stage 1: Warning
Stage 2: Services disabled
Stage 3: Building enters abandoned state
Stage 4: Property can be reclaimed, packed, or auctioned
Stage 5: Structure removed only after long grace period
```

Do not delete valuable items without recovery.

Stored items should go to:

```text id="upkeep-items"
Mail
Bank overflow
Property recovery NPC
Account storage
Guild recovery log
```

---

# 29. Demolition System

Players may demolish buildings they own.

Demolition rules:

```text id="demolition-rules"
Only owner or authorized role can demolish.
Warn clearly.
Return some materials.
Remove decorations safely.
Move stored items to recovery.
Update map and permissions.
Clear plot.
Log transaction.
```

Demolition warning:

```text id="demolition-warning"
Demolish Small Cottage?
This will remove the building and return part of its materials.
All stored items will be moved to Property Recovery.
Type DEMOLISH to confirm.
```

---

# 30. Selling Property

Players can sell buildings.

Sale options:

```text id="sale-options"
Sell to NPC
List on player property market
Transfer to guild
Gift to another player
Auction property
Abandon property
```

Rules:

```text id="sale-rules"
Seller must own property.
Property taxes must be settled or transferred.
Bound/special properties may not be sellable.
Stored items must be removed or transferred.
Buyer must meet requirements.
Server validates sale atomically.
```

```text id="building-map-icons"
Construction Site: Crane/hammer icon
```

Visibility rules:

```text id="map-visibility"
Private homes appear only to owner/guests.
Public shops appear to everyone.
Guild halls appear to guild members or public if enabled.
Damaged public buildings appear to nearby players.
For-sale properties appear on property map.
Secret/illegal buildings appear only after discovery.
```

# 33. Interior System

Buildings can have interiors.

Options:

```text id="interior-options"
Seamless open-world interior
Instanced interior
Shared interior
Private phased interior
Guild instance
Apartment instance
```

Recommended:

```text id="interior-recommended"
Use instanced interiors for personal housing to avoid world clutter.
Use open-world exteriors for presence.
Use shared/public interiors for shops, guild halls, and taverns.
```

Interior rules:

```text id="interior-rules"
Entry point must match exterior door.
Exit must return player to valid exterior position.
Interior must have enough room.
Furniture cannot block exit.
NPCs need valid anchors.
Guests need permissions.
Combat rules must be clear.
```

```text id="decoration-types"
Statues
```

Decoration placement rules:

```text id="decoration-placement"
Furniture must sit on floor or valid surface.
Wall items must attach to walls.
Lights must hang, stand, or mount correctly.
Items cannot float unless magical.
Items cannot block exits.
Items cannot clip into walls excessively.
Items cannot block NPC anchors.
Large objects require enough room.
```

# 38. Security System

Buildings need security.

Security features:

```text id="security-features"

Guard NPCs

Traps
Reinforced doors
Permission lists
Trespass warnings
Security logs
Hidden rooms
Vaults
```

Rules:

```text id="security-rules"
Security must obey legal/PvP rules.
Traps cannot kill random players in safe towns unless illegal/criminal systems support it.
Guards respond based on law and permissions.
Stolen goods may be tracked.
```

---

# 39. Trespassing Rules

Entering private property without permission can be trespassing.

Trespass flow:

```text id="trespass-flow"
1. Player enters restricted area.
2. Warning appears.
3. Timer starts.
4. If player leaves, no penalty or minor warning.
5. If player remains, guards/owner react.
6. Theft/combat increases severity.
```

Example:

```text id="trespass-warning"
You are trespassing on private property.
Leave within 10 seconds or guards may be called.
```

---

```text id="building-events"
War
Player PvP siege
```

Damage should affect:

```text id="building-damage-effects"
Visual damage
Condition score
Service availability
NPC behavior
Repair quests
Town economy
Map markers
```

Example:

```text id="damage-example"
Dragon attacks town.
Several houses burn.
Blacksmith roof damaged.
Repair project appears.

Town Likeability increases for contributors.
```

# 41. Public Building Repair

Players can repair town buildings.

Public repair should be treated as a quest/project.

Public repair flow:

```text id="public-repair-flow"
1. Building is damaged.
2. Map shows repair marker.
3. NPC posts repair request.
4. Players contribute materials/labor.
5. Repair progress increases.
6. Building visually improves.
7. Service reopens.
8. Contributors receive rewards.
```

Rewards:

```text id="public-repair-rewards"
Gold
XP
Crafting XP
Town Likeability
Legal Standing, if official work
Faction reputation
Discounts
Titles
Access to services
```

# 44. Construction and Economy

Consumes:

```text id="construction-consumes"
Wood
Stone
Iron
Clay
Glass
Cloth
Rope
Nails
Furniture
Tools
Gold
Labor
Rare decorations
Magical materials
```

Economy effects:

```text id="construction-economy-effects"
Raises demand for raw materials.
Creates jobs for gatherers and crafters.
Creates gold sinks through permits/taxes/upkeep.
Creates player shop economy.
Creates long-term guild goals.
Creates repair demand after attacks.
```

# 45. Construction and Gathering

Buildings should require gathered/refined materials.

Example:

```text id="gathering-construction-example"
Build a barn:
- Gather logs
- Refine logs into planks
- Mine ore
- Craft iron nails
- Gather straw for roof
- Pay builder or contribute labor
```

This connects gathering, crafting, economy, and housing.


```text id="building-service-rules"

```


---

# 48. Building Condition and Services

Condition affects services.

```text id="condition-service-thresholds"
Condition 75%+:
All services available.

Condition 50% to 74%:
Services available, possible reduced efficiency.

Condition 25% to 49%:
Some services disabled.

Condition 1% to 24%:
Building unsafe, most services disabled.

Condition 0%:
Destroyed/ruined. No services.
```

Example:

```text id="condition-service-example"
Blacksmith condition drops below 40%.
Repair service still works.
Advanced crafting disabled until forge is repaired.
```

---

# 49. Player Building in Open World vs Instanced

## Open-World Building

Pros:

```text id="open-world-pros"
Feels physical and persistent.
Players see each other's work.
Supports territory and community.
```

Cons:

```text id="open-world-cons"
Can clutter world.
Can block paths.
Can be abused.
Requires strict placement rules.
```

## Instanced Building

Pros:

```text id="instanced-pros"
No world clutter.
Easy to scale.
More decoration freedom.
Better performance.
```

Cons:

```text id="instanced-cons"
Less visible in shared world.
Feels less physical.
```

Recommended:

```text id="building-recommendation"
Use open-world plots for towns, shops, guild halls, farms, and territory.
Use instanced interiors for personal housing and apartments.
Use strict placement validation for all open-world construction.
```

---

# 50. Building Limits

To prevent clutter and abuse:

```text id="building-limits"
Limit number of properties per player/account.
Limit open-world structures per plot.
Limit decorations per building.
Limit NPC workers per building.
Limit storage by upgrade.
Limit shop listings by building tier.
Limit defensive structures by territory rules.
```

Example:

```text id="building-limit-example"
Account Limits:
- 1 primary home
- 1 apartment
- 1 shop
- 1 farm plot
- Guild properties separate
```

---

# 51. Property Taxes

Taxes are a gold sink and town economy tool.

Tax types:

```text id="property-tax-types"
Residential tax
Commercial tax
Guild property tax
Land lease
Market stall fee
Luxury estate tax
Defensive structure upkeep
```

Tax rules:

```text id="tax-rules-building"
Taxes must be visible.
Players receive warnings before due date.
Taxes should not be brutal for casual players.
Commercial and guild properties can have higher taxes.
High-reputation players may get discounts.
Outlaws may be denied property or charged more.
```

---

# 52. Property and Reputation

Reputation affects property access.

## Likeability

```text id="property-likeability"
High Likeability:
Discounts, better locations, town support.

Low Likeability:
Higher prices, refusal, protests, vandalism risk in some systems.
```

## Legal Standing

```text id="property-legal"
High Legal Standing:
Eligible for official property, permits, town projects.

Low Legal Standing:
Cannot buy lawful property.
May lose permit.
May rely on black market hideouts.
```

## Notoriety

```text id="property-notoriety"
High positive Notoriety:
Nobles invite player to buy estates.

High negative Notoriety:
Towns may refuse, but criminals may offer hideouts.
```

---

Rules:

```text id="illegal-building-rules"
Requires criminal contacts.

May require bribes.
Can store stolen goods.
May be discovered through investigation.
```

# 55. Building and NPC Pathing

Buildings must not break navigation.

Rules:

```text id="building-pathing-rules"
Do not place buildings on NPC routes unless route updates.
Do not block roads.
Do not block gates.
Do not block quest NPCs.
Do not block resource access.
Do not block town services.
After construction, navmesh must update or use predefined plots.
NPCs must route around new obstacles.
```

Recommended:

```text id="pathing-recommended"
Use predefined build plots in towns.
Use dynamic navmesh only in controlled areas.
```

---

# 56. Building and Player Movement

Buildings must leave space for:

```text id="movement-space"
Players
NPCs
Pets
Mounts
Carts
Combat movement
Door opening
Stairs
Ramps
Bridges
Shop customers
Crowds
```

Minimum clearances:

```text id="building-clearances"
Front door: 2 meters clear
Main shop area: 4 meters customer space
Roadside building setback: 2 to 4 meters
Mount path: 5+ meters
Cart path: 6+ meters
Stair width: 2 meters
Public building entrance: 3+ meters
```

---

# 57. Building Placement Edge Cases

## Building Floats Above Ground

Rule:

```text id="building-floating-rule"
Placement invalid unless building has approved support system.
Snap to terrain or require foundation/pillars.
```

## Building Clips Into Terrain

Rule:

```text id="building-clipping-rule"
Allow minor foundation embedding only.
Reject major wall/floor clipping.
```

## Building Blocks Road

Rule:

```text id="building-block-road-rule"
Reject placement unless building is an approved gate, bridge, archway, or checkpoint.
```

## Building Door Is Inaccessible

Rule:

```text id="building-door-inaccessible-rule"
Reject placement.
Entrance must have valid path.
```

## Building Traps Player

Rule:

```text id="building-trap-rule"
Reject interior layout or decoration placement that blocks exits.
Provide unstuck option.
```

## Building Overlaps Resource Node

Rule:

```text id="building-resource-overlap-rule"
Reject unless plot explicitly allows resource building, such as mine/farm.
```

## Building Overlaps Quest Area

Rule:

```text id="building-quest-overlap-rule"
Reject placement.
Quest areas are protected no-build zones.
```

---

# 58. Repair Edge Cases

## Player Repairs Without Permission

Rule:

```text id="repair-no-permission"
Block repair unless public repair project allows it.
```

## Building Is Under Attack

Rule:

```text id="repair-under-attack"
Use combat repair rules.
Repair may be interrupted or reduced.
```

## Materials Are Consumed But Repair Fails

Rule:

```text id="repair-fail-atomic"
Repair contribution must be atomic.
If repair cannot apply, materials are not consumed.
```

## Building Reaches 0 Condition

Rule:

```text id="zero-condition"
Building becomes ruined/destroyed.
Services stop.
Repair may become rebuild project.
Stored items remain protected or moved to recovery.
```

## Multiple Players Repair Same Building

Rule:

```text id="multi-repair"
All valid contributions count.
Do not overconsume materials past required amount.
Excess contribution is rejected or refunded.
```

---

# 59. Buying Edge Cases

## Player Buys Property But Server Crashes

Rule:

```text id="buy-crash"
Purchase must be atomic.
Either player keeps gold and no deed, or loses gold and gets deed.
Never neither.
```

## Player Inventory Full During Deed Grant

Rule:

```text id="deed-inventory-full"
Deed should be stored in property system, not normal inventory.
Optional document copy can be mailed.
```

## Two Players Buy Same Property

Rule:

```text id="double-buy"
Server locks property during purchase.
First valid confirmed transaction wins.
Second transaction fails and refunds.
```

## Player Becomes Outlaw After Buying

Rule:

```text id="outlaw-after-buy"
Property may remain owned, but legal access/services may change.
Extreme crimes may allow seizure through legal system if designed.
Warn players before confiscation systems.
```

---

# 60. Building Damage Edge Cases

## Siege Damage Hits Safe-Zone House

Rule:

```text id="safe-house-siege"
Block unless declared siege/event allows it.
```

## Fire Spreads Through Whole Town

Rule:

```text id="fire-spread-rule"
Fire spread must have caps and event controls.
Do not let one player accidentally destroy entire city unless game is designed for that.
```

## Building Destroyed With Players Inside

Rule:

```text id="building-destroyed-inside"
Teleport occupants to nearest safe exit or rubble escape point.
Apply damage only if rules allow and warning exists.
```

## Building Owner Offline During Attack

Rule:

```text id="offline-owner-attack"
For normal housing, protect from random destruction.
For PvP territory, use declared vulnerability windows.
```

---

# 61. PvP and Siege Building Rules

Buildings can be involved in PvP if the game supports it.

Siege-vulnerable structures:

```text id="siege-vulnerable"
Guild forts
Faction gates
Watchtowers
Walls
Barricades
Resource outposts
War camps
Territory buildings
```

Protected structures:

```text id="protected-structures"
Personal homes in safe zones
Starter town buildings
Banks
Major quest hubs
Essential service buildings
```

Siege rules:

```text id="siege-rules-building"
Only during declared war/siege windows.
Only eligible attackers can damage.
Siege weapons required for major structures.
Defenders can repair.
Destroyed structures enter ruined state.
Rewards based on objectives, not random griefing.
```

---

# 62. Vulnerability Windows

To protect offline players/guilds:

```text id="vulnerability-windows"
Guild/faction structures can only be attacked during scheduled vulnerability windows.
Window is visible to attackers and defenders.
Defenders can prepare.
Attackers can plan.
```

Example:

```text id="vulnerability-example"
Guild Fort Vulnerable:
Saturday 7 PM to 9 PM server time.
```

---

```text id="construction-ui"
Available plots
Owned properties
Blueprints
Required materials
Gold cost
Permit requirements
Build preview
Placement validity
Construction stages
Current progress
Worker assignments
Repair needs
Upgrade options
Tax/upkeep
Permissions
Map icon settings
```

Placement preview should show:

```text id="placement-preview"
Green = valid
Red = invalid
Yellow = warning
Entrances
Plot boundaries
Collision bounds
Road clearance
NPC path conflicts
Slope warning
```

# 64. Repair UI

Repair UI should show:

```text id="repair-ui"
Building condition
Damaged components
Required materials
Repair cost
Repair time
Who can repair
Current contributors
Service restoration thresholds
Visual damage preview
```

Example:

```text id="repair-ui-example"
Harthmere Bridge
Condition: 42%

Needed for full repair:
- Stone Block: 120
- Wood Beam: 40
- Iron Brace: 20
- Labor: 300 points

Current progress: 63%
```

# 65. Property Management UI

Owner UI should include:

```text id="property-management-ui"
Property name
Owner
Location
Condition
Storage
Taxes/upkeep
Permissions
Guest list
Blocked list
Shop settings
Worker settings
Upgrade options
Repair options
Sell/rent options
Decoration mode
Security log
Visitor log
Revenue log
```

---

# 66. Server Authority

The server must validate all building actions.

Server validates:

```text id="server-validates-building"
Ownership
Permissions
Gold/currency
Materials
Blueprint requirements
Permit requirements
Plot boundaries
Collision
Terrain
Pathing
Entrance accessibility
No-build zones
NPC route conflicts
Quest area conflicts
Tax status
Repair state
Upgrade state
Transaction atomicity
```

The client cannot decide:

```text id="client-cannot-building"
Property ownership
Placement validity
Material consumption
Gold payment
Repair completion
Building damage
Building destruction
Shop sale
Tax payment
Permission bypass
```

---

# 67. Building Transaction Pipeline

For buying/building/repairing:

```text id="building-transaction-pipeline"
1. Player sends request.
2. Server validates player state.
3. Server validates property/plot/building.
4. Server validates permissions.
5. Server validates materials/currency.
6. Server validates physical placement or repair target.
7. Server applies transaction atomically.
8. Server updates world/property state.
9. Server logs transaction.
10. Server sends result to client.
```

---

# 68. Building Logs

Log all important property events.

Logs:

```text id="building-logs"
Purchase
Sale
Rent
Tax payment
Construction contribution
Material deposit
Repair contribution
Upgrade
Demolition
Permission change
Storage withdrawal
Storage deposit
Shop sale
Worker hire/fire
Building damage
Building destruction
Trespass
Lockpick
Guard response
```

Log fields:

```text id="building-log-fields"
Player ID
Account ID
Property ID
Building ID
Action
Materials
Gold
Location
Timestamp
Before/after state
Target player, if any
Guild ID, if any
```

---

# 69. Anti-Abuse Rules

Prevent players from using buildings to grief.

Abuse prevention:

```text id="building-anti-abuse"
No blocking roads.
No blocking doors.
No blocking NPCs.
No trapping players.
No placing buildings on resource spawns.
No building in quest areas.
No floating structures unless allowed.
No obscene/offensive arrangements.
No infinite storage exploit.
No tax bypass duplication.
No shop price scam without clear UI.
No demolition to delete other players' items.
No permission exploit to steal guild storage.
```

---

# 70. Building System Checklist

Before allowing a building:

```text id="building-checklist"
Is the plot valid?
Is the player allowed to build here?
Does the building fit?
Is the foundation supported?
Is the entrance accessible?
Does it avoid roads and quest areas?
Does it avoid NPC paths?
Does it avoid resource nodes?
Does it have valid collision?
Does it have valid interior/exit?
Are materials available?
Is payment valid?
Are permits valid?
Are permissions initialized?
Is map visibility correct?
Is storage safe?
Are logs created?
```

---

# 71. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text id="building-ai-prompt"
Design and implement a complete player building, repairing, buying, and property ownership system for this MMO.

The system must support:
- Buying buildings
- Buying land
- Renting property
- Building new structures
- Repairing damaged buildings
- Upgrading buildings
- Decorating interiors
- Selling property
- Guild-owned buildings
- Player shops
- Workshops
- Farms
- Warehouses

- Clip heavily into terrain
- Spawn inside other buildings
- Block roads
- Block doors
- Block NPC paths

Every building placement must validate:
- Terrain

Players may buy property only if they meet requirements:

Every owned property must have:

- Repair state
- Interior state
- Map visibility
- Logs

Construction must use:
- Blueprints
- Materials
- Gold
- Permits
- Labor
- Construction stages
- Optional builder profession
- Optional NPC workers

Construction stages should include:
- Site preparation
- Foundation
- Frame
- Walls
- Roof
- Interior
- Utility setup
- Final completion

Repair must support:
- Quick repair
- Full repair
- Emergency combat repair
- NPC repair
- Guild repair
- Public town repair projects

Building condition must affect services:
- 75%+ condition: services normal
- 50% to 74%: reduced efficiency
- 25% to 49%: some services disabled
- 1% to 24%: unsafe, most services disabled
- 0%: ruined/destroyed, rebuild required

Player shops must support:
- Listed goods
- Prices
- Shopkeeper NPC
- Public/private access
- Taxes
- Storage
- Revenue logs
- Map icons
- Security rules

Permissions must control:
- Enter
- Use storage

- Withdraw items
- Use crafting stations
- Decorate
- Repair
- Upgrade
- Manage NPCs
- Sell goods
- Collect revenue

- Sell property
- Demolish property

The building system must connect to:
- Gathering
- Crafting
- Economy
- Repairs
- Town projects
- Guild projects
- NPC workers
- Reputation
- Legal system
- PvP/siege system
- Quest system
- Map system

The server must be authoritative:
- Server validates ownership
- Server validates permissions
- Server validates placement
- Server validates materials
- Server validates gold
- Server validates repairs
- Server validates taxes

- Server validates shop sales

Handle edge cases:
- Two players buying same property
- Server crash during purchase
- Building floats
- Building clips into terrain
- Door inaccessible
- Building blocks road
- Building overlaps quest area
- Building overlaps resource node
- Player repairs without permission
- Materials consumed but repair fails
- Multiple players repair same building
- Building destroyed with players inside
- Owner offline during attack
- Upkeep unpaid
- Storage items during demolition
- Player loses access due to legal status
- Property sold with items inside
- NPC worker spawns inside wall
- Decoration blocks exit
- Siege damage hits protected house

The system should make building feel useful, physical, fair, economically meaningful, and connected to the world.
```
