# Final Building System Rule

Every building should answer:

```text id="final-building-rule"
Who owns it?
Who can enter it?
What is it used for?
Where is it placed?
Is the foundation valid?
Is the entrance accessible?
What materials built it?
What condition is it in?
Can it be repaired?
Can it be upgraded?
Can it be damaged?
Can it be sold?
Can it be taxed?
Can it be abused to block or grief players?
```

Every construction action should answer:

```text id="final-construction-rule"
Is the player allowed to build here?
Does the building physically fit?
Does it make sense in this location?
Does it preserve roads, NPC movement, quest access, and resource access?
Are the materials and costs valid?
What changes in the world after construction?
```

That is how you make player buildings feel like part of the MMO world instead of random objects dropped on the map.


Below is a full **MMO Gathering, Resources, and Raw Materials System**.

Core rule:

```text
Gathering should make the world feel useful, alive, and worth exploring.

Resources should appear where they make sense, be gathered with the right tools or skills, feed the crafting/economy systems, and avoid becoming boring, botted, or frustrating.
```

---

# 1. Gathering System Overview

The gathering system should allow players to collect raw materials from the world.

Players can gather:

```text
Ore
Stone
Wood
Herbs
Plants
Fiber
Leather
Hides
Meat
Bones
Fish
Water
Clay
Sand
Gems
Crystals
Magical essence
Monster parts
Animal parts
Mushrooms
Food ingredients
Relics
Ancient fragments
Rare world materials
```

Raw materials should support:

```text
Crafting
Cooking
Alchemy
Blacksmithing
Tailoring
Leatherworking
Carpentry
Enchanting
Engineering
Housing
Guild projects
Town projects
Trade routes
Quest objectives
Economy
Player trading
Faction supply missions
```

---

# 2. Resource Categories

Resources should be divided into categories.

```text
Mining Resources
Logging Resources
Herbalism Resources
Skinning Resources
Fishing Resources
Farming Resources
Monster Harvest Resources
Scavenging Resources
Magical Resources
Archaeology Resources
Environmental Resources
Rare Event Resources
```

Each category should have:

```text
Required tool
Required skill/profession
Spawn locations
Respawn rules
Material tiers
Gather time
Yield amount
Failure chance, if any
Rare bonus chance
Anti-bot protection
Crafting uses
Market value
```

---

# 3. Gathering Professions

Players may gather through professions.

## Main Gathering Professions

```text
Mining
Logging
Herbalism
Skinning
Fishing
Farming
Scavenging
Archaeology
Monster Harvesting
Magical Harvesting
```

## Profession Purpose

```text
Mining:
Ore, stone, gems, crystals.

Logging:
Wood, bark, resin, branches, sap.

Herbalism:
Herbs, flowers, roots, mushrooms, seeds.

Skinning:
Hides, leather, fur, scales.

Fishing:
Fish, shells, pearls, water creatures.

Farming:
Crops, fruit, vegetables, grain, livestock products.

Scavenging:
Scrap metal, cloth, broken parts, old tools, battlefield debris.

Archaeology:
Relics, bones, ancient tablets, fragments, artifacts.

Monster Harvesting:
Claws, fangs, organs, venom, scales, monster cores.

Magical Harvesting:
Essence, mana crystals, spirit residue, elemental motes.
```

---

# 4. Resource Placement Rules

Resources must appear where they make sense.

Bad:

```text
Iron ore floating in the sky.
Fish node in the middle of a dry road.
Palm wood in a frozen mountain.
Rare flowers growing inside stone walls.
Ore vein placed where the player cannot reach it.
Tree node clipping through a building.
Herb node spawning under terrain.
```

Good:

```text
Iron ore appears in caves, cliffs, mines, rocky hills, and mountains.
Medicinal herbs grow near forests, rivers, fields, and temple gardens.
Rare mushrooms grow in caves, swamps, and dark forests.
Fish appear in rivers, lakes, oceans, and ponds.
Ancient relics appear near ruins, tombs, battlefields, and old roads.
Magical crystals appear near ley lines, ruins, caves, meteor sites, and enchanted zones.
```

---

# 5. Resource Location Logic

Each resource should have valid biomes.

## Ore and Stone

```text
Valid locations:
Mountains
Caves
Mines
Cliffs
Rocky hills
Quarries
Underground tunnels
Volcanic regions
Ancient ruins

Invalid locations:
Open water
Wooden floors
Soft farmland
Inside houses unless stored as cargo
Floating in air
```

## Herbs and Plants

```text
Valid locations:
Forests
Fields
Swamps
Riverbanks
Gardens
Mountainsides
Jungles
Caves, for fungi
Magical groves

Invalid locations:
Bare stone
Inside walls
Deep underwater unless aquatic plant
Lava zones unless fire-resistant plant
```

## Wood

```text
Valid locations:
Forests
Groves
Jungles
Village outskirts
Orchards
Dead woods
Magical forests

Invalid locations:
Deserts, unless deadwood/cactus
Open ocean
Stone caves, unless roots or fungus wood
Urban interiors unless stored lumber
```

## Fish

```text
Valid locations:
Rivers
Lakes
Oceans
Ponds
Swamps
Underground lakes
Frozen lakes with ice fishing
Magical waters

Invalid locations:
Dry land
Air
Lava, unless fire fish
Non-water decorative blue surfaces
```

## Monster Parts

```text
Valid sources:
Dead monsters
Bosses
Creature nests
Hunting grounds
Dungeons
World events

Invalid sources:
Random crates unless stored by hunters
Peaceful NPCs unless morally/legally allowed
```

---

# 6. Resource Nodes

A resource node is an interactable gathering point.

Examples:

```text
Iron Vein
Copper Vein
Ancient Tree
Medicinal Herb
Fishing Pool
Clay Deposit
Crystal Cluster
Fallen Beast Corpse
Mushroom Patch
Ruined Crate
Battlefield Scrap
Mana Rift
```

Each node should define:

```text
Node ID
Resource type
Required tool
Required profession
Required skill level
Gather time
Yield table
Rare drop table
Respawn timer
Spawn location rules
Maximum active nodes
Depletion rules
Ownership rules
Visual state
Sound effects
Interaction animation
```

---

# 7. Resource Node Data Example

```json
{
  "node_id": "iron_vein_tier_2",
  "name": "Iron Vein",
  "category": "mining",
  "required_tool": "pickaxe",
  "required_profession": "mining",
  "required_skill_level": 25,
  "gather_time_seconds": 4,
  "base_yield": {
    "iron_ore": {
      "min": 2,
      "max": 5
    }
  },
  "rare_yield": {
    "rough_garnet": {
      "chance": 0.04,
      "min": 1,
      "max": 1
    },
    "dense_stone": {
      "chance": 0.15,
      "min": 1,
      "max": 3
    }
  },
  "respawn_seconds": 300,
  "valid_biomes": ["mountain", "cave", "mine", "rocky_hills"],
  "requires_navmesh_access": true,
  "depletes_after_gathers": 1,
  "shared_node": true
}
```

---

# 8. Resource Tiers

Materials should have tiers based on region, level, danger, and crafting value.

Example:

```text
Tier 1:
Copper, softwood, peacebloom, rabbit hide.

Tier 2:
Iron, oak, silverleaf, wolf hide.

Tier 3:
Steel ore, ashwood, bloodroot, bear hide.

Tier 4:
Mithril, ironwood, ghost orchid, wyvern scale.

Tier 5:
Adamantite, elderwood, star lotus, dragon scale.

Legendary:
Meteorite ore, worldroot, phoenix feather, ancient dragon heart.
```

Tier rules:

```text
Higher-tier resources appear in higher-level or more dangerous zones.
Low-tier resources should remain useful in recipes, repairs, housing, or refinement.
Rare resources should not be required in huge quantities for basic progression.
Legendary materials should come from meaningful achievements, not random spam farming.
```

---

# 9. Gathering Tools

Players need proper tools.

Tools include:

```text
Pickaxe
Axe
Sickle
Skinning knife
Fishing rod
Shovel
Hammer
Chisel
Surveying kit
Magical extractor
Essence vial
Trap
Net
Bucket
Sieve
Archaeology brush
```

Tool rules:

```text
A player must have the right tool to gather certain resources.
Better tools reduce gather time or improve rare yield chance.
Tools may have durability.
Tools may require profession level.
Tools may be equipped or kept in inventory.
```

Example:

```text
Rusty Pickaxe:
Can mine Tier 1 ore.
Slow gather speed.

Iron Pickaxe:
Can mine Tier 1–2 ore.
Normal gather speed.

Masterwork Pickaxe:
Can mine Tier 1–4 ore.
Small rare-gem bonus.

Runed Pickaxe:
Can mine magical ore and crystals.
Requires Mining 75 and Arcane Handling.
```

---

# 10. Gathering Skill Levels

Each gathering profession should have progression.

Example:

```text
Mining Level 1–25:
Copper, stone, tin.

Mining Level 26–50:
Iron, coal, silver.

Mining Level 51–75:
Mithril, dense stone, gems.

Mining Level 76–100:
Adamantite, rare crystals, magical ore.

Mining Level 100+:
Legendary veins, meteor ore, ancient metal.
```

Skill level affects:

```text
Which nodes can be gathered
Gather speed
Yield amount
Rare drop chance
Failure chance
Tool efficiency
Special discoveries
Tracking ability
Resource quality
```

---

# 11. Gathering Attempt Pipeline

When player gathers a node:

```text
1. Player interacts with resource node.
2. Server validates node exists.
3. Server checks player distance.
4. Server checks line of sight.
5. Server checks tool requirement.
6. Server checks profession requirement.
7. Server checks skill level.
8. Server checks player state.
9. Server starts gathering action.
10. Player animation plays.
11. Server checks interruption.
12. Server rolls yield.
13. Server adds materials to inventory/material storage.
14. Server grants profession XP.
15. Node depletes or updates.
16. Server starts respawn timer if depleted.
17. Client updates UI.
```

The server must be authoritative. The client cannot decide that gathering succeeded.

---

# 12. Player State Rules

A player cannot gather if they are:

```text
Dead
Downed
Stunned
In combat, unless resource allows combat gathering
Mounted, unless mounted gathering is allowed
Swimming, unless gathering aquatic resources
Flying, unless gathering aerial resources
Trading
Teleporting
Loading
In a cutscene
Too far from node
Without required tool
Without required skill
In wrong quest phase
In a restricted area
```

Optional:

```text
Allow gathering while stealthed, but gathering may break stealth.
Allow gathering while mounted for special mounts or upgrades.
Allow underwater gathering with proper breathing tools.
```

---

# 13. Gathering Time

Gathering should take time.

Example:

```text
Common herb: 1.5 seconds
Common ore: 3 seconds
Tree chopping: 4 seconds
Fishing cast: 5 to 15 seconds
Rare crystal: 6 seconds
Ancient relic excavation: 8 to 20 seconds
Monster harvest: 3 to 6 seconds
```

Rules:

```text
Higher-tier resources take longer.
Better tools reduce gather time.
Taking damage can interrupt gathering.
Movement cancels gathering.
Some gathering actions make sound and can attract enemies.
```

---

# 14. Gathering Success and Failure

Most simple gathering should succeed if requirements are met.

Recommended:

```text
Common nodes:
Guaranteed success if requirements are met.

Rare/advanced nodes:
May have partial success, bonus success, or failure chance.

Dangerous magical nodes:
May backfire if skill is too low.
```

Avoid making normal gathering annoying with constant failures.

Better failure design:

```text
Low skill does not destroy the node.
Low skill gives lower yield.
High skill gives bonus yield.
```

Example:

```text
Player has Mining 30.
Iron Vein requires Mining 25.
Result: normal yield.

Player has Mining 25 exactly.
Result: lower chance for bonus gems.

Player has Mining 60.
Result: faster gather and higher bonus gem chance.
```

---

# 15. Yield System

Gathering should produce materials through yield tables.

## Basic Yield

```text
Common result from node.
```

Example:

```text
Iron Vein:
2–5 Iron Ore
1–3 Stone
```

## Bonus Yield

```text
Extra materials based on skill/tool/luck.
```

Example:

```text
+1 Iron Ore if Mining skill is high.
+1 rare gem if lucky.
```

## Rare Yield

```text
Low chance valuable resource.
```

Example:

```text
4% chance Rough Garnet
1% chance Flawless Garnet
0.1% chance Ancient Metal Shard
```

---

# 16. Yield Formula

Example formula:

```text
Final Yield =
Base Yield
+ Skill Bonus
+ Tool Bonus
+ Buff Bonus
+ Region Bonus
+ Rare Roll
```

Skill bonus example:

```text
If player skill is 25+ above node requirement:
+10% yield.

If player skill is 50+ above node requirement:
+20% yield.

If player skill is 75+ above node requirement:
+30% yield and bonus rare chance.
```

Keep caps:

```text
Maximum normal yield bonus: +50%
Maximum rare chance bonus: +100% relative, not flat
```

This prevents high-level gatherers from flooding the economy too hard.

---

# 17. Rare Material Rules

Rare materials should feel exciting but not mandatory for every basic recipe.

Rare materials can come from:

```text
Rare node spawn
Bonus gather roll
Dangerous zones
Elite monsters
World bosses
Hidden areas
Profession quests
Events
Resource refinement
Treasure maps
Guild projects
```

Rules:

```text
Rare materials should have clear sources.
Rare materials should be tradeable unless tied to personal progression.
Rare power materials should be controlled.
Rare cosmetic materials can be more flexible.
Rare resource spawns should not be easy for bots to camp.
```

---

# 18. Node Respawn Rules

Nodes should respawn after depletion.

Respawn timer examples:

```text
Common herb: 2 to 5 minutes
Common ore: 3 to 8 minutes
Common tree: 5 to 10 minutes
Rare herb: 15 to 45 minutes
Rare ore: 30 to 90 minutes
Legendary node: event-based, daily, weekly, or random world spawn
```

Rules:

```text
Use variable respawn timers.
Do not respawn instantly in the exact same pattern.
Avoid predictable bot routes.
Respawn only in valid locations.
Do not spawn nodes inside walls, under terrain, or unreachable places.
```

---

# 19. Shared vs Personal Nodes

Decide how nodes are shared.

## Shared Node

```text
One player gathers it, then it depletes for everyone.
```

Pros:

```text
Creates competition.
Supports economy scarcity.
```

Cons:

```text
Can cause frustration.
Bots can monopolize routes.
Players can grief each other.
```

## Personal Node

```text
Each player sees or can gather their own version.
```

Pros:

```text
Less frustration.
Better for quest resources.
Better for casual players.
```

Cons:

```text
Can flood economy if not controlled.
```

## Hybrid Node

Recommended.

```text
Common resources are shared or semi-shared.
Quest resources are personal.
Event resources are contribution-based.
Rare resources use dynamic spawn protection.
```

---

# 20. Semi-Shared Node Rule

A good compromise:

```text
After one player gathers a node, the node remains available to other nearby players for 30 to 60 seconds before disappearing.
```

This prevents one player from stealing a node while another is fighting nearby.

---

# 21. Resource Ownership Rules

When a player discovers or starts gathering a node:

```text
Node may become temporarily reserved.
Reservation lasts only a short time.
Taking too long releases the node.
Combat or death releases the node.
Reservation should not allow griefing.
```

Example:

```text
Player begins mining Iron Vein.
Node is reserved for 10 seconds.
If player cancels or moves away, node becomes available again.
```

---

# 22. Quest Resource Rules

Quest gathering should be less frustrating.

Rules:

```text
Quest resources should be personal or fast-respawning.
Quest resources should be marked clearly.
Quest resources should not be monopolized by other players.
Quest items should go to quest pouch.
Quest resources should appear in logical locations.
```

Example:

```text
Quest: Gather 5 Moonflowers.
Moonflowers appear in the marked forest area.
Each player sees their own Moonflowers.
Moonflowers glow softly when nearby.
```

---

# 23. Resource Tracking

Players should be able to track resources.

Tracking can unlock through profession skill.

Examples:

```text
Mining 10:
Show nearby copper veins on minimap.

Mining 30:
Show iron and silver veins.

Herbalism 20:
Show common herbs.

Fishing 25:
Show active fishing pools.

Perception attribute:
Slightly improves detection radius.
```

Tracking UI:

```text
Minimap icons
Compass hints
World shimmer
Sound cue
Profession sense ability
Map filter
```

Do not show every rare node globally. That makes farming too mechanical.

---

# 24. Resource Map Symbols

Map icons should be clear.

```text
Ore vein: Pickaxe icon
Tree/logging node: Axe icon
Herb: Leaf icon
Fishing pool: Fish hook icon
Animal harvest: Hide icon
Archaeology dig: Shovel/brush icon
Crystal: Crystal icon
Magical essence: Spark icon
Scrap pile: Gear icon
Water source: Droplet icon
Clay: Clay lump icon
Rare node: Star border
Quest resource: Quest marker overlay
Dangerous resource: Warning marker
```

Rules:

```text
Common nodes may appear only on minimap when nearby.
Resource-rich areas can be marked on the world map.
Rare nodes should appear only after discovery, tracking, rumor, or profession skill.
```

---

# 25. Gathering and Inventory

Materials should not make inventory miserable.

Recommended:

```text
Raw materials go directly into Material Storage when possible.
Quest materials go to Quest Item Pouch.
Currency-like fragments go to Currency Wallet.
Normal gathered items go to backpack if material storage is unavailable.
```

If inventory is full:

```text
Do not delete gathered material.
If material storage has room, deposit there.
If no storage, block gathering before completion or send to overflow if allowed.
Show clear message.
```

Example:

```text
Inventory full. Iron Ore was sent to Material Storage.
```

or:

```text
Inventory full. You need space before gathering this.
```

---

# 26. Material Storage

Material storage should support:

```text
High stack limits
Auto-deposit
Search
Sort
Filter by profession
Show crafting uses
Show market value
Show region demand
```

Material filters:

```text
Ore
Wood
Herbs
Leather
Cloth
Gems
Fish
Meat
Monster Parts
Magical
Archaeology
Housing
Quest
```

---

# 27. Gathering XP

Gathering should level professions.

Players gain gathering XP from:

```text
Successful gathering
Rare resource discovery
First-time gathering of a material
Profession quests
Refinement
Supplying town projects
Completing gathering contracts
```

XP should scale by:

```text
Node tier
Required skill
Danger level
Rarity
Time investment
Daily/weekly bonuses
```

Example:

```text
Copper Vein: 5 Mining XP
Iron Vein: 15 Mining XP
Mithril Vein: 40 Mining XP
Rare Crystal: 100 Mining XP
Legendary Meteorite: 500 Mining XP
```

No XP or reduced XP if:

```text
Node is far below player skill
Player is AFK
Player repeats exploit route too long
Node was spawned by abuse
```

---

# 28. Gathering Contracts

NPCs, towns, and guilds can request materials.

Examples:

```text
Bring 20 Iron Ore to the blacksmith.
Deliver 10 Healing Herbs to the temple.
Supply 50 Wood for bridge repairs.
Collect 5 Venom Sacs for alchemist.
Gather 30 Fish for festival feast.
```

Contract rewards:

```text
Gold
Profession XP
Faction reputation
Likeability
Crafting recipes
Town project progress
Trade tokens
Special tools
```

Rules:

```text
Contracts create material sinks.
Repeatable contracts need caps.
High-demand contracts should change with town economy.
```

---

# 29. Gathering and Town Economy

Gathering should feed the economy.

Town needs:

```text
Food
Wood
Ore
Medicine
Stone
Cloth
Tools
Weapons
Water
Luxury goods
Construction materials
```

If players gather and deliver supplies:

```text
Town wealth increases.
Shops restock faster.
Prices improve.
Buildings repair.
Guards become better equipped.
New quests unlock.
```

If supplies are low:

```text
Prices rise.
NPCs complain.
Quests appear.
Crime may rise.
Sickness may spread.
Construction halts.
```

---

# 30. Gathering and Crafting

Every raw material should have uses.

Examples:

```text
Iron Ore:
Smelt into Iron Bars.
Used for weapons, armor, nails, tools, town projects.

Oak Wood:
Used for bows, shields, furniture, carts, ships, buildings.

Peacebloom:
Used for healing potions, temple offerings, cooking.

Wolf Hide:
Used for leather armor, bags, tents, drum heads.

Crystal Shard:
Used for enchantments, magic focuses, lamps, teleport stones.
```

Bad design:

```text
Material exists but has no use except selling to vendor.
```

Good design:

```text
Material is used in crafting, quests, trade, town projects, and economy.
```

---

# 31. Refinement System

Raw materials can be refined.

Examples:

```text
Iron Ore → Iron Bar
Raw Hide → Cured Leather
Logs → Planks
Herbs → Extracts
Fish → Fillets
Crystal Shards → Arcane Dust
Clay → Bricks
Sand → Glass
```

Refinement rules:

```text
Requires crafting station or tool.
May require fuel.
May grant profession XP.
Can create higher-quality outputs.
Can consume multiple low-tier materials into higher-tier material.
```

Example:

```text
3 Iron Ore + 1 Coal = 1 Iron Bar
10 Rough Herbs = 1 Herbal Extract
5 Crystal Shards = 1 Arcane Dust
```

---

# 32. Material Quality

Materials can have quality tiers.

```text
Poor
Common
Fine
Rare
Pristine
Mythic
```

Quality affects:

```text
Crafted item stats
Crafting success chance
Rare recipe requirements
Vendor value
Market value
Quest turn-in quality
Upgrade strength
```

Example:

```text
Common Iron Ore:
Makes normal Iron Bar.

Pristine Iron Ore:
Higher chance to craft Uncommon Iron Sword.

Meteoric Iron:
Required for legendary weapon recipe.
```

---

# 33. Gathering Mini-Games

Optional, but use carefully.

Good mini-game design:

```text
Short
Optional or rewarding, not mandatory
Skill-based
Not annoying after repetition
Accessible
Works with latency
Gives bonus yield, not basic success
```

Examples:

```text
Fishing timing mini-game
Mining weak-point strike
Herbalism careful extraction
Archaeology brush pattern
Magical essence stabilization
```

Bad mini-game design:

```text
Every herb requires a 30-second puzzle.
Lag causes failure.
Failure destroys rare node.
Players must do it hundreds of times.
```

Recommended:

```text
Basic gather succeeds normally.
Mini-game improves yield or rare chance.
```

---

# 34. Dangerous Gathering

Some resources should require risk.

Dangerous resource zones:

```text
Monster caves
War zones
PvP zones
Volcanoes
Poison swamps
Haunted forests
Dragon lairs
Ancient ruins
Underwater trenches
Blizzard mountains
```

Risk factors:

```text
Hostile enemies
Environmental damage
Limited breathing
Weather exposure
PvP threat
Traps
Elite creatures
Resource guardians
Timed events
```

Rewards:

```text
Higher yield
Rare materials
Unique crafting components
Faction reputation
High market value
Special recipes
```

Rule:

```text
Higher risk should mean better rewards, but the danger must be clear.
```

---

# 35. Resource Guardians

Rare resources can be protected.

Examples:

```text
Crystal golem guarding crystal cluster
Ancient treant protecting sacred wood
Giant spider nest around silk
Dragon near rare scales
Water spirit near moonwell
Bandits guarding mine
Undead around grave relics
```

Rules:

```text
Guardian must match resource theme.
Players must defeat, avoid, pacify, or bargain with guardian.
High-level resource guardians should not spawn in starter areas.
```

---

# 36. Environmental Conditions

Some resources depend on world conditions.

Examples:

```text
Moonflower only blooms at night.
Storm Crystal appears during thunderstorms.
Frost Lotus appears during snow.
Sun Herb appears at dawn.
Ghost Mushroom appears in haunted areas.
Meteor Ore appears after meteor event.
Fire Bloom grows near lava.
Tide Pearls appear at low tide.
```

Rules:

```text
Condition-based resources should be discoverable through lore, NPC hints, profession knowledge, or map notes.
Do not make essential materials available only at extremely inconvenient times.
```

---

# 37. Seasonal Resources

Seasonal resources appear during events or seasons.

Examples:

```text
Winterberry
Spring Blossom
Harvest Pumpkin
Festival Fish
Starfall Crystal
Holiday Spice
```

Rules:

```text
Seasonal resources should mostly support cosmetics, event foods, housing, or limited recipes.
Avoid making core progression depend on seasonal materials unless alternatives exist.
```

---

# 38. Player Farms and Gardens

Players may grow resources.

Systems:

```text
Personal farm
Guild farm
Town farm
Herb garden
Animal pen
Fishing pond
Tree nursery
Mushroom cellar
Alchemy greenhouse
```

Rules:

```text
Farming takes time.
Requires seeds or animals.
Requires water/fertilizer/tools.
Yields are predictable but slower than wild gathering.
Rare crops require special conditions.
Farming should not completely replace exploration gathering.
```

Example:

```text
Plant Peacebloom Seed.
Water once per day.
Harvest after 12 hours.
Yield: 3–6 Peacebloom.
Small chance: Fine Peacebloom.
```

---

# 39. Animal Harvesting

Animals can provide materials.

Animal resources:

```text
Hide
Meat
Bones
Fat
Fur
Antlers
Feathers
Eggs
Milk
Wool
Venom
Claws
Fangs
Scales
```

Rules:

```text
Killing wild animals may allow skinning.
Domestic animals may produce repeatable resources if owned.
Killing protected/domestic animals may be illegal.
Rare animal parts should come from rare or dangerous creatures.
```

Example:

```text
Cow:
Milk if owned or permitted.
Hide/meat only if slaughtered, which may be illegal if not owned.

Wolf:
Hide, meat, fang, bone.

Wyvern:
Scale, venom gland, wing membrane, claw.
```

---

# 40. Monster Harvesting

Some monsters can be harvested after death.

Examples:

```text
Spider venom
Dragon scale
Troll blood
Slime core
Demon horn
Undead bone dust
Elemental essence
Golem heart
Ghost residue
Kraken ink
```

Rules:

```text
Requires Monster Harvesting skill or proper tool.
Boss materials may have lockouts.
Party/raid contribution determines eligibility.
Rare parts may be personal loot.
Harvesting should not block normal loot.
```

---

# 41. Fishing System

Fishing should have its own rules.

Fishing requires:

```text
Fishing rod
Water body
Bait, optional
Fishing skill
Valid fishing location
```

Fish types depend on:

```text
Water type
Region
Time of day
Weather
Season
Bait
Fishing skill
Depth
Special events
```

Examples:

```text
River:
Trout, salmon, river eel.

Ocean:
Cod, tuna, pearls, crab.

Swamp:
Mudfish, leech, poisonous eel.

Underground lake:
Blind fish, cave shrimp, glowing algae.

Magical lake:
Mana koi, spirit fish, moon pearl.
```

Fishing results:

```text
Fish
Trash
Treasure
Rare fish
Quest item
Cooking ingredient
Alchemy ingredient
Pet/mount cosmetic
```

---

# 42. Mining System

Mining should use veins and deposits.

Mining resources:

```text
Copper
Tin
Iron
Coal
Silver
Gold
Mithril
Adamantite
Stone
Salt
Gems
Crystals
Meteorite
Obsidian
```

Mining rules:

```text
Ore appears in rocky areas.
Higher-tier ore appears in more dangerous zones.
Mines should have higher density but more enemy risk.
Mining makes sound and may attract enemies in dangerous areas.
Rare gems are bonus drops.
```

---

# 43. Logging System

Logging resources:

```text
Softwood
Hardwood
Oak
Ash
Ironwood
Elderwood
Bark
Sap
Resin
Branches
Charcoal wood
Magical wood
```

Rules:

```text
Trees must exist physically.
Do not allow cutting important decorative or quest trees unless intended.
Some trees are protected by law or spirits.
Logging near towns may be restricted.
Forests can have regrowth timers.
```

Optional:

```text
Cutting too many trees in an area may reduce wildlife, change visuals, or anger druids.
```

Use carefully so players do not destroy the world unintentionally.

---

# 44. Herbalism System

Herbalism resources:

```text
Healing herbs
Poison herbs
Flowers
Roots
Mushrooms
Seeds
Berries
Vines
Rare blossoms
Magical plants
```

Rules:

```text
Herbs appear in appropriate biomes.
Some herbs require time/weather conditions.
Poisonous herbs may harm low-skill gatherers.
Rare herbs may be guarded or hidden.
```

Example:

```text
Bloodroot:
Grows near old battlefields.
Used in strength potions and blood magic.

Moonflower:
Blooms at night near water.
Used in mana potions and illusion spells.
```

---

# 45. Scavenging System

Scavenging allows gathering from ruins, battlefields, junk piles, and abandoned places.

Scavenged resources:

```text
Scrap metal
Broken gears
Cloth scraps
Old coins
Glass shards
Rope
Nails
Bones
Weapon fragments
Armor plates
Ancient parts
```

Valid locations:

```text
Ruins
Battlefields
Bandit camps
Abandoned houses
Crashed caravans
Shipwrecks
Dungeons
Sewers
War zones
```

Rules:

```text
Scavenging should not produce high-value items constantly.
Rare finds should be possible.
Scavenging may trigger traps, enemies, or legal issues if looting owned property.
```

---

# 46. Archaeology System

Archaeology is slower, discovery-based gathering.

Resources:

```text
Relic fragments
Ancient coins
Pottery shards
Tablets
Bones
Fossils
Lost maps
Ancient mechanisms
Artifact pieces
```

Rules:

```text
Requires survey tool or brush.
Found near ruins, tombs, old roads, ancient battlefields.
Often uses search areas rather than visible nodes.
May unlock lore, quests, recipes, or museum turn-ins.
Rare artifacts should be protected by puzzles, enemies, or long-term collection goals.
```

---

# 47. Magical Gathering

Magical materials require special rules.

Resources:

```text
Mana essence
Elemental motes
Spirit residue
Leyline crystal
Void shard
Holy dust
Demonic ichor
Arcane thread
Soul fragment
```

Rules:

```text
Requires magical extractor, vial, or spell.
May require class, spell, or profession.
May be dangerous if unstable.
Can be affected by moon phase, leyline activity, world events, or nearby magic.
```

Hazards:

```text
Arcane explosion
Mana burn
Temporary debuff
Summoned elemental
Curse
Teleport displacement
```

---

# 48. Water and Environmental Resources

Environmental resources include:

```text
Fresh water
Salt water
Clay
Sand
Mud
Snow
Ice
Lava rock
Volcanic ash
Honey
Wax
Feathers
Eggs
```

Rules:

```text
Water from wells, rivers, lakes.
Clay from riverbanks and wetlands.
Sand from beaches and deserts.
Ice from frozen regions.
Volcanic ash near volcanoes.
Honey from beehives.
```

These should support cooking, alchemy, building, crafting, and quests.

---

# 49. Resource Depletion

Resources may deplete after gathering.

Options:

```text
Single-use node:
One gather depletes node.

Multi-use node:
Several gathers before depletion.

Shared timed node:
Node remains for short time after first gather.

Personal node:
Only depletes for that player.

Regenerating node:
Slowly restores gather charges.
```

Recommended:

```text
Common resources: shared timed or multi-use.
Rare resources: single-use or limited-use.
Quest resources: personal.
Public event resources: contribution-based.
```

---

# 50. Resource Quality by Region

Same material can vary by region.

Examples:

```text
Mountain Iron:
Higher chance of dense ore.

Swamp Herbs:
Higher poison properties.

Sacred Grove Wood:
Better for bows and holy items.

Volcanic Crystal:
Better for fire magic.

Frozen Lake Fish:
Better for stamina food.
```

This encourages trade and exploration.

---

# 51. Gathering Buffs

Players can improve gathering with buffs.

Sources:

```text
Food
Potions
Tools
Gear
Enchantments
Profession talents
Guild bonuses
Town bonuses
Weather
Companion help
Mount bonuses
```

Buff effects:

```text
Faster gather time
Increased yield
Increased rare chance
Reduced tool durability loss
Better tracking range
Reduced interruption chance
Special material discovery
```

Rules:

```text
Buffs should stack with caps.
Do not allow infinite rare chance stacking.
Show active gathering bonuses in UI.
```

---

# 52. Gathering Gear

Players can wear gathering gear.

Stats:

```text
Mining skill
Logging skill
Herbalism skill
Fishing skill
Gather speed
Rare find chance
Yield bonus
Tool durability reduction
Environmental resistance
Stealth while gathering
```

Rules:

```text
Gathering gear should help gatherers.
Combat gear should not always be best for gathering.
Gathering gear may make player weaker in combat, creating risk in dangerous zones.
```

---

# 53. Gathering Mounts and Pets

Optional systems.

Examples:

```text
Pack mule:
Increases material carry capacity.

Mining mole pet:
Small chance to find extra ore.

Falcon:
Highlights herbs or tracks animals.

Fishing boat:
Improves ocean fishing.

Logging ox:
Helps carry logs.
```

Rules:

```text
Pets/mounts should assist, not automate gathering fully.
Avoid AFK farming.
Bonuses should be modest or require active play.
```

---

# 54. Anti-Bot and Anti-Exploit Rules

Gathering systems are bot targets.

Protections:

```text
Server-authoritative gathering
Movement validation
Randomized node spawn locations
Variable respawn timers
Diminishing returns on repetitive routes
Suspicious behavior tracking
No client-side item granting
Gather action requires valid distance/line of sight
Rare nodes require active interaction
New account trade limits for high-value materials
Transaction logs
```

Suspicious behavior:

```text
Same path for hours
Gathering 24/7
Impossible reaction times
Instant node targeting
Teleport-like movement
Gathering through walls
Repeated node use beyond timer
No normal human pauses
Mass transfer of materials to one account
```

Avoid punishing normal players. Use flags, review, and measured throttles.

---

# 55. Diminishing Returns

Diminishing returns can reduce bot farming.

Example:

```text
If player gathers the same node type in the same small area for too long:
Normal yield for first hour.
-10% yield after suspicious repetition.
-25% yield after extended repetition.
Rare drops temporarily reduced if bot-like pattern continues.
```

Better alternative:

```text
Encourage route variety with regional bonuses.
Add daily gathering contracts.
Use rested/profession bonus for normal players.
```

Do not punish legitimate players who enjoy gathering.

---

# 56. Gathering and PvP

Some resources may appear in PvP zones.

Rules:

```text
PvP resource zones must be clearly marked.
Resources in PvP zones can have higher value.
Death may drop gathered resources only if zone rules clearly say so.
Players need safe exits or alternate PvE sources for core progression materials.
```

Good use:

```text
PvP zones have faster access to rare trade goods.
PvE players can still get materials through slower or safer methods.
```

Bad use:

```text
Mandatory crafting material only exists in full-loot PvP zone.
```

Unless the entire MMO is built around that risk.

---

# 57. Gathering and Legal System

Not all gathering should be legal.

Illegal gathering examples:

```text
Cutting protected trees
Poaching royal deer
Mining in private mine
Picking herbs from temple garden
Stealing crops
Fishing in restricted waters
Harvesting graves
Taking resources from noble estate
```

Consequences:

```text
Legal Standing loss
Fines
Confiscation
Guard response
Faction reputation loss
NPC hostility
Bounty if repeated
```

Example:

```text
Player chops sacred grove tree.
Druid faction reputation decreases.
Legal penalty applies if region protects grove.
Treant guardian may attack.
```

---

# 58. Resource Ownership

Some resources are owned.

Owned resources:

```text
Farm crops
Private mine ore
Orchard fruit
Temple herbs
Guild lumber
Noble hunting grounds
Player housing garden
Town storage crates
Caravan cargo
```

Rules:

```text
Gathering owned resource without permission counts as theft.
Permission can come from quest, purchase, faction rank, job contract, or ownership.
Stolen materials may be marked stolen.
```

---

# 59. Gathering Permissions

Players may gain permission through:

```text
Quest
Faction rank
Town reputation
Guild membership
Land ownership
Profession license
Legal permit
Bribe
Black market contract
```

Example:

```text
Mining License:
Allows mining in Ironhold public mine.
Without license, guards may fine the player.
```

---

# 60. Gathering UI

Gathering UI should show:

```text
Resource name
Required tool
Required skill
Gather progress bar
Expected material type
Rare material hint, if discovered
Node difficulty
Ownership warning
PvP/danger warning
Inventory/storage status
Tool durability
Profession XP gained
```

Example:

```text
Iron Vein
Requires: Pickaxe
Mining Skill: 25
You have: Mining 32
Gathering...
```

Warning:

```text
This tree is protected by the Druid Circle.
Harvesting it is illegal.
```

---

# 61. Resource Tooltips

Material tooltip should show:

```text
Material name
Tier
Category
Quality
Stack size
Used by professions
Known recipes
Vendor value
Market average, if known
Region demand
Quest usage
Stolen status
```

Example:

```text
Iron Ore
Tier 2 Mining Material
Used in: Blacksmithing, Engineering, Town Projects
Can be refined into Iron Bar.
Market Demand: High in Warfront City
Stack: 37 / 200
```

---

# 62. Gathering Sound and Visual Feedback

Gathering should feel satisfying.

Feedback:

```text
Pickaxe strike sound
Tree chopping sound
Herb pluck sound
Fishing splash
Crystal hum
Rare material sparkle
Profession XP popup
Material gain popup
Node depletion animation
Tool hit animation
```

Rare find example:

```text
Rare Find!
You discovered Rough Garnet.
```

Do not spam too many popups. Stack repeated materials into concise messages.

---

# 63. Gathering and NPC Reactions

NPCs should react to gathering.

Examples:

```text
Farmer yells if player steals crops.
Guard questions player mining without permit.
Druid warns player not to cut sacred trees.
Merchant thanks player for supply delivery.
Bandits attack players carrying rare ore.
Villagers appreciate players gathering medicine during plague.
```

NPC reaction depends on:

```text
Resource ownership
Player reputation
Legal standing
Faction
Quest permission
Witnesses
Notoriety
```

---

# 64. Gathering and Events

World events can create gathering opportunities.

Examples:

```text
Meteor falls:
Meteorite ore appears.

Dragon attacks:
Dragon scales and burned wood appear.

Flood:
Rare river clay appears.

Plague:
Medicinal herbs become high demand.

War:
Battlefield scrap appears.

Festival:
Special flowers and food ingredients appear.

Drought:
Water and food resources become valuable.
```

Rules:

```text
Event resources should have timers.
Event materials should not permanently break economy.
Event resources should be discoverable through map, rumors, or announcements.
```

---

# 65. Resource Scarcity

Scarcity should be intentional.

Scarcity can be created by:

```text
Limited spawn locations
Dangerous zones
Long respawn timers
Seasonal availability
Boss drops
Crafting cooldowns
Faction access
Town state
Weather/time conditions
```

Rules:

```text
Scarce materials should be valuable.
Scarce materials should not block basic progression.
Scarce materials should have multiple acquisition paths if required for important systems.
```

---

# 66. Resource Surplus

Too much material can hurt economy.

Controls:

```text
Material sinks
Town projects
Crafting recipes
Repair recipes
Refinement
Vendor demand caps
Trade route exports
Daily contracts
Guild projects
Housing construction
```

Example:

```text
If too much Iron Ore exists, towns may post construction contracts that consume Iron Bars.
```

---

# 67. Gathering and Trade Routes

Raw materials can become trade goods.

Example:

```text
Mountain town exports ore.
Forest village exports lumber.
Coastal town exports fish.
Swamp village exports herbs.
Desert town exports glass and spices.
```

Players can:

```text
Gather local goods
Sell to merchants
Transport to high-demand regions
Use auction house
Fulfill contracts
Supply caravans
Smuggle restricted goods
```

---

# 68. Group Gathering

Players can gather together.

Group gathering can support:

```text
Shared nodes
Large resource events
Guild mining expeditions
Lumber camps
Fishing parties
Archaeology digs
Monster harvesting after raids
Town supply drives
```

Rules:

```text
Contribution determines reward.
Support roles can help.
Players should not be able to AFK and receive full reward.
Guild projects can require large quantities.
```

Example:

```text
Guild discovers Ancient Quarry.
Members gather stone, defend miners, repair carts, and transport supplies.
Rewards based on contribution.
```

---

# 69. Large Resource Nodes

Some resources require multiple players.

Examples:

```text
Ancient tree
Massive ore deposit
Whale carcass
Dragon corpse
Collapsed ruin
Meteor crater
Giant crystal
Sunken treasure ship
```

Rules:

```text
Requires group effort.
May have phases.
May attract enemies.
Rewards all contributors.
May support guild/town projects.
Should have lockout or depletion rules.
```

---

# 70. Gathering From Corpses

After killing certain creatures, players can harvest.

Rules:

```text
Creature must be dead.
Player must have required tool/skill.
Corpse must not already be fully harvested.
Harvest eligibility follows loot/contribution rules.
Quest harvests should be personal.
Rare boss harvests may be locked weekly.
```

Example:

```text
Dragon boss dies.
Eligible raid members can receive Dragon Scale through personal loot.
High-skill skinner may harvest extra Dragonhide for raid crafting pool.
```

---

# 71. Server Authority Rules

The server must validate gathering.

Server validates:

```text
Node exists
Node is active
Player is close enough
Player has line of sight
Player has required tool
Player has required skill
Player is allowed to gather
Player is not dead/stunned/loading
Node has not been depleted
Inventory/storage can receive item
Yield roll
Profession XP
Node depletion
Respawn timer
Anti-bot flags
```

The client cannot decide:

```text
Node yield
Rare drops
Profession XP
Node depletion
Respawn
Tool requirement
Skill requirement
Inventory gain
```

---

# 72. Gathering Logs

Log important gathering actions.

Log fields:

```text
Player ID
Account ID
Node ID
Resource type
Location
Tool used
Profession level
Yield items
Rare drops
Timestamp
Session route pattern
Inventory destination
Quest/event ID, if any
Legal/ownership status
```

Logs help with:

```text
Economy balancing
Bot detection
Bug reports
Dupe investigation
Resource tuning
Quest debugging
```

---

# 73. Gathering Edge Cases

## Node Spawns Inside Wall

Rule:

```text
Do not spawn node.
Try fallback location.
Log placement error.
```

## Node Is Floating

Rule:

```text
Snap node to valid surface.
If no valid surface exists, do not spawn.
```

## Node Is Unreachable

Rule:

```text
Requires valid path or interaction line.
If unreachable, remove and respawn elsewhere.
```

## Player Starts Gathering and Node Despawns

Rule:

```text
If gathering already validated, allow completion or cancel gracefully.
Do not grant duplicate items.
```

## Two Players Gather Same Node

Rule depends on node type:

```text
Shared node:
First completed gather depletes node.

Semi-shared node:
Both can gather within grace period.

Personal node:
Each gets own result.
```

## Player Disconnects While Gathering

Rule:

```text
Cancel gather.
Do not grant materials unless server had already completed action.
```

## Player Dies While Gathering

Rule:

```text
Cancel gather.
No materials granted unless action completed before death.
```

## Inventory Full at Completion

Rule:

```text
Send to material storage if possible.
Otherwise cancel pickup and keep node depleted only if yield was successfully stored.
Do not delete material silently.
```

## Tool Breaks During Gathering

Rule:

```text
If tool was valid at start, gather may complete.
After completion, tool breaks.
Next gather requires repair/replacement.
```

## Player Gathers Owned Resource Without Permission

Rule:

```text
Mark action as theft.
Apply legal/reputation effects if witnessed or detected.
Material may be marked stolen.
```

## Player Gathers in Wrong Phase

Rule:

```text
Only show/interact with nodes valid for player's current phase.
If party members are phased differently, warn them.
```

## Rare Node Camped by Bots

Rule:

```text
Use variable spawn locations.
Track suspicious behavior.
Apply account-level detection.
Do not make rare node spawn perfectly predictable.
```

## Player Attempts to Gather Through Wall

Rule:

```text
Server line-of-sight check fails.
Show "Cannot reach that."
```

## Player Uses Speed Hack to Gather Fast

Rule:

```text
Server controls gather timer.
Client animation speed cannot shorten server gather duration.
```

## Player Sends Duplicate Gather Requests

Rule:

```text
Use action IDs.
Process only one gather attempt at a time per node/player.
```

---

# 74. Example Resource Data Structure

```json
{
  "resource_id": "peacebloom",
  "name": "Peacebloom",
  "category": "herb",
  "tier": 1,
  "quality": "common",
  "stack_size": 200,
  "used_by": ["alchemy", "cooking", "temple_offerings"],
  "base_value": 4,
  "valid_biomes": ["field", "forest_edge", "garden"],
  "required_profession": "herbalism",
  "required_skill": 1,
  "refines_into": ["peacebloom_extract"],
  "quest_relevant": true,
  "tradeable": true,
  "stolen_possible": true
}
```

```json
  "spawn_id": "node_spawn_harthmere_herb_001",
  "resource_node": "peacebloom_patch",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "biome": "field",
  "position": [112.4, 0.0, -45.8],
  "rotation": [0, 80, 0],
  "spawn_radius": 8,
  "requires_ground": true,
  "requires_navmesh_access": true,
  "min_respawn_seconds": 120,
  "max_respawn_seconds": 300,
  "max_active_in_area": 12,
  "ownership": "public",
  "phase": "default"
}
```

```json

  "player_id": "player_123",
  "node_id": "iron_vein_tier_2",
  "profession": "mining",
  "profession_level": 34,
  "tool": "iron_pickaxe",
  "location": "north_mine",
  "success": true,
  "yield": [
    {
      "item_id": "iron_ore",
      "quantity": 4
    },
    {
      "item_id": "dense_stone",
      "quantity": 1
    }
  ],
  "profession_xp": 18,
  "rare_find": false,
  "node_depleted": true,
  "timestamp": "2026-05-13T03:30:00Z"
}
```

---

# 77. Gathering Checklist

Before adding a resource, check:

```text
Does this resource make sense in this location?
Is it physically reachable?
Is it on valid ground/surface/water?
Does it require the right tool?
Does it require the right profession?
Does it have a crafting/economic use?
Does it have a respawn rule?
Does it have a visual model?
Does it have gathering feedback?
Does it avoid spawning inside walls or under terrain?
Does it support inventory/material storage correctly?
Does it have anti-bot protections?
Does it affect quests, economy, or town systems?
Does it have legal ownership rules if needed?
```

---

# 78. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design and implement a complete gathering, resource, and raw material system for this MMO.

The system must support:
- Mining
- Logging
- Herbalism
- Skinning
- Fishing
- Farming
- Scavenging
- Archaeology
- Monster harvesting
- Magical resource harvesting
- Environmental resources

Resources must appear only where they make physical and ecological sense:
- Ore in caves, cliffs, mines, rocky hills, mountains
- Herbs in forests, fields, swamps, riverbanks, gardens
- Fish in water
- Wood in forests and groves
- Clay near rivers and wetlands
- Relics near ruins, tombs, battlefields
- Magical crystals near ley lines, ruins, caves, magical zones
- Monster parts from dead monsters
- Animal products from appropriate animals

Resources must never:
- Float in the air
- Spawn inside walls
- Spawn under terrain
- Spawn inside props
- Spawn where players cannot reach them
- Appear in illogical biomes
- Block important paths
- Be gathered through walls

Every resource node must define:
- Node ID
- Resource type
- Required tool
- Required profession
- Required skill level
- Gather time
- Yield table
- Rare yield table
- Respawn timer
- Spawn rules
- Depletion rules
- Ownership rules
- Visual state
- Sound/animation feedback

Gathering must be server-authoritative.
The server validates:
- Player distance
- Line of sight
- Node existence
- Node active state
- Tool requirement
- Skill requirement
- Profession requirement
- Player state
- Inventory/material storage capacity
- Yield roll
- Rare drop roll
- Profession XP
- Node depletion
- Respawn timer

The client may show animation and UI, but the server decides success, yield, rare drops, XP, and depletion.

Gathered materials should feed:
- Crafting
- Refinement
- Cooking
- Alchemy
- Blacksmithing
- Leatherworking
- Tailoring
- Carpentry
- Enchanting
- Engineering
- Housing
- Guild projects
- Town projects
- Quests
- Trade routes
- Economy

Use resource tiers:
- Tier 1 starter materials
- Tier 2 regional materials
- Tier 3 advanced materials
- Tier 4 rare materials
- Tier 5 endgame materials
- Legendary event/boss materials

Low-tier materials should remain useful through recipes, repairs, housing, refinement, or town projects.

Gathering should grant profession XP based on:
- Node tier
- Required skill
- Rarity
- Danger level
- Time investment
- First-time discovery
- Contract completion

The system must support:
- Shared nodes
- Personal nodes
- Semi-shared nodes
- Quest-specific nodes
- Event nodes
- Rare nodes
- Group gathering nodes

Quest resources should usually be personal or fast-respawning so other players cannot block progress.

Gathering must connect to the economy:
- Local resources are cheaper in their region
- Scarce resources become more valuable
- Town projects consume resources
- Trade routes move resources
- War, famine, plague, festivals, and world events affect demand
- Materials can be sold, traded, auctioned, refined, or crafted

Gathering can be legal or illegal:
- Public resources are safe
- Owned crops, private mines, temple herbs, noble hunting grounds, and protected trees require permission
- Illegal gathering may cause fines, reputation loss, guards, confiscation, or bounty
- Stolen materials may be marked stolen

The system must handle edge cases:
- Node spawns inside wall
- Node is floating
- Node is unreachable
- Player disconnects while gathering
- Player dies while gathering
- Inventory is full
- Tool breaks
- Two players gather same node
- Player gathers owned resource without permission
- Player gathers in wrong quest phase
- Player tries to gather through wall
- Player sends duplicate gather requests
- Rare node is camped by bots
- Speed hack tries to reduce gather time
- Server crash during gather

Add anti-bot protections:
- Variable respawn timers
- Randomized spawn points within valid areas
- Server movement validation
- Diminishing returns for suspicious repetitive routes
- Logging of gathering behavior
- Rare node unpredictability
- Transaction IDs
- New account limits for high-value material transfers if needed

Gathering UI must show:
- Resource name
- Required tool
- Required skill
- Gather progress
- Ownership warning
- Danger/PvP warning
- Tool durability
- Materials gained
- Rare finds
- Profession XP
- Inventory/storage status

The system should make gathering feel useful, believable, rewarding, and connected to crafting, quests, towns, NPCs, economy, and exploration.
```

---

# Final Gathering Rule

Every resource should answer:

```text
What is it?
Where does it naturally appear?
Who owns it, if anyone?
What tool gathers it?
What skill gathers it?
What does it produce?
What is it used for?
How often does it respawn?
Can it be traded?
Can it be stolen?
Can it be refined?
Can it be crafted with?
Can it be exploited?
How does the server validate it?
```

And every gathering node should answer:

```text
Is it physically reachable?
Is it on valid ground, water, wall, tree, corpse, or surface?
Is it in the right biome?
Is it visible enough to find?
Does it avoid walls, props, and blocked paths?
Does it reward the player fairly?
Does it support the economy without flooding it?
```

That is how you make gathering feel like part of the world instead of random glowing objects scattered everywhere.

Below is a full **MMO PvP, Raid, Party, and Cooperative Combat System**.

Core rule:

```text
Players should be able to fight with and against each other in ways that feel fair, readable, rewarding, and hard to exploit.

PvP should create competition.
Raids should create large-scale teamwork.
Parties should make players stronger together without letting them abuse rewards.
```

---

# 1. Multiplayer Combat System Overview

The MMO should support:

```text
Solo combat
Small parties
Raiding parties
World boss groups
Dungeon groups
Guild groups
PvP duels
Arena PvP
Battleground PvP
Open-world PvP
Faction wars
Guild wars
Bounty hunting
Siege warfare
Public events
Cooperative world defense
```

Every multiplayer combat system must define:

```text
Who is allowed to fight?
Who is allied?
Who is hostile?
Who can damage whom?
Who gets credit?
Who gets loot?
Who gets XP?
Who gets reputation?
Who can revive whom?
What happens on death?
What prevents griefing?
What prevents reward abuse?
```

---

# 2. Player Combat Relationship States

Every player-to-player relationship should have a combat state.

```text
Friendly
Neutral
Hostile
Duel Opponent
Party Member
Raid Member
Guild Member
Faction Ally
Faction Enemy
Criminal Target
Bounty Target
Arena Opponent
Battleground Opponent
War Target
Protected Player
Safe-Zone Player
```

These states decide whether players can:

```text
Damage each other
Heal each other
Buff each other
Trade with each other
Revive each other
Share quest progress
Share loot
Share XP
See each other on map
Enter the same instance
Capture objectives together
```

---

# 3. PvP System Overview

PvP means player versus player combat.

PvP should exist in controlled forms.

```text
Duels
Arenas
Battlegrounds
Open-world PvP zones
Faction war zones
Guild war zones
Bounty hunting
Criminal justice PvP
Siege warfare
Hardcore PvP zones
Tournament PvP
```

PvP should never be unclear. The player should always know when they are entering a PvP situation.

---

# 4. PvP Flagging Rules

Players should have clear PvP flags.

```text
Unflagged
Duel Flagged
Voluntary PvP Flagged
Faction War Flagged
Guild War Flagged
Arena Flagged
Battleground Flagged
Criminal Flagged
Bounty Target
Hardcore PvP Flagged
Protected / Spawn Protected
```

## Unflagged

```text
Player cannot be attacked by other players in normal zones.
Player cannot attack unflagged players.
Player can still fight NPCs.
```

## Voluntary PvP Flagged

```text
Player has chosen to enable PvP.
Other PvP-flagged players may attack them.
Flag remains for a timer after hostile action.
```

Recommended timer:

```text
PvP flag remains active for 5 minutes after last PvP action.
```

## Criminal Flagged

A player becomes criminal flagged when they unlawfully attack or harm protected players/NPCs.

```text
Criminal players may be attacked by guards, bounty hunters, and lawful players depending on region rules.
```

## Bounty Target

```text
Player has a bounty.
Eligible players can hunt them.
Killing/capturing the bounty target gives reward.
Repeated farming of the same bounty is prevented.
```

---

# 5. PvP Consent Rules

PvP should not surprise players in normal safe content.

Rules:

```text
Safe zones disable PvP.
Starter zones disable hostile PvP.
Players must knowingly enter PvP zones.
Dueling requires consent from both players.
Arena requires queue/entry consent.
Battleground requires queue/entry consent.
Faction war zones must clearly warn players.
Hardcore PvP zones must clearly warn players before entry.
```

Warning example:

```text
Warning:
You are entering the Ashen Warfront.
PvP is enabled in this area.
Other players may attack you.
```

---

# 6. Safe Zone Rules

Safe zones include:

```text
Starter towns
Inns
Banks
Temples
Respawn shrines
Major quest hubs
Player housing
Auction houses
Important service areas
```

Rules:

```text
Players cannot attack other players inside safe zones.
Players cannot cast hostile spells from safe zones into combat zones.
Players cannot abuse safe zones by attacking then instantly hiding.
Recent aggressors may be denied safe-zone protection for a short time.
```

Aggression timer:

```text
If player attacks another player, they cannot gain full safe-zone protection for 30 to 60 seconds.
```

---

# 7. Duel System

Duels are controlled one-on-one PvP.

## Duel Rules

```text
Both players must accept.
Duel countdown starts.
Duel area is defined.
Leaving duel area forfeits.
Duel ends at surrender, low HP threshold, or death depending on rules.
Duel does not cause durability loss unless desired.
Duel does not create legal penalties.
Duel cannot happen in crowded service areas unless allowed.
```

Recommended duel ending:

```text
Player loses when reduced to 1 HP instead of dying.
```

This keeps duels fun and low-risk.

---

# 8. Arena PvP

Arena PvP is structured competitive PvP.

Arena modes:

```text
1v1
2v2
3v3
5v5
Solo queue
Ranked team queue
Unranked practice
Tournament mode
```

## Arena Rules

```text
Players enter a controlled arena.
Teams are equal size.
Outside buffs are removed or normalized.
Consumables may be limited.
Gear may be normalized depending on mode.
Respawns are usually disabled during round.
Victory comes from defeating all opponents or objective rules.
```

## Ranked Arena

Ranked arena should track:

```text
Rating
Win/loss record
Matchmaking rating
Season rank
Rewards
Titles
Leaderboard
Class/spec data
Disconnects
Forfeits
Suspicious match history
```

Anti-abuse:

```text
No reward for win trading.
No reward for repeated surrender abuse.
No queue dodging exploit.
No duplicate account farming.
No boosting through fake matches.
```

---

# 9. Battleground PvP

Battlegrounds are larger structured PvP matches.

Examples:

```text
Capture the Flag
King of the Hill
Control Points
Payload Escort
Resource Race
Fortress Assault
Team Deathmatch
Relic Capture
Siege Battlefield
```

## Battleground Rules

```text
Players queue into teams.
Teams should be balanced by size, level, and role if needed.
Respawns happen in waves.
Objectives give more rewards than random kills.
Spawn areas are protected.
AFK players are removed.
Match has time limit.
Rewards are based on participation and objectives.
```

Recommended reward rule:

```text
Winning gives bonus rewards.
Losing still gives partial rewards if player participated meaningfully.
Objective play pays more than farming kills.
```

---

# 10. Open-World PvP

Open-world PvP must be handled carefully.

## Open-World PvP Zones

```text
Faction borders
Warfronts
Resource zones
Contested cities
Wilderness danger zones
Criminal regions
Guild war territories
Hardcore loot zones
```

Rules:

```text
Warn players before entering.
Show PvP zone on map.
Show faction control.
Show recent danger/activity.
Give alternate PvE paths where possible.
Prevent high-level griefing in low-level routes.
```

---

# 11. Low-Level Grief Protection

High-level players should not be rewarded for killing weak players.

Rules:

```text
No rewards for killing players far below your level.
Killing low-level players may reduce Likeability.
Killing low-level players in lawful areas may reduce Legal Standing.
Repeated low-level kills may trigger bounty.
Starter zones prevent PvP entirely.
Respawn protection prevents spawn camping.
```

Example:

```text
If attacker is 10+ levels above victim:
No PvP reward.
No honor.
No currency.
Possible criminal penalty if victim was not PvP-flagged.
```

---

# 12. PvP Death Rules

When a player dies in PvP:

```text
Assign kill credit by contribution.
Apply PvP death rules.
Respawn at valid PvP respawn point.
Apply respawn protection.
Check repeat-kill abuse.
Update scoreboard.
Update quests/objectives.
Update bounty/criminal status if relevant.
```

PvP death should not normally destroy a player’s whole inventory unless the zone is explicitly hardcore.

Recommended normal PvP death penalty:

```text
No item drop.
Small durability loss or none.
Respawn timer.
Objective loss if carrying flag/relic/package.
PvP score changes.
```

---

# 13. Hardcore PvP Zones

Hardcore PvP zones are optional high-risk areas.

Rules:

```text
Must be clearly marked.
Player must knowingly enter.
Some items may drop on death.
Bound items should not drop.
Quest items should not drop.
Spellbook, mounts, pets, cosmetics, and keyring should not drop.
Respawn points must be safe.
Loot rules must be visible before entry.
```

Example warning:

```text
Warning:
You are entering the Red Expanse.
Players may attack you.
Unbound trade goods and gathered resources may drop on death.
```

---

# 14. PvP Reward System

PvP rewards can include:

```text
Honor
Arena rating
PvP currency
Faction reputation
Bounty rewards
Titles
Cosmetics
Mounts
Gear
Achievements
Leaderboard rank
Season rewards
Guild war points
Territory control points
```

Reward rules:

```text
Reward objectives more than kill farming.
Reward fair fights more than griefing.
Reward participation, healing, tanking, support, and objective play.
Prevent repeated kill farming.
Prevent win trading.
Prevent AFK farming.
```

---

# 15. PvP Contribution System

PvP contribution should include more than damage.

Contribution sources:

```text
Damage to enemy players
Healing allies
Shielding allies
Capturing objectives
Defending objectives
Interrupting key casts
Crowd-controlling enemies
Carrying flags/relics
Destroying siege equipment
Reviving allies
Scouting, if system supports it
```

Contribution should not reward useless spam.

Bad:

```text
Player heals full-health allies forever and farms contribution.
```

Good:

```text
Only meaningful healing, shielding, objective play, and combat support count.
```

---

# 16. Friendly Fire Rules

Decide whether players can hurt allies.

Recommended default:

```text
No friendly fire in normal parties, raids, dungeons, and battlegrounds.
```

Optional modes:

```text
Friendly fire in hardcore PvP zones
Friendly fire in special arenas
Friendly fire from siege weapons
Friendly fire from dangerous boss mechanics
Friendly fire in betrayal/criminal systems
```

If friendly fire exists:

```text
Warn players.
Log it.
Apply legal/reputation consequences if abused.
Prevent griefing in normal content.
```

---

# 17. Party System

A party is a small group of players fighting together.

Recommended party size:

```text
Normal Party: 2 to 5 players
Large Party: 6 to 10 players
Raid: 10+ players
```

Typical MMO dungeon party:

```text
5 players:
1 tank
1 healer
3 damage/support
```

---

# 18. Party Roles

Common party roles:

```text
Tank
Healer
Damage Dealer
Support
Controller
Scout
Summoner
Buffer
Debuffer
Utility
```

## Tank

```text
Holds enemy attention.
Survives heavy damage.
Uses taunts, blocks, mitigation, positioning.
Protects weaker allies.
```

## Healer

```text
Restores HP.
Cleanses debuffs.
Revives allies.
Prevents wipes.
Manages resources.
```

## Damage Dealer

```text
Deals damage.
Kills priority targets.
Interrupts dangerous abilities.
Avoids unnecessary damage.
```

## Support

```text
Buffs allies.
Debuffs enemies.
Controls battlefield.
Improves group survival or damage.
```

## Controller

```text
Stuns, roots, slows, interrupts, displaces, or locks down enemies.
Must respect diminishing returns.
```

---

# 19. Party Formation Rules

Players should be able to form parties through:

```text
Direct invite
Friend list
Guild list
Nearby player invite
Looking-for-group tool
Dungeon finder
Raid finder
Public event auto-group
Manual group listing
```

Party rules:

```text
Party leader can invite/kick/promote.
Party members can leave at any time.
Leader can set loot rules.
Leader can queue group for content.
Leadership transfers if leader disconnects.
Party disbands when empty.
```

---

# 20. Party UI

Party UI should show:

```text
Player name
Class/role
Level
HP
Mana/resource
Buffs/debuffs
Distance/range
Dead/downed status
Ready check status
Voice/chat indicator
Threat warning, if relevant
Quest/instance phase warning
```

For healers, party UI must be readable. Bad party UI kills group play.

---

# 21. Party XP Rules

XP should be shared fairly.

Rules:

```text
Party members must be nearby or in same instance.
Party members must be eligible for content.
XP is based on contribution and party rules.
Large level gaps reduce XP unless level sync is active.
Dead players may receive XP if they contributed recently.
AFK players do not receive rewards.
```

Recommended:

```text
If party members are within 5 levels:
Normal XP sharing.

If one player is 10+ levels higher:
Reduced XP unless level sync/mentoring is active.
```

---

# 22. Party Quest Progress

Quest progress can be:

```text
Individual
Shared by party
Shared only if nearby
Shared only if player has quest
Shared only if same quest stage
Instance-wide
```

Rules:

```text
Kill quests should usually share progress with nearby eligible party members.
Collection quests may be personal or shared depending on design.
Escort quests should share progress with nearby party members.
Dialogue choices should usually be personal unless instance/party story rules apply.
```

Edge case:

```text
If party members are in different quest phases, warn them and offer phase sync when possible.
```

---

# 23. Party Loot Rules

Loot systems can include:

```text
Personal loot
Group loot
Need/greed
Round robin
Master loot
Free-for-all
Contribution-based loot
Class-restricted loot
Guild raid loot
```

Recommended default:

```text
Use personal loot for normal dungeons and public events.
Use group loot or personal loot for raids depending on design.
Avoid free-for-all loot except in hardcore/survival systems.
```

---

# 24. Loot Fairness Rules

```text
Last hit should not decide loot.
Contribution should matter.
Party members should not lose loot because they played healer or tank.
Dead players should receive loot if they contributed meaningfully and remained eligible.
Players outside the area should not receive loot.
AFK players should not receive loot.
```

---

# 25. Party Revive Rules

Party members should be able to help each other recover.

Rules:

```text
Party members can revive downed allies if class/item/rules allow.
Revive may require cast time.
Revive can be interrupted.
Combat resurrection may be limited.
Out-of-combat resurrection is easier.
Dead party members show on UI and map.
```

---

# 26. Raid System

A raid is a large organized group for difficult content.

Recommended raid sizes:

```text
Small Raid: 10 players
Standard Raid: 20 players
Large Raid: 25 to 40 players
World Raid: 40+ players, if engine/server supports it
```

Raids are used for:

```text
Major bosses
Large dungeons
World bosses
Faction war events
Sieges
Guild content
Legendary quests
Server-wide events
```

---

# 27. Raid Roles

Raids need stronger organization than parties.

Raid roles:

```text
Main Tank
Off Tank
Main Healer
Raid Healer
Damage Dealer
Support
Crowd Control
Interrupt Team
Mechanic Team
Add Control
Siege Operator
Scout
Raid Leader
Assistant Leader
Loot Master, if used
```

---

# 28. Raid Leadership Rules

Raid leader can:

```text
Invite players
Remove players
Promote assistants
Set raid markers
Start ready check
Set loot rules
Assign groups
Place world markers
Queue or enter raid content
Start pull timer
Manage raid warnings
```

Assistant leaders can:

```text
Invite players
Set markers
Start ready checks
Manage subgroups
Help coordinate mechanics
```

---

# 29. Raid UI Rules

Raid UI must show:

```text
All raid members
Groups/subgroups
Roles
HP/resource
Dead/downed state
Debuffs
Important buffs
Distance/range
Ready check
Role assignments
Boss warnings
Threat warnings
Resurrection availability
```

Raid UI should allow filtering:

```text
Show my group
Show tanks
Show healers
Show debuffed players
Show dead players
Show out-of-range players
```

---

# 30. Raid Encounter Rules

Raid fights should be mechanics-based, not just giant HP bars.

Good raid bosses should include:

```text
Phases
Telegraphed attacks
Tank mechanics
Healer pressure
Damage checks
Movement checks
Interrupts
Add waves
Target swapping
Area denial
Cleansing
Stack/spread mechanics
Environmental hazards
Enrage timer
Recovery windows
Clear visual/audio warnings
```

Bad raid boss:

```text
Boss has 500 million HP and only auto-attacks.
```

Good raid boss:

```text
Boss changes phases, summons adds, marks players, requires interrupts, forces movement, and rewards coordination.
```

---

# 31. Raid Boss Phase Rules

Boss phases create structure.

Example:

```text
Phase 1:
Tank boss, dodge cone breath, interrupt cultists.

Phase 2:
Boss flies away, raid fights adds and avoids fire.

Phase 3:
Boss lands enraged, tanks swap on armor-break debuff.

Final Phase:
Soft enrage, healer pressure, burn boss before arena collapses.
```

Rules:

```text
Each phase should have clear mechanics.
Transitions should be readable.
Players should understand why they died.
Mechanics should punish mistakes but not feel random.
```

---

# 32. Raid Difficulty Modes

Optional raid modes:

```text
Story
Normal
Heroic
Mythic
Challenge
Hardcore
Time Trial
```

Rules:

```text
Story mode teaches mechanics.
Normal mode is accessible to organized groups.
Heroic adds harder mechanics.
Mythic requires high coordination.
Challenge modes reward prestige, cosmetics, titles, and leaderboard placement.
```

---

# 33. Raid Lockout Rules

Raid lockouts prevent infinite farming.

Lockout types:

```text
Daily lockout
Weekly lockout
Boss-based lockout
Loot-only lockout
Instance ID lockout
Flexible lockout
Personal loot lockout
```

Recommended:

```text
Players can repeat raid for practice.
Major loot rewards are limited by weekly/personal lockout.
```

This avoids making players feel punished for helping friends.

---

# 34. Raid Loot Rules

Raid loot should be fair and controlled.

Options:

```text
Personal loot
Token loot
Boss currency
Need/greed
Guild loot rules
Crafting material drops
Legendary quest drops
Cosmetic drops
Mount drops
```

Recommended modern approach:

```text
Personal loot + raid currency + rare cosmetic drops.
```

This reduces drama.

---

# 35. Raid Contribution Rules

Players should receive rewards if they meaningfully participated.

Contribution includes:

```text
Damage
Healing
Tank threat
Mechanic completion
Interrupts
Cleansing
Revives
Add control
Objective interaction
Survival
```

AFK players should not receive full rewards.

---

# 36. Raid Wipe Rules

A raid wipes when all eligible players die or encounter fails.

On wipe:

```text
Boss resets.
Adds despawn.
Boss arena resets.
Debuffs clear.
Players respawn at checkpoint or entrance.
Durability penalty may apply.
No loot is awarded.
Encounter logs are saved.
```

The raid should not remain bugged after a wipe.

---

# 37. World Boss Rules

World bosses are open-world raid-level enemies.

Rules:

```text
World boss spawns in public zone.
Many players can participate.
Boss has contribution-based rewards.
Boss has anti-zerg scaling if needed.
Boss mechanics should be readable even with many players.
Boss cannot be dragged into towns unless event allows it.
Boss has leash/arena boundaries.
Rewards may have daily/weekly lockout.
```

---

## World Boss Scaling

World bosses may scale based on participants.

```text
Boss HP scales with number of active participants.
Boss damage should not scale so high that tanks are randomly deleted.
Mechanics may add more targets or zones with more players.
Rewards require meaningful contribution.
```

Avoid:

```text
Boss becomes impossible because 100 low-contribution players are nearby.
Boss becomes trivial because 200 players zerg it with no mechanics.
```

---

# 38. Public Event Grouping

For public events, players should be able to fight together without manually grouping.

Rules:

```text
Players in event area can contribute.
Contribution is tracked individually.
Healing/support counts.
Objectives count.
Rewards scale by participation tier.
Players do not need to be in same party.
```

Reward tiers:

```text
Bronze: minimal participation
Silver: meaningful participation
Gold: strong participation
Legendary: exceptional contribution or event success
```

---

# 39. Fighting Together With Players

Cooperative combat should reward teamwork.

Players should be able to:

```text
Tank enemies for allies
Heal allies
Shield allies
Buff allies
Revive allies
Share quest progress
Coordinate crowd control
Interrupt dangerous enemies
Use combo abilities
Protect weaker players
Complete group objectives
```

---

# 40. Cooperative Ability Rules

Abilities can interact between players.

Examples:

```text
Mage freezes enemy.
Warrior shatters frozen enemy for bonus damage.

Ranger marks target.
Rogue gets bonus critical chance against marked target.

Priest blesses tank.
Tank gains extra threat and mitigation.

Druid roots enemies.
Mage casts area spell on rooted group.

Paladin shields ally.
Healer gets time to recover them.
```

Rules:

```text
Combos should reward teamwork but not be mandatory for basic content.
Combos should be readable.
Combos should not create infinite damage loops.
Combos should have cooldowns or limits.
```

---

# 41. Buff and Debuff Rules in Groups

Buffs and debuffs need stacking rules.

```text
Same buff from same source refreshes duration.
Same buff from multiple sources may not stack unless designed.
Stronger buff may override weaker buff.
Some buffs are unique.
Some raid buffs are group-wide.
Some buffs are raid-wide.
Some buffs are party-only.
```

Avoid unlimited stacking.

Bad:

```text
20 players stack the same damage buff and one-shot boss.
```

Good:

```text
Only strongest version of the same buff applies.
Different categories can stack with caps.
```

---

# 42. Threat and Aggro in Groups

NPCs need to decide who to attack.

Threat comes from:

```text
Damage
Healing
Taunts
Buffing
Debuffing
Proximity
Objective interaction
Scripted boss mechanics
```

Tank rules:

```text
Tanks generate extra threat.
Taunt forces target temporarily.
Bosses may require tank swaps.
Healers generate threat through healing.
Damage dealers must manage burst threat.
```

Threat UI should warn:

```text
You are close to pulling threat.
You have aggro.
Tank lost aggro.
Boss is fixating you.
```

---

# 43. Tank Swap Rules

Raid bosses often require tank swaps.

Example:

```text
Boss applies Armor Break to current tank every 15 seconds.
At 3 stacks, tank takes massive damage.
Off-tank must taunt.
Stacks expire after 30 seconds.
```

Rules:

```text
Tank swap mechanics must be visible.
Debuff stacks must be clear.
Taunt immunity should be used carefully.
Boss should not randomly ignore tanks unless mechanic explains it.
```

---

# 44. Healing Rules in Groups

Healing should be meaningful but not infinite.

Rules:

```text
Healing generates threat.
Healing can overheal but overhealing may not count for contribution.
Some debuffs reduce healing.
Some mechanics require cleansing.
Mana/resource management matters.
Raid-wide damage creates healer pressure.
Avoid unavoidable one-shots with no counterplay.
```

---

# 45. Crowd Control Rules in Groups

Crowd control can break encounters if unlimited.

Rules:

```text
Normal enemies can be crowd-controlled.
Elites may resist some control.
Bosses are immune or partially affected by hard control.
Interrupts should matter.
Diminishing returns prevent stun-locks.
```

Crowd control categories:

```text
Stun
Root
Slow
Fear
Silence
Knockback
Sleep
Charm
Disarm
Taunt
```

---

# 46. Diminishing Returns in PvP

PvP must prevent stun-locking.

Example:

```text
First stun: 100% duration
Second stun within 15 seconds: 50% duration
Third stun within 15 seconds: 25% duration
Fourth stun within 15 seconds: immune briefly
```

Apply to:

```text
Stuns
Fears
Roots
Silences
Knockbacks
Charms
Sleeps
```

Do not make one player unable to act for 20 seconds straight.

---

# 47. Reviving Players in Group Content

Revive rules differ by content.

## Open World

```text
Most players can revive out of combat.
Healers can revive faster or in combat.
Revive items may work.
```

## Dungeon

```text
Out-of-combat revive allowed.
Combat revive limited.
Party wipe resets encounter.
```

## Raid

```text
Combat revive has limited charges.
Charges may be shared by raid.
Revive targets must be chosen carefully.
```

## PvP

```text
Revive may be limited.
Reviving can be interrupted.
Some PvP modes disable revive except respawn.
```

---

# 48. Group Finder System

The game should help players find groups.

Group finder should support:

```text
Dungeon groups
Raid groups
Quest groups
World boss groups
PvP groups
Guild recruitment
Role selection
Level range
Language/region
Experience level
Voice required flag
Casual/hardcore flag
```

Listing example:

```text
Old Mill Crypt
Need: 1 Tank, 1 Healer
Level: 12–15
Goal: Full clear
Style: Casual
```

---

# 49. Matchmaking Rules

For automated matchmaking:

```text
Match by role.
Match by level.
Match by gear/power if needed.
Match by region/latency.
Avoid placing blocked players together.
Avoid repeated toxic pairings.
Respect queue type.
```

Dungeon role example:

```text
1 Tank
1 Healer
3 Damage/Support
```

PvP matchmaking should consider:

```text
Rating
Win/loss history
Class composition
Premade group size
Latency
Level bracket
Gear bracket, if applicable
```

---

# 50. Ready Check System

Before raids, bosses, and queues:

```text
Leader starts ready check.
Players choose Ready / Not Ready.
AFK players are marked.
Leader sees status.
Encounter starts when group is ready.
```

Ready check prevents accidental pulls and wasted time.

---

# 51. Pull Timer System

Raid leaders need pull timers.

Example:

```text
Pulling boss in 10 seconds.
10, 9, 8...
```

Rules:

```text
Visible to raid.
Audio/visual warning.
Can be canceled.
Useful for pre-casting and preparation.
```

---

# 52. Raid Markers and World Markers

Raids need coordination tools.

Marker types:

```text
Target markers over enemies
World markers on ground
Danger markers
Stack marker
Spread marker
Interrupt marker
Tank marker
Healer marker
Objective marker
```

Rules:

```text
Only raid leader/assistants can place markers unless allowed.
Markers should be visible but not visually overwhelming.
Markers should not be usable to grief or obscure gameplay.
```

---

# 53. Voice and Chat Rules

Group content needs communication.

Channels:

```text
Party chat
Raid chat
Guild chat
Officer chat
Instance chat
Battleground chat
Whisper
Voice chat, if supported
Ping system
```

Ping system should include:

```text
Attack
Defend
Move here
Need help
Danger
Stack here
Spread out
On my way
Objective
```

This helps players who do not use voice.

---

# 54. Group Scaling Rules

Content should scale by group size when appropriate.

## Fixed Content

```text
Dungeon designed for 5 players.
Raid designed for 20 players.
Stats do not scale much.
```

## Flexible Content

```text
Boss HP and mechanics scale between 10 and 30 players.
Rewards scale by difficulty.
```

## Public Content

```text
World event scales with active contribution.
Boss HP adjusts based on participants.
```

Rules:

```text
Do not scale based on nearby idle players.
Do not punish groups because spectators are nearby.
Lock scaling when combat starts or update gradually.
```

---

# 55. Level Sync and Mentoring

Players should be able to fight together even at different levels.

Rules:

```text
High-level player can sync down to party/zone level.
Stats scale down.
Gear scales down.
Abilities may be normalized.
Rewards are adjusted.
Low-level friend still gets meaningful XP.
High-level player gets scaled rewards, currency, reputation, or mentor rewards.
```

Without level sync:

```text
High-level carrying should reduce XP rewards.
Low-level players should not be power-leveled too easily.
```

---

# 56. PvP Level Brackets

PvP should use brackets or scaling.

Example:

```text
Level 1–9
Level 10–19
Level 20–29
Level 30–39
Level 40–49
Level 50
```

For arena/battlegrounds:

```text
Normalize stats within bracket.
Separate new players from veterans where possible.
Prevent max-level players from farming low-level players.
```

---
