# 57. Gear Scaling in PvP

Options:

```text
Full gear power applies
Partial normalization
Full normalization
PvP-specific templates
PvP gear scaling
```

Recommended:

```text
Open-world PvP: gear matters, but extreme gaps are controlled.
Arena/ranked PvP: normalize enough to keep skill important.
Battlegrounds: bracket and normalize moderately.
```

Bad PvP:

```text
A geared veteran one-shots new players with no counterplay.
```

Good PvP:

```text
Gear helps, but positioning, cooldowns, teamwork, and skill still matter.
```

---

# 58. Anti-AFK Rules

Group content and PvP must detect AFK players.

AFK signs:

```text
No movement
No abilities
No damage/healing/support
No objective interaction
Repeated idle behavior
Following bot patterns
```

Rules:

```text
Warn AFK player.
Remove from battleground/queue if inactive.
Reduce or remove rewards.
Allow party vote-kick.
Do not punish players who are dead but actively waiting for revive/respawn.
```

---

# 59. Vote Kick Rules

Parties need tools to remove griefers, but vote kick can be abused.

Rules:

```text
Vote kick requires majority.
Cannot spam vote kicks.
Cannot kick during loot roll unless abuse prevention allows.
Cannot instantly kick right before final boss loot without safeguards.
Kicked player gets replacement queue protection if unfair.
Vote reason should be selected.
```

Reasons:

```text
AFK
Disconnected
Harassment
Refusing role
Griefing
Other
```

---

# 60. Replacement System

If a party member leaves:

```text
Group can request replacement.
New player enters at safe checkpoint.
New player receives current instance state.
New player cannot loot already-defeated bosses unless rules allow.
```

Raid replacement:

```text
Raid leader can invite replacement.
Lockout rules apply.
Replacement eligibility is checked.
```

---

# 61. Disconnect Rules in Group Content

If player disconnects:

```text
Keep their spot briefly.
Mark as disconnected.
Allow reconnect.
After timeout, party may replace them.
In combat, character remains vulnerable for combat logout timer.
```

Recommended timeout:

```text
Dungeon disconnect grace: 3 to 5 minutes
Raid disconnect grace: 5 to 10 minutes
PvP disconnect grace: shorter, mode-specific
```

---

# 62. Raid Lockout and Replacement Edge Cases

Rules:

```text
A player should know if joining a raid will bind them to a lockout.
A player should confirm before accepting a lockout.
A raid should not trick players into losing weekly rewards.
```

Warning:

```text
Joining this raid will bind you to 3 defeated bosses.
You will not receive loot from those bosses this week.
Continue?
```

---

# 63. PvP Objectives

PvP should focus on objectives, not only kills.

Objective types:

```text
Capture point
Defend point
Carry flag
Escort payload
Destroy gate
Hold relic
Gather resources
Kill commander
Control towers
Protect NPC leader
Siege castle
```

Reward rule:

```text
Objective contribution should pay more than farming kills away from objectives.
```

---

# 64. Siege Warfare

Sieges are large-scale PvP or PvE/PvP events.

Siege systems:

```text
Walls
Gates
Siege engines
Defensive towers
Supply lines
Repair crews
Commanders
Respawn waves
Control points
Guild/faction ownership
```

Siege roles:

```text
Attackers
Defenders
Siege operators
Wall repairers
Scouts
Healers
Frontline fighters
Assassins
Supply runners
Commanders
```

Rules:

```text
Siege objectives must be clear.
Spawn points must be protected.
Siege weapons must have counters.
Defenders need repair/supply mechanics.
Attackers need progress mechanics.
Rewards must favor objectives and teamwork.
```

---

# 65. Guild Wars

Guilds can fight over territory, honor, or resources.

Rules:

```text
Guild war must be declared or mutually triggered by system.
War duration is limited.
War objectives are clear.
Safe zones remain protected unless special rules apply.
Guild war cannot be used to grief starter players.
Guild members should know they are war-flagged.
```

Guild war rewards:

```text
Territory control
Guild currency
Resource access
Titles
Cosmetics
Taxes from controlled market
Guild ranking
```

---

# 66. Faction Wars

Faction wars are large-scale conflicts.

Faction rules:

```text
Faction allies cannot attack each other unless betrayal systems exist.
Faction enemies can fight in contested zones.
Faction reputation affects access to vendors, guards, quests, and cities.
Changing faction should have cooldowns and consequences.
```

Faction war objectives:

```text
Capture forts
Escort supplies
Defend towns
Kill enemy commanders
Control mines
Protect trade routes
Sabotage siege engines
```

---

# 67. Bounty Hunting PvP

Bounty hunting connects PvP and the legal system.

## Bounty Creation

Bounties can come from:

```text
Murder
Theft
Repeated crime
Killing guards
Public enemy status
Faction war
Player-created bounty, if allowed
NPC legal system
```

## Bounty Rules

```text
Only eligible hunters can claim bounty.
Bounty target must be valid.
Repeated farming of the same target is blocked.
Capture may pay more than killing.
Bounty payout may come from fines, player deposits, or system rewards.
```

Capture example:

```text
Hunter defeats outlaw.
Outlaw enters captured state.
Hunter gets bounty reward.
Outlaw wakes in jail.
Stolen goods may be confiscated.
```

---

# 68. Cooperation With Non-Party Players

Players should be able to help each other casually.

Rules:

```text
Healing another player should be allowed if same faction or neutral rules allow it.
Buffing strangers may be allowed in PvE zones.
Helping kill public enemies should grant contribution.
Quest credit should be personal or shared based on quest rules.
Do not punish players for helping unless faction/legal rules say otherwise.
```

But prevent abuse:

```text
No contribution from meaningless heals.
No reward for tagging once and leaving.
No XP for AFK nearby players.
No griefing by healing hostile flagged targets unless PvP rules allow it.
```

---

# 69. Shared Enemy Tagging

Enemy tagging determines who gets credit.

Options:

```text
First tag owns enemy
Faction tag
Party tag
Open tag
Contribution tag
Personal loot tag
```

Recommended:

```text
Use open contribution tagging for public PvE.
Use party tagging for private quest mobs.
Use personal loot to avoid kill stealing.
```

Rules:

```text
Players must contribute meaningfully.
Last hit does not steal credit.
Support actions count.
Quest targets respawn quickly or are personal.
```

---

# 70. Public Quest and Event Credit

For public events:

```text
Track contribution.
Track objective work.
Track healing/support.
Track time active.
Track proximity.
Track AFK status.
```

Reward tiers:

```text
No reward: no meaningful participation
Bronze: small contribution
Silver: solid contribution
Gold: major contribution
Legendary: exceptional contribution
```

---

# 71. Group Combat Edge Cases

## Player Joins Party Mid-Fight

Rule:

```text
Player can join party.
They do not get full credit for enemies already nearly dead unless they contribute meaningfully.
Raid boss eligibility depends on encounter rules.
```

## Player Leaves Party Mid-Fight

Rule:

```text
They keep personal contribution already earned.
They may lose shared party benefits.
Loot eligibility depends on contribution and proximity.
```

## Player Dies Early in Boss Fight

Rule:

```text
If they contributed meaningfully and stayed in encounter, they may receive credit.
If they died immediately and went AFK, contribution may decay.
```

## Player Is Out of Range

Rule:

```text
No shared XP/loot if too far away unless instance rules allow it.
Party UI should show out-of-range status.
```

## Player Is in Wrong Phase

Rule:

```text
Warn party.
Offer phase sync if possible.
Do not silently separate players with no explanation.
```

## Player Is AFK

Rule:

```text
Warn.
Reduce rewards.
Allow vote kick.
Remove from PvP match if needed.
```

## Tank Disconnects

Rule:

```text
Mark disconnected.
Encounter continues if possible.
Party can wipe, recover, or replace after combat.
Do not instantly delete character if in combat.
```

## Healer Leaves Dungeon

Rule:

```text
Allow group to queue for replacement.
Pause/slow matchmaking penalties if departure was early.
Protect group from being trapped.
```

---

# 72. PvP Edge Cases

## Player Attacks From Safe Zone

Rule:

```text
Block attack.
Projectiles and spells cannot originate from protected safe zone into PvP area.
```

## Player Runs Into Safe Zone After Attacking

Rule:

```text
Aggression timer prevents immediate protection.
Guards may intervene if lawful area.
```

## Player Gets Spawn Camped

Rule:

```text
Increase protection.
Offer alternate respawn.
Suppress rewards for campers.
Add guards/barriers if needed.
```

## Player Kills Same Player Repeatedly

Rule:

```text
Apply diminishing rewards.
Possible grief flag.
Possible bounty/criminal penalty.
```

## Player Logs Out During PvP

Rule:

```text
Character remains in world during combat logout timer.
If killed during timer, death is valid.
```

## Player Disconnects Due to Server Issue

Rule:

```text
Do not apply harsh penalties if server-side failure caused death.
```

## Player Is Crowd-Controlled Forever

Rule:

```text
Diminishing returns and immunity windows prevent control chains.
```

---

# 73. Raid Edge Cases

## Boss Is Pulled Before Ready

Rule:

```text
Raid leader can use ready check/pull timer.
Accidental pull starts fight unless encounter has countdown gate.
Some raids may require leader confirmation to start boss.
```

## Boss Gets Dragged Out of Arena

Rule:

```text
Boss enters evade.
Boss becomes immune.
Boss returns to arena.
Boss resets if exploit detected.
```

## Player Re-enters Boss Arena After Death

Rule:

```text
Depends on encounter.
Some bosses lock arena after pull.
Others allow re-entry during fight.
Rule must be clear.
```

## Raid Splits Boss Mechanics Incorrectly

Rule:

```text
Mechanics should punish but be readable.
Death recap should explain cause.
```

## Loot Dispute

Rule:

```text
Use personal loot or transparent loot rules.
Log all loot.
Show who received what.
```

## Raid Leader Kicks Before Loot

Rule:

```text
If player contributed to boss kill, they keep loot eligibility.
Kicking after contribution should not remove earned rewards.
```

---

# 74. Multiplayer Combat Server Rules

The server must be authoritative.

The server validates:

```text
PvP flag status
Faction relationship
Safe zone status
Attack legality
Target eligibility
Damage
Healing
Buffs
Debuffs
Crowd control
XP credit
Loot credit
Quest progress
PvP rewards
Raid lockouts
Party membership
Raid membership
Contribution
Death and respawn
```

The client cannot decide:

```text
That a player was hit
That a kill counted
That loot is earned
That PvP was legal
That a player has raid credit
That a player can bypass lockout
That a player can damage safe-zone targets
```

---

# 75. Multiplayer Combat Logs

Log important group and PvP actions.

Logs should include:

```text
Attacker
Target
Ability
Damage/healing
Buff/debuff
PvP flag state
Party/raid ID
Zone
Position
Timestamp
Kill credit
Loot credit
Objective credit
Death cause
Crowd control duration
Interrupts
Threat events
Boss mechanic failures
```

Needed for:

```text
Debugging
Balance
Anti-cheat
PvP disputes
Raid analysis
Toxicity reports
Exploit detection
Reward validation
```

---

# 76. Social Rules for Group Content

Group systems should reduce toxicity.

Support:

```text
Clear role expectations
Ready checks
Vote kick with limits
Report system
Block/mute
Commendations
Mentor rewards
New player indicators
Practice modes
Story difficulty
Clear mechanics
Death recap
```

Avoid:

```text
Punishing new players for learning.
Letting one player ruin everyone’s quest permanently.
Allowing loot leaders to steal rewards.
Rewarding AFK players.
Making required group content impossible to find groups for.
```

---

# 77. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design a complete PvP, party, raid, and cooperative combat system for an MMO.

The system must support:
- Duels
- Arena PvP
- Battlegrounds
- Open-world PvP
- Faction wars
- Guild wars
- Bounty hunting
- Siege warfare
- Parties
- Raids
- World bosses
- Public events
- Cooperative fighting with other players

PvP must use clear flagging:
- Unflagged
- Duel flagged
- Voluntary PvP flagged
- Arena flagged
- Battleground flagged
- Faction war flagged
- Guild war flagged
- Criminal flagged
- Bounty target
- Hardcore PvP flagged
- Spawn protected

Players must always know when PvP is active.
Safe zones must prevent PvP abuse.
Starter zones should prevent hostile PvP.
Aggression timers must stop players from attacking and instantly hiding in safe zones.

PvP rewards must be based on meaningful contribution:
- Damage
- Healing
- Shielding
- Objective capture
- Defense
- Crowd control
- Reviving
- Siege work
- Support

Do not reward:
- Repeatedly killing the same player
- Killing much lower-level players
- AFK participation
- Win trading
- Spawn camping
- Fake objective farming

Parties must support:
- Invites
- Leaving
- Leadership
- Role assignment
- Party chat
- Shared XP
- Shared quest progress
- Loot rules
- Reviving allies
- Dungeon queueing
- Phase warnings
- Level sync

Raids must support:
- Raid leader
- Assistant leaders
- Subgroups
- Raid markers
- World markers
- Ready checks
- Pull timers
- Raid chat
- Role assignments
- Raid UI
- Boss mechanics
- Lockouts
- Contribution-based rewards

Raid bosses must have mechanics:
- Phases
- Tank swaps
- Adds
- Interrupts
- Cleanses
- Stack/spread mechanics
- Movement mechanics
- Enrage timers
- Area hazards
- Clear telegraphs
- Death recap explanations

World bosses must:
- Use contribution-based rewards
- Scale carefully by active participants
- Prevent idle players from scaling the boss unfairly
- Stay within leash boundaries
- Avoid being dragged into towns unless intended
- Have daily/weekly reward lockouts if needed

Cooperative combat must support:
- Tanking
- Healing
- Shielding
- Buffing
- Debuffing
- Crowd control
- Interrupts
- Revives
- Combo abilities
- Public event contribution
- Helping non-party players

The system must handle edge cases:
- Player joins party mid-fight
- Player leaves party mid-fight
- Player dies early in boss fight
- Player is out of range
- Player is in different quest phase
- Player is AFK
- Player disconnects
- Tank/healer leaves dungeon
- Raid leader kicks player before loot
- Boss is pulled before ready
- Boss is dragged out of arena
- Player attacks from safe zone
- Player runs into safe zone after attacking
- Player is spawn camped
- Player logs out during PvP
- Crowd control chains become too long
- World boss has too many idle spectators
- Loot disputes occur

The server must be authoritative:
- Server validates PvP legality
- Server validates party/raid membership
- Server validates attacks, heals, buffs, debuffs, and crowd control
- Server validates safe zones and aggression timers
- Server calculates contribution
- Server grants loot, XP, quest progress, and PvP rewards
- Server enforces raid lockouts
- Server prevents duplicate rewards
- Server logs all important combat and reward events

The system should make fighting with players feel cooperative, organized, rewarding, and readable.
PvP should feel fair and competitive.
Raids should feel challenging and teamwork-based.
Public events should let players help each other without needing perfect party setup.
```

---

# Final PvP / Raid / Party Rule

Every multiplayer fight should answer:

```text
Who is on my side?
Who is against me?
Can I damage this target?
Can I heal this player?
What is the objective?
What role am I playing?
How do I get credit?
What happens if I die?
What happens if we win?
What happens if we lose?
What prevents griefing or abuse?
```

And every raid should answer:

```text
What is the boss doing?
What should tanks do?
What should healers do?
What should damage dealers do?
What mechanics must be handled?
What happens on wipe?
Who gets loot?
What lockout applies?
How does the group know what went wrong?
```

That is how you make PvP, raids, and group fighting feel like a real MMO system instead of chaotic players hitting things with no structure.

Below is a full **MMO Dialogue and Choice System** designed to be useful, immersive, and **not annoying**.

Core rule:

```text
Dialogue should help the player understand the world, make choices, build relationships, receive quests, and express their character without wasting their time.
```

A good dialogue system should:

```text
Respect player time.
Avoid forced walls of text.
Make choices clear.
Make consequences understandable.
Let players skip, review, or replay important information.
Let NPCs remember what happened.
React to reputation, class, level, faction, crime, and prior choices.
Avoid fake choices unless they are clearly flavor choices.
```

---

# 1. Dialogue System Goals

The dialogue system should support:

```text
Quest conversations
Merchant conversations
Guard/legal conversations
NPC small talk
Relationship conversations
Faction conversations
Combat surrender/threat dialogue
Companion dialogue
Rumors and world news
Moral choices
Skill checks
Class-specific responses
Reputation-based responses
Branching quest outcomes
```

Every conversation should answer:

```text
Who am I talking to?
Why does this conversation matter?
What does this NPC want?
What does the player want?
What information is important?
What choices does the player have?
What happens after the choice?
Can the player leave without being punished unfairly?
```

---

# 2. Non-Annoying Dialogue Rules

## Keep Most Dialogue Short

Most NPC dialogue should be short unless the moment is important.

Good default:

```text
1 to 3 short paragraphs.
1 clear point per paragraph.
No unnecessary lore dump.
No repeated explanation unless the player asks.
```

Bad:

```text
NPC gives 12 paragraphs before allowing the player to accept a simple rat-killing quest.
```

Good:

```text
"The rats got into my grain cellar again. I need them gone before they ruin the winter stores. Can you help?"
```

Then offer optional details:

```text
[Accept Quest]
[Where is the cellar?]
[Why are there so many rats?]
[Ask about reward]
[Leave]
```

---

## Put Important Information First

NPCs should not hide the useful part at the end of a long speech.

Bad:

```text
Five paragraphs of family history before saying the target is north of town.
```

Good:

```text
"My son is missing near the old bridge north of town. Please find him. I can explain more if you need."
```

Then optional:

```text
[Tell me what happened.]
[Where exactly was he last seen?]
[Accept Quest]
[Leave]
```

---

## Separate Required Dialogue From Optional Lore

Players who care can dig deeper. Players in a hurry can move on.

Required:

```text
What happened?
What do I need to do?
Where do I go?
What do I get?
```

Optional:

```text
History
Politics
Personal backstory
World lore
Rumors
Faction details
Emotional context
```

---

## Always Give a Clean Exit

Every conversation should have a clear way out.

```text
[Goodbye]
[Leave]
[Maybe later]
[End conversation]
```

Do not trap players in dialogue unless it is a deliberate cutscene, arrest, trial, boss negotiation, or major story event.

---

## Do Not Repeat the Same Greeting Forever

NPCs should vary greetings.

Example:

```text
First time:
"New face in Harthmere? Keep your purse close near the market."

After helping town:
"Good to see you again. Folks still talk about what you did at the north fence."

If outlaw:
"Do not bring trouble to my door."

If famous:
"Wait... are you the one who killed the Ashen Drake?"
```

---

# 3. Dialogue Pacing Rules

## Normal NPC Conversation

```text
Greeting
Purpose
Player choice
Result
Exit
```

Example:

```text
NPC: "Need supplies? Roads are dangerous this week."

Choices:
[Show me your goods.]
[What happened on the roads?]
[Heard any rumors?]
[Goodbye.]
```

---

## Quest Dialogue

```text
Problem
Request
Location
Reward
Accept/decline
```

Example:

```text
NPC: "Wolves are getting through the north fence. Kill six before they reach the sheep pens, and I will pay you."

Choices:
[Accept: Wolves at the North Fence]
[Where is the north fence?]
[Can you pay more?]
[Not now.]
```

---

## Major Story Dialogue

Major story dialogue can be longer, but must be paced.

Rules:

```text
Break long speeches into small sections.
Let players ask questions.
Summarize key objectives after the conversation.
Store details in the quest journal.
Do not require players to remember names, places, or clues from one-time dialogue.
```

---

# 4. Dialogue UI Rules

The UI should be clean and readable.

## Dialogue Window Should Show

```text
NPC name
NPC role or title
NPC portrait or model
Current line
Player choices
Quest icons
Trade/service buttons
Reputation hints, if relevant
Important consequence warnings
Exit button
Dialogue history button
```

---

## Choice Layout Rules

Choices should be clear and not too many.

Recommended:

```text
2 to 5 choices at a time
Use categories when there are more
Keep choice text short
Make the tone obvious
Do not hide hostile choices beside friendly ones without warning
```

Bad:

```text
[Okay]
[Okay.]
[Sure]
[Fine]
```

Good:

```text
[Accept the job.]
[Ask for more pay.]
[Refuse politely.]
[Threaten him.]
[Leave.]
```

---

## Important Choice Warning

If a choice has serious consequences, warn the player.

Example:

```text
[Attack the guard.] 
Warning: This will make you a criminal in Harthmere.
```

Or:

```text
[Reveal the thief's identity.]
Warning: This may permanently damage your relationship with the Thieves Guild.
```

Do not surprise players with major irreversible consequences from vague wording.

---

# 5. Choice Types

The system should support different types of choices.

```text
Flavor choice
Information choice
Quest choice
Moral choice
Faction choice
Combat choice
Trade choice
Persuasion choice
Intimidation choice
Bribe choice
Lie choice
Truth choice
Class-specific choice
Attribute-based choice
Reputation-based choice
Secret/unlocked choice
```

---

## Flavor Choices

Flavor choices let the player roleplay without major consequences.

Example:

```text
[That sounds dangerous.]
[I have seen worse.]
[Get to the point.]
```

Rules:

```text
Flavor choices can change NPC tone slightly.
They should not secretly cause major quest failure.
They are good for roleplay.
```

---

## Information Choices

These ask for more details.

Example:

```text
[Where is the old mill?]
[Who took the necklace?]
[Why not ask the guards?]
[What is the reward?]
```

Rules:

```text
Information choices should not punish players.
They should update journal hints if useful.
They should help confused players.
```

---

## Quest Choices

These accept, refuse, continue, or turn in quests.

Example:

```text
[Accept Quest]
[Decline]
[Complete Quest]
[Ask for reward]
```

Rules:

```text
Accepting should update quest tracker immediately.
Declining should not permanently remove normal quests unless clearly stated.
Turn-in should show rewards before final confirmation.
```

---

## Moral Choices

Moral choices change reputation, story, or relationships.

Example:

```text
[Return the medicine to the noble.]
[Give the medicine to the sick villagers.]
[Sell the medicine for profit.]
[Destroy the medicine so no faction benefits.]
```

Rules:

```text
Moral choices should have understandable consequences.
Do not make one option obviously correct every time.
Different factions should react differently.
```

---

## Faction Choices

Faction choices affect alliances.

Example:

```text
[Support the city guard.]
[Help the rebels escape.]
[Warn the merchant guild.]
[Stay out of it.]
```

Rules:

```text
Faction choices should change access, prices, quests, guards, and NPC reactions.
Players should be warned if a faction becomes hostile.
```

---

## Skill / Attribute Choices

Choices can unlock based on attributes.

Examples:

```text
[Strength] Break the prisoner’s chains.
[Dexterity] Pick the lock quietly.
[Intelligence] Decode the ancient seal.
[Wisdom] Calm the grieving spirit.
[Charisma] Persuade the guard to let you pass.
[Perception] Notice the merchant is lying.
[Willpower] Resist the cursed voice.
[Luck] Guess the correct cup.
```

Rules:

```text
Show why the option is available.
Show if the attempt may fail.
Do not overuse random failure.
Let different builds solve problems differently.
```

---

## Class-Specific Choices

Classes should get unique conversation options.

Examples:

```text
[Warrior] Challenge him to honorable combat.
[Rogue] Recognize the thieves’ hand signal.
[Mage] Identify the unstable spell.
[Priest] Offer a blessing.
[Ranger] Track the footprints.
[Paladin] Invoke legal authority.
[Necromancer] Speak with the corpse.
```

Rules:

```text
Class choices should feel special.
They should provide alternate paths, not always superior paths.
```

---

# 6. Meaningful Choice Rules

## Choices Should Have Clear Intent

The player should understand what their character is about to do.

Bad:

```text
[Fine.]
```

Then the character threatens the NPC.

Good:

```text
[Threaten him to reveal the truth.]
```

---

## Do Not Use Fake Choices Too Often

A fake choice is when all options lead to the same result.

Sometimes this is fine for flavor, but not for major decisions.

Bad:

```text
[Save the village.]
[Abandon the village.]
```

Both lead to saving the village.

Good:

```text
[Defend the village.]
Result: battle event starts.

[Evacuate the villagers.]
Result: fewer deaths, but village buildings burn.

[Refuse to help.]
Result: village suffers, reputation decreases.
```

---

## Consequences Should Match the Choice

If the player chooses mercy, the world should not treat them like they chose murder.

If the player chooses theft, lawful NPCs should react.

If the player lies, the lie may matter later.

```text
Choice → Immediate result → Delayed result → Reputation/faction/world effect
```

---

## Fail Forward

Failure should not always mean the story stops.

Example:

```text
Player fails to persuade guard.
Bad design: quest blocked forever.

Good design:
Guard refuses.
Player can:
- Pay a fee
- Sneak in
- Find another entrance
- Get a permit
- Bribe another guard
- Return with higher reputation
```

---

# 7. Dialogue Consequence System

Every important choice can affect:

```text
Likeability
Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction reputation
Personal NPC relationship
Quest branch
Reward
Merchant prices
Guard behavior
World state
Future dialogue
Companion approval
Access to areas
Availability of future quests
```

Example:

```text
Player lies to a grieving mother about her son.

Immediate:
She feels comforted.

Later:
If truth is revealed, she loses trust.

Effects:
+Small Likeability now
-Large Likeability later if discovered
Possible quest branch
Companion disapproval if they value honesty
```

---

# 8. Consequence Visibility

Not every consequence must be fully revealed, but the player should understand the type of consequence.

Use labels:

```text
Minor consequence
Major consequence
Faction consequence
Legal consequence
Relationship consequence
Permanent consequence
Combat consequence
```

Examples:

```text
[Steal the medicine.]
Warning: Illegal action. Guards may respond if witnessed.

[Side with the rebels.]
Warning: This will reduce your standing with the Royal Guard.

[Execute the prisoner.]
Warning: Permanent choice. Some companions may leave.
```

---

# 9. NPC Memory Rules

NPCs should remember meaningful choices.

Memory types:

```text
Short-term memory
Personal memory
Family memory
Faction memory
Town memory
Regional rumor
World news
```

NPCs should remember:

```text
Player helped them
Player insulted them
Player lied to them
Player threatened them
Player stole from them
Player saved their family
Player killed their ally
Player failed their quest
Player completed their quest
Player spared them
Player betrayed them
```

Example:

```text
Before helping:
"Stranger, I do not know you."

After helping:
"You saved my daughter. You will always have a place at my fire."

After betrayal:
"Do not speak to me. I trusted you."
```

---

# 10. Reputation-Based Dialogue

NPC dialogue should change based on player standing.

## High Likeability

```text
NPCs greet warmly.
NPCs offer help.
NPCs give rumors.
NPCs offer discounts.
NPCs ask for personal favors.
```

Example:

```text
"You again! Come in, friend. I saved the good apples for you."
```

---

## Low Likeability

```text
NPCs avoid the player.
NPCs refuse optional dialogue.
NPCs charge more.
NPCs call guards faster.
NPCs may insult or fear player.
```

Example:

```text
"Take your business elsewhere. We know what you did."
```

---

## High Legal Standing

```text
Guards trust player.
Officials share information.
Lawful NPCs cooperate.
Legal shortcuts unlock.
```

Example:

```text
"Captain said you could pass. We trust your judgment."
```

---

## Low Legal Standing / Outlaw

```text
Guards challenge player.
Merchants refuse service.
Civilians fear or report player.
Criminals may approach player.
```

Example:

```text
"Hands where I can see them. You are wanted in this city."
```

---

## High Notoriety

```text
NPCs recognize player.
Fans gather.
Enemies prepare.
Quest givers seek player.
Dialogue references achievements.
```

Example:

```text
"You are the one who slew the Ashen Drake. I never thought I would meet you."
```

---

# 11. Conversation Branching Rules

Use branching where it matters.

## Simple Branch

```text
NPC Greeting
→ Player asks question
→ NPC answers
→ Return to main menu
```

## Quest Branch

```text
NPC gives problem
→ Player accepts/refuses
→ Objective begins
→ Player returns
→ Reward/consequence
```

## Moral Branch

```text
NPC asks for action
→ Player chooses side
→ World state changes
→ Future NPC dialogue changes
```

## Relationship Branch

```text
NPC reacts to player history
→ Trust increases/decreases
→ New choices unlock or close
```

---

# 12. Dialogue Tags

Use tags to organize tone and function.

```text
[Friendly]
[Rude]
[Lie]
[Truth]
[Threaten]
[Persuade]
[Bribe]
[Intimidate]
[Flirt]
[Joke]
[Ask]
[Accept]
[Refuse]
[Attack]
[Leave]
[Trade]
[Quest]
[Class]
[Reputation]
[Illegal]
[Permanent]
```

Example:

```text
[Persuade] "Let me speak to the prisoner. I may get him to confess."
[Bribe] "Perhaps this gold will make the paperwork easier."
[Threaten] "Move, or I will move you."
[Leave] "I have heard enough."
```

Tags help players understand tone.

---

# 13. Avoiding Annoying Dialogue

## Do Not Force Repeated Dialogue

If the player has heard a line before, offer shorter versions.

Example:

```text
First time:
NPC explains full quest.

Second time:
"You remember the plan. The bandits are still at the old mill."
```

---

## Add “Summarize” Option

For long conversations:

```text
[Give me the short version.]
```

NPC response:

```text
"Bandits took the medicine. Find their camp east of the bridge, recover the crate, and bring it back before nightfall."
```

---

## Add “Repeat Instructions” Option

Players forget. Let them ask again.

```text
[Remind me where to go.]
[What was I supposed to find?]
[Who am I returning this to?]
```

This should also update the quest journal.

---

## Do Not Hide Basic Systems Behind Dialogue Spam

Vendors, banks, trainers, and healers should expose service buttons quickly.

Example:

```text
[Shop]
[Repair]
[Train]
[Ask about rumors]
[Goodbye]
```

---

## Skip and Auto-Advance Rules

Players should be able to:

```text
Skip already-seen dialogue
Fast-forward text
Disable typewriter effect
Review conversation history
Replay major story dialogue from journal
```

Do not force slow text unless it is a deliberate dramatic moment.

---

# 14. Dialogue and Quest System Integration

Quest dialogue must update the quest system.

When accepting a quest:

```text
Quest appears in journal.
Quest tracker updates.
Map marker appears.
Compass marker appears.
Current objective is shown.
NPC dialogue changes.
```

When completing a quest:

```text
NPC recognizes completion.
Rewards are previewed.
Player confirms reward choice.
Quest is marked completed.
Follow-up quest may unlock.
Reputation changes are applied.
```

When failing a quest:

```text
NPC reacts.
Journal explains failure.
Possible restart/alternate path is shown.
Reputation consequences apply if needed.
```

---

# 15. Dialogue and Merchant System

Merchants should have dialogue, but services should be fast.

Merchant menu:

```text
[Show me your goods.]
[Can you repair my equipment?]
[Heard any rumors?]
[Do you buy stolen goods?] only if appropriate
[Ask about local prices.]
[Goodbye.]
```

Merchant responses should change based on:

```text
Likeability
Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction
Stolen goods
Recent theft
Town economy
Personal relationship
```

Example:

```text
Liked player:
"For you? I can lower the price a bit."

Outlaw:
"I do not want guards at my door. Leave."

Criminal-friendly fence:
"Depends. Are the goods hot?"
```

---

# 16. Dialogue and Guard System

Guards need special dialogue.

Guard dialogue options:

```text
[Ask for directions.]
[Ask about local laws.]
[Report a crime.]
[Pay fine.]
[Submit to arrest.]
[Bribe guard.] if corrupt or possible
[Threaten guard.]
[Leave.]
```

Guard responses based on legal standing:

```text
Trusted:
"Good to see you. Keep an eye out near the east road."

Neutral:
"Move along."

Suspicious:
"State your business."

Outlaw:
"You are under arrest."

Public Enemy:
"Sound the alarm!"
```

---

# 17. Dialogue and Social Class

NPC class should affect dialogue.

## Nobles

```text
Formal language
Sensitive to respect/status
May refuse low-status players
Offer political choices
Protected by guards
Care about reputation and appearances
```

Example:

```text
"Mind your tone. You stand before House Veyran."
```

## Peasants

```text
Practical language
Concerned with food, safety, taxes, family
Offer small grounded quests
React strongly to kindness
```

Example:

```text
"I do not need speeches. I need my son found before dark."
```

## Thieves

```text
Indirect language
Use coded phrases
Distrust lawful players
Offer illegal choices
React to guards and risk
```

Example:

```text
"Looking for honest work? Wrong alley."
```

## Priests

```text
Moral language
Healing, mercy, guilt, faith
React to cruelty and corruption
```

Example:

```text
"The wounded need medicine, not politics."
```

## Scholars

```text
Curious language
Lore, puzzles, ancient texts
React to Intelligence and discoveries
```

Example:

```text
"You recognized the script? Interesting. Very interesting."
```

---

# 18. Dialogue and Combat

Some dialogue can lead to combat.

Rules:

```text
Hostile choices should be clearly labeled.
Players should not accidentally start combat through vague wording.
Combat-starting dialogue should close services/trade first.
NPC allies and guards should react.
Reputation/legal changes should apply.
```

Examples:

```text
[Attack him.] Starts combat.
[Threaten him.] May start combat if he resists.
[Draw weapon.] Escalates tension.
[Back away.] Ends conversation.
```

---

# 19. Surrender, Mercy, and Negotiation

Combat should sometimes end through dialogue.

NPCs may surrender when:

```text
Low HP
Leader defeated
Outnumbered
Player has high intimidation
Player has high reputation
Player offers mercy
Faction morale breaks
```

Player options:

```text
[Spare them.]
[Demand information.]
[Take them prisoner.]
[Let them flee.]
[Execute them.]
```

Consequences:

```text
Mercy may increase Likeability.
Execution may increase fear or reduce Likeability.
Capturing may improve Legal Standing.
Letting criminals flee may anger guards.
```

---

# 20. Dialogue Skill Checks

Skill checks should be fair and readable.

## Check Types

```text
Pass/fail check
Partial success
Guaranteed success if requirement met
Random chance modified by stat
Hidden check
Opposed check
```

Recommended:

```text
Use guaranteed success for many stat-gated options.
Use partial success for interesting uncertainty.
Avoid random failure when the player clearly built for that option.
```

Example:

```text
[Charisma 15] Persuade the guard.
Success guaranteed if Charisma >= 15.
```

Better than:

```text
Player has Charisma 30 and still randomly fails a basic persuasion check.
```

---

# 21. Partial Success Rules

Partial success is often better than hard failure.

Example persuasion:

```text
Success:
Guard lets player in.

Partial success:
Guard allows entry after a small fee.

Failure:
Guard refuses but hints at another way.
```

This keeps quests moving.

---

# 22. Hidden Choice Rules

Some choices should appear only if unlocked.

Unlock conditions:

```text
Class
Attribute
Faction
Reputation
Known secret
Quest item
Previous choice
Disguise
High Notoriety
Companion present
Learned language
Special title
```

Example:

```text
[Perception] "That wine stain is fresh. Someone moved the body."
```

Do not show every locked choice unless it helps players understand possible builds.

Options:

```text
Show locked choices for learning.
Hide locked choices for mystery.
Show in accessibility/casual mode.
```

---

# 23. Dialogue History and Journal

Players should be able to review important dialogue.

Store:

```text
Quest instructions
Important names
Locations
Clues
Player choices
NPC promises
Warnings
Consequences
```

Quest journal should summarize:

```text
What happened
What the player chose
What to do next
Who to return to
Any consequences
```

Example:

```text
You promised Mira you would search the old bridge for her missing satchel. She said it may be under a broken cart near the east side.
```

---

# 24. Companion Dialogue

Companions should react to player choices.

Companion reactions:

```text
Approval
Disapproval
Fear
Respect
Romance interest
Loss of trust
Leaving party
Intervention
Private conversation later
```

Example:

```text
Player executes a surrendered bandit.

Merciful companion:
"That was not justice."

Ruthless companion:
"Efficient. He would have betrayed us later."
```

Rules:

```text
Do not make companions complain constantly.
Save strong reactions for meaningful choices.
Let players understand companion values over time.
```

---

# 25. Multiplayer Dialogue Rules

MMOs need special handling for parties.

## Party Dialogue

Options:

```text
Party leader chooses.
Each player chooses individually.
Group votes.
Instance owner chooses.
Quest owner chooses.
```

Recommended:

```text
For shared story instances, use party leader or vote.
For personal quests, each player chooses individually.
For faction-changing choices, each player confirms individually.
```

---

## Party Choice Warning

If one player’s choice affects others:

```text
"Party leader is choosing to side with the rebels. This may affect your instance progress. Accept?"
```

Do not let one random party member permanently damage another player’s faction reputation without consent.

---

# 26. Voice Acting Rules

If using voiced dialogue:

```text
Keep critical lines voiced.
Allow text skip after line is known.
Do not delay UI until voice finishes unless dramatic.
Use subtitles.
Make subtitles readable.
Let players review transcript.
```

If only some dialogue is voiced:

```text
Voice main story, important NPCs, major choices, and combat barks.
Text-only is fine for minor NPCs and repeatable quests.
```

---

# 27. NPC Ambient Dialogue

NPCs should talk without annoying players.

Good ambient dialogue:

```text
Short
Varied
Contextual
Not too frequent
Changes by events
Does not repeat every 5 seconds
```

Bad ambient dialogue:

```text
"Have you heard about the rats?" repeated every time player walks by.
```

Good:

```text
"Market closes early tonight. Guards found tracks by the mill."
```

Frequency rule:

```text
Do not repeat the same ambient line to the same player within a short period.
Use cooldowns.
Use line pools.
```

---

# 28. Dialogue Cooldowns

Prevent repeated spam.

```text
Greeting cooldown
Ambient bark cooldown
Quest reminder cooldown
Combat taunt cooldown
Merchant advertisement cooldown
Follower comment cooldown
```

Example:

```text
Same NPC greeting line cannot repeat to same player for 10 minutes.
Same companion observation cannot repeat for 30 minutes.
```

---

# 29. Emotional Tone Rules

NPC tone should match the situation.

Bad:

```text
NPC jokes while their child is missing.
Merchant casually sells goods while town is burning.
Guard ignores player murdering someone nearby.
```

Good:

```text
Crisis changes dialogue.
Danger changes behavior.
Grief changes tone.
Victory changes mood.
Weather, time, and events affect speech.
```

---

# 30. Dialogue for World Events

Dialogue should react to world state.

Examples:

```text
Before dragon attack:
"Skies have been strange lately."

During attack:
"Run! To the cellars!"

After player saves town:
"You stood against the dragon. We owe you our lives."

If player ignored event:
"We needed help. Where were you?"
```

---

# 31. Dialogue Localization Rules

If the MMO supports multiple languages:

```text
Avoid overly long sentences.
Avoid jokes that only work in one language.
Support text expansion.
Do not hardcode word order.
Keep variables clear.
Use translation keys.
Avoid embedding UI labels inside sentences in ways that break grammar.
```

Example variable-safe text:

```text
"{npc_name} wants you to bring {item_count} {item_name} to {location_name}."
```

---

# 32. Accessibility Rules

Dialogue should support:

```text
Readable font size
Subtitle options
High contrast text
No color-only choice meaning
Screen reader support
Text speed control
Skip/auto-advance control
Dialogue history
Clear icons and labels
Controller/keyboard navigation
Reduced motion mode
```

Bad:

```text
Only red text means dangerous choice.
```

Good:

```text
[Illegal] Steal the medicine.
Warning: Guards may respond if witnessed.
```

---

# 33. Dialogue Data Structure

Each dialogue node should be data-driven.

```json
{
  "node_id": "farmer_kell_greeting",
  "npc_id": "npc_farmer_kell",
  "text": "The wolves are getting through the north fence again. I need help before they reach the sheep pens.",
  "conditions": {
    "quest_status": {
      "wolves_north_fence": "available"
    }
  },
  "choices": [
    {
      "choice_id": "accept_wolves_quest",
      "text": "I will handle the wolves.",
      "type": "accept_quest",
      "effects": {
        "start_quest": "wolves_north_fence"
      },
      "next_node": "farmer_kell_accept"
    },
    {
      "choice_id": "ask_location",
      "text": "Where is the north fence?",
      "type": "information",
      "next_node": "farmer_kell_location"
    },
    {
      "choice_id": "decline",
      "text": "Not right now.",
      "type": "leave",
      "next_node": null
    }
  ]
}
```

---

# 34. Choice Data Structure

```json
{
  "choice_id": "bribe_guard_gate",
  "text": "Offer the guard 10 gold to let you pass.",
  "tag": "Bribe",
  "requirements": {
    "gold": 10,
    "guard_corruptible": true
  },
  "warnings": [
    "Illegal action if reported."
  ],
  "success_effects": {
    "remove_gold": 10,
    "unlock_door": "east_gate",
    "legal_score_change": -25
  },
  "failure_effects": {
    "legal_score_change": -100,
    "guard_alert": true
  },
  "next_node_success": "guard_accepts_bribe",
  "next_node_failure": "guard_refuses_bribe"
}
```

---

# 35. Dialogue Processing Pipeline

When player selects a choice:

```text
1. Server receives choice request.
2. Server verifies player is in valid conversation.
3. Server verifies NPC exists and is alive/available.
4. Server verifies choice is currently available.
5. Server checks requirements.
6. Server applies effects.
7. Server updates quest/reputation/faction/inventory/legal state.
8. Server records player choice.
9. Server sends next dialogue node to client.
10. Client updates UI, journal, map, and quest tracker if needed.
```

The server must be authoritative. The client cannot decide that a locked choice was available.

---

# 36. Dialogue Edge Cases

## NPC Dies During Conversation

Rule:

```text
Close dialogue.
Process NPC death.
Update quest state.
If important, provide alternate NPC or failure state.
```

---

## Player Is Attacked During Conversation

Rule:

```text
Close or minimize dialogue.
Enter combat.
Allow player control immediately.
```

Never trap the player in dialogue while being killed.

---

## Player Walks Away During Conversation

Rule:

```text
End conversation if distance exceeds limit.
Save progress only after confirmed choices.
Do not apply unfinished choices.
```

---

## Player Disconnects During Choice

Rule:

```text
If choice was already confirmed server-side, keep result.
If not confirmed, do not apply it.
```

---

## Player Clicks Choice Twice

Rule:

```text
Use request IDs.
Process only once.
Prevent duplicate rewards, duplicate quest accepts, or duplicate payments.
```

---

## Player Opens Vendor Then Becomes Criminal

Rule:

```text
Close vendor.
Merchant/guard reacts.
Apply legal response.
```

---

## Player Starts Dialogue With Hostile NPC

Rule:

```text
Only allow if NPC supports negotiation, surrender, intimidation, or scripted dialogue.
Otherwise NPC attacks.
```

---

## Player Lacks Required Item After Dialogue Opened

Rule:

```text
Revalidate on choice selection.
If item missing, choice fails gracefully.
```

---

## Player’s Reputation Changes During Conversation

Rule:

```text
Revalidate choices before applying.
NPC may change tone if major reputation shift occurs.
```

---

## Party Leader Makes Major Choice

Rule:

```text
Require confirmation from affected players if choice impacts their personal quest, faction, or reputation.
```

---

## Player Skips Dialogue But Needs Information

Rule:

```text
Quest journal receives summary.
Map/compass updates.
Player can review transcript.
```

---

# 37. Common Dialogue Mistakes to Avoid

```text
Too much text for simple tasks.
Choices with unclear tone.
Fake choices in major decisions.
No way to exit.
NPC repeats same line forever.
Important instructions are not stored in journal.
Consequences are hidden unfairly.
Player gets punished for asking questions.
Skill checks randomly fail despite strong build.
Dialogue starts combat without warning.
NPC ignores major player reputation.
NPC talks normally during emergencies.
Party member can ruin another player’s story without consent.
Dialogue cannot be skipped or reviewed.
```

---

# 38. Good Dialogue Checklist

Before finalizing an NPC conversation, check:

```text
Is the dialogue short enough?
Is the objective clear?
Can the player ask for more details?
Can the player leave?
Are choices labeled clearly?
Are serious consequences warned?
Does the NPC react to reputation?
Does the NPC remember important history?
Does the dialogue update quest/map/journal correctly?
Can repeated dialogue be shortened?
Does the tone match the situation?
Are there alternative paths for different builds?
Can the player recover from failure?
Are multiplayer edge cases handled?
Is the dialogue accessible?
```

---

# 39. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design a non-annoying dialogue and choice system for an MMO.

Dialogue must:
- Respect player time
- Avoid unnecessary walls of text
- Put important information first
- Separate required information from optional lore
- Always provide a clear exit
- Support skipping, fast-forwarding, and reviewing dialogue
- Store important quest instructions in the journal
- React to player reputation, legal standing, notoriety, faction, class, level, attributes, and prior choices
- Support multiple NPC responses instead of repeating the same line forever

Every NPC conversation should support:
- Greeting
- Role-specific dialogue
- Quest dialogue
- Service/trade dialogue if applicable
- Rumors or local information
- Reputation-based responses
- Crime/danger responses
- Goodbye/exit
- Memory of important past interactions

Choices must be clear.
Use labels such as:
- Friendly
- Rude
- Lie
- Truth
- Threaten
- Persuade
- Bribe
- Intimidate
- Attack
- Accept
- Refuse
- Trade
- Leave
- Illegal
- Permanent
- Class
- Attribute
- Reputation

Major consequences must be warned.
Warn players before choices that:
- Start combat
- Break the law
- Change faction reputation
- Fail a quest
- Permanently kill or betray an NPC
- Lock a quest branch
- Spend large amounts of currency
- Cause companions to leave
- Make a faction hostile

The system should support:
- Flavor choices
- Information choices
- Quest choices
- Moral choices
- Faction choices
- Combat choices
- Merchant choices
- Guard/legal choices
- Skill checks
- Class-specific choices
- Attribute-based choices
- Reputation-based choices
- Hidden/unlocked choices

Avoid fake choices for major decisions.
If all options lead to the same result, make it a flavor choice, not a major story choice.

Use fail-forward design.
If a player fails persuasion, stealth, bribery, or intimidation, the quest should usually provide another path:
- Pay fee
- Sneak in
- Fight
- Find key
- Ask another NPC
- Return with better reputation
- Use class ability
- Accept different consequence

NPCs must remember important player actions:
- Helped them
- Lied to them
- Threatened them
- Stole from them
- Saved family
- Killed ally
- Completed quest
- Failed quest
- Spared enemy
- Betrayed faction

Dialogue must integrate with:
- Quest journal
- Quest tracker
- Map markers
- Compass markers
- Reputation system
- Legal system
- Notoriety system
- Merchant prices
- Guard behavior
- Companion approval
- World state

The server must be authoritative:
- Server validates available dialogue choices
- Server checks requirements
- Server applies consequences
- Server records choices
- Server updates quests, inventory, currency, reputation, faction, and legal state
- Client only displays dialogue and sends selected choice requests

Handle edge cases:
- NPC dies during conversation
- Player is attacked during conversation
- Player walks away
- Player disconnects
- Player clicks choice twice
- Player lacks required item
- Player reputation changes during conversation
- Party leader makes major choice
- Player skips dialogue but needs instructions
- Dialogue starts combat
- Quest NPC is unavailable
- Service NPC becomes hostile

The dialogue system should make NPCs feel alive, choices feel meaningful, and conversations feel useful instead of annoying.
```

---

# Final Dialogue Rule

Every conversation should answer:

```text
Why am I talking to this NPC?
What do they want?
What can I ask?
What can I choose?
What happens if I choose it?
Can I leave?
Will the game remember this?
Where is the important information stored if I forget?
```

And every choice should answer:

```text
Is this choice clear?
Is the tone clear?
Is the consequence fair?
Is the result meaningful?
Does it respect the player’s time?
Does it update the world, NPC, quest, or reputation correctly?
```

That is how you make dialogue feel like a real MMO system instead of a wall of text players mash through.


Below is a full **MMO Economic System Rule Set**.

The core rule:

```text id="economy-core-rule"
The economy must feel alive, useful, fair, and hard to exploit.

Players should be able to earn, spend, trade, craft, sell, buy, invest, and lose resources in ways that make sense for the world.
```

A good MMO economy should control:

```text id="economy-controls"
Currency creation
Currency destruction
Item creation
Item destruction
Vendor prices
Player trading
Auction house
Crafting
Gathering
Regional supply and demand
Taxes and fees
Inflation
Item rarity
Repair costs
Loot value
Quest rewards
NPC merchant behavior
Illegal markets
Guild economies
Player housing
Banking
Economic abuse prevention
```

---

# 1. Economic System Overview

Every MMO economy needs three major flows:

```text id="economic-flows"
1. Sources
How money and items enter the world.

2. Sinks
How money and items leave the world.

3. Exchanges
How players, NPCs, vendors, guilds, and factions trade value.
```

If there are too many sources and not enough sinks, inflation happens.

If there are too many sinks and not enough sources, players feel poor and frustrated.

If rare items enter the world too easily, rewards feel meaningless.

If everything is too rare, players feel stuck.

---

# 2. Main Economic Categories

The economy should include:

```text id="economic-categories"
Currencies
Vendor goods
Loot drops
Crafting materials
Crafted items
Equipment
Consumables
Quest rewards
Trade goods
Luxury goods
Housing items
Mounts
Pets
Cosmetics
Services
Taxes
Fees
Auction house
Banking
Guild economy
Regional markets
Black market
Stolen goods
```

---

# 3. Currency System

Use a primary currency and optional secondary currencies.

## Primary Currency

Example:

```text id="primary-currency"
Gold
Silver
Copper
```

Recommended conversion:

```text id="currency-conversion"
100 Copper = 1 Silver
100 Silver = 1 Gold
```

Primary currency is used for:

```text id="primary-currency-uses"
Vendor purchases
Repairs
Trading
Auction house
Crafting fees
Travel fees
Housing upkeep
Guild costs
Bank upgrades
Training
Taxes
```

---

## Secondary Currencies

Secondary currencies are earned from specific activities.

Examples:

```text id="secondary-currencies"
Faction Tokens
Arena Marks
Dungeon Seals
Raid Crests
Crafting Writs
Festival Tokens
Guild Marks
Bounty Tokens
Honor Points
Black Market Coins
Reputation Vouchers
```

Rules:

```text id="secondary-currency-rules"
Secondary currencies should have clear sources and uses.
They should not all replace gold.
They should help control special reward systems.
Some may be account-wide.
Some may be character-bound.
Some may expire after seasons/events.
```

Example:

```text id="currency-example"
Arena Marks:
Earned from PvP.
Used for PvP gear, cosmetics, titles, and mounts.

Dungeon Seals:
Earned from dungeon bosses.
Used for dungeon gear and upgrade materials.

Festival Tokens:
Earned during seasonal events.
Expire when event ends or convert to minor currency.
```

---

# 4. Currency Sources

Currency enters the world through sources.

## Gold Sources

```text id="gold-sources"
Quest rewards
Enemy loot
Vendor sale of items
Dungeon rewards
Raid rewards
Daily/weekly quests
Public events
Crafting orders
Gathering contracts
PvP rewards
Treasure chests
Exploration rewards
Trade route payouts
Bounty rewards
NPC work tasks
```

---

## Currency Source Rules

```text id="currency-source-rules"
Gold rewards should scale with level, difficulty, and time investment.
Repeatable gold sources need daily/weekly caps or diminishing returns.
Low-level enemies should not generate meaningful gold for high-level players.
Bosses should reward meaningful value but not flood the economy.
Quest gold should be predictable and fair.
```

---

## Gold Reward Scaling Example

```text id="gold-scaling-example"
Minor local quest: 5 to 25 silver
Normal quest: 25 silver to 2 gold
Elite quest: 2 to 8 gold
Dungeon quest: 5 to 20 gold
Raid quest: 20 to 100 gold
World event: 5 to 50 gold depending on contribution
```

Scale these numbers to your game’s economy.

---

# 5. Currency Sinks

Currency sinks remove gold from the world.

A healthy MMO needs strong but fair sinks.

## Gold Sinks

```text id="gold-sinks"
Equipment repairs
Vendor purchases
Crafting fees
Auction house listing fees
Auction house sale taxes
Fast travel
Mount training
Skill training
Spell training
Bank upgrades
Bag upgrades
Housing purchase
Housing upkeep
Guild hall upkeep
Transmog/cosmetic changes
Item reforging
Enchanting fees
Respec costs
Teleportation
NPC services
Mail fees
Death recovery fees
Faction bribes
Legal fines
Taxes
```

---

## Sink Design Rules

```text id="sink-rules"
Sinks should feel like services, convenience, luxury, risk, or maintenance.
Do not make basic gameplay feel constantly punished.
Luxury sinks can be expensive.
Mandatory sinks should be moderate.
Endgame players need larger optional sinks.
New players need gentle costs.
```

Good sinks:

```text id="good-sinks"
Repair fees
Auction taxes
Housing decoration
Mount cosmetics
Guild hall upgrades
Fast travel convenience
Crafting station fees
Item appearance changes
```

Bad sinks:

```text id="bad-sinks"
Charging players constantly just to walk, talk, loot, or accept quests.
Repair costs so high players avoid combat.
Taxes so harsh players avoid trading.
Death costs so punishing players stop exploring.
```

---

# 6. Inflation Rules

Inflation happens when too much currency enters the world and not enough leaves.

## Inflation Causes

```text id="inflation-causes"
Too much gold from quests
Too much gold from enemy drops
Bots farming currency
No repair costs
No auction taxes
No luxury sinks
Repeatable quests with high gold rewards
Vendor trash worth too much
Item duplication exploits
Gold selling
High-level players farming low-level content
```

---

## Inflation Controls

```text id="inflation-controls"
Repair costs
Auction house taxes
Vendor fees
Travel fees
Housing upkeep
Crafting station fees
Seasonal luxury items
Gold caps on repeatable rewards
Diminishing returns on farmed mobs
Anti-bot detection
No full gold rewards from low-level content
Item binding
Material sinks
Upgrade systems
```

---

## Inflation Monitoring

Track:

```text id="inflation-monitoring"
Total gold in circulation
Average gold per player level
Auction house prices
Vendor sale volume
Gold created per day
Gold destroyed per day
Bot-like farming behavior
Price spikes on core materials
Quest reward inflation
Repair fee burden
```

A healthy economy should know how much currency is being created and destroyed every day.

---

# 7. Vendor Economy

NPC vendors should sell and buy items based on role, location, reputation, and economy.

## Vendor Types

```text id="vendor-types"
General Goods Merchant
Blacksmith
Armorer
Weapon Vendor
Alchemist
Food Vendor
Innkeeper
Stable Master
Tailor
Jeweler
Book Seller
Magic Vendor
Fence
Black Market Vendor
Faction Quartermaster
Auctioneer
Banker
Traveling Merchant
Luxury Vendor
Profession Supplier
```

---

## Vendor Price Formula

```text id="vendor-price-formula"
Final Vendor Price =
Base Item Value
× Vendor Type Modifier
× Regional Modifier
× Supply Modifier
× Demand Modifier
× Reputation Modifier
× Legal Modifier
× Scarcity Modifier
```

---

## Normal Vendor Rules

```text id="normal-vendor-rules"
Vendors sell items above base value.
Vendors buy items below base value.
Vendors do not buy everything.
Vendors have limited or themed inventory.
Vendors may restock over time.
Vendors may refuse stolen, illegal, or bound items.
```

Recommended baseline:

```text id="vendor-baseline"
Normal vendor sells at 120% of base value.
Normal vendor buys at 40% to 50% of base value.
Friendly vendor sells at 105% to 115%.
Friendly vendor buys at 55% to 75%.
Hostile/disliked vendor sells at 140% to 200%.
Hostile/disliked vendor buys at 20% to 35%.
```

---

## Vendor Reputation Effects

Player standing should affect prices.

```text id="vendor-reputation-effects"
High Likeability:
Discounts, better buy prices, rare stock.

High Legal Standing:
Lawful merchants trust player, offer official goods.

Low Legal Standing:
Lawful merchants may refuse service or call guards.

High Notoriety:
Special goods may unlock, but prices may rise if merchant fears player.

Criminal reputation:
Fences and black market vendors offer services.
Lawful vendors become suspicious.
```

Example:

```text id="vendor-reputation-example"
Beloved Hero:
Blacksmith sells sword for 95 gold instead of 120.

Known Outlaw:
Blacksmith refuses service.
Fence buys stolen sword for 35 gold.

Famous Villain:
Merchant may sell out of fear but charges 200%.
```

---

# 8. Vendor Inventory Rules

Vendor inventory should match location and profession.

## Blacksmith

```text id="blacksmith-inventory"
Sells:
Weapons, armor, tools, repair kits, ingots.

Buys:
Weapons, armor, ore, tools, metal goods.

Services:
Repair, reforging, sharpening, crafting orders.
```

## Alchemist

```text id="alchemist-inventory"
Sells:
Potions, antidotes, herbs, reagents, elixirs.

Buys:
Herbs, monster organs, bottles, magical ingredients.

Services:
Potion crafting, poison removal, rare elixir brewing.
```

## Innkeeper

```text id="innkeeper-inventory"
Sells:
Food, drink, rooms, rumors.

Buys:
Food supplies, alcohol, local goods.

Services:
Rested XP, room rental, local gossip, small jobs.
```

## Stable Master

```text id="stable-inventory"
Sells:
Mounts, feed, saddles, travel services.

Buys:
Animal feed, tack, mount supplies.

Services:
Mount care, stable rental, travel routes.
```

## Fence

```text id="fence-inventory"
Sells:
Lockpicks, disguises, illegal tools, stolen goods.

Buys:
Stolen items, contraband, rare illicit goods.

Services:
Launder items, arrange bribes, connect criminals.
```

---

# 9. Supply and Demand

The economy should react to supply and demand, but not become impossible to balance.

## Supply

Supply increases when:

```text id="supply-increases"
Players sell many of the same item
Region produces the item locally
Trade routes are safe
NPC workers are active
Faction controls resource nodes
Seasonal harvest is good
```

Supply decreases when:

```text id="supply-decreases"
Players buy many of the same item
War disrupts trade
Bandits attack caravans
Resource nodes are depleted
Town is under siege
Weather or plague affects production
```

---

## Demand

Demand increases when:

```text id="demand-increases"
Players need item for crafting
Item is used in popular gear
Faction requests item
War event needs supplies
Quest requires item
Seasonal event increases need
NPC town project consumes item
```

Demand decreases when:

```text id="demand-decreases"
Item becomes outdated
Event ends
Too many players craft it
Better substitute appears
Faction project is completed
```

---

## Price Adjustment Example

```text id="supply-demand-example"
Iron Ore Base Price: 10 silver

Mining town:
High supply, low price: 6 silver

Warfront city:
High demand, low supply: 18 silver

Blockaded port:
Supply shortage: 25 silver
```

---

# 10. Regional Economy

Different regions should have different prices and goods.

## Regional Production

```text id="regional-production"
Forest region:
Wood, herbs, leather, game meat.

Mountain region:
Ore, gems, stone, rare minerals.

Coastal region:
Fish, salt, pearls, ship parts.

Desert region:
Spices, glass, rare metals, survival gear.

Magic region:
Crystals, scrolls, enchanted goods, reagents.

Farming region:
Grain, vegetables, livestock, cloth.
```

---

## Regional Economy Rules

```text id="regional-rules"
Items are cheaper where produced.
Items are expensive where imported.
Trade routes move goods between regions.
Dangerous roads increase prices.
Player actions can improve or disrupt trade.
```

Example:

```text id="regional-example"
A mining town sells iron cheaply.
A coastal city sells fish cheaply.
A desert city sells water and food at higher prices.
A war-torn region has expensive weapons and medicine.
```

---

# 11. Trade Routes

Trade routes make the economy feel alive.

## Trade Route System

```text id="trade-route-system"
Goods move from production regions to demand regions.
Caravans, ships, wagons, and couriers carry goods.
Players can protect, attack, invest in, or participate in trade routes.
```

---

## Trade Route Events

```text id="trade-route-events"
Caravan escort
Bandit ambush
Bridge destroyed
Port blockade
Storm delays shipment
Merchant strike
Tax increase
Smuggler route discovered
Faction war blocks road
```

---

## Player Impact

Players can:

```text id="player-trade-impact"
Escort caravans
Raid caravans
Smuggle goods
Invest in routes
Clear roads of monsters
Build bridges
Negotiate trade agreements
Sabotage rival routes
Protect merchants
```

Results:

```text id="trade-results"
Safe routes lower prices.
Dangerous routes raise prices.
Destroyed routes cause shortages.
Strong trade increases town wealth.
Bandit-controlled routes increase black market activity.
```

---

# 12. Player Trading

Players should be able to trade eligible goods.

## Trade Rules

```text id="player-trade-rules"
Both players must be nearby or use mail/market systems.
Both players must confirm trade.
Changing any item or currency removes confirmation.
Bound items cannot be traded.
Quest items cannot be traded unless designed.
Stolen items may be tradable only through criminal systems.
Trade must be atomic.
```

Atomic means:

```text id="trade-atomic"
Either both sides receive everything correctly, or nothing changes.
```

---

## Trade Edge Warnings

Show warnings for:

```text id="trade-warnings"
Item is damaged.
Item will bind when received.
Item is stolen.
Item expires soon.
Item is unusually valuable.
Trade partner changed offer.
Currency amount changed.
Item cannot be used by your class.
```

---

# 13. Auction House / Market Board

An auction house lets players sell items widely.

## Auction House Rules

```text id="auction-rules"
Players list tradeable items.
Items are held in escrow while listed.
Buyers search, filter, and purchase.
Seller receives gold minus fees/taxes.
Expired listings return to seller.
Bound and quest items cannot be listed.
```

---

## Auction House Fees

Fees help control inflation.

```text id="auction-fees"
Listing Fee:
Small upfront fee to discourage spam.

Sale Tax:
Percentage of final sale price removed from economy.

Deposit:
Returned if item sells, lost if listing expires.
```

Recommended:

```text id="auction-fee-values"
Listing fee: 1% to 5% of vendor value
Sale tax: 5% to 15% of final sale price
Luxury item tax: 10% to 20%
```

---

## Auction House Filters

```text id="auction-filters"
Item type
Level requirement
Rarity
Price
Stat
Crafting profession
Material type
Usable by class
Region
Seller
Buyout price
Time remaining
```

---

# 14. Player Shops

Optional system where players run shops.

## Player Shop Rules

```text id="player-shop-rules"
Player rents stall or shop space.
Player lists items with prices.
Shop has tax/upkeep cost.
Shop can be public, guild-only, or faction-only.
Illegal items require black market shop.
```

Good for:

```text id="player-shop-uses"
Crafters
Roleplay cities
Guild towns
Player housing
Merchant gameplay
Regional markets
```

---

# 15. Crafting Economy

Crafting should be a major part of the economy.

## Crafting Inputs

```text id="crafting-inputs"
Materials
Recipes
Tools
Crafting stations
Profession level
Optional reagents
Fuel
Currency fees
Time
Skill checks
```

---

## Crafting Outputs

```text id="crafting-outputs"
Weapons
Armor
Potions
Food
Scrolls
Runes
Tools
Furniture
Mount gear
Ship parts
Siege gear
Cosmetics
Repair kits
Enchantments
Trade goods
```

---

## Crafting Rules

```text id="crafting-rules"
Crafted items should fill real economic needs.
Crafting consumes materials and sometimes currency.
Higher profession level unlocks better recipes.
Rare materials should come from dangerous or limited content.
Crafted items should compete with loot, but not always replace it.
Crafting should create both consumables and long-term goods.
```

---

## Crafting Quality

Crafted item quality can depend on:

```text id="crafting-quality-factors"
Profession level
Tool quality
Material quality
Optional reagents
Crafting station quality
Recipe rarity
Player attributes
Luck, slightly
Guild bonuses
Regional bonuses
```

Example:

```text id="crafting-quality-example"
Iron Sword:
Common result with normal materials.
Uncommon result with refined iron.
Rare result with masterwork tool and rare flux.
```

---

# 16. Material Economy

Materials should have clear sources and uses.

## Material Sources

```text id="material-sources"
Mining
Herbalism
Skinning
Logging
Fishing
Farming
Monster drops
Dungeon nodes
World bosses
Salvaging
Recycling
Trade routes
Vendors
Player farms
```

---

## Material Sinks

```text id="material-sinks"
Crafting
Repair kits
Enchanting
Upgrading
Guild projects
Town projects
Housing construction
Quest turn-ins
Faction supply missions
Consumables
Siege equipment
```

---

## Material Tiering

```text id="material-tiers"
Tier 1: Starter materials
Tier 2: Regional materials
Tier 3: Advanced materials
Tier 4: Rare materials
Tier 5: Endgame materials
Legendary: Boss/event materials
```

Rule:

```text id="material-tier-rule"
Low-tier materials should remain useful through recipes, housing, repairs, or refinement.
Do not make old materials completely worthless.
```

---

# 17. Gathering Economy

Gathering should be valuable but protected from bots.

## Gathering Rules

```text id="gathering-rules"
Resource nodes spawn in logical places.
Ore appears near mountains/caves.
Herbs appear in fields/forests/swamps.
Fish appear in water.
Rare nodes appear in dangerous or hidden areas.
Nodes respawn over time.
High-value nodes need anti-bot rules.
```

---

## Gathering Anti-Bot Rules

```text id="gathering-anti-bot"
Randomized spawn locations within valid zones
Skill checks or mini-interactions
Diminishing returns from repetitive routes
Node ownership or shared nodes
Server-side movement validation
Suspicious farming detection
Material caps from repeatable public nodes
Rare node protection
```

---

# 18. Item Rarity and Drop Economy

Loot must be controlled.

## Drop Rarity

```text id="drop-rarity"
Common:
Frequent, low value.

Uncommon:
Useful, moderate value.

Rare:
Valuable, limited.

Epic:
Very valuable, difficult content.

Legendary:
Extremely rare, major achievement.

Artifact:
Unique or story-linked.
```

---

## Drop Rules

```text id="drop-rules"
Low-level enemies should not drop high-level power gear.
Bosses should drop better loot than normal enemies.
Rare items should have controlled drop rates.
Personal loot reduces conflict.
Bound items reduce market flooding.
Crafting materials can drop more freely than finished gear.
```

---

## Loot Table Rules

Every loot table should define:

```text id="loot-table-fields"
Enemy type
Enemy level
Enemy rank
Region
Faction
Rarity chances
Material drops
Currency drops
Quest drops
Unique drops
Player level restrictions
Daily/weekly lockouts if needed
```

---

# 19. Item Binding and Economy

Binding controls item circulation.

## Binding Types

```text id="binding-types"
Unbound
Bind on Pickup
Bind on Equip
Bind on Use
Bind to Account
Quest Bound
Faction Bound
Season Bound
```

---

## Binding Economy Rules

```text id="binding-economy-rules"
Bind on Pickup keeps boss loot from flooding markets.
Bind on Equip allows trade before use.
Consumables should usually be tradeable.
Crafted gear can be Bind on Equip or unbound.
Quest items should be bound.
Legendary/artifact items should usually be bound.
```

Binding is one of the strongest tools to prevent the economy from being overwhelmed.

---

# 20. Durability and Repair Economy

Durability creates an ongoing gold sink.

## Durability Rules

```text id="durability-rules"
Equipment loses durability from combat and death.
Broken gear loses stats or cannot be used.
Repairs cost gold or materials.
Higher-quality gear costs more to repair.
Repair costs should scale with item level and rarity.
```

---

## Repair Formula

```text id="repair-formula"
Repair Cost =
Item Base Value
× Damage Percent
× Quality Modifier
× Repair Service Modifier
```

Quality modifiers:

```text id="repair-quality-modifiers"
Common: x1.0
Uncommon: x1.2
Rare: x1.5
Epic: x2.0
Legendary: x3.0
Artifact: x5.0
```

Rule:

```text id="repair-balance-rule"
Repair costs should matter over time but should not make normal combat feel unaffordable.
```

---

# 21. Salvage and Recycling

Players should be able to break items into materials.

## Salvage Rules

```text id="salvage-rules"
Eligible items can be salvaged.
Salvage returns some materials.
Higher-quality items return better materials.
Bound items may be salvageable.
Quest items cannot be salvaged unless designed.
Locked items cannot be salvaged.
```

---

## Salvage Benefits

```text id="salvage-benefits"
Removes excess items from economy.
Creates material supply.
Gives value to unwanted loot.
Supports crafting.
Reduces vendor-trash clutter.
```

---

# 22. Item Upgrade Economy

Upgrading items is a powerful sink.

## Upgrade Inputs

```text id="upgrade-inputs"
Gold
Materials
Runes
Gems
Essences
Duplicate items
Faction tokens
Boss materials
Crafting services
```

---

## Upgrade Rules

```text id="upgrade-rules"
Upgrades consume resources.
Upgrades may bind the item.
Failure rules must be clear.
Expensive upgrades should not be required for basic play.
High-end upgrades can serve as endgame sinks.
```

Avoid predatory systems:

```text id="upgrade-avoid"
Do not hide upgrade failure odds.
Do not destroy rare gear without clear warning.
Do not make success depend only on paid currency.
```

---

# 23. Consumable Economy

Consumables create repeat demand.

Consumables include:

```text id="consumable-types"
Potions
Food
Drink
Scrolls
Ammunition
Poisons
Elixirs
Buff items
Repair kits
Teleport stones
Bombs
Traps
Reagents
```

Rules:

```text id="consumable-rules"
Consumables should be useful but not mandatory for every minor fight.
High-end content may encourage preparation.
Crafted consumables should support player economy.
Vendors can sell basic versions.
Crafters should make better versions.
```

---

# 24. Services Economy

Players should pay for useful services.

## NPC Services

```text id="npc-services"
Repair
Training
Respec
Fast travel
Teleportation
Bank expansion
Mount training
Housing purchase
Housing upkeep
Crafting station rental
Item identification
Enchanting
Transmog/cosmetic change
Legal fine payment
Bribe
Mail delivery
Storage rental
```

---

## Player Services

Players may provide:

```text id="player-services"
Crafting
Enchanting
Repair
Gathering
Transport
Protection
Caravan escort
Mercenary work
Dungeon carrying, if allowed
Bounty hunting
Information brokering
Housing decoration
Guild labor
```

Rule:

```text id="service-rule"
Services should create economic roles beyond combat.
```

---

# 25. Banking System

Players need storage and financial services.

## Bank Rules

```text id="bank-rules"
Players can store items.
Players can upgrade bank space.
Players can access currency wallet.
Players can deposit materials if allowed.
Account bank may hold account-bound items.
Guild bank supports shared storage.
```

---

## Bank Fees

Possible fees:

```text id="bank-fees"
Bank slot upgrades
Material storage expansion
Guild bank tabs
Remote bank access
Item recovery
Safe deposit boxes
```

Avoid charging players for basic safe storage too aggressively.

---

# 26. Guild Economy

Guilds should have shared economic systems.

## Guild Currency and Storage

```text id="guild-economy"
Guild bank
Guild treasury
Guild tax
Guild crafting projects
Guild hall upkeep
Guild vendor unlocks
Guild repair funds
Guild trade contracts
Guild-owned shops
```

---

## Guild Tax

Optional:

```text id="guild-tax"
A small percentage of gold earned by members goes to guild treasury.
Must be visible and configurable.
```

Recommended:

```text id="guild-tax-values"
0% to 10%
Default 0%
Member-visible
Leader-controlled with permissions
```

---

## Guild Projects

Guilds can spend resources on:

```text id="guild-projects"
Guild hall
Crafting stations
Defenses
Banners
Mount stables
Bank tabs
Teleport portals
Siege gear
Town upgrades
NPC workers
Trade routes
```

This creates major endgame resource sinks.

---

# 27. Housing Economy

Housing is a strong optional sink.

## Housing Costs

```text id="housing-costs"
Purchase price
Rent/upkeep
Decoration costs
Crafted furniture
Land taxes
Repair costs
Upgrade costs
Guild neighborhood fees
```

---

## Housing Rules

```text id="housing-rules"
Housing should be useful but not required for basic progression.
Housing should support decorators, crafters, gatherers, and social players.
Upkeep should not delete homes too aggressively.
Rare decorations can be valuable long-term rewards.
```

---

# 28. Taxes and Fees

Taxes remove gold and support world systems.

## Tax Types

```text id="tax-types"
Auction house tax
Trade tax
Vendor tax
Property tax
Guild tax
Caravan toll
City gate toll
Crafting station fee
Market stall fee
Luxury tax
Import/export tax
Criminal fine
```

---

## Tax Rules

```text id="tax-rules"
Taxes should be clear before transaction.
Taxes should not surprise players.
Taxes can vary by region, faction, or legal status.
High taxes may encourage black markets or smuggling.
Low taxes may attract merchants.
```

Example:

```text id="tax-example"
Capital City Auction Tax: 10%
Freeport Auction Tax: 5%
Black Market Cut: 20%, but accepts stolen goods
```

---

# 29. Black Market Economy

A black market supports outlaw gameplay.

## Black Market Services

```text id="black-market-services"
Buy stolen goods
Sell illegal items
Launder stolen items
Sell disguises
Sell forged papers
Arrange bribes
Sell poison
Sell lockpicks
Sell forbidden spells
Offer criminal contracts
```

---

## Black Market Rules

```text id="black-market-rules"
Requires criminal contacts, reputation, or discovery.
Prices are risky and variable.
Guards may raid black markets.
Players may be betrayed.
Black market vendors charge high cuts.
Stolen goods sell for less than legal goods.
```

Example:

```text id="black-market-example"
Stolen Ruby Necklace:
Base Value: 500 gold
Normal Merchant: refuses
Fence: buys for 175 gold
After laundering fee: can sell legally for 350 gold
```

---

# 30. Stolen Goods Economy

If theft exists, stolen goods need rules.

## Stolen Item Rules

```text id="stolen-rules"
Stolen items are marked.
Normal vendors may refuse them.
Guards may confiscate them.
Fences buy them at reduced prices.
Laundering can remove stolen status.
Famous stolen items may remain traceable.
```

---

## Laundering

```text id="laundering"
Player pays fee or completes task.
Item loses stolen tag.
Rare/famous items may require special laundering.
Laundering may fail if player is betrayed or caught.
```

---

# 31. Legal Economy

The law should affect economy.

## Legal Standing Effects

```text id="legal-economy-effects"
High Legal Standing:
Lower fines
Access to official contracts
Better prices from lawful merchants
Access to guard/faction vendors

Low Legal Standing:
Higher fines
Service refusal
Confiscation risk
No access to lawful markets
More reliance on black market
Higher bribe costs
```

---

## Fines

Fines remove gold and punish crime.

Fine formula:

```text id="fine-formula"
Fine =
Crime Severity
+ Value of Damages
+ Value of Stolen Goods
+ Repeat Offender Modifier
+ Notoriety Modifier
```

Example:

```text id="fine-example"
Stealing bread: 10 silver
Stealing horse: 10 gold
Assaulting guard: 50 gold
Murder: 500+ gold or jail/capture
Treason: prison, exile, bounty, confiscation
```

---

# 32. Reputation and Economy

Reputation should directly affect economic access.

## Likeability Effects

```text id="likeability-economic-effects"
High Likeability:
Discounts
Gifts
Better quest rewards
Free lodging
Extra merchant stock
Better buy prices

Low Likeability:
Higher prices
Refused service
Worse buy prices
NPCs hide goods
Merchants demand payment upfront
```

---

## Notoriety Effects

```text id="notoriety-economic-effects"
High positive Notoriety:
Rare merchants seek player.
Luxury goods unlock.
Fans offer gifts.

High negative Notoriety:
Prices rise from fear.
Some merchants refuse service.
Criminal vendors may offer special deals.
Bounty hunters appear.
```

---

# 33. Class and Profession Economy

Different classes and professions should interact with the economy differently.

## Class Economic Roles

