```text id="class-economic-roles"
Warrior:
Weapon/armor demand, repair demand, mercenary work.

Mage:
Reagents, scrolls, enchanted items, teleport services.

Rogue:
Lockpicks, poisons, fences, stolen goods, stealth contracts.

Priest:
Healing services, blessings, holy items, temple donations.

Ranger:
Animal goods, arrows, traps, leather, tracking services.

Crafter:
Materials, recipes, tools, commissions, crafted gear.

Merchant:
Trade routes, buying low/selling high, player shops.
```

---

# 35. Profession Progression

Profession level should affect:

```text id="profession-effects"
Recipe access
Material efficiency
Crafting quality
Crafting speed
Repair skill
Rare proc chance
Item customization
Commission access
NPC respect
Economic opportunities
```

Profession XP comes from:

```text id="profession-xp"
Crafting items
Gathering resources
Completing profession quests
Fulfilling work orders
Repairing items
Discovering recipes
Participating in town projects
```

---

# 36. Work Order System

Work orders let players request crafted goods.

## Work Order Rules

```text id="work-order-rules"
Player posts request.
Player supplies materials, gold, or both.
Crafter accepts order.
Server holds materials in escrow.
Crafter completes item.
Requester receives item.
Crafter receives payment and profession XP.
```

This prevents scams and supports crafters.

---

# 37. NPC Labor Economy

NPCs should also produce and consume goods.

## NPC Economic Roles

```text id="npc-economic-roles"
Farmers produce food.
Miners produce ore.
Blacksmiths consume ore and produce tools/weapons.
Merchants move goods.
Guards consume taxes and protect trade.
Nobles consume luxury goods.
Peasants consume basics.
Thieves steal and redistribute goods.
Temples consume donations and provide healing.
```

---

## Town Wealth

Each town can have a wealth score.

```text id="town-wealth"
Town Wealth affects:
Vendor stock
Building quality
Guard strength
Road quality
NPC clothing
Crime rate
Available quests
Tax rate
Prices
Public events
```

Town wealth increases from:

```text id="town-wealth-gains"
Safe trade routes
Player investments
Completed town projects
Successful harvests
Protected caravans
Cleared monsters
Faction prosperity
```

Town wealth decreases from:

```text id="town-wealth-losses"
Raids
Bandits
War
Plague
Famine
Trade disruption
High crime
Player sabotage
Boss attacks
```


# 39. Trade Goods

Trade goods are items mainly used for economy.

Examples:

```text id="trade-goods"
Spices
Silk
Ore crates
Wine
Salt
Fish barrels
Grain sacks
Luxury cloth
Gem parcels
Medicine crates
Weapon shipments
Religious relics
Illegal contraband
```

Rules:

```text id="trade-goods-rules"
Buy where cheap.
Sell where expensive.
Prices vary by region.
Trade goods may be heavy or risky.
Trade goods may attract bandits.
Trade goods may be taxed.
Smuggling avoids tax but risks legal penalties.
```

---

# 40. Player Caravans

Optional advanced economy system.

## Caravan Rules

```text id="caravan-rules"
Player buys or crafts trade goods.
Player loads caravan, cart, ship, or mount.
Player chooses destination.
Route has risk and reward.
Other players may protect or attack depending on PvP rules.
Successful delivery pays profit.
```

---

## Caravan Risks

```text id="caravan-risks"
Bandits
Monsters
Weather
Road damage
Tolls
Taxes
PvP attackers
Smugglers
Rival factions
Spoilage
```

---

# 41. Scarcity Rules

Scarcity creates value.

## Scarcity Types

```text id="scarcity-types"
Limited spawn materials
Boss-only materials
Seasonal items
Faction-limited goods
Crafting cooldown items
Rare recipes
Regional goods
Event rewards
Prestige cosmetics
```

Rules:

```text id="scarcity-rules"
Scarce items should have clear sources.
Scarce power items need careful balance.
Scarce cosmetics are safer than scarce combat power.
Avoid extreme scarcity for mandatory progression items.
```

---

# 42. Time-Based Economy

The economy can change with time.

## Time Effects

```text id="time-effects"
Markets busier during day.
Black markets active at night.
Fish prices change by season.
Food prices rise during famine.
Weapon prices rise during war.
Festival items appear during events.
Certain merchants travel on schedules.
```

---

# 43. Seasonal Economy

Seasonal events can add temporary items.

Rules:

```text id="seasonal-rules"
Seasonal currency should have clear expiration or conversion.
Seasonal items should not break long-term economy.
Cosmetics are ideal seasonal rewards.
Temporary power items should expire or be limited.
```

---

# 44. Food, Survival, and Consumable Economy

If survival systems exist:

```text id="survival-economy"
Food and drink matter.
Climate gear matters.
Medicine matters.
Shelter matters.
```

Rules:

```text id="survival-rules"
Survival should create demand but not become constant annoyance.
Food can provide buffs instead of mandatory hunger.
Harsh survival can exist in specific zones.
```

Recommended:

```text id="survival-recommended"
Normal zones:
Food gives bonuses.

Harsh zones:
Food, water, warmth, or medicine may become necessary.
```

---

# 45. Item Decay and Spoilage

Optional system.

## Spoilage Examples

```text id="spoilage"
Fresh fish spoils.
Raw meat spoils.
Magic crystals decay.
Summoned items expire.
Event items expire.
```

Rules:

```text id="spoilage-rules"
Do not spoil expensive items unexpectedly.
Show timers clearly.
Allow preservation methods.
Spoilage can support cooking, alchemy, and trade timing.
```

---

# 46. Economic Progression by Level

Level affects economy access.

```text id="level-economic-access"
Low level:
Basic vendors, starter materials, simple crafting.

Mid level:
Regional markets, profession tools, better gathering, trade routes.

High level:
Rare materials, luxury markets, guild projects, endgame crafting.

Max level:
Endgame upgrades, high-value trade, rare recipes, housing, guild economy.
```

Rule:

```text id="level-economy-rule"
Level should unlock economic opportunity, but low-level players should still have useful roles.
```

Example:

```text id="low-level-economy"
A low-level gatherer can collect herbs used by high-level alchemists.
A new crafter can make basic consumables needed by everyone.
```

---

# 47. Economy and Quest Rewards

Quest rewards should support the economy without flooding it.

## Quest Reward Types

```text id="quest-reward-types"
Gold
Items
Materials
Reputation
Currency tokens
Recipes
Trade goods
Crafting tools
Housing items
Consumables
```

Rules:

```text id="quest-reward-rules"
Gold rewards scale with level.
Repeatable quests need caps.
Quest gear may be bound.
Quest materials should not crash markets.
Players should sometimes choose between gold, item, or reputation.
```

---

# 48. Economy and Loot Drops

Enemy loot should make sense.

## Enemy Loot Logic

```text id="enemy-loot-logic"
Wolves drop hide, meat, teeth.
Bandits drop coins, weapons, stolen goods.
Undead drop cursed bones, old coins, relics.
Guards drop official gear only if loot rules allow it.
Dragons drop scales, claws, gems, rare magic materials.
```

Bad:

```text id="bad-loot"
Wolf drops plate armor and 50 gold.
Rat drops legendary sword.
Poor farmer drops rare magic gems.
```

Good:

```text id="good-loot"
Wolf drops pelt, meat, fang.
Bandit drops dagger, coins, stolen ring.
Mage drops scroll, robe cloth, arcane dust.
```

---

# 49. Item Value Rules

Item value should consider:

```text id="item-value-factors"
Base usefulness
Rarity
Level
Quality
Crafting use
Demand
Supply
Durability
Binding
Stolen status
Region
Vendor type
Event status
```

---

## Example Value Formula

```text id="item-value-formula"
Item Value =
Base Value
× Level Modifier
× Quality Modifier
× Demand Modifier
× Scarcity Modifier
× Condition Modifier
```

Condition modifier:

```text id="condition-modifier"
100% durability: x1.0
75% durability: x0.85
50% durability: x0.65
25% durability: x0.40
Broken: x0.10
```

---

# 50. Economic UI

Players need clear economic information.

## Inventory Tooltip Should Show

```text id="economic-tooltip"
Vendor value
Auction average, if known
Item rarity
Item binding
Crafting uses
Quest uses
Stolen status
Durability
Repair cost
Demand indicator
Region price hint
```

Example:

```text id="tooltip-example"
Iron Ore
Crafting Material

Vendor Value: 4 silver
Average Market Price: 9 silver
Used in: Blacksmithing, Engineering
Regional Demand: High in Warfront City
Stack: 128 / 200
```

---

## Market UI Should Show

```text id="market-ui"
Current listings
Average price
Lowest price
Recent sale price
Price history
Stack size
Seller
Time remaining
Tax/fee
Total cost
Usable by player
```

---

# 51. Price History

Players should see market trends.

Useful data:

```text id="price-history"
Average price over 24 hours
Average price over 7 days
Lowest listing
Highest listing
Recent sale range
Volume sold
```

This helps players trade intelligently and reduces scams.

---

# 52. Anti-Scam Rules

The game should protect players from obvious scams without removing trade freedom.

## Trade Protection

```text id="anti-scam-trade"
Show item names clearly.
Show currency clearly.
Warn when trade changes.
Warn for damaged items.
Warn for item that cannot be used.
Warn for binding on receipt.
Final confirmation required.
```

## Auction Protection

```text id="anti-scam-auction"
Clearly show unit price and total price.
Prevent misleading stack listings.
Warn if price is far above recent average.
Require confirmation for very expensive purchases.
```

---

# 53. Anti-Dupe Rules

Duplication destroys economies.

Mandatory rules:

```text id="anti-dupe"
All item/currency movement must be server-authoritative.
All trades must be atomic.
All auction sales must be atomic.
All mail attachments must be atomic.
All crafting transactions must be atomic.
Use transaction IDs.
Ignore duplicate requests.
Log every important item movement.
Never trust client item counts.
```

---

# 54. Bot and Real-Money Trading Prevention

Bots and gold sellers damage the economy.

## Detection Signals

```text id="bot-signals"
Repeated farming path
24/7 gathering
Impossible reaction times
Identical movement loops
Massive currency transfers
Low-level accounts mailing large gold
Repeated auction undercutting patterns
High-value trades with no fair exchange
New accounts funneling gold to one account
```

---

## Prevention Tools

```text id="bot-prevention"
Diminishing returns
Gathering route randomization
Server movement validation
Trade limits for new accounts
Mail limits for new accounts
Auction limits for new accounts
Suspicious transaction flags
Captcha-like world interactions only if absolutely necessary
Ban waves
Item/currency rollback tools
```

Do not punish normal players with heavy friction. Target suspicious patterns.

---

# 55. New Player Economy Protection

New players need protection.

Rules:

```text id="new-player-protection"
Starter repairs should be cheap.
Starter gear should be affordable.
Basic travel should be affordable.
Quest rewards should cover normal costs.
Early crafting should be simple.
New players should not need auction house to progress.
New players should not be easily scammed.
```

---

# 56. Endgame Economy

Endgame players need meaningful uses for wealth.

## Endgame Gold Sinks

```text id="endgame-sinks"
Legendary crafting
Housing mansions
Guild halls
Mount cosmetics
Rare transmog
High-end enchants
Raid consumables
Town projects
Faction investments
Siege warfare
Player shops
Luxury vendors
Prestige titles
Special effects
```

Important:

```text id="endgame-rule"
Endgame sinks should mostly be optional power refinement, convenience, cosmetics, guild projects, and prestige.
Do not make wealth the only path to power.
```

---

# 57. Economy and PvP

PvP can have its own economy.

## PvP Currency

```text id="pvp-currency"
Earned from:
Battlegrounds
Arena
Faction war
Bounty hunting
Objective captures

Used for:
PvP gear
Cosmetics
Mounts
Titles
Consumables
Siege gear
```

Rules:

```text id="pvp-economy-rules"
No rewards for repeatedly killing the same player.
No rewards for low-level griefing.
Objective play should pay better than farming kills.
PvP gear should not destroy PvE economy unless intended.
```

---

# 58. Siege and War Economy

War consumes resources.

## War Costs

```text id="war-costs"
Siege weapons
Wall repairs
Ammunition
Food supplies
Medical supplies
Armor repairs
Mercenary contracts
Fort upgrades
Transport
```

This creates large-scale material sinks.

---

# 59. Economy and Factions

Factions can control prices and access.

## Faction Effects

```text id="faction-economy"
High faction reputation:
Discounts
Special vendors
Unique recipes
Faction mounts
Lower taxes
Better contracts

Low faction reputation:
Higher prices
Blocked vendors
Restricted services
Higher taxes
Confiscation risk
```

---

# 60. Economic Consequences of World Events

World events should affect the economy.

Examples:

```text id="world-event-economy"
Dragon attacks farms:
Food prices rise.

Bandits control road:
Imported goods become expensive.

Players clear mine:
Ore supply increases, ore price falls.

War begins:
Weapon, armor, and potion demand rises.

Plague spreads:
Medicine prices rise, labor output drops.

Festival begins:
Luxury goods, food, and decorations rise in demand.
```

---

# 61. Economic Data Structures

## Item Economy Data

```json id="item-economy-data"
{
  "item_id": "iron_ore",
  "base_value": 8,
  "category": "crafting_material",
  "tier": 1,
  "stack_size": 200,
  "vendor_sellable": true,
  "auctionable": true,
  "tradeable": true,
  "regional_prices": {
    "harthmere": 8,
    "ironhold": 5,
    "warfront_city": 16
  },
  "demand_tags": ["blacksmithing", "engineering", "town_projects"],
  "supply_sources": ["mining", "vendor", "salvage"],
  "sink_uses": ["crafting", "repair", "guild_projects"]
}
```

---

## Vendor Data

```json id="vendor-data"
{
  "vendor_id": "npc_blacksmith_elda",
  "vendor_type": "blacksmith",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "sells_categories": ["weapons", "armor", "tools", "repair_kits"],

    "beloved": 0.85,

    "neutral": 1.0,

    "hated": 1.75
  }
}
```

```json id="auction-listing-data"
{
  "listing_id": "auction_901",

```

## Town Economy Data

```json id="town-economy-data"
{
  "town_id": "harthmere",
  "wealth": 42,
  "security": 65,
  "crime_rate": 18,
  "food_supply": 80,
  "ore_supply": 25,
  "medicine_supply": 40,
  "trade_route_status": {
    "north_road": "safe",
    "east_road": "bandit_threat",
    "river_route": "open"
  },
  "tax_rate": 0.08,
  "active_shortages": ["medicine"],
  "active_surpluses": ["grain"],
  "active_projects": ["repair_north_bridge"]
}
```

---

# 62. Economic Transaction Pipeline

Every economic transaction should follow this flow:

```text id="transaction-pipeline"
1. Player requests transaction.
2. Server validates player.
3. Server validates item/currency ownership.
4. Server validates price, tax, and permissions.
5. Server checks inventory/bank/storage space.
6. Server checks binding/trade/quest/stolen rules.
7. Server performs transaction atomically.
8. Server updates inventories/currency.
9. Server logs transaction.
10. Server sends result to client.
```

---

# 63. Economic Logs

Log all important transactions.

```text id="economic-logs"
Vendor buy/sell
Auction listing
Auction sale
Player trade
Mail attachment
Crafting
Salvage
Repair
Gold reward
Quest reward
Loot pickup
Guild bank withdrawal
Guild bank deposit
Housing purchase
Tax payment
Fine payment
Item upgrade
Currency conversion
```

Logs should include:

```text id="economic-log-fields"
Player ID
Account ID
Item ID
Item instance ID
Quantity
Currency amount
Source
Destination
Transaction type
Timestamp
Region
Related NPC/vendor
Related quest/event
Before/after state
```

---

# 64. Economic Edge Cases

## Player Buys Item But Inventory Is Full

Rule:

```text id="buy-inventory-full"
Block purchase or send item to overflow/mail if allowed.
Never delete the purchased item.
```

---

## Player Sells Item By Accident

Rule:

```text id="sell-accident"
Use buyback tab.
Locked items cannot be sold.
Rare+ items require confirmation.
Quest items cannot be sold.
```

---

## Auction Expires While Player Is Offline

Rule:

```text id="auction-expire"
Return item by mail or auction storage.
Notify player.
Do not delete item.
```

---

## Auction Sale Happens During Server Crash

Rule:

```text id="auction-crash"
Transaction must be atomic.
Either buyer gets item and seller gets gold, or transaction rolls back.
```

---

## Player Trades Then Disconnects

Rule:

```text id="trade-disconnect-economy"
Cancel trade.
Return items and gold to original owners.
```

---

## Item Price Updates During Vendor Transaction

Rule:

```text id="price-update-during-transaction"
Lock quoted price for a short time during open vendor interaction.
Recalculate after timeout or vendor refresh.
```

---

## Player Lists Bound Item

Rule:

```text id="bound-auction"
Block listing.
Show message explaining item is bound.
```

---

## Player Tries to Sell Stolen Goods to Lawful Vendor

Rule:

```text id="stolen-lawful-vendor"
Vendor refuses.
Possible guard alert depending on item and legal standing.
```

---

## Player Transfers Huge Gold to New Account

Rule:

```text id="huge-gold-transfer"
Allow if legitimate, but flag for review if suspicious.
Apply new-account trade limits if needed.
```

---

## Resource Node Is Farmed Too Hard

Rule:

```text id="node-overfarm"
Apply diminishing returns, spawn variation, or temporary depletion.
High-demand materials may shift to alternate sources.
```

---

## Market Manipulation

Rule:

```text id="market-manipulation"
Market manipulation is allowed to some extent in player economies.
But duping, botting, laundering, exploit pricing, or real-money trading is not allowed.
Track suspicious behavior.
```

---

## Negative Currency

Rule:

```text id="negative-currency"
Player currency cannot go below 0 unless debt systems exist.
All purchases must validate funds server-side.
```

---

## Tax Calculation Rounding

Rule:

```text id="tax-rounding"
Use consistent rounding rules.
Avoid exploitable tiny transactions that bypass tax.
Minimum tax may apply to auction listings.
```

---

# 65. Economic Balance Rules

Use these principles:

```text id="economic-balance-rules"
Currency sources must be balanced by currency sinks.
Item sources must be balanced by item sinks.
Crafting must consume meaningful materials.
Vendors must not pay more than market creation value.
Repeatable rewards need caps.
Low-level farming should not be profitable for high-level players.
Rare items need controlled supply.
Consumables create healthy recurring demand.
Luxury items create safe gold sinks.
Taxes remove gold without blocking trade.
Player trading must be safe and atomic.
```

---

# 66. Economic Health Checklist

Regularly check:

```text id="economic-health-checklist"
Is too much gold entering the world?
Are players hoarding gold with nothing to spend on?
Are repair costs fair?
Are basic goods affordable for new players?
Are bots controlling material prices?
Are crafted items useful?
Are dropped items making crafting useless?
Are auction taxes high enough?
Are auction taxes too high?
Are rare items too common?
Are materials too scarce?
Are players using vendors?
Are players using the auction house?
Are black markets useful but risky?
Are town economies reacting to world events?
```

---

# 67. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text id="economy-ai-prompt"
Design a complete economic system for an MMO.

The economy must support:
- Primary currency
- Secondary currencies
- Vendors
- Player trading
- Auction house
- Crafting
- Gathering
- Materials
- Repairs
- Durability
- Banking
- Guild banks
- Housing costs
- Taxes
- Trade routes
- Regional prices
- Supply and demand
- Black markets
- Stolen goods
- Fines
- Services
- Item upgrades
- Salvaging
- Anti-inflation systems
- Anti-dupe systems
- Anti-bot systems

The economy must have sources and sinks.

Currency sources include:
- Quest rewards
- Enemy loot
- Vendor sales
- Dungeons
- Raids
- Public events
- Crafting orders
- Trade routes
- Bounties
- Exploration

Currency sinks include:
- Repairs
- Vendor purchases
- Auction house fees
- Auction house taxes
- Fast travel
- Training
- Respec
- Housing
- Guild halls
- Crafting fees
- Bank upgrades
- Mount training
- Legal fines
- Transmog/cosmetics
- Item upgrades
- Town projects

Vendors must:
- Sell items above base value
- Buy items below base value
- Have role-based inventory
- Restock over time
- React to player reputation
- Refuse stolen or illegal goods if lawful
- Offer discounts to liked/trusted players
- Charge more or refuse hated/outlaw players

The auction house must:
- Hold listed items in escrow
- Charge listing fees
- Charge sale taxes
- Return expired listings
- Prevent bound/quest items from being listed
- Show unit price and total price clearly
- Support search, filters, and price history

Crafting must:
- Consume materials
- Create useful items
- Support professions
- Use recipes, tools, stations, and optional reagents
- Support quality differences
- Connect to gathering and regional resources
- Avoid making loot or vendors useless

Gathering must:
- Place resources logically in the world
- Use respawn rules
- Prevent bot farming
- Support rare materials
- Feed crafting and town projects

Regional economy must:
- Make local goods cheaper
- Make imported goods more expensive
- Use trade routes
- React to war, famine, bandits, weather, festivals, and player actions

Black markets must:
- Buy stolen goods
- Sell illegal goods
- Launder stolen items
- Require criminal contacts or discovery
- Carry legal risk
- Charge high cuts

The legal system must affect the economy:
- Fines remove gold
- Guards confiscate stolen goods
- Outlaws may lose lawful vendor access
- High legal standing grants better official contracts
- Bribes and black markets create alternate paths

The reputation system must affect the economy:
- High Likeability gives discounts and better buy prices
- Low Likeability increases prices or blocks service
- High Notoriety unlocks rare economic opportunities or fear-based reactions
- Criminal reputation unlocks fences and illegal services

The server must be authoritative:
- Never trust client item counts
- Never trust client gold amount
- Never trust client prices
- Every trade, auction, mail, crafting, vendor sale, repair, and reward must be validated server-side
- All economic transactions must be atomic
- Every important transaction must be logged

The system must handle edge cases:
- Inventory full during purchase
- Accidental sale
- Auction expires while offline
- Server crash during auction sale
- Player disconnects during trade
- Bound item listed for auction
- Stolen goods sold to lawful vendor
- Huge suspicious gold transfer
- Resource node overfarming
- Negative currency attempts
- Duplicate transaction requests
- Item duplication exploits
- Market manipulation
- Bot farming

The economy should feel alive, fair, player-driven, regionally varied, and connected to quests, NPCs, crafting, towns, factions, law, reputation, and world events.
```

---

# Final Economic System Rule

Every economic action should answer:

```text id="final-economy-rule"
Where did the money or item come from?
Where can it go?
Can it be traded?
Can it be sold?
Can it be crafted with?
Can it be stolen?
Can it be taxed?
Can it leave the economy?
Can it be exploited?
What does the server need to validate?
```

And every town economy should answer:

```text id="final-town-economy-rule"
What does this town produce?
What does this town need?
What is cheap here?
What is expensive here?
Who controls trade?
Who protects trade?
Who exploits trade?
What happens if trade is disrupted?
How can players change the economy?
```

That is how you make an MMO economy feel like a real world instead of just shops with random prices.

Below is a full **MMO Death and Respawn System** with rules for players, NPCs, creatures, bosses, PvP, quests, loot, penalties, edge cases, and server validation.

Core rule:

```text id="death-core-rule"
Death should matter, but it should not make the game frustrating, unfair, or easy to exploit.

A good MMO death system should:
- Clearly tell the player what happened
- Give fair recovery options
- Prevent spawn camping
- Prevent corpse griefing
- Handle disconnects and edge cases
- Support PvE, PvP, quests, bosses, NPCs, and world events
- Create consequences without making players want to quit
```

---

# 1. Death System Overview

Every entity in the game can have a death state.

This includes:

```text id="death-entities"
Players
NPCs
Guards
Merchants
Civilians
Animals
Creatures
Monsters
Bosses
Pets
Summons
Companions
Mounts
Quest NPCs
Faction leaders
```

When an entity reaches 0 HP, the system must decide:

```text id="death-questions"
Is the entity dead, downed, unconscious, wounded, captured, or destroyed?
Can it be revived?
Where does it respawn?
What penalties apply?
Who gets credit?
What loot drops?
What quests update?
What reputation changes?
What legal consequences happen?
What world state changes?
```

---

# 2. Player Death States

Player death does not have to mean immediate final death.

Use these states:

```text id="player-death-states"
Alive
Downed
Dead
Reviving
Respawning
Ghost / Spirit
Protected After Respawn
Captured
Unconscious
Permadead, only in hardcore modes
```

---

## Alive

Normal gameplay state.

```text id="alive-state"
Player can move, fight, talk, trade, loot, cast spells, use items, and interact normally.
```

---

## Downed

The player has reached 0 HP but is not fully dead yet.

Recommended for most MMOs.

```text id="downed-state"
When HP reaches 0, player enters Downed state for a short time.
The player cannot fight normally.
The player may crawl slowly, call for help, use limited abilities, or wait for revival.
Allies can revive the player.
Enemies may finish the player if rules allow it.
```

Recommended downed timer:

```text id="downed-timer"
PvE Downed Timer: 30 to 60 seconds
PvP Downed Timer: 15 to 30 seconds
Boss Fight Downed Timer: encounter-specific
```

---

## Dead

The player is fully dead.

```text id="dead-state"
Player cannot move, attack, cast normal spells, trade, loot, or interact normally.
Player can choose a respawn option.
Player may wait for resurrection if allowed.
```

---

## Ghost / Spirit

Optional system where dead players become ghosts.

```text id="ghost-state"
Player appears as a ghost or spirit.
Player can move to corpse, shrine, graveyard, or healer.
Player cannot fight normal enemies.
Player may see spirit-only NPCs or paths.
```

This works well for fantasy MMOs.

---

## Captured

Used when death should lead to prison, slavery, ransom, or story consequences.

```text id="captured-state"
Instead of dying, the player is captured by guards, bandits, monsters, or enemy factions.
Player wakes up in prison, camp, cage, dungeon, or hostile base.
Escape or rescue quest begins.
```

Good for:

```text id="captured-uses"
Being defeated by guards
Failing certain quests
Losing to bandits
Story missions
Hardcore survival events
PvP bounty capture
```

---

# 3. Recommended Player Death Flow

Default MMO flow:

```text id="player-death-flow"
1. Player HP reaches 0.
2. Server validates fatal damage.
3. Player enters Downed state.
4. If revived before timer ends, player returns with partial HP.
5. If not revived, player becomes Dead.
6. Player chooses respawn location.
7. Player respawns with temporary protection.
8. Death penalties apply.
9. Combat, quest, loot, and reputation consequences are processed.
```

---

# 4. Downed State Rules

## Downed Player Can

```text id="downed-can"
Call for help
Ping location to party
Crawl slowly, if allowed
Use limited downed abilities
Accept resurrection
Release spirit
View death recap
Chat with party/guild
```

## Downed Player Cannot

```text id="downed-cannot"
Use normal attacks
Use most spells
Trade
Loot
Open vendors
Fast travel
Mount
Start quests
Use normal consumables unless allowed
Change equipment
Enter safe zone protection by crawling into it
```

---

## Downed Abilities

Optional downed abilities:

```text id="downed-abilities"
Call for Help
Weak Strike
Bandage Self, slow and interruptible
Crawl
Play Dead
Last Stand, class-specific
Self-Revive, rare cooldown
```

Keep these weak. Downed state should create tension, not become a second health bar that always saves the player.

---

# 5. Revival System

Players can be revived by:

```text id="revive-sources"
Healer classes
Resurrection spells
Revival scrolls
Shrines
Spirit healers
Party members, if the game allows basic revive
NPC priests
Guild banners
Battlefield medics
Rare self-revive abilities
```

---

## Revival Rules

A revive should check:

```text id="revive-checks"
Target is dead or downed.
Target is eligible for revive.
Reviver is alive.
Reviver is close enough.
Reviver has line of sight, if required.
Reviver is not stunned, silenced, or interrupted.
Revive spell/item is not on cooldown.
Zone allows revival.
PvP rules allow revival.
Boss encounter rules allow revival.
```

---

## Revive Types

```text id="revive-types"
Quick Revive:
Used on downed players.
Short cast time.
Returns player with low HP.

Combat Resurrection:
Works during combat.
Limited cooldown.
Usually healer-only.

Out-of-Combat Resurrection:
Works after combat.
Longer cast time.
No major cooldown.

Shrine Resurrection:
Respawn at shrine.
Applies resurrection penalty.

Self-Revive:
Rare ability or item.
Long cooldown.
May apply penalty.
```

---

## Revive Health Rules

Example:

```text id="revive-health"
Basic revive: 20% HP, 10% mana/stamina
Healer revive: 40% HP, 30% mana/stamina
High-level resurrection: 70% HP, 50% mana/stamina
Shrine respawn: 50% HP, 50% mana/stamina
Self-revive: 30% HP, 0% mana/stamina
```

---

# 6. Respawn System

When a player fully dies, they need a respawn point.

Respawn options can include:

```text id="respawn-options"
Nearest graveyard
Nearest shrine
Bound home point
Last visited town
Dungeon entrance
Raid checkpoint
Faction camp
Guild hall
Player house
Spirit healer
PvP base
Safe zone
Checkpoint
```

---

## Respawn Location Rules

A respawn location must be:

```text id="respawn-location-valid"
Safe enough to prevent immediate death
On valid ground
Not inside a wall
Not inside another player or NPC
Not inside enemy AoE
Not inside locked geometry
Not inside a hostile spawn point
Connected to navigation
Appropriate to the player’s faction, quest phase, and zone
```

Bad respawn:

```text id="bad-respawn"
Player respawns inside wall.
Player respawns in lava.
Player respawns inside boss arena after wipe.
Player respawns directly under enemy attacks.
Player respawns in enemy faction base without PvP intent.
Player respawns at a point they have not unlocked.
```

Good respawn:

```text id="good-respawn"
Player respawns at a shrine outside the dungeon.
Player respawns at a town healer.
Player respawns at the latest checkpoint.
Player respawns at faction camp with guards nearby.
Player respawns with temporary protection.
```

---

# 7. Respawn Protection

Players need protection after respawning.

```text id="respawn-protection"
After respawn, player receives temporary protection.
Protection prevents immediate damage, crowd control, and targeting.
Protection ends early if the player attacks, casts hostile spells, captures objectives, or leaves the safe area.
```

Recommended duration:

```text id="respawn-protection-duration"
PvE respawn protection: 10 to 20 seconds
PvP respawn protection: 20 to 30 seconds
Dungeon respawn protection: 5 to 10 seconds
Town respawn protection: until leaving shrine area or 30 seconds
```

Protection should not allow abuse.

```text id="protection-abuse"
Protected players cannot attack.
Protected players cannot capture PvP objectives.
Protected players cannot body-block combat.
Protected players cannot scout invisible or immune forever.
Protected players lose protection when performing hostile actions.
```

---

# 8. Death Penalties

Death should have consequences, but avoid excessive punishment.

Recommended standard MMO penalties:

```text id="death-penalties-standard"
Equipment durability loss
Temporary resurrection sickness
Return to checkpoint/shrine
Lost time and positioning
Possible quest failure for timed/escort objectives
Possible reputation/legal consequences depending on cause
```

Avoid for normal MMO play:

```text id="death-penalties-avoid"
Huge XP loss
Permanent item loss
Permanent level loss
Long corpse runs with no option
Loss of rare gear from normal PvE death
Punishment so severe players stop exploring
```

---

## Durability Loss

Common penalty.

```text id="durability-loss"
On normal PvE death:
Equipped gear loses 5% to 10% durability.

On PvP death:
Equipped gear loses 0% to 5%, depending on design.

On boss wipe:
Equipped gear loses 5% durability.

On hardcore zone death:
Equipped gear may lose 10% to 20%.
```

At 0 durability:

```text id="broken-gear"
Item becomes broken.
Broken gear provides no stats or reduced stats.
Player must repair item at blacksmith, repair NPC, repair kit, or spell.
```

---

## Resurrection Sickness

Temporary debuff after respawning.

Example:

```text id="res-sickness"
Resurrection Sickness:
Duration: 2 to 5 minutes
Effects:
-10% damage dealt
-10% healing done
-10% max HP
Cannot enter ranked PvP while active
```

Use stronger sickness if the player chooses instant respawn far from corpse.

Do not apply harsh sickness if revived by another player.

---

## XP Loss

Generally not recommended for a modern standard MMO.

If used:

```text id="xp-loss-rules"
Only apply small XP debt, not direct level loss.
Never reduce player below current level unless hardcore mode.
Allow debt to be cleared by playing normally.
Do not apply XP loss in unfair deaths, disconnects, bugs, or PvP griefing.
```

Example:

```text id="xp-debt"
XP Debt:
Next 2,000 XP earned is reduced by 25%.
No level loss.
Debt clears over time or through healer service.
```

---

# 9. Corpse System

Decide whether the player leaves a corpse.

## Option A: No Corpse Run

```text id="no-corpse-run"
Player respawns at shrine.
Gear durability penalty applies.
No need to return to corpse.
```

Best for casual MMOs.

---

## Option B: Corpse Run

```text id="corpse-run"
Player becomes ghost.
Player must return to corpse to revive with reduced penalty.
Player can choose spirit healer for faster revival with stronger penalty.
```

Rules:

```text id="corpse-run-rules"
Corpse must be visible on map.
Corpse must not be unreachable.
If corpse is inside invalid geometry, move it to nearest safe location.
If corpse is inside dungeon after instance reset, move to entrance.
If corpse is in PvP danger zone, allow alternate respawn.
```

---

## Option C: Lootable Corpse

Use only in hardcore PvP/survival zones.

```text id="lootable-corpse"
Some carried items may drop on corpse.
Other players may loot eligible items.
Bound items do not drop.
Quest items do not drop.
Critical gear may be protected.
```

Use carefully. Full-loot systems create strong emotions and griefing risk.

---

# 10. PvE Death Rules

PvE deaths include deaths from:

```text id="pve-death-causes"
Monsters
Bosses
Traps
Falling
Lava
Poison
Drowning
Environmental hazards
Quest failures
World events
```

## PvE Death Flow

```text id="pve-death-flow"
Player reaches 0 HP.
Player enters Downed state if allowed.
Party may revive.
If not revived, player respawns at nearest shrine/checkpoint.
Durability penalty applies.
Quest state updates.
Enemy may reset or continue depending on encounter.
```

---

## Boss Wipe Rules

When all eligible party/raid members die:

```text id="boss-wipe"
Boss enters reset state.
Boss returns to arena.
Boss clears debuffs.
Boss restores HP unless encounter supports partial progress.
Adds despawn.
Arena resets.
Players respawn at checkpoint or entrance.
Loot is not awarded.
Durability loss applies.
```

Boss should not remain bugged after wipe.

---

## Dungeon Death Rules

Recommended:

```text id="dungeon-death"
If one player dies:
Party can revive them.

If party wipes:
Players respawn at dungeon entrance or last checkpoint.

Trash mobs:
May stay dead for a while.

Bosses:
Usually reset on wipe.

Dungeon progress:
Should persist long enough to prevent frustration.
```

---

# 11. PvP Death Rules

PvP death needs stricter handling.

PvP deaths can happen in:

```text id="pvp-contexts"
Duels
Arenas
Battlegrounds
Faction wars
Open-world PvP zones
Guild wars
Bounty hunts
Criminal encounters
Hardcore zones
```

---

## PvP Death Flow

```text id="pvp-death-flow"
Player reaches 0 HP.
Player enters Downed state if PvP mode supports it.
Enemy may finish player.
Kill credit is assigned.
Rewards and penalties are calculated.
Player respawns at faction base, graveyard, or safe point.
Respawn protection applies.
Spawn camping checks activate.
```

---

## PvP Kill Credit

Credit should consider:

```text id="pvp-credit"
Damage dealt
Healing support
Crowd control
Objective contribution
Recent participation
Group membership
Level difference
Repeat kill status
```

Do not reward only last hit.

---

## PvP Anti-Grief Rules

```text id="pvp-anti-grief"
No rewards for repeatedly killing the same player.
No rewards for killing players far below your level.
Spawn camping gives reduced rewards or penalties.
Safe-zone abuse is prevented.
Killing unflagged players creates legal/outlaw penalties if the system allows it.
```

Example repeat kill rule:

```text id="repeat-pvp-kill"
First kill: normal reward
Second kill within 15 minutes: 50% reward
Third kill within 15 minutes: 10% reward
Fourth+ kill: no reward and possible grief flag
```

---

## PvP Respawn Protection

```text id="pvp-respawn-protection"
Player respawns immune for 20 to 30 seconds.
Protection ends when player attacks, heals a combatant, captures an objective, or leaves protected area.
Enemy players cannot camp directly on respawn point.
Respawn areas should have guards, barriers, or multiple exits.
```

---

# 12. Criminal / Legal Death Rules

Death should connect to the legal system.

## If Player Dies While Wanted

Possible outcomes:

```text id="wanted-death"
Respawns normally but bounty remains
Guards capture player instead of killing
Player wakes in jail
Stolen goods are confiscated
Fine is applied
Bounty is reduced or cleared
Reputation changes
```

Recommended:

```text id="wanted-recommendation"
If guards defeat a wanted player, capture them instead of normal death.
```

Example:

```text id="guard-capture"
Player is defeated by city guards.
Player wakes in jail.
Stolen goods are confiscated.
Legal score improves slightly after serving sentence.
Likeability may remain damaged.
Escape quest becomes available.
```

---

## If Player Dies to Bounty Hunter

Options:

```text id="bounty-death"
Bounty hunter captures player.
Player pays fine.
Player goes to prison.
Bounty hunter receives reward.
Player loses some stolen goods.
Player's outlaw status changes based on region.
```

---

# 13. NPC Death System

NPCs need death rules too.

When an NPC reaches 0 HP:

```text id="npc-death-flow"
1. Stop AI behavior.
2. Stop movement.
3. Cancel attacks and casts.
4. Play death animation.
5. Remove from combat threat tables.
6. Assign kill credit.
7. Drop loot if eligible.
8. Update quests.
9. Apply reputation/legal changes.
10. Start corpse/despawn timer.
11. Start respawn timer if applicable.
```

---

# 14. NPC Death Categories

Not all NPC deaths should work the same way.

## Normal Enemy

```text id="normal-enemy-death"
Dies normally.
Drops loot.
Gives XP.
Respawns after timer.
```

## Animal

```text id="animal-death"
Dies normally.
May drop meat, hide, bones, feathers, claws.
Killing protected/domestic animals may cause legal or reputation penalty.
```

## Civilian

```text id="civilian-death"
May die, flee, or become wounded depending on design.
Killing civilians causes major Likeability and Legal penalties.
May trigger guards, witnesses, and bounty.
```

## Merchant

```text id="merchant-death"
May become unavailable temporarily.
Shop closes.
Guards respond.
Family/faction remembers.
Economy may be affected.
Respawn or replacement should be handled carefully.
```

## Guard

```text id="guard-death"
Major legal penalty.
Reinforcements respond.
Bounty increases.
Nearby civilians flee.
Guard faction becomes hostile.
```

## Quest NPC

```text id="quest-npc-death"
Can be essential, wounded, replaceable, or permanently dead depending on quest design.
Must not silently break quests.
```

## Boss

```text id="boss-death"
Drops special loot.
Gives major XP/notoriety.
Updates quest/world state.
Starts long respawn timer or instance lockout.
May trigger cutscene or world event.
```

---

# 15. Essential NPC Rules

Some NPCs are too important to permanently die.

Options:

```text id="essential-npc-options"
Essential:
Cannot die. Drops to 1 HP or becomes immune.

Wounded:
Can be defeated but not killed. Becomes unavailable temporarily.

Replaceable:
Dies, but replacement NPC appears.

Branching:
Dies and quest/story changes.

Permanent:
Dies permanently, only for systems designed around permanent world change.
```

Recommended MMO rule:

```text id="essential-recommendation"
Main quest NPCs should not permanently die in shared open-world spaces.
They may become wounded, captured, phased, or temporarily unavailable.
```

---

# 16. NPC Respawn Timers

NPC respawn time depends on importance.

```text id="npc-respawn-timers"
Critter: 15 to 60 seconds
Normal monster: 1 to 5 minutes
Elite monster: 5 to 15 minutes
Quest target: 30 seconds to 3 minutes, or personal spawn
Rare enemy: 30 minutes to several hours
Dungeon boss: instance reset or lockout
World boss: hours to days
Civilian: respawn through schedule/system, not instant
Guard: 2 to 10 minutes, or reinforcements
Merchant: replacement or recovery after town state update
```

---

## Respawn Validation

Before respawning an NPC:

```text id="npc-respawn-validation"
Check spawn point is valid.
Check ground exists.
Check navmesh exists if needed.
Check no wall/prop overlap.
Check no player is standing inside spawn area.
Check world event allows respawn.
Check quest phase allows respawn.
Check population cap.
```

If invalid:

```text id="npc-respawn-invalid"
Try nearby fallback spawn.
Delay respawn.
Use alternate spawn point.
Log placement error.
```

---

# 17. Spawn Camping Prevention

Players should not camp respawns for easy farming.

Rules:

```text id="spawn-camping-prevention"
Use multiple spawn points.
Use variable respawn timers.
Require meaningful contribution.
Reduce rewards for repeated kills of the same spawn.
Add patrols or guards near player respawns.
Give respawn protection to players.
Prevent enemy AoE from covering respawn points.
```

For PvE farming:

```text id="pve-farm-diminishing"
Killing the same enemy group repeatedly may reduce XP/loot after excessive farming.
Rare drops should have anti-bot checks.
```

---

# 18. Death Recap System

Players should know why they died.

Death recap should show:

```text id="death-recap"
Killer name
Damage sources
Last 5 to 10 major hits
Critical hits
Debuffs active
Environmental damage
Healing received
Blocked/absorbed damage
Time from first hit to death
Suggested cause
Optional tip
```

Example:

```text id="death-recap-example"
You were killed by Forest Troll.

Final damage:
- Forest Troll Smash: 420 physical
- Bleed: 36
- Fall Damage: 55

Tip:
Troll Smash can be dodged when the troll raises both arms.
```

This reduces frustration.

---

# 19. Quest Death Rules

Death can affect quests.

## Normal Quest

```text id="normal-quest-death"
Death does not fail quest.
Progress remains.
Player respawns and continues.
```

## Escort Quest

```text id="escort-death"
If player dies and escort NPC is left defenseless, quest may fail.
If party member survives and protects escort, quest continues.
Escort NPC may flee to safe point.
```

## Timed Quest

```text id="timed-quest-death"
Timer may continue during death.
Player can respawn and continue if time remains.
Quest fails if timer expires.
```

## Stealth Quest

```text id="stealth-death"
Death may fail quest.
Capture may create alternate path.
Alarm state may persist.
```

## Hardcore Story Quest

```text id="hardcore-story-death"
Death may branch the quest instead of simple failure.
Player wakes captured, wounded, or rescued.
```

---

# 20. Death and Reputation

Death can affect reputation depending on context.

## If Player Dies Defending Town

```text id="death-defending-town"
Likeability may increase.
Notoriety may increase if public.
Quest may still progress if contribution was meaningful.
```

## If Player Dies While Committing Crime

```text id="death-while-crime"
Legal penalty remains.
Stolen goods may be confiscated.
Bounty may remain or change.
Witnesses still remember.
```

## If Player Kills NPC Before Dying

```text id="kill-before-death"
NPC death still counts.
Legal/reputation consequences still apply.
Player death does not erase crime.
```

## If Player Is Killed by Guards

```text id="killed-by-guards"
May become captured instead of dead.
May lose stolen goods.
May receive jail state.
```

---

# 21. Death and Loot

Default MMO rule:

```text id="death-and-loot-default"
Normal PvE death should not drop player inventory.
```

Possible loot consequences:

```text id="death-loot-options"
Durability loss
Lost temporary buffs
Lost summoned items
Dropped stolen goods
Dropped trade packs
Dropped PvP objective items
Dropped hardcore-zone loot
Corpse loot in survival zones
```

---

## Protected Items

These should usually not drop:

```text id="protected-items"
Bound equipment
Quest items
Spellbook entries
Mounts
Pets
Cosmetics
Currencies in wallet, unless hardcore rules
Keyring items
Main story items
Account-bound items
```

---

## Droppable Items

These may drop in special rulesets:

```text id="droppable-items"
Stolen goods
Trade packs
Gathered resources
Unbound loot
PvP objective flags
Hardcore-zone treasure
Smuggled contraband
Temporary event items
```

---

# 22. Death and Buffs/Debuffs

On death, decide what happens.

## Usually Removed

```text id="death-removes"
Poisons
Bleeds
Burns
Combat debuffs
Temporary crowd control
Short combat buffs
Threat/aggro
```

## Usually Preserved

```text id="death-preserves"
Long-term food buff, optional
Rested XP
Quest states
Reputation
Cooldowns
Durability damage
Long-term curses, if story-based
PvP deserter debuff
```

## Rule

```text id="death-buff-rule"
Death should clear normal combat effects but preserve meaningful long-term consequences.
```

---

# 23. Pets, Summons, and Companions

## Pet Death

Options:

```text id="pet-death-options"
Pet is defeated and can be revived.
Pet despawns and can be resummoned after cooldown.
Pet runs away and returns later.
Pet permanently dies only in hardcore systems.
```

Recommended:

```text id="pet-death-recommended"
Combat pets become defeated.
Owner can revive them after combat.
If owner dies, pet despawns or becomes passive.
```

---

## Summon Death

```text id="summon-death"
Summons disappear when killed.
Summons disappear when duration ends.
Summons disappear when owner dies, logs out, or leaves zone.
```

---

## Companion Death

Companion NPCs should usually be downed, not permanently killed.

```text id="companion-death"
Companion reaches 0 HP.
Companion becomes downed.
Player can revive after combat.
If party wipes, companion returns at respawn point.
Major story companions should not vanish without explanation.
```

---

# 24. Environmental Death Rules

Players can die from the environment.

## Fall Damage

```text id="fall-damage"
Small falls: no damage
Medium falls: partial damage
Large falls: lethal damage
Certain classes/items reduce fall damage
Landing in water may reduce or change damage
```

Edge rule:

```text id="fall-edge"
Do not apply fall damage during loading, teleporting, cutscenes, or server correction.
```

---

## Drowning

```text id="drowning"
Player has breath meter underwater.
When breath reaches 0, player takes periodic damage.
Water-breathing spells/items prevent drowning.
Respawn should place player on land or safe water surface.
```

---

## Lava / Acid / Hazard Zones

```text id="hazard-death"
Hazards deal periodic damage.
Hazard damage should be clearly communicated.
Respawn must not place player back inside hazard.
```

---

## Out-of-Bounds Death

```text id="out-of-bounds"
If player falls out of world, teleport to last safe location or kill and respawn safely.
Do not trap player in infinite falling.
Log the location as a bug.
```

Recommended:

```text id="oob-recommendation"
Teleport to last safe ground if possible.
Use death only if fall was legitimate gameplay.
```

---

# 25. Safe Zone Death Rules

Safe zones should prevent most combat deaths.

Safe zones include:

```text id="safe-zones"
Town centers
Inns
Temples
Banks
Player housing
Respawn shrines
Protected quest hubs
Starter areas
```

Rules:

```text id="safe-zone-rules"
Players cannot be attacked by other players in safe zones.
Hostile NPCs should not enter safe zones unless event allows it.
Players cannot attack outward from safe zones.
Recent aggressors may be denied safe-zone protection briefly.
```

---

# 26. Aggression Timer

Prevents safe-zone abuse.

```text id="aggression-timer"
If player attacks another player/NPC unlawfully, they receive aggression timer.
During aggression timer, they cannot gain full safe-zone protection.
```

Recommended timer:

```text id="aggression-timer-duration"
PvE aggression: 10 to 20 seconds
PvP aggression: 30 to 60 seconds
Criminal aggression: 60 to 180 seconds
```

---

# 27. Disconnect During Death or Combat

Disconnect rules must be strict.

## Disconnect While Alive and Out of Combat

```text id="disconnect-safe"
Character leaves world after short logout timer.
```

## Disconnect While In Combat

```text id="disconnect-combat"
Character remains in world for combat logout timer.
Can still be killed.
Death is valid.
```

Recommended:

```text id="combat-logout-timer"
PvE combat logout: 30 seconds
PvP combat logout: 60 seconds
Boss encounter logout: until wipe, death, or timeout
```

## Disconnect While Downed

```text id="disconnect-downed"
Player remains downed until timer expires.
If revived, returns alive when reconnecting.
If timer expires, becomes dead.
```

## Disconnect While Dead

```text id="disconnect-dead"
Death state persists.
Player chooses respawn after reconnecting.
```

---

# 28. Server Crash / Unfair Death Handling

Some deaths are not the player's fault.

Unfair death causes:

```text id="unfair-death-causes"
Server crash
Zone crash
Known bug
Invalid spawn
Falling through world
Loading screen damage
Teleport bug
NPC attacking through wall
Desync causing impossible damage
```

Rules:

```text id="unfair-death-rules"
Do not apply harsh penalty.
Restore player to safe location.
Refund durability if appropriate.
Restore lost items if caused by bug.
Log death reason for review.
```

---

# 29. Death During Loading Screen

```text id="loading-death"
Players cannot take damage while loading.
After loading, player receives short protection.
Protection ends when player moves, attacks, or after timer.
```

This is mandatory. Death during loading feels terrible.

---

# 30. Death During Teleport

```text id="teleport-death"
While teleporting, player is untargetable.
If teleport fails, return player to last safe position.
Do not leave player in half-loaded combat state.
```

---

# 31. Death During Cutscene

```text id="cutscene-death"
Players should not take normal gameplay damage during non-interactive cutscenes.
If the cutscene is meant to be dangerous, communicate it clearly and allow player control.
```

---

# 32. Death in Instanced Content

Instances need special handling.

## Dungeon Instance

```text id="dungeon-instance-death"
Dead players can be revived by party.
If all players die, party respawns at checkpoint.
Boss resets.
Trash may or may not reset depending on dungeon rules.
```

## Raid Instance

```text id="raid-death"
Combat resurrection is limited.
Dead players may watch until boss wipe or kill.
Boss death may grant credit if player contributed enough before dying.
```

## Solo Story Instance

```text id="solo-instance-death"
Player respawns at last checkpoint.
Temporary quest objects reset.
Enemies may reset.
Story state restores to checkpoint.
```

---

# 33. Checkpoint System

Checkpoints reduce frustration.

Checkpoint locations:

```text id="checkpoint-locations"
Dungeon entrance
After major boss
Before boss arena
After puzzle section
Before dangerous quest event
Inside long story instance
Near world event staging area
```

Rules:

```text id="checkpoint-rules"
Checkpoint must be safe.
Checkpoint must be valid ground.
Checkpoint should not place player behind locked progression.
Checkpoint should preserve completed objectives.
Checkpoint should not duplicate loot or rewards.
```

---

# 34. Hardcore Death System

Optional mode.

Hardcore death may include:

```text id="hardcore-death"
Permanent character death
Dropped inventory
Loss of carried gold
Character becomes ghost
Character becomes retired
Death creates grave marker
Death becomes server history
```

Use only if clearly labeled.

Rules:

```text id="hardcore-rules"
Player must opt into hardcore.
Warn clearly.
Never apply hardcore death due to server bug or loading issue.
Store death recap permanently.
Allow memorialization.
```

---

# 35. Death and World Events

In public events:

```text id="world-event-death"
Death does not erase contribution.
Player can respawn nearby if event allows.
Contribution decays if player does not return.
Rewards depend on meaningful participation.
```

Example:

```text id="world-event-example"
Player helps defend town, dies near end, and respawns.
If contribution was meaningful, player still receives event reward.
```

---

# 36. Death and Kill Credit

Death should not automatically remove credit.

Rules:

```text id="death-credit"
If player contributed meaningfully before dying, they may receive credit.
If player dies early and does not return, contribution may decay.
If player is dead when boss dies but contributed enough, they may receive partial or full credit depending on rules.
```

Recommended:

```text id="death-credit-recommendation"
For bosses and public events, grant credit if the player contributed enough and remained in the encounter/zone.
```

---

# 37. Death and Threat / Aggro

When a player dies:

```text id="death-threat"
Remove player from active threat table.
NPC may switch to next highest threat.
If all threats are dead or unreachable, NPC resets.
Pets/summons may inherit or drop threat depending on rules.
```

When revived:

```text id="revive-threat"
Player should not instantly regain full threat unless a mechanic says so.
Revived player starts with low or reduced threat.
```

---

# 38. Death and NPC Memory

NPCs should remember meaningful deaths and kills.

Examples:

```text id="npc-memory-death"
Player kills merchant, then dies to guards:
Merchant death still happened.
Town remembers.
Legal penalty remains.

Player is defeated by bandits:
Bandits may mock player later.
A revenge quest may unlock.

Player dies saving village:
Villagers may honor player.
Likeability may increase.
```

---

# 39. Death and Notoriety

Death can affect notoriety.

```text id="death-notoriety"
Dying normally to a wolf: no notoriety.
Dying heroically against a world boss: small notoriety.
Killing a major boss and dying in process: major notoriety still possible.
Being publicly executed: notoriety may increase.
Being defeated by famous rival: notoriety may shift.
```

Death can be part of the story, not just a penalty.

---

# 40. Respawn UI

When dead, the UI should show:

```text id="respawn-ui"
Death recap
Killer / cause of death
Respawn options
Distance to corpse, if applicable
Revive availability
Party revive status
Penalty information
Timer until forced respawn, if any
Map of respawn locations
Warning for dangerous respawn
```

Example:

```text id="respawn-ui-example"
You died.

Cause:
Killed by Bandit Captain.

Options:
- Wait for revive
- Respawn at North Shrine, 180m away
- Respawn at Harthmere Temple, 600m away
- Release Spirit

Penalty:
5% durability loss
No resurrection sickness if revived by ally
```

---

# 41. Respawn Map Rules

The respawn map should show:

```text id="respawn-map"
Available respawn points
Unavailable respawn points
Distance
Danger level
Faction control
Corpse location
Party members
Quest objective location
Dungeon checkpoint
```

Unavailable reasons:

```text id="respawn-unavailable"
Wrong faction
Not discovered
Under enemy control
Blocked by event
Inside locked instance
Too close to active boss
Disabled by PvP objective
```

---

# 42. Forced Respawn

Sometimes players should not stay dead forever.

```text id="forced-respawn"
After a long time dead, player may be automatically respawned.
Do not force respawn during active party revive unless rules require it.
```

Recommended:

```text id="forced-respawn-timer"
Open world: optional, 5 to 10 minutes
PvP battleground: 10 to 30 seconds wave respawn
Dungeon/raid: encounter-specific
```

---

# 43. Wave Respawns

Useful for PvP and large events.

```text id="wave-respawn"
Dead players respawn in groups every X seconds.
Encourages team play.
Prevents constant trickle deaths.
```

Example:

```text id="pvp-wave-respawn"
Battleground wave respawn:
Every 20 seconds at faction graveyard.
```

---

# 44. Death Data Structure

Player death record:

```json id="player-death-record"
{
  "death_id": "death_12345",
  "player_id": "player_001",
  "state": "dead",
  "zone": "harthmere_north_fields",
  "position": [120.5, 0.0, -84.2],
  "cause": "npc_attack",
  "killer_type": "npc",
  "killer_id": "npc_bandit_captain_01",
  "damage_summary": [
    {
      "source": "Bandit Captain",
      "ability": "Heavy Slash",
      "damage": 312,
      "type": "slashing"
    },
    {
      "source": "Bleed",
      "ability": "Deep Wound",
      "damage": 48,
      "type": "bleed"
    }
  ],
  "durability_loss_percent": 5,
  "xp_debt": 0,
  "corpse_position": [120.5, 0.0, -84.2],
  "available_respawns": [
    "north_field_shrine",
    "harthmere_temple"
  ],
  "created_at": "2026-05-12T22:15:00Z"
}
```

---

# 45. NPC Respawn Data Structure

```json id="npc-respawn-record"
{
  "npc_id": "npc_forest_wolf_001",
  "spawn_group": "north_forest_wolves",
  "state": "dead",
  "death_time": "2026-05-12T22:15:00Z",
  "respawn_time": "2026-05-12T22:18:00Z",
  "spawn_point": "wolf_spawn_03",
  "fallback_spawn_points": [
    "wolf_spawn_04",
    "wolf_spawn_05"
  ],
  "respawn_rules": {
    "requires_valid_navmesh": true,
    "requires_no_player_overlap": true,
    "population_cap": 8,
    "event_required": null
  }
}
```

---

# 46. Server Death Pipeline

When damage may kill a player:

```text id="server-death-pipeline"
1. Server receives damage event.
2. Server validates attacker, target, ability, range, line of sight, and damage.
3. Server applies damage.
4. If HP > 0, continue combat.
5. If HP <= 0, clamp HP to 0.
6. Determine death state: downed, dead, captured, or special.
7. Cancel invalid actions.
8. Remove or update threat.
9. Create death record.
10. Notify client.
11. Apply penalties.
12. Update quests, reputation, legal state, and combat logs.
13. Start revive/respawn timers.
```

---

# 47. Server Respawn Pipeline

When player respawns:

```text id="server-respawn-pipeline"
1. Player selects respawn option.
2. Server validates respawn point.
3. Server checks faction, phase, zone, safety, and availability.
4. Server removes death state.
5. Server teleports player to respawn point.
6. Server restores HP/resources based on respawn type.
7. Server applies resurrection sickness if needed.
8. Server applies respawn protection.
9. Server updates client.
10. Server logs respawn.
```

---

# 48. Death Edge Cases

## Player Dies Twice From Duplicate Damage

Rule:

```text id="duplicate-death"
Once player is downed or dead, ignore duplicate fatal damage unless it is a valid finishing action.
Use death state lock to prevent double penalties.
```

---

## Player Is Revived At Same Time As Forced Respawn

Rule:

```text id="revive-respawn-race"
Server processes the first valid state transition.
If revive succeeds first, cancel respawn.
If respawn succeeds first, cancel revive.
Never duplicate player or apply both.
```

---

## Player Dies While Trading

Rule:

```text id="death-while-trading"
Cancel trade.
Return all items to original owners.
Then process death.
```

---

## Player Dies While Crafting

Rule:

```text id="death-while-crafting"
Cancel crafting if not completed.
If crafting transaction already completed, keep result.
Use atomic operations to prevent item loss or duplication.
```

---

## Player Dies While Looting

Rule:

```text id="death-while-looting"
If loot was already claimed server-side, player keeps it.
If not claimed, loot remains.
Close loot window.
```

---

## Player Dies While In Dialogue

Rule:

```text id="death-while-dialogue"
Close dialogue.
Process death.
NPC may react to the death if appropriate.
```

---

## Player Dies While Mounted

Rule:

```text id="death-mounted"
Dismount player.
Mount despawns or flees.
Apply death state.
```

---

## Player Dies While Carrying Quest Object

Rule:

```text id="death-carrying-object"
If object is normal quest item, keep it.
If object is physical carried object, drop it at death location or reset it to spawn.
If object is PvP objective, drop it for others to capture.
```

---

## Player Dies in Water

Rule:

```text id="death-water"
Corpse/respawn marker should be accessible.
If underwater corpse run is impossible, move corpse to nearest shore or safe underwater point.
Respawn should not place player underwater unless they can breathe.
```

---

## Player Dies in Lava

Rule:

```text id="death-lava"
Corpse should move to nearest safe edge.
Do not force player to retrieve corpse from lethal hazard unless hardcore design.
```

---

## Player Dies Inside Wall Due to Bug

Rule:

```text id="death-inside-wall"
Move corpse/respawn to nearest valid safe point.
Do not apply harsh penalties.
Log bug.
```

---

## Player Dies During Boss Kill

Rule:

```text id="death-during-boss-kill"
If player contributed enough and remained eligible, grant credit.
If player released/left before kill, apply encounter rules.
```

---

## NPC Dies Before Quest Player Tags It

Rule:

```text id="quest-target-dies-before-tag"
If NPC is public quest target, allow shared credit.
If private quest target, respawn quickly or use personal spawn.
Avoid making players wait too long.
```

---

## Respawn Point Is Camped

Rule:

```text id="camped-respawn"
Offer alternate respawn points.
Increase protection.
Add guards or barriers.
Suppress enemy rewards for spawn kills.
```

---

# 49. Death Messages

Use clear messages.

Examples:

```text id="death-messages"
You were slain by Forest Wolf.
You were defeated by City Guard and taken to jail.
You drowned.
You fell to your death.
You were killed by another player: Ravax.
You died from burning.
You were crushed by falling rocks.
You were executed for your crimes.
You were defeated, but not killed.
```

---

# 50. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text id="death-ai-prompt"
Design a complete death and respawn system for an MMO.

The system must support:
- Player death
- Downed state
- Revival
- Respawn
- Corpse handling
- NPC death
- NPC respawn
- Boss death
- PvP death
- PvE death
- Quest failure from death
- Environmental death
- Legal/criminal death consequences
- Respawn protection
- Death penalties
- Death recap
- Server-authoritative validation

When a player reaches 0 HP:
1. Server validates fatal damage.
2. Player enters Downed state if allowed.
3. Allies may revive the player.
4. If not revived, player becomes Dead.
5. Player chooses respawn location.
6. Server validates respawn point.
7. Player respawns with partial HP/resources.
8. Temporary respawn protection applies.
9. Penalties and quest/reputation/legal consequences are processed.

Death states must include:
- Alive
- Downed
- Dead
- Reviving
- Respawning
- Ghost/Spirit
- Protected After Respawn
- Captured
- Unconscious

Respawn locations must be:
- On valid ground
- Outside walls
- Outside hazards
- Not inside NPCs/players
- Not under enemy AoE
- Connected to navigation
- Safe enough to prevent instant death
- Valid for player faction, quest phase, and zone

Death penalties may include:
- Durability loss
- Temporary resurrection sickness
- Return to checkpoint
- Lost temporary buffs
- Quest failure for escort/timed missions
- Legal penalties if death happened during crime

Avoid harsh penalties in normal MMO play:
- No major XP loss
- No permanent level loss
- No permanent gear loss
- No unreachable corpse runs
- No death during loading screens

NPC death must:
- Stop AI
- Stop movement
- Cancel combat actions
- Play death animation
- Remove from threat tables
- Assign kill credit
- Drop loot if eligible
- Update quests
- Apply reputation/legal changes
- Start corpse/despawn timer
- Start respawn timer if applicable

NPC respawn must validate:
- Ground
- Navmesh
- Collision
- Spawn population cap
- Player overlap
- Event/phase state
- Fallback spawn points

Boss death must:
- Assign contribution-based credit
- Drop loot
- Update quests
- Increase notoriety if appropriate
- Trigger lockout or respawn timer
- Trigger world state changes if appropriate

Boss wipe must:
- Reset boss HP
- Clear debuffs
- Despawn adds
- Return boss to arena
- Respawn players at checkpoint
- Avoid bugged combat state

PvP death must:
- Assign kill credit by contribution, not only last hit
- Prevent spawn camping
- Prevent repeated-kill farming
- Prevent low-level grief rewards
- Apply legal/outlaw penalties if unlawful
- Use safe respawn points and protection

The system must handle edge cases:
- Duplicate fatal damage
- Revive and respawn happening at same time
- Player disconnects while downed
- Player disconnects during combat
- Player dies during loading screen
- Player dies during teleport
- Player dies inside wall
- Player dies in lava/water
- Player dies while trading/crafting/looting
- Player dies during boss kill
- Respawn point is camped
- Corpse is unreachable
- Quest NPC dies
- Boss resets incorrectly
- NPC respawns inside wall
- Server crash causes unfair death

The server must be authoritative:
- Client cannot decide death
- Client cannot decide revive success
- Client cannot decide respawn location validity
- Client cannot skip penalties
- Client cannot fake kill credit
- Client cannot duplicate rewards from death/respawn race conditions

The system should make death meaningful, clear, fair, recoverable, and hard to exploit.
```

---

# Final Death and Respawn Rule

Every death should answer:

```text id="final-death-rule"
Who died?
Why did they die?
Who caused it?
Can they be revived?
Where can they respawn?
What penalty applies?
What rewards or credit are given?
What quests changed?
What reputation or legal consequences happened?
What prevents this from being exploited?
```

And every respawn should answer:

```text id="final-respawn-rule"
Is this location valid?
Is it safe?
Is it accessible?
Is it fair?
Does it prevent spawn camping?
Does it preserve the correct quest, faction, dungeon, PvP, and world state?
```

That is how you make death feel like a real MMO system instead of just a reset button.


Below is a full **MMO Level, Experience, Attribute, and Scaling System**.

The core rule:

```text
Levels should make players and NPCs stronger, but not so much stronger that old zones become meaningless, low-level players become useless, or balance breaks.
```

A good MMO level system should control:

```text
Experience gain
Level progression
Player stats
NPC stats
Damage scaling
Defense scaling
Quest rewards
Combat difficulty
World zones
Gear requirements
Ability unlocks
Attribute effects
Party scaling
PvP fairness
Anti-farming rules
```

---

# 1. Level System Overview

Every player and NPC should have a level.

```text
Player Level:
Represents the player’s overall growth, combat power, access to content, and progression.

NPC Level:
Represents the NPC’s danger, durability, combat strength, and expected player challenge.
```

Level affects:

```text
Max HP
Attack Points
Defense
Armor
Magic Resistance
Accuracy
Evasion
Critical Chance
Resource pools
Damage dealt
Damage received
Quest eligibility
Item eligibility
Ability unlocks
NPC aggression
Loot quality
XP rewards
World difficulty
```

---

# 2. Recommended Level Range

For an MMO, use a clear level cap.

Example:

```text
Starting Level: 1
Soft Cap: 50
Expansion Cap: 60, 70, 80, etc.
NPC Level Range: 1 to 100+
Boss Level Range: Player cap + 3 to Player cap + 20
```

Recommended base:

```text
Player Level Cap: 50
Normal NPC Max Level: 50
Elite NPC Max Level: 55
Dungeon Boss Max Level: 60
World Boss Max Level: 70+
```

---

# 3. Player Level Progression

Players gain **Experience Points**, also called XP.

Players gain XP from:

```text
Completing quests
Killing enemies
Exploring new locations
Completing dungeons
Defeating bosses
Crafting
Gathering
PvP objectives
Public events
Daily/weekly missions
Discovering secrets
Helping other players
Reputation milestones
```

Players should not gain meaningful XP from:

```text
Killing harmless civilians
Killing very low-level enemies
Repeatedly farming the same exploit target
Killing the same player repeatedly
Completing fake/abandoned loops
AFK participation
```

---

# 4. XP Required Per Level

XP required should increase each level.

Simple formula:

```text
XP Required for Next Level = Base XP × Level ^ Growth Rate
```

Recommended:

```text
Base XP = 100
Growth Rate = 1.6
```

Example table:

| Level | XP Needed for Next Level |
| ----: | -----------------------: |
|     1 |                      100 |
|     2 |                      300 |
|     3 |                      600 |
|     4 |                      900 |
|     5 |                    1,300 |
|    10 |                    4,000 |
|    20 |                   12,000 |
|    30 |                   25,000 |
|    40 |                   45,000 |
|    50 |                      Cap |

For smoother control, use a designed table instead of pure formula.

```text
Early levels should be fast.
Middle levels should feel steady.
Late levels should require commitment.
Max level should shift progression toward gear, skills, reputation, crafting, PvP, and world content.
```

---

# 5. XP Reward Rules

## Quest XP

Quest XP should scale by quest level and difficulty.

```text
Quest XP = Base Quest XP × Quest Level Modifier × Difficulty Modifier
```

Example difficulty modifiers:

```text
Trivial: x0.25
Easy: x0.75
Normal: x1.0
Hard: x1.25
Elite: x1.75
Group: x2.0
Dungeon: x2.5
Raid: x5.0
```

Example:

```text
Level 10 normal quest:
Base XP: 1,000
Difficulty: Normal x1.0
Reward: 1,000 XP

Level 10 elite quest:
Base XP: 1,000
Difficulty: Elite x1.75
Reward: 1,750 XP
```

---

## Enemy Kill XP

Enemy kill XP should depend on enemy level compared to player level.

```text
Enemy XP = Base Enemy XP × Level Difference Modifier × Rank Modifier
```

NPC rank modifiers:

```text
Civilian: x0 or very low
Critter: x0.05
Normal Enemy: x1.0
Strong Enemy: x1.3
Elite Enemy: x2.5
Mini-Boss: x8
Dungeon Boss: x25
World Boss: x100+
```

---

## Level Difference XP Rules

| Enemy Compared to Player |            XP Modifier |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------: |
| 10+ levels lower         |                   0 XP |
| 6–9 levels lower         |                  x0.10 |
| 3–5 levels lower         |                  x0.40 |
| 1–2 levels lower         |                  x0.75 |
| Same level               |                   x1.0 |
| 1–2 levels higher        |                  x1.15 |
| 3–5 levels higher        |                   x1.4 |
| 6+ levels higher         | x1.75, but high danger |

This prevents farming weak enemies forever.

---

# 6. Player Attributes

Every player should have core attributes.

Recommended attributes:

```text
Strength
Dexterity
Intelligence
Wisdom
Constitution
Charisma
Perception
Willpower
Luck
```

These attributes should affect both combat and the world.

---

# 7. Attribute Definitions

## Strength

Strength affects physical power.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases melee Attack Points
Increases heavy weapon damage
Increases block power
Increases carrying capacity
Improves shove, knockback, and grapple resistance
```

World effects:

```text
Move heavy objects
Break weak doors
Lift gates
Carry more items
Win intimidation checks
Use heavy armor/weapons effectively
Help NPCs with labor tasks
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Strength = +2 melee Attack Points
+1 Strength = +0.5 carrying capacity
+10 Strength = unlock heavy object interactions
```

---

## Dexterity

Dexterity affects speed, precision, and agility.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases ranged Attack Points
Increases evasion
Increases attack speed slightly
Increases critical chance
Improves stealth movement
Improves lockpicking and trap disarming
```

World effects:

```text
Pick locks
Disarm traps
Climb better
Sneak past guards
Win agility checks
Perform acrobatics
Pickpocket more successfully
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Dexterity = +1 ranged Attack Point
+1 Dexterity = +0.2% evasion
+10 Dexterity = unlock advanced lockpicking tiers
```

---

## Intelligence

Intelligence affects magic power and knowledge.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases spell Attack Points
Increases mana
Improves spell critical chance
Improves magic accuracy
Improves magical crafting
```

World effects:

```text
Read ancient languages
Solve puzzles
Identify magical items
Understand lore
Unlock scholar dialogue
Craft advanced magical items
Use complex artifacts
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Intelligence = +2 spell power
+1 Intelligence = +5 mana
+10 Intelligence = unlock advanced magic interactions
```

---

## Wisdom

Wisdom affects healing, resistance, insight, and spiritual power.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases healing power
Increases magic resistance
Improves mana regeneration
Improves cleanse effects
Improves resistance against fear, charm, and illusion
```

World effects:

```text
Detect lies
Understand spiritual visions
Calm angry NPCs
Interpret omens
Make better moral judgments
Negotiate peaceful outcomes
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Wisdom = +1.5 healing power
+1 Wisdom = +0.2% magic resistance
+1 Wisdom = +0.5 mana regeneration
```

---

## Constitution

Constitution affects survival.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases Max HP
Increases stamina
Increases poison resistance
Increases disease resistance
Reduces bleed duration
Improves survival under harsh conditions
```

World effects:

```text
Survive cold, heat, poison, disease, hunger, exhaustion
Work longer
Swim longer
Resist environmental hazards
Carry injuries better
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Constitution = +10 Max HP
+1 Constitution = +3 stamina
+10 Constitution = reduced environmental damage
```

---

## Charisma

Charisma affects social influence.

Combat effects:

```text
Improves companion morale
Improves summoned ally effectiveness
Improves group buff strength
May reduce enemy aggression through intimidation/persuasion
```

World effects:

```text
Better prices
Better persuasion
Better intimidation
Better diplomacy
More quest options
Easier recruitment
Better companion loyalty
More favorable NPC reactions
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Charisma = +0.2% vendor discount, capped
+5 Charisma = unlock minor persuasion choices
+15 Charisma = unlock major diplomatic choices
```

---

## Perception

Perception affects awareness.

Combat effects:

```text
Increases ranged accuracy
Improves detection of stealthed enemies
Improves trap detection
Improves critical weak-point chance
```

World effects:

```text
Find hidden doors
Spot traps
Notice clues
See tracks
Detect ambushes
Find hidden loot
Reveal secret quest options
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Perception = +0.3% ranged accuracy
+1 Perception = +0.2% hidden object detection
+10 Perception = reveal hidden environmental clues
```

---

## Willpower

Willpower affects mental resistance and resource stability.

Combat effects:

```text
Reduces fear duration
Reduces charm duration
Improves concentration while casting
Improves resistance to silence/interruption
Improves stamina/mana stability
```

World effects:

```text
Resist intimidation
Resist corruption
Resist mind control
Endure torture/interrogation
Maintain focus during rituals
Make brave choices under pressure
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Willpower = +0.3% control resistance
+1 Willpower = +0.2% casting interruption resistance
```

---

## Luck

Luck affects rare outcomes, but must be controlled carefully.

Combat effects:

```text
Slightly improves critical chance
Slightly improves rare proc chance
Slightly improves dodge/parry edge cases
```

World effects:

```text
Rare loot chance
Gambling
Fishing rare catch chance
Treasure discovery
Random encounter quality
Crafting bonus chance
```

Important:

```text
Luck should never replace skill, planning, or gear.
Luck should slightly improve outcomes, not dominate the game.
```

Example scaling:

```text
+1 Luck = +0.05% rare loot chance
+1 Luck = +0.1% critical chance
```

---

# 8. Derived Stats

Attributes create derived stats.

```text
Max HP
Max Mana
Max Stamina
Attack Points
Spell Power
Healing Power
Defense
Armor
Magic Resistance
Accuracy
Evasion
Critical Chance
Critical Damage
Attack Speed
Movement Speed
Carry Capacity
Control Resistance
Threat Generation
Resource Regeneration
```

Example formulas:

```text
Max HP = Base HP + Level HP Bonus + Constitution × 10

Melee Attack Points = Base Attack + Level Attack Bonus + Strength × 2

Ranged Attack Points = Base Attack + Level Attack Bonus + Dexterity × 1.5

Spell Power = Base Spell Power + Level Spell Bonus + Intelligence × 2

Healing Power = Base Healing Power + Wisdom × 1.5

Defense = Base Defense + Level Defense Bonus + Constitution × 1.5

Magic Resistance = Base Magic Resistance + Wisdom × 1.5 + Willpower × 1

Accuracy = Base Accuracy + Dexterity × 0.5 + Perception × 0.5

Evasion = Base Evasion + Dexterity × 0.4

Critical Chance = Base Crit + Dexterity × 0.05% + Luck × 0.1%

Carry Capacity = Base Carry + Strength × 0.5 + Constitution × 0.25
```

---

# 9. Level-Up Rewards

When a player levels up, they should receive clear benefits.

Every level should grant:

```text
Increased Max HP
Increased base Attack Points
Increased base Defense
Attribute points
Possibly new skill/spell access
Possibly new gear access
Possibly new quest access
```

Recommended:

```text
Each level:
+10 to +25 Max HP
+2 to +5 base Attack Points
+2 to +5 base Defense
+1 to +3 Attribute Points
```

Every few levels:

```text
New ability
New passive
New talent point
New dungeon access
New equipment tier
New mount speed
New profession recipe
```

Example:

```text
Level 2: +2 attribute points
Level 3: Unlock second ability slot
Level 5: Unlock class ability
Level 10: Unlock talent tree
Level 15: Unlock mount training
Level 20: Unlock specialization
Level 30: Unlock advanced dungeons
Level 40: Unlock heroic quests
Level 50: Max-level systems unlock
```

---

# 10. Attribute Point Allocation

Players should receive attribute points when leveling.

Example:

```text
Each level grants 2 attribute points.
Every 5 levels grants 1 bonus attribute point.
Players spend points on Strength, Dexterity, Intelligence, Wisdom, Constitution, Charisma, Perception, Willpower, or Luck.
```

Optional class bias:

