```text
Warrior gains bonus Strength/Constitution.
Rogue gains bonus Dexterity/Perception.
Mage gains bonus Intelligence/Willpower.
Priest gains bonus Wisdom/Charisma.
Ranger gains bonus Dexterity/Perception.
Paladin gains Strength/Wisdom/Charisma.
```

Important design rule:

```text
Players should be able to build creatively, but bad builds should not make the game unplayable.
```

---

# 11. Level Impact on Combat

Level should matter, but should not be the only thing that matters.

Combat should be affected by:

```text
Level
Attributes
Gear
Abilities
Skill choice
Positioning
Tactics
Buffs/debuffs
Enemy type
Damage type
Resistance
Player execution
```

Do not make level alone decide everything.

---

## Level Difference Combat Modifier

Use a level difference modifier.

```text
Level Difference = Attacker Level - Defender Level
```

Damage dealt modifier:

|    Level Difference | Damage Dealt Modifier |
| ------------------: | --------------------: |
|  Attacker 10+ lower |                 x0.25 |
|  Attacker 6–9 lower |                 x0.50 |
|  Attacker 3–5 lower |                 x0.75 |
|          Same level |                 x1.00 |
| Attacker 1–2 higher |                 x1.10 |
| Attacker 3–5 higher |                 x1.25 |
| Attacker 6–9 higher |                 x1.50 |
| Attacker 10+ higher |                 x2.00 |

Damage received can mirror this, but be careful.

```text
Higher-level enemies should be dangerous.
Lower-level enemies should not be completely irrelevant in large numbers.
```

---

# 12. Hit Chance and Level Difference

Level difference should affect hit chance.

```text
Final Hit Chance = Base Hit Chance + Accuracy Bonus - Evasion Bonus + Level Difference Modifier
```

Level hit modifiers:

|    Level Difference | Hit Modifier |
| ------------------: | -----------: |
|  Attacker 10+ lower |         -30% |
|  Attacker 6–9 lower |         -20% |
|  Attacker 3–5 lower |         -10% |
|          Same level |           0% |
| Attacker 1–2 higher |          +5% |
| Attacker 3–5 higher |         +10% |
|  Attacker 6+ higher |         +15% |

Clamp:

```text
Minimum Hit Chance: 5%
Maximum Hit Chance: 95%
```

---

# 13. Defense and Level Scaling

Defense should scale with level.

Example:

```text
Base Defense = Level × 5
Armor Contribution = Gear Armor
Constitution Contribution = Constitution × 1.5

Total Defense = Base Defense + Armor + Constitution Contribution
```

Damage reduction formula:

```text
Damage Reduction = Defense / (Defense + Attacker Level × 100)
```

Cap:

```text
Normal Damage Reduction Cap: 75%
Temporary Buff Cap: 85%
Boss Mechanics may bypass cap
```

This prevents infinite defense scaling.

---

# 14. Player Stat Formula Example

Level 10 warrior:

```text
Level: 10
Strength: 24
Dexterity: 10
Constitution: 22
Intelligence: 6
Wisdom: 8
Gear Attack: 40
Gear Armor: 120
```

Derived stats:

```text
Max HP = 100 + (10 × 20) + (22 × 10)
Max HP = 520

Melee Attack Points = 10 + (10 × 4) + (24 × 2) + 40
Melee Attack Points = 138

Defense = (10 × 5) + 120 + (22 × 1.5)
Defense = 203
```

---

# 15. NPC Level System

Every NPC should have a level.

NPC level affects:

```text
HP
Attack Points
Defense
Armor
Magic Resistance
Accuracy
Evasion
Aggro range
Loot value
XP value
Quest difficulty
Dialogue intimidation
Guard response strength
```

NPCs should not all scale the same way.

A level 10 rat and level 10 guard should not feel identical.

Use:

```text
NPC Level
NPC Role
NPC Rank
NPC Species
NPC Class
NPC Equipment
NPC Faction
NPC Behavior
```

---

# 16. NPC Rank System

NPCs should have rank multipliers.

```text
Critter
Civilian
Normal
Strong
Elite
Mini-Boss
Dungeon Boss
World Boss
Legendary Boss
```

Rank multipliers:

| Rank         | HP Multiplier | Attack Multiplier | Defense Multiplier |
| ------------ | ------------: | ----------------: | -----------------: |
| Critter      |          x0.1 |              x0.1 |               x0.1 |
| Civilian     |          x0.5 |              x0.3 |               x0.4 |
| Normal       |          x1.0 |              x1.0 |               x1.0 |
| Strong       |          x1.5 |             x1.25 |               x1.2 |
| Elite        |          x3.0 |             x1.75 |               x1.5 |
| Mini-Boss    |          x8.0 |              x2.5 |               x2.0 |
| Dungeon Boss |         x30.0 |              x4.0 |               x3.0 |
| World Boss   |         x500+ |              x10+ |                x5+ |

---

# 17. NPC Base Scaling Formulas

Basic NPC formula:

```text
NPC Max HP = Base Species HP × Level Multiplier × Rank Multiplier

NPC Attack Points = Base Species Attack × Level Multiplier × Rank Multiplier

NPC Defense = Base Species Defense × Level Multiplier × Rank Multiplier
```

Level multiplier:

```text
Level Multiplier = 1 + (Level × 0.15)
```

Example level 10 wolf:

```text
Base Wolf HP: 100
Base Wolf Attack: 14
Base Wolf Defense: 20
Level Multiplier: 1 + (10 × 0.15) = 2.5
Rank: Normal x1.0

HP = 100 × 2.5 = 250
Attack = 14 × 2.5 = 35
Defense = 20 × 2.5 = 50
```

Example level 10 elite wolf:

```text
HP = 100 × 2.5 × 3.0 = 750
Attack = 14 × 2.5 × 1.75 = 61
Defense = 20 × 2.5 × 1.5 = 75
```

---

# 18. NPC Type Scaling

Different NPC types should scale differently.

## Civilian NPCs

```text
Low HP
Low Attack
Low Defense
May flee or call guards
Level mostly represents age, toughness, profession, or importance
```

Example:

```text
Level 5 Farmer
HP: 160
Attack Points: 20
Defense: 15
Behavior: defensive/flee
```

---

## Guard NPCs

```text
High HP
High Attack
High Defense
Strong crowd control
Legal authority
Better equipment
```

Example:

```text
Level 15 Town Guard
HP: 1,200
Attack Points: 180
Defense: 250
Abilities: Shield Bash, Arrest, Call Reinforcements
```

---

## Merchant NPCs

```text
Moderate HP
Low to moderate Attack
May have guards nearby
Usually avoids direct combat
```

Example:

```text
Level 8 Merchant
HP: 240
Attack Points: 20
Defense: 30
Behavior: flee/call guards
```

---

## Animal NPCs

```text
Stats based on species
Predators have higher attack
Prey have higher movement/evasion
Large animals have high HP
```

Example:

```text
Level 12 Bear
HP: 1,100
Attack Points: 140
Defense: 100
Behavior: territorial
```

---

## Monster NPCs

```text
Higher combat stats
Special abilities
Territory behavior
Resistances
May scale by zone danger
```

Example:

```text
Level 20 Undead Knight
HP: 2,200
Attack Points: 240
Defense: 300
Magic Resistance: 150
Immune to poison and fear
```

---

## Boss NPCs

Bosses should not just be high-level enemies. They need mechanics.

Boss scaling includes:

```text
Large HP
High attack
Mechanics
Phases
Resistances
Enrage timers
Adds/summons
Anti-exploit leash
Contribution-based rewards
```

---

# 19. Zone Level System

World zones should have level ranges.

Example:

```text
Starter Village: Levels 1–5
North Fields: Levels 5–10
Old Forest: Levels 10–15
Bandit Hills: Levels 15–20
Ruined City: Levels 20–30
Dragon Mountains: Levels 30–40
Cursed Wastes: Levels 40–50
Endgame Zones: 50+
```

Rules:

```text
NPCs in a zone should mostly match zone level.
Dangerous sub-areas can have higher-level enemies.
Safe roads should have lower danger.
Dungeons can be higher difficulty than surrounding zone.
World bosses can exceed zone level.
```

---

# 20. Dynamic Level Scaling

Decide if the world scales to the player.

## Option A: Fixed-Level World

```text
Each zone has fixed NPC levels.
Players can outlevel old zones.
High-level areas are dangerous.
```

Pros:

```text
Strong sense of progression
World feels dangerous
Players feel growth
```

Cons:

```text
Old content becomes too easy
Low-level friends may be hard to play with
```

## Option B: Full Scaling World

```text
NPCs scale to player level.
```

Pros:

```text
Content stays relevant
Players can go anywhere
Friends can play together
```

Cons:

```text
Leveling can feel meaningless
World danger becomes less clear
```

## Option C: Banded Scaling

Recommended.

```text
Zones have minimum and maximum scaling levels.
NPCs scale within that range.
```

Example:

```text
North Fields: scales from Level 5 to Level 12
Old Forest: scales from Level 10 to Level 18
Bandit Hills: scales from Level 15 to Level 25
```

This keeps content relevant without destroying progression.

---

# 21. NPC Scaling With Player

If using scaling:

```text
NPC Effective Level = Clamp(Player Level, Zone Min Level, Zone Max Level)
```

Example:

```text
Player Level: 30
Zone Range: 5–12
NPC Effective Level: 12

Player Level: 3
Zone Range: 5–12
NPC Effective Level: 5

Player Level: 8
Zone Range: 5–12
NPC Effective Level: 8
```

Important:

```text
Bosses, guards, elite creatures, and story enemies may ignore normal scaling.
```

---

# 22. Gear Scaling and Level

Gear should matter alongside level.

Item level should affect:

```text
Attack Points
Armor
Magic Resistance
Stat bonuses
Special effects
Required level
Durability
Value
```

Gear formula example:

```text
Weapon Attack = Item Level × Weapon Type Modifier × Quality Modifier

Armor Value = Item Level × Armor Type Modifier × Quality Modifier
```

Quality modifiers:

```text
Common: x1.0
Uncommon: x1.1
Rare: x1.25
Epic: x1.45
Legendary: x1.75
Artifact: x2.0+
```

---

# 23. Preventing Bad Scaling

Avoid these mistakes:

```text
Level 20 player does 100x more damage than Level 10 player.
Defense makes tanks immortal.
Low-level enemies cannot hurt high-level players at all.
High-level players one-shot all old bosses.
Boss HP becomes absurdly high without mechanics.
Gear completely overrides level.
Level completely overrides gear.
Attributes are meaningless.
Attributes scale too hard and break builds.
```

Better rule:

```text
Level gives a steady advantage.
Gear gives specialization.
Attributes give identity.
Abilities give playstyle.
Skill gives mastery.
```

---

# 24. Suggested Scaling Curve

Use moderate growth.

Recommended per level growth:

```text
Max HP: +5% to +8%
Attack Points: +4% to +6%
Defense: +4% to +6%
Resources: +3% to +5%
```

Avoid:

```text
+20% power per level
```

That makes level gaps too extreme.

---

# 25. Power Ratio Guidelines

A player should feel stronger as they level, but not godlike too early.

Recommended:

```text
A Level 10 player should be about 1.5x to 2x stronger than Level 5.
A Level 20 player should be about 2x to 3x stronger than Level 10.
A Level 50 player should be much stronger than Level 10, but old group bosses may still require attention if mechanics matter.
```

---

# 26. Ability Unlocks by Level

Levels should unlock abilities.

Example:

```text
Level 1: Basic attack, starter ability
Level 2: Defensive ability
Level 3: Utility ability
Level 5: Class identity ability
Level 8: Interrupt or crowd control
Level 10: Talent tree
Level 15: Movement ability
Level 20: Specialization
Level 25: Advanced passive
Level 30: Group utility
Level 35: Major damage/healing ability
Level 40: Ultimate ability
Level 45: Master passive
Level 50: Endgame class feature
```

---

# 27. Talent / Skill Point System

In addition to attributes, players can gain talent points.

```text
Every 2 levels: +1 talent point
Every 5 levels: +1 major talent point
```

Talents can improve:

```text
Abilities
Damage type
Defense style
Healing
Crafting
Mobility
Pets
Summons
Trading
Stealth
Social interactions
Gathering
Magic schools
```

Important:

```text
Talent trees should offer meaningful choices, not mandatory traps.
```

---

# 28. Class Scaling

Different classes should use stats differently.

## Warrior

```text
Primary: Strength, Constitution
Secondary: Willpower
Effects:
High melee damage, high HP, armor scaling, threat generation
```

## Rogue

```text
Primary: Dexterity, Perception
Secondary: Luck
Effects:
Critical damage, evasion, stealth, lockpicking, traps
```

## Mage

```text
Primary: Intelligence, Willpower
Secondary: Perception
Effects:
Spell power, mana, magic accuracy, ritual use
```

## Priest / Healer

```text
Primary: Wisdom, Charisma
Secondary: Willpower
Effects:
Healing, blessings, cleansing, diplomacy, morale
```

## Ranger

```text
Primary: Dexterity, Perception
Secondary: Wisdom
Effects:
Ranged damage, tracking, animal handling, survival
```

## Paladin

```text
Primary: Strength, Wisdom, Charisma
Secondary: Constitution
Effects:
Hybrid melee, defense, healing, lawful/social influence
```

## Necromancer

```text
Primary: Intelligence, Willpower
Secondary: Constitution
Effects:
Dark magic, summons, curses, corpse mechanics
```

---

# 29. Non-Combat World Effects of Level

Level should affect more than combat.

Higher level can unlock:

```text
New areas
New dungeons
New mounts
New professions
New crafting recipes
New guild ranks
New political access
New dialogue options
New housing options
New trade routes
New followers
New world events
New titles
New faction missions
```

Example:

```text
Level 10:
Can join local guilds.

Level 20:
Can own basic housing.

Level 30:
Can command small NPC followers.

Level 40:
Can participate in regional wars.

Level 50:
Can influence world events, politics, raids, and high-end crafting.
```

---

# 30. Attribute World Effects

Attributes should unlock world interactions.

## Strength Checks

```text
Move fallen tree
Open stuck gate
Carry injured NPC
Break weak wall
Win arm-wrestling contest
Intimidate bandit
```

## Dexterity Checks

```text
Pick lock
Disarm trap
Sneak across narrow beam
Steal keys
Escape bindings
Climb tower
```

## Intelligence Checks

```text
Solve ancient puzzle
Identify magical trap
Read old language
Understand machine
Improve crafting formula
Decode map
```

## Wisdom Checks

```text
Detect lie
Calm ghost
Understand sacred ritual
Mediate conflict
Resist cursed shrine
Recognize moral consequence
```

## Constitution Checks

```text
Survive poison
Cross freezing pass
Resist disease
Swim long distance
Endure desert heat
Continue fighting while wounded
```

## Charisma Checks

```text
Persuade guard
Negotiate discount
Recruit follower
Calm angry mob
Influence noble court
Convince thief to talk
```

## Perception Checks

```text
Spot hidden door
Notice ambush
Find tracks
Detect pickpocket
See weak point
Discover hidden quest clue
```

## Willpower Checks

```text
Resist mind control
Refuse dark bargain
Hold spell concentration
Ignore fear aura
Endure interrogation
Complete dangerous ritual
```

## Luck Checks

```text
Find rare coin
Avoid minor trap trigger
Win gambling
Catch rare fish
Find bonus loot
Trigger lucky escape event
```

---

# 31. Level and Reputation Interaction

Level should interact with reputation systems.

Example:

```text
A Level 5 outlaw may be chased by town guards.
A Level 40 outlaw may trigger elite bounty hunters.
A Level 50 public enemy may cause city lockdowns.
```

High-level players should have greater consequences because they are more dangerous.

```text
Crime severity can scale with player level and Notoriety.
NPC fear can scale with player level and reputation.
Quest rewards can scale with level and standing.
```

---

# 32. NPC Reaction to Player Level

NPCs should react to level.

```text
Low-level player:
NPCs may underestimate them.
Guards give basic warnings.
Dangerous NPCs may threaten them.

Equal-level player:
NPCs treat them as capable.

High-level player:
NPCs recognize power.
Weak enemies may flee.
Civilians may ask for help.
Nobles may request service.
Bandits may avoid direct attack.
Guards may call reinforcements sooner if hostile.
```

Example:

```text
A Level 2 player insulting a guard:
Guard says, "Move along."

A Level 45 outlaw entering the same gate:
Guard signals reinforcements immediately.
```

---

# 33. NPC Aggression by Level

Enemy aggression should consider level difference.

```text
Predators may attack weaker players.
Intelligent enemies may avoid much stronger players.
Undead may attack regardless of level.
Guards may challenge criminals regardless of level.
Bosses attack all intruders.
Animals may flee from high-level players.
```

Example rules:

```text
Enemy 5+ levels lower:
May ignore/flee unless aggressive or territorial.

Enemy within 3 levels:
Normal aggression.

Enemy 5+ levels higher:
May attack if player enters territory.

Intelligent weak enemy:
May call allies instead of attacking alone.
```

---

# 34. Party Level Scaling

Parties create scaling problems.

Rules:

```text
XP should be shared based on contribution, level range, and proximity.
Very high-level players should not power-level low-level players too easily.
Low-level players should still get credit if they meaningfully participate.
```

Party XP example:

```text
If party members are within 5 levels:
Normal shared XP.

If one member is 10+ levels higher:
Low-level members receive reduced XP unless content is scaled or mentored.

If high-level player is mentoring:
High-level player scales down temporarily and rewards adjust.
```

---

# 35. Mentoring / Level Sync

Recommended for MMOs.

```text
High-level players can sync down to lower-level friends.
Their stats scale to the zone or party level.
They can help without ruining balance.
They receive scaled rewards.
```

Rules:

```text
Level sync reduces effective level.
Gear stats are scaled down.
Abilities may remain, but overpowered effects are normalized.
Rewards become appropriate but not exploitable.
```

---

# 36. PvP Level Scaling

PvP needs extra care.

Options:

## Open World PvP

```text
Level and gear matter.
But killing much lower-level players gives no reward and may create legal/reputation penalties.
```

## Battleground / Arena PvP

```text
Normalize stats within brackets.
Separate players by level ranges.
Use PvP templates or scaling.
```

PvP brackets:

```text
Levels 1–9
10–19
20–29
30–39
40–49
50
```

Rules:

```text
Do not let Level 50 players farm Level 10 players for rewards.
Do not let low-level players become stronger than veterans through bad scaling.
Normalize enough for fairness, but preserve build identity.
```

---

# 37. NPC Level Visibility

Players should see NPC difficulty clearly.

Use indicators:

```text
Green name: much lower level
White name: similar level
Yellow name: slightly higher
Orange name: dangerous
Red name: very dangerous
Skull icon: far above player level
Elite border: elite enemy
Boss frame: boss enemy
```

Example:

```text
Forest Wolf — Level 6
Elite Bandit Captain — Level 12 Elite
Ancient Dragon — Skull Boss
```

---

# 38. Level-Based Quest Availability

Quests should have level rules.

```text
Minimum Level
Recommended Level
Maximum Reward Level
Difficulty
Scaling Rules
```

Example:

```text
Quest: Wolves at the North Fence
Minimum Level: 4
Recommended Level: 6
Reward scales until Level 9
After Level 12, XP reduced heavily
```

This allows players to do old quests for story but prevents XP abuse.

---

# 39. Level-Based Loot Rules

Loot should scale to content.

```text
Enemy level affects item level.
Boss level affects item quality.
Player level affects usable requirements.
Zone level affects drop tables.
```

Avoid:

```text
Level 5 wolf dropping Level 50 sword.
Level 50 player farming starter rats for high-end loot.
```

Rules:

```text
Normal enemies drop gear near their level.
Bosses drop better gear near their level.
Low-level enemies can drop cosmetics, materials, or collectibles, but not high-power gear.
```

---

# 40. Level-Up UI

When a player levels up, show:

```text
New level
Stat increases
Attribute points gained
Talent points gained
New abilities unlocked
New quests unlocked
New dungeons unlocked
New equipment tiers available
New world features unlocked
```

Example:

```text
Level Up! You are now Level 10.

Gained:
+20 Max HP
+4 Attack Points
+3 Defense
+2 Attribute Points
+1 Talent Point

Unlocked:
Talent Tree
Ability Slot 4
Dungeon Finder: Old Mill Crypt
```

---

# 41. Death and Level

Death penalties can scale with level.

Recommended:

```text
No XP loss for normal death.
Equipment durability loss.
Temporary resurrection weakness.
Hardcore zones may have stronger penalties.
```

Avoid heavy XP loss unless the game is intentionally old-school/hardcore.

```text
Losing hours of XP on death can make players quit.
```

---

# 42. Rested XP

Rested XP rewards players who return later.

Rules:

```text
Players gain rested XP while logged out in inns, cities, homes, or safe zones.
Rested XP gives bonus kill XP.
Quest XP may or may not benefit.
Rested XP has a cap.
```

Example:

```text
Rested XP Bonus: +50% kill XP
Max Rested Pool: 1.5 levels worth of XP
Inns generate rested XP faster than wilderness logout.
```

---

# 43. Catch-Up Mechanics

For long-running MMOs, help new players catch up.

Options:

```text
Rested XP
XP boosts for old expansions
Mentor system
Account-wide bonuses
Story skips after completion
Level-scaling zones
Bonus XP for lower-level friends
Daily first dungeon bonus
```

Be careful:

```text
Catch-up should reduce boring grind, not erase learning or progression.
```

---

# 44. Endgame Progression

At level cap, XP should still matter somehow.

Options:

```text
Paragon levels
Mastery levels
Talent refinements
Cosmetic rewards
Reputation progression
Profession mastery
Champion points
Seasonal rank
Artifact leveling
```

Important:

```text
Endgame progression should not create infinite power gaps that make new players useless.
```

Recommended:

```text
After level cap, XP goes into Mastery Progression.
Mastery gives mostly utility, cosmetics, small stat bonuses, and specialization options.
```

---

# 45. Anti-Farming Rules

Players will exploit XP.

Prevent:

```text
Killing low-level enemies for full XP
AFK farming spawn points
Alt-account kill trading
Quest abandon/reaccept loops
Pet/summon AFK farming
Healing bots in combat
Repeated public event leeching
High-level carrying low-level players too efficiently
```

Rules:

```text
No XP from enemies far below level.
Contribution required for XP.
Diminishing returns from same spawn farming.
No XP if player is AFK.
No PvP XP from repeated kills against same player.
Quest rewards only once unless repeatable.
Repeatable quests have caps.
```

---

# 46. Level and Economy

Level affects economy.

Higher-level players can access:

```text
Better gathering nodes
Advanced crafting recipes
Higher-tier markets
Better trade routes
More expensive repairs
Higher-value loot
Greater tax impact
Guild production
Rare material processing
```

But:

```text
Low-level materials should still matter for crafting, housing, alts, and economy.
```

Avoid making starter materials worthless.

---

# 47. Level and Crafting

Crafting should have separate progression.

```text
Player Combat Level
Profession Level
Recipe Level
Tool Level
Material Tier
```

Crafting level should affect:

```text
Recipe unlocks
Crafting success
Quality chance
Material efficiency
Repair ability
Enhancement strength
Specialization
```

Example:

```text
Player Level 20
Blacksmithing Level 35

Can craft high-quality Level 15 weapons, but cannot equip some combat gear if combat level is too low.
```

---

# 48. Level and Dialogue

Level should unlock some dialogue, but reputation and attributes should matter more.

Examples:

```text
High-level warrior can intimidate bandits.
High-level mage can discuss advanced magic.
High-level healer can diagnose illness.
High-level thief can recognize criminal signs.
```

Dialogue checks can use:

```text
Level + Attribute + Reputation + Class + Quest History
```

Example:

```text
Persuade Guard:
Requires Charisma 15 or Legal Standing +2,000 or Level 25 Noble Title.
```

---

# 49. Level and World Events

World events can have level bands.

```text
Local events: Levels 5–15
Regional events: Levels 15–35
Major invasions: Levels 35–50
World bosses: Level cap
```

If mixed-level players join, use contribution tiers.

```text
Low-level players can help with support tasks.
High-level players handle combat objectives.
Everyone gets appropriate rewards.
```

Example:

```text
Dragon Attack Event:
Low-level players rescue civilians and put out fires.
Mid-level players fight dragon cultists.
High-level players fight the dragon.
```

---

# 50. Level Edge Cases

## Player Levels Up During Combat

Rule:

```text
Apply level-up after combat event completes.
Increase Max HP immediately.
Optionally heal a portion of HP.
Do not interrupt combat state.
```

Recommended:

```text
On level-up:
Restore 25% HP and resources.
Show notification.
Do not reset cooldowns.
```

---

## Player Gains Enough XP for Multiple Levels

Rule:

```text
Allow multiple level-ups.
Process each level reward.
Show summary if many levels gained at once.
```

Example:

```text
You gained 3 levels.
You are now Level 8.
You gained 6 attribute points and 1 talent point.
```

---

## Player Is at Level Cap

Rule:

```text
XP goes to mastery, rested pool, currency, or is ignored with clear messaging.
```

---

## Player Delevels

Generally avoid deleveling.

If used:

```text
Only in hardcore or cursed systems.
Never unequip items without clear handling.
Never break character permanently.
```

Recommended:

```text
Do not use deleveling in a standard MMO.
```

---

## NPC Level Changes While In Combat

Rule:

```text
Do not dynamically change NPC level during active combat unless it is a scripted boss phase.
```

Bad:

```text
Wolf becomes Level 20 while player is fighting it because another player walks nearby.
```

Good:

```text
NPC level is locked when combat starts.
```

---

## Player Enters Low-Level Zone at High Level

Rule options:

```text
Fixed world: player is powerful.
Scaled world: player syncs down.
Banded world: NPCs scale to zone max.
```

Recommended:

```text
Use banded scaling or optional level sync.
```

---

## High-Level Player Helping Low-Level Friend

Rule:

```text
Offer level sync.
Without level sync, reduce XP rewards for the low-level player if high-level player carries too much.
```

---

## Low-Level Player Tags High-Level Enemy

Rule:

```text
Require meaningful contribution.
Do not give full XP for tapping an enemy killed by others.
```

---

## NPC Is Much Higher Level Than Player

Rule:

```text
Show danger clearly.
Use skull/red marker.
Let player escape if possible.
Do not hide impossible enemies without warning in starter routes.
```

---

## Attribute Respec

Players need respec options.

Rules:

```text
Allow attribute reset through trainer, item, shrine, or paid service.
Do not make early mistakes permanent.
Increase cost with frequency, not abusively.
```

---

# 51. Example Player Level Data Structure

```json
{
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "level": 12,
  "xp_current": 3400,
  "xp_required_next": 5200,
  "attribute_points_unspent": 4,
  "talent_points_unspent": 2,
  "attributes": {
    "strength": 18,
    "dexterity": 12,
    "intelligence": 8,
    "wisdom": 10,
    "constitution": 20,
    "charisma": 9,
    "perception": 11,
    "willpower": 13,
    "luck": 5
  },
  "derived_stats": {
    "max_hp": 560,
    "max_mana": 140,
    "max_stamina": 180,
    "attack_points": 126,
    "defense": 215,
    "accuracy": 82,
    "evasion": 7.5,
    "critical_chance": 6.2
  }
}
```

---

# 52. Example NPC Level Data Structure

```json
{
  "npc_id": "npc_forest_wolf_001",
  "name": "Forest Wolf",
  "type": "animal",
  "level": 10,
  "rank": "normal",
  "species": "wolf",
  "faction": "wildlife",
  "hp": 250,
  "max_hp": 250,
  "attack_points": 35,
  "defense": 50,
  "armor": 40,
  "magic_resistance": 15,
  "accuracy": 75,
  "evasion": 8,
  "critical_chance": 3,
  "xp_reward": 120,
  "aggro_range": 12,
  "leash_range": 40,
  "abilities": ["bite", "lunge", "howl"]
}
```

---

# 53. Level-Up Processing Pipeline

When player gains XP:

```text
1. Server receives XP event.
2. Server validates XP source.
3. Server checks anti-farming rules.
4. Server applies XP modifier.
5. Server adds XP to player.
6. Server checks if XP >= required XP.
7. If yes, level up.
8. Grant stat increases.
9. Grant attribute/talent points.
10. Unlock abilities/features.
11. Recalculate derived stats.
12. Notify player.
13. Log XP and level change.
```

Server must be authoritative.

The client can display XP, but cannot decide XP or level.

---

# 54. XP Source Validation

Every XP event should include:

```text
Source type
Source ID
Player contribution
Party contribution
Level difference
Repeat count
Location
Timestamp
Quest/event ID
Anti-abuse flags
```

This prevents exploits.

---

# 55. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design a complete level, experience, attribute, and scaling system for this MMO.

Every player and NPC must have a level.

Player levels affect:
- Max HP
- Attack Points
- Defense
- Armor
- Magic Resistance
- Accuracy
- Evasion
- Critical Chance
- Mana/stamina/resources
- Ability unlocks
- Gear access
- Quest access
- NPC reactions
- World interactions

NPC levels affect:
- HP
- Attack Points
- Defense
- Armor
- Magic Resistance
- Accuracy
- Evasion
- Aggro range
- XP reward
- Loot quality
- Difficulty
- Behavior

Players gain XP from:
- Quests
- Enemy kills
- Exploration
- Dungeons
- Bosses
- PvP objectives
- Public events
- Crafting
- Gathering
- Helping others
- Discovering secrets

Players should not gain meaningful XP from:
- Killing harmless civilians
- Killing enemies far below their level
- Repeatedly farming exploit targets
- Killing the same player repeatedly
- AFK participation
- Quest duplication exploits

Use core attributes:
- Strength
- Dexterity
- Intelligence
- Wisdom
- Constitution
- Charisma
- Perception
- Willpower
- Luck

Attributes must affect both combat and the world:
- Strength affects melee damage, carrying, breaking, lifting, intimidation.
- Dexterity affects evasion, ranged damage, lockpicking, stealth, traps.
- Intelligence affects spell power, mana, puzzles, lore, magical crafting.
- Wisdom affects healing, resistance, insight, spiritual choices.
- Constitution affects HP, stamina, poison/disease/environment resistance.
- Charisma affects persuasion, prices, followers, morale, diplomacy.
- Perception affects accuracy, hidden objects, traps, tracks, ambushes.
- Willpower affects fear/charm resistance, concentration, corruption resistance.
- Luck affects rare outcomes slightly, but must not dominate gameplay.

Derived stats should be calculated from level, attributes, gear, buffs, and class.

NPCs must scale by:
- Level
- Rank
- Species
- Role
- Equipment
- Faction
- Behavior

NPC ranks include:
- Critter
- Civilian
- Normal
- Strong
- Elite
- Mini-Boss
- Dungeon Boss
- World Boss
- Legendary Boss

Use rank multipliers so a Level 10 guard, Level 10 wolf, Level 10 merchant, and Level 10 boss do not have identical stats.

Combat must account for level difference:
- Higher-level attackers deal more damage and hit more often.
- Lower-level attackers deal less damage and miss more often.
- Clamp hit chance between 5% and 95%.
- Do not let level alone override gear, attributes, abilities, and player skill.

Use zone level ranges:
- Starter zones are low level.
- Dangerous areas have higher-level NPCs.
- Bosses can exceed normal zone level.
- Use banded scaling if content should remain relevant.

Handle edge cases:
- Player levels up during combat.
- Player gains multiple levels at once.
- Player is at level cap.
- High-level player helps low-level friend.
- Low-level player tags high-level enemy.
- NPC changes level during combat.
- Player enters low-level zone at high level.
- Attribute respec.
- XP farming abuse.
- Party XP sharing.
- PvP level imbalance.
- Level sync and mentoring.

The server must be authoritative:
- Server grants XP.
- Server validates XP source.
- Server checks anti-farming rules.
- Server processes level-up.
- Server grants attribute/talent points.
- Server recalculates stats.
- Server unlocks abilities and content.
- Client only displays XP, level, stat changes, and unlocks.

The system should make leveling feel rewarding, balanced, understandable, and connected to the world.
```

---

# Final Level System Rule

Every level should answer:

```text
What got stronger?
What new choices opened?
What new content became available?
What changed in combat?
What changed in the world?
What can the player do now that they could not do before?
```

And every stat should answer:

```text
How does this affect combat?
How does this affect exploration?
How does this affect NPC interaction?
How does this affect the economy, crafting, or quests?
```

That is how you make levels feel meaningful instead of just making numbers go up.


Below is a full **MMO Inventory, Item, Equipment, and Spell System**.

The core rule:

```text
The inventory system must let players clearly hold, equip, use, sort, trade, store, sell, craft with, and track items, while also supporting spells, quest items, currencies, materials, equipment, consumables, pets, mounts, keys, and special magical objects.
```

---

# MMO Inventory System Overview

Every player should have several connected storage systems:

```text
Backpack Inventory
Equipment Slots
Hotbar / Action Bar
Spellbook
Quest Item Pouch
Currency Wallet
Crafting Materials Storage
Keyring
Mount / Pet Collection
Bank Storage
Shared Account Storage
Mail Attachments
Trade Window
Vendor Sell / Buyback Window
```

Not everything should go into the same bag.

A sword, a spell, a quest key, a mount, and gold are all “owned” by the player, but they should not all behave the same way.

---

# 1. Inventory Core Rules

Every inventory system must answer:

```text
What does the player own?
Where is the item stored?
Can the item stack?
Can the item be equipped?
Can the item be used?
Can the item be traded?
Can the item be sold?
Can the item be destroyed?
Can the item be dropped?
Can the item be crafted with?
Can the item be lost on death?
Can the item be stolen?
Can the item expire?
Can the item be moved to the bank?
Can the item be used in combat?
Can the item be used by this class, level, race, faction, or profession?
```

---

# 2. Inventory Categories

Items should be separated by type.

```text
Weapons
Armor
Accessories
Consumables
Crafting Materials
Quest Items
Currency
Keys
Books
Scrolls
Spells
Runes
Mounts
Pets
Tools
Gathering Gear
Trade Goods
Junk
Trophies
Cosmetics
Housing Items
Containers
Food
Drink
Ammunition
Magical Artifacts
Stolen Goods
Event Items
```

Each category has different rules.

---

# 3. Backpack Inventory

The backpack is the player’s main item storage.

## Backpack Rules

```text
The backpack holds normal items.
The backpack has limited capacity.
Items may use slot-based, weight-based, or hybrid storage.
Players can sort, search, filter, move, split, stack, use, equip, sell, destroy, or link items from the backpack.
```

Recommended MMO approach:

```text
Use slot-based inventory for clarity.
Optionally add weight for survival/hardcore systems.
```

Example:

```text
Starter Backpack: 24 slots
Small Bag: +8 slots
Traveler Bag: +16 slots
Adventurer Bag: +24 slots
Bank Bag: +32 slots
Premium/rare bags may add special filters, not unfair power.
```

---

# 4. Slot-Based Inventory

In a slot-based system:

```text
Each item occupies one or more inventory slots.
Stackable items share one slot up to their stack limit.
Equipment usually takes one slot.
Large items may take multiple slots if the game supports grid inventory.
```

Simple MMO version:

```text
Every item takes 1 slot unless stackable.
```

More complex survival version:

```text
Large weapons, armor, crates, and furniture can take multiple slots.
```

For a broad MMO, the simpler version is usually better.

---

# 5. Weight-Based Inventory

If weight exists:

```text
Every item has weight.
The player has max carry weight.
Going over max weight causes penalties.
```

Example penalties:

```text
0% to 100% carry weight: normal
100% to 125%: slower movement
125% to 150%: cannot sprint or fast travel
150%+: cannot pick up more heavy items
```

Important rule:

```text
Do not make inventory weight so punishing that normal questing becomes annoying.
```

---

# 6. Stack Rules

Stackable items include:

```text
Potions
Food
Arrows
Ore
Herbs
Cloth
Crafting materials
Coins, if not wallet-based
Quest drops, if appropriate
Event tokens
```

Non-stackable items include:

```text
Most weapons
Most armor
Unique items
Enchanted items with different stats
Items with durability differences
Items with owner history
Bound items
Personalized quest items
```

## Stack Limit Examples

```text
Potions: 20
Food: 50
Arrows: 999
Ore: 200
Herbs: 200
Cloth: 200
Quest drops: 20 to 100
Event tokens: 9999
```

## Stack Compatibility

Items can stack only if they have matching:

```text
Item ID
Quality
Durability, if durability matters for stacking
Bound state
Stolen state
Expiration time
Enchantments
Custom stats
Quest ownership
```

Example:

```text
Normal Healing Potion can stack with Normal Healing Potion.
Stolen Healing Potion may not stack with legal Healing Potion.
Bound potion may not stack with unbound potion.
Potion expiring in 1 hour may not stack with potion expiring in 5 days.
```

---

# 7. Equipment System

Players should have equipment slots.

## Common Equipment Slots

```text
Head
Chest
Legs
Feet
Hands
Shoulders
Back
Waist
Neck
Ring 1
Ring 2
Trinket 1
Trinket 2
Main Hand
Off Hand
Ranged Weapon
Tool Slot
Mount Equipment
Cosmetic Outfit Slots
```

Optional:

```text
Earrings
Bracelets
Relic
Spell Focus
Ammo Slot
Profession Tool Slot
Lantern Slot
Companion Gear
```

---

## Equipment Rules

An item can only be equipped if:

```text
Player meets level requirement.
Player meets class requirement.
Player meets race/species requirement, if any.
Player meets faction requirement, if any.
Player meets reputation requirement, if any.
Item is not broken.
Item is not locked by quest state.
Item is not in trade.
Item is not in mail.
Item is not already equipped elsewhere.
```

Example:

```text
Iron Longsword
Required Level: 5
Allowed Classes: Warrior, Paladin, Ranger
Slot: Main Hand
Damage Type: Slashing
```

---

# 8. Weapon Rules

Weapons affect:

```text
Attack Points
Attack Speed
Damage Type
Range
Critical Chance
Abilities available
Animation style
Durability loss
```

Weapon types:

```text
Sword
Axe
Mace
Dagger
Spear
Bow
Crossbow
Staff
Wand
Orb
Shield
Greatsword
Hammer
Throwing weapon
Gun, if setting supports it
Magic focus
```

## Weapon Edge Rules

```text
Two-handed weapons disable off-hand slot.
Dual-wielding requires allowed weapon types.
Ranged weapons require ammunition only if the game uses ammo.
Magic weapons may scale with spell power instead of attack power.
Broken weapons cannot be used or have reduced damage.
```

---

# 9. Armor Rules

Armor affects:

```text
Defense
Armor
Magic resistance
Movement speed
Stealth
Durability
Class identity
Appearance
```

Armor types:

```text
Cloth
Leather
Mail
Plate
Robes
Shields
Magical barriers
Natural armor
```

Example:

```text
Cloth:
Low defense, high magic stats.

Leather:
Medium evasion, medium defense.

Mail:
Good physical defense, moderate weight.

Plate:
High defense, low stealth, heavier.
```

---

# 10. Item Quality System

Items should have quality tiers.

```text
Poor / Broken / Junk
Common
Uncommon
Rare
Epic
Legendary
Mythic
Artifact
Unique
Quest
Event
```

Quality affects:

```text
Stats
Visual border color
Drop rate
Vendor value
Repair cost
Upgrade potential
Binding rules
Special effects
```

Important rule:

```text
Higher quality should usually mean more interesting, not just bigger numbers.
```

---

# 11. Item Binding Rules

Binding controls trading.

## Binding Types

```text
Unbound
Bind on Pickup
Bind on Equip
Bind on Use
Bind to Account
Quest Bound
Soulbound
Faction Bound
Temporary Bound
```

## Binding Behavior

```text
Unbound:
Can trade, sell, mail, auction.

Bind on Pickup:
Binds immediately when obtained.

Bind on Equip:
Can trade until equipped.

Bind on Use:
Can trade until used.

Bind to Account:
Can move between characters on same account.

Quest Bound:
Only exists for quest and may be removed after completion.

Faction Bound:
Only usable by members of a faction.

Temporary Bound:
Cannot trade for a limited time after loot, then may unlock or expire.
```

---

# 12. Durability System

Equipment can have durability.

```text
Current Durability
Max Durability
```

Durability decreases from:

```text
Combat
Death
Blocking
Being hit
Using tools
Environmental hazards
Failed crafting
Special enemy abilities
```

## Durability Rules

```text
At 100% durability: normal.
At 25% durability: warning appears.
At 0% durability: item is broken.
Broken item cannot be used or has greatly reduced stats.
```

Repair options:

```text
Blacksmith
Armor repair NPC
Repair kit
Guild repair
Magic repair spell
Player profession repair
```

Do not let durability become pure annoyance. It should support economy and preparation, not punish players constantly.

---

# 13. Consumable Items

Consumables are used and then removed or reduced.

Types:

```text
Health potions
Mana potions
Stamina potions
Food
Drink
Scrolls
Bombs
Elixirs
Antidotes
Buff items
Teleport stones
Repair kits
Temporary enchantments
```

## Consumable Rules

```text
Can have cooldowns.
Can have level requirements.
Can be usable in combat or out of combat only.
Can stack.
Can expire.
Can be interrupted if use has cast time.
Can be restricted in PvP.
```

Example:

```text
Health Potion
Restores 300 HP.
Cooldown: 60 seconds.
Usable in combat.
Stack size: 20.
```

---

# 14. Quest Item System

Quest items should not clutter or confuse the player.

Recommended:

```text
Quest items go into a separate Quest Item Pouch.
They do not consume normal backpack slots unless intentionally designed.
```

## Quest Item Rules

```text
Marked clearly as Quest Item.
Shows which quest uses it.
Cannot be accidentally sold.
Cannot be accidentally destroyed without warning.
Removed when quest completes or is abandoned.
Can be reacquired if lost.
Can be hidden from vendor sell lists.
```

Example tooltip:

```text
Old Bridge Satchel
Quest Item
Used for: The Missing Satchel
Return this to Mira in Harthmere.
```

---

# 15. Currency Wallet

Currencies should not use normal inventory slots.

## Currency Types

```text
Gold
Silver
Copper
Faction tokens
Dungeon tokens
Raid tokens
PvP currency
Event currency
Crafting writs
Honor points
Arena points
Guild marks
Premium currency, if applicable
```

## Currency Rules

```text
Currencies live in wallet.
Wallet shows amount and source.
Currency has cap if needed.
Some currencies are character-bound.
Some currencies are account-wide.
Some currencies expire after season.
```

Example:

```text
Gold: 12,450
Harthmere Favor: 320
Arena Marks: 75
Spring Festival Tokens: 18, expires in 12 days
```

---

# 16. Spell Inventory / Spellbook System

Magic spells should not behave exactly like normal items.

The player should have a **Spellbook**.

```text
The Spellbook stores learned spells, abilities, magical techniques, passive powers, and unlocked rituals.
```

Spells can be learned from:

```text
Leveling up
Class trainers
Spell scrolls
Quest rewards
Boss drops
Faction vendors
Ancient books
Skill trees
Crafted tomes
Hidden teachers
Magical artifacts
```

---

## Spellbook Rules

A spell entry should include:

```text
Spell ID
Name
School of magic
Rank
Description
Mana cost
Cooldown
Cast time
Range
Area type
Damage/healing/effect
Scaling stat
Required level
Required class
Required weapon/focus
Required reagent, if any
PvP rules
Upgrade path
Known/unknown status
Equipped/not equipped status
```

---

## Spell Categories

```text
Damage Spells
Healing Spells
Buff Spells
Debuff Spells
Crowd Control Spells
Summoning Spells
Teleport Spells
Utility Spells
Crafting Spells
Transformation Spells
Passive Spells
Ultimate Spells
Ritual Spells
```

---

## Spell Schools

```text
Fire
Ice
Lightning
Earth
Water
Wind
Arcane
Holy
Dark
Nature
Poison
Blood
Illusion
Necromancy
Summoning
Time
Space
Spirit
```

---

# 17. Learning Spells

Spells can be learned in different ways.

## Scroll-Based Learning

```text
Player obtains spell scroll.
Player uses scroll.
Server checks requirements.
If valid, spell is added to Spellbook.
Scroll is consumed.
```

Example:

```text
Scroll of Fireball
Requires Level 8
Requires Mage, Sorcerer, or Spellblade
On use: teaches Fireball Rank 1
```

## Trainer-Based Learning

```text
Player visits trainer.
Trainer lists available spells.
Player pays gold or currency.
Spell is added to Spellbook.
```

## Quest-Based Learning

```text
Complete special quest.
NPC teaches spell.
Spell appears in Spellbook.
```

## Discovery-Based Learning

```text
Player finds ancient rune.
Solves puzzle.
Unlocks hidden spell.
```

---

# 18. Spell Equip / Action Bar Rules

Players should not always be able to cast every known spell at once unless your design allows it.

Recommended system:

```text
Players can know many spells.
Players equip a limited number of active spells to their action bar.
Passive spells may be always active or slotted separately.
```

Example:

```text
Known Spells: 80
Active Spell Slots: 8
Passive Slots: 3
Ultimate Slot: 1
```

This creates build choices.

---

## Spell Slot Types

```text
Basic Spell Slot
Utility Spell Slot
Defensive Spell Slot
Ultimate Spell Slot
Passive Spell Slot
Profession Spell Slot
Mount/Pet Ability Slot
```

---

## Spell Use Rules

A spell can be cast only if:

```text
Player knows the spell.
Spell is equipped or accessible.
Player meets class/level/faction requirements.
Player has enough mana/resource.
Spell is not on cooldown.
Player is not silenced/stunned/dead.
Target is valid.
Target is in range.
Line of sight is valid, if required.
Required reagent is present, if any.
Zone allows the spell.
PvP rules allow the spell.
```

---

# 19. Spell Items vs Learned Spells

Some magical things are items, not learned spells.

## Spell Scroll

```text
One-time-use item.
Casts spell once or teaches spell permanently.
Stored in inventory.
Consumed on use.
```

## Spellbook Entry

```text
Permanent learned ability.
Stored in Spellbook.
Not a normal inventory item.
```

## Rune

```text
Can modify spells, equipment, or abilities.
Stored in inventory or rune collection.
```

## Magic Artifact

```text
Equippable item that grants or modifies spells.
Stored in inventory/equipment.
```

Example:

```text
Ring of Embers
Equipped effect:
Fireball deals +10% damage.
Unlocks Flame Shield while equipped.
```

---

# 20. Runes and Spell Modifiers

Runes can customize spells.

Rune examples:

```text
Increase damage
Reduce mana cost
Increase range
Add burn effect
Change element
Reduce cooldown
Increase area size
Add healing effect
Split projectile
Change spell appearance
```

Rules:

```text
Each spell has limited rune slots.
Runes may be removable or permanent.
Rare runes may require a mage NPC to socket.
Some runes conflict with each other.
PvP may normalize rune effects.
```

Example:

```text
Fireball Rune Slots:
- Power Rune
- Effect Rune
- Shape Rune

Equipped:
Power Rune: +12% damage
Effect Rune: Adds burn
Shape Rune: Smaller explosion, faster projectile
```

---

# 21. Hotbar / Action Bar

The player needs quick access to items and spells.

## Hotbar Can Hold

```text
Spells
Skills
Consumables
Mounts
Pets
Tools
Emotes
Quest items
Profession abilities
Equipment swap sets
```

## Hotbar Rules

```text
If item stack reaches 0, hotbar slot becomes empty or grayed out.
If spell is removed from build, hotbar slot grays out.
If item is on cooldown, show cooldown overlay.
If spell lacks mana, show unavailable state.
If target invalid, show unusable feedback.
```

---

# 22. Item Use Rules

When the player uses an item:

```text
1. Server checks player owns item.
2. Server checks item is usable.
3. Server checks item location is valid.
4. Server checks cooldown.
5. Server checks combat restrictions.
6. Server checks target.
7. Server applies item effect.
8. Server reduces stack or removes item.
9. Server updates inventory.
10. Client updates UI.
```

Never trust the client to say an item was used successfully.

---

# 23. Item Cooldowns

Items and spells can have cooldowns.

## Cooldown Types

```text
Individual cooldown
Shared category cooldown
Global cooldown
Combat-only cooldown
Out-of-combat cooldown
Daily cooldown
Weekly cooldown
Charges
```

Example:

```text
Health Potion:
60-second potion category cooldown.

Teleport Stone:
30-minute cooldown.

Resurrection Scroll:
1-hour cooldown.
Cannot be used in ranked PvP.
```

---

# 24. Charges

Items can have charges.

```text
Current Charges
Max Charges
Recharge Rule
```

Example:

```text
Wand of Sparks
Charges: 20 / 20
Consumes 1 charge per use.
Can be recharged by mage vendor.
```

---

# 25. Containers and Bags

Players may equip bags or containers.

## Bag Types

```text
General Bag
Herb Pouch
Ore Satchel
Potion Case
Ammo Quiver
Quest Pouch
Gem Bag
Rune Bag
Food Basket
Soul Shard Pouch
```

## Bag Rules

```text
Specialized bags can auto-store matching items.
General bags can hold most items.
Some items may be too large or special for bags.
Bags cannot be placed inside themselves.
Prevent infinite bag nesting exploits.
```

Important edge rule:

```text
A bag cannot contain itself directly or indirectly.
```

---

# 26. Bank Storage

Players need long-term storage.

## Bank Rules

```text
Bank has larger storage than backpack.
Bank is accessible from bank NPCs, cities, housing, or guild halls.
Bank can store most non-quest items.
Bound items can be banked by owner.
Account-bound items can go into shared bank.
```

Optional:

```text
Material storage tab
Currency storage tab
Cosmetic wardrobe
Pet/mount collection
Guild bank
```

---

# 27. Account-Wide Storage

Some things should be account-wide.

```text
Cosmetics
Mounts
Pets
Achievements
Some currencies
Heirloom gear
Unlocked appearances
Account-bound items
```

Rules:

```text
Account-wide items can be used by eligible characters.
Class/race/faction restrictions still apply.
Do not allow account storage to bypass binding rules unless intended.
```

---

# 28. Trading System

Players should be able to trade eligible items.

## Trade Rules

```text
Both players must be nearby.
Both players must accept trade.
Trade window locks when accepted.
Changing items or gold unlocks confirmation.
Server validates all items and currencies.
Bound, quest, locked, or expired items cannot be traded.
Trade completes atomically.
```

Atomically means:

```text
Either both players receive the trade successfully, or neither does.
```

This prevents item duplication or loss.

---

## Trade Window Should Show

```text
Items offered by Player A
Items offered by Player B
Currency offered
Warnings for unusual items
Final confirmation
Locked state
Accepted state
```

Warning examples:

```text
This item will bind to you when received.
This item is damaged.
This item expires in 2 hours.
This player changed their offer.
```

---

# 29. Auction House / Market

If the MMO has an auction house:

```text
Players can list tradeable items.
Players can search and filter items.
Server holds listed items in escrow.
Buyer pays currency.
Seller receives currency by mail or wallet.
Auction fees remove currency from economy.
```

Rules:

```text
Bound items cannot be listed.
Quest items cannot be listed.
Stolen goods may be blocked or require black market.
Expired listings return to seller.
Market should prevent duplicate listing bugs.
```

---

# 30. Vendor System

Vendors buy and sell items.

## Vendor Rules

```text
Player can buy merchant inventory.
Player can sell eligible items.
Vendor prices depend on base value and modifiers.
Vendor has buyback tab.
Vendor may refuse stolen, bound, illegal, or worthless items.
```

Buyback rule:

```text
Recently sold items appear in buyback for a limited time.
Buyback cost may equal sale price or slightly higher.
Buyback prevents accidental loss.
```

---

# 31. Item Value System

Item value can be calculated from:

```text
Base value
Item level
Quality
Rarity
Durability
Enchantments
Stack count
Demand
Merchant type
Player reputation
Regional economy
Stolen state
```

Example:

```text
Vendor Sell Price to Player = Base Value × Merchant Sell Modifier
Vendor Buy Price from Player = Base Value × Merchant Buy Modifier
```

Normal:

```text
Merchant sells at 120%.
Merchant buys at 50%.
```

---

# 32. Crafting Materials Storage

Crafting materials can overwhelm inventory.

Recommended:

```text
Use a separate crafting material storage system.
Materials automatically deposit into material storage.
Crafting stations can pull directly from storage.
```

Material categories:

```text
Ore
Wood
Herbs
Leather
Cloth
Gems
Monster parts
Fish
Cooking ingredients
Runes
Essences
Alchemy reagents
Engineering parts
```

Rules:

```text
Materials can stack very high.
Materials can be deposited all at once.
Materials should be searchable and filterable.
Rare materials may require special storage.
```

---

# 33. Keys and Access Items

Keys should not clutter bags.

Recommended:

```text
Use a Keyring.
```

Keyring stores:

```text
Dungeon keys
House keys
Gate keys
Faction passes
Attunement tokens
Access badges
Magical seals
```

Rules:

```text
Keys unlock doors, zones, chests, or quests.
Keys may be consumed or permanent.
Keyring should show where key is used.
Quest keys may disappear when quest completes.
```

---

# 34. Cosmetics and Wardrobe

Players like collecting appearances.

## Wardrobe Rules

```text
When eligible cosmetic/equipment appearance is collected, unlock it in wardrobe.
Wardrobe does not consume inventory slots.
Appearance can be applied without changing stats.
Some appearances require class, faction, achievement, or event unlock.
```

This keeps inventories clean.

---

# 35. Mount and Pet Collection

Mounts and pets should not fill normal bags after learned.

## Mount Rules

```text
Mount item may start in inventory.
Using it teaches mount.
Mount is added to collection.
Original item is consumed.
Mount can be summoned from collection.
```

## Pet Rules

```text
Pet item teaches pet.
Pet appears in collection.
Pets can be cosmetic, combat, gathering, or utility depending on game design.
```

---

# 36. Item Ownership Rules

Every item instance should have ownership data.

```text
Owner ID
Creator ID, if crafted
Original source
Bound state
Trade history, optional
Stolen state
Expiration
Durability
Enchantments
Custom name, if allowed
```

This matters for:

```text
Anti-cheat
Trading
Binding
Stolen goods
Crafting credit
Economy tracking
Rollback recovery
```

---

# 37. Stolen Goods

If your MMO has theft, items can be marked stolen.

## Stolen Item Rules

```text
Stolen items may be confiscated by guards.
Normal merchants may refuse stolen goods.
Fences may buy stolen goods at reduced value.
Stolen state may disappear after laundering.
Victim/faction may recognize famous stolen items.
```

Example:

```text
Stolen Noble Ring
Base Value: 500 gold
Normal Merchant: refuses
Fence: buys for 180 gold
Guard inspection: confiscates and fines player
```

---

# 38. Item Locking

Players should be able to lock important items.

## Lock Rules

```text
Locked items cannot be sold.
Locked items cannot be destroyed.
Locked items cannot be traded.
Locked items cannot be used as crafting material.
Locked items can still be equipped if allowed.
```

This prevents accidental mistakes.

---

# 39. Sorting, Filtering, and Search

Inventory UI must help players manage items.

## Sorting Options

```text
Sort by type
Sort by rarity
Sort by item level
Sort by value
Sort by newest
Sort by name
Sort by equipment slot
Sort by usable now
Sort by crafting material
Sort by quest relevance
```

## Filters

```text
All
Weapons
Armor
Consumables
Crafting
Quest
Junk
Magic
Trade Goods
Equipment
Usable
Sellable
Tradable
Locked
Stolen
```

## Search

Players should be able to search by:

```text
Item name
Item type
Stat
Quest name
Profession
Rarity
Source
Required level
```

---

# 40. Inventory UI Rules

Inventory UI should clearly show:

```text
Item icon
Stack count
Rarity border
Durability
Bound status
Quest marker
Locked status
Stolen marker
Cooldown
Expiration timer
Usable/equippable status
Comparison tooltip
Value
Required level/class
```

## Tooltip Should Show

```text
Name
Quality
Item type
Slot
Stats
Required level
Required class
Durability
Effects
Use effect
Equip effect
Set bonus
Sell value
Binding state
Trade rules
Quest usage
Flavor text
```

Example:

```text
Iron Longsword
Uncommon Sword
Main Hand
Damage: 18–26
Attack Speed: 2.4
+4 Strength
Durability: 42 / 50
Requires Level 5
Sell Price: 12 silver
Bind on Equip
```

---

# 41. Item Comparison

When hovering equipment, show comparison.

```text
Current equipped item vs hovered item.
Show stat increases in green.
Show stat decreases in red.
Show special effects clearly.
Account for class-relevant stats.
```

Do not compare a sword to boots. Compare only valid slots.

---

# 42. Auto-Loot and Loot Rules

Looting should be smooth.

## Loot Rules

```text
Player can loot eligible corpses/chests.
Server validates loot rights.
Items go to inventory if space exists.
Currencies go to wallet.
Materials may go to material storage.
Quest items go to quest pouch.
If inventory is full, show warning.
```

## Auto-Loot

```text
Auto-loot can be enabled.
Auto-loot should skip items filtered by player settings if allowed.
Quest items should always be collected or clearly shown.
```

---

# 43. Loot Eligibility

Loot rights can depend on:

```text
Damage contribution
Party membership
Raid rules
Quest state
First tag
Personal loot rules
Faction ownership
Chest ownership
Event participation
```

Recommended:

```text
Use personal loot for most MMO content to reduce conflict.
Use contribution-based rewards for public events.
Use group rules for dungeons and raids.
```

---

# 44. Inventory Full Rules

This is a major edge case.

When inventory is full:

```text
Do not delete the item silently.
Show warning.
Offer options.
```

Options:

```text
Send item to overflow mailbox.
Place item in temporary overflow inventory.
Prevent pickup.
Drop item on ground only if safe and intended.
Ask player to free space.
```

Recommended:

```text
Quest items should still be collectable through quest pouch.
Important rewards should go to mail/overflow if inventory is full.
```

---

# 45. Mail Attachments

Mail can hold items.

Rules:

```text
Mail items are not usable until claimed.
Mail expires after a set time.
Expired player-sent mail returns to sender.
System reward mail may last longer.
Mail attachments must be server-validated.
```

---

# 46. Destroying Items

Players can destroy eligible items.

## Destroy Rules

```text
Ask confirmation for rare or better items.
Ask confirmation for equipped items.
Ask confirmation for locked items, or block entirely.
Quest items require special warning.
Destroyed items may be recoverable through item restoration for limited time.
```

Warning:

```text
Destroy Legendary Sword?
This item cannot be recovered normally.
Type DELETE to confirm.
```

---

# 47. Dropping Items

Decide whether dropping is allowed.

## Drop Options

```text
No item dropping.
Allow dropping normal items.
Allow dropping only in housing.
Dropped items are visible to others.
Dropped items decay after time.
```

For most MMOs:

```text
Avoid free world item dropping except for special systems.
It causes clutter, exploits, and performance issues.
```

---

# 48. Item Expiration

Some items expire.

Examples:

```text
Event tokens
Temporary buffs
Summoned items
Trial gear
Borrowed quest items
Food that spoils, if survival system
```

Rules:

```text
Show expiration timer.
Warn before expiration if valuable.
Expired items are removed or transformed.
Expired quest items should not break quest permanently.
```

---

# 49. Soulbound and Unique Items

## Unique Item Rules

```text
Unique means player can only own one copy.
Unique-equipped means player can own several but equip only one.
```

Example:

```text
Unique:
Ancient Royal Seal. Player may own only one.

Unique-Equipped:
Ring of Ember. Player may own multiple but equip only one.
```

---

# 50. Item Sets

Equipment can belong to sets.

```text
2-piece bonus
4-piece bonus
6-piece bonus
Full set bonus
```

Rules:

```text
Set bonuses activate only when required pieces are equipped.
Cosmetic appearances do not count unless explicitly allowed.
Broken gear may not count if durability is 0.
```

---

# 51. Enchanting and Upgrading

Items can be improved.

## Upgrade Types

```text
Enchantments
Runes
Gems
Reforging
Level upgrades
Quality upgrades
Stat rerolls
Transmutation
Infusion
Durability reinforcement
```

Rules:

```text
Upgrades should be server-validated.
Some upgrades may bind the item.
Some upgrades may fail.
Failure should not destroy expensive items unless clearly warned.
Upgrade history should be stored on item instance.
```

---

# 52. Crafting Integration

Inventory should connect to crafting.

Crafting uses:

```text
Materials
Recipes
Tools
Stations
Skill level
Profession level
Optional reagents
Gold/currency
Time
```

Rules:

```text
Crafting should pull from backpack and material storage.
Locked items cannot be consumed.
Quest items cannot be consumed unless required.
Server validates all materials before crafting.
Result item appears in inventory or overflow.
```

---

# 53. Action Restrictions

Some actions cannot happen in certain states.

## Player Cannot Manage Inventory Fully While

```text
Dead
Stunned
Trading
In cutscene
Loading
Teleporting
Being inspected by guard
During certain PvP combat
While casting
While looting another container
```

But basic viewing can still be allowed.

---

# 54. Combat Restrictions

Inventory use during combat must be controlled.

Examples:

```text
Health potions usable in combat.
Food not usable in combat.
Gear swapping may be blocked in combat.
Mount summoning blocked in combat.
Teleport item blocked in combat.
Quest item use allowed only if quest requires it.
```

Recommended:

```text
Block equipment changes during combat unless the game specifically supports weapon swapping.
Allow consumables with cooldowns.
```

---

# 55. Death and Inventory

Decide what happens on death.

Common MMO approach:

```text
Player keeps inventory.
Equipment loses durability.
Some temporary items may be lost.
Hardcore zones may drop special loot.
```

Optional harsh rules:

```text
Drop carried gold.
Drop trade goods.
Drop stolen goods.
Drop PvP loot.
Corpse retrieval required.
```

For a broad MMO, avoid punishing normal players too much.

---

# 56. Inventory Security and Server Authority

The server must own inventory truth.

The client can request:

```text
Move item
Use item
Equip item
Sell item
Buy item
Trade item
Destroy item
Split stack
Merge stack
Craft item
Learn spell
Cast spell
Deposit item
Withdraw item
```

The server decides whether the action is valid.

Never trust the client for:

```text
Item ownership
Stack count
Currency amount
Item stats
Quest item possession
Spell knowledge
Cooldowns
Trade state
Vendor price
Crafting materials
```

---

# 57. Inventory Action Pipeline

Every item action should follow this:

```text
1. Receive player request.
2. Validate player exists and is active.
3. Validate item exists.
4. Validate player owns item or has access.
5. Validate item is in correct location.
6. Validate action is allowed.
7. Validate restrictions: combat, binding, cooldown, level, class, trade, quest.
8. Apply action atomically.
9. Update inventory state.
10. Log transaction.
11. Send UI update to client.
```

---

# 58. Atomic Inventory Operations

Inventory changes must be atomic.

Example:

```text
Crafting consumes 5 ore and creates 1 sword.
```

Bad:

```text
Server removes ore.
Server crashes before adding sword.
Player loses materials.
```

Good:

```text
Server performs remove materials + create sword in one transaction.
If any part fails, nothing changes.
```

Atomic operations are mandatory for:

```text
Trading
Crafting
Auction purchases
Vendor buy/sell
Mail attachments
Loot claims
Stack splitting
Quest rewards
Bank transfers
```

---

# 59. Inventory Logs

Every important inventory change should be logged.

Log:

```text
Player ID
Item ID
Item instance ID
Action type
Source inventory
Destination inventory
Quantity
Timestamp
Currency change
Trade partner, if any
Vendor, if any
Quest, if any
Before/after state
```

This helps with:

```text
Bug recovery
Dupe detection
Player support
Economy analysis
Rollback
Anti-cheat
```

---

# 60. Item Data Structure

Use item definitions plus item instances.

## Item Definition

This is the template.

```json
{
  "id": "iron_longsword",
  "name": "Iron Longsword",
  "category": "weapon",
  "subtype": "sword",
  "quality": "uncommon",
  "slot": "main_hand",
  "stackable": false,
  "max_stack": 1,
  "required_level": 5,
  "allowed_classes": ["warrior", "paladin", "ranger"],
  "base_value": 120,
  "bind_type": "bind_on_equip",
  "durability_max": 50,
  "stats": {
    "attack_points": 18,
    "strength": 4
  },
  "damage": {
    "min": 18,
    "max": 26,
    "type": "slashing",
    "attack_speed": 2.4
  },
  "use_effect": null,
  "equip_effects": [],
  "description": "A reliable iron blade used by village guards and young adventurers."
}
```

## Item Instance

This is the specific copy the player owns.

```json
{
  "instance_id": "item_8f31a91",
  "item_id": "iron_longsword",
  "owner_id": "player_123",
  "location": "backpack",
  "slot_index": 4,
  "quantity": 1,
  "durability": 42,
  "bound": false,
  "stolen": false,
  "locked": false,
  "created_by": null,
  "enchantments": [],
  "expiration": null,
  "custom_name": null
}
```

---

# 61. Spell Definition Structure

```json
{
  "id": "fireball_rank_1",
  "name": "Fireball",
  "school": "fire",
  "category": "damage",
  "rank": 1,
  "required_level": 8,
  "allowed_classes": ["mage", "sorcerer", "spellblade"],
  "mana_cost": 35,
  "cast_time_seconds": 2.0,
  "cooldown_seconds": 6,
  "range": 30,
  "requires_line_of_sight": true,
  "can_crit": true,
  "damage": {
    "type": "fire",
    "base": 120,
    "scaling_stat": "spell_power",
    "scaling_ratio": 0.8
  },
  "effects": [
    {
      "type": "burn",
      "duration_seconds": 6,
      "tick_damage": 12
    }
  ],
  "description": "Hurls a ball of fire at the enemy, dealing fire damage and burning the target."
}
```

---

# 62. Player Spellbook Structure

```json
{
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "known_spells": [
    {
      "spell_id": "fireball_rank_1",
      "learned_at": "2026-05-12T18:00:00Z",
      "source": "scroll",
      "equipped_slot": "action_bar_1",
      "runes": ["minor_burn_rune"]
    }
  ],
  "active_spell_slots": {
    "slot_1": "fireball_rank_1",
    "slot_2": "frost_barrier_rank_1",
    "slot_3": null
  },
  "passive_slots": {
    "passive_1": "flame_affinity_rank_1"
  }
}
```

---

# 63. Player Inventory Structure

```json
{
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "backpack": {
    "max_slots": 32,
    "items": [
      {
        "slot": 0,
        "instance_id": "item_8f31a91"
      },
      {
        "slot": 1,
        "instance_id": "item_91ff001"
      }
    ]
  },
  "equipment": {
    "head": null,
    "chest": "item_chest_001",
    "main_hand": "item_8f31a91",
    "off_hand": null,
    "ring_1": "item_ring_001",
    "ring_2": null
  },
  "quest_pouch": [
    "quest_item_old_bridge_satchel"
  ],
  "currency_wallet": {
    "gold": 12450,
    "harthmere_favor": 320,
    "arena_marks": 75
  },
  "material_storage": {
    "iron_ore": 128,
    "peacebloom": 42,
    "wolf_hide": 19
  },
  "keyring": [
    "harthmere_town_gate_key",
    "old_mill_cellar_key"
  ]
}
```

---

# 64. Inventory Edge Cases

## Item Picked Up When Inventory Is Full

Rule:

```text
If normal item: block pickup or send to overflow.
If quest item: put in quest pouch.
If currency: put in wallet.
If crafting material: put in material storage.
If reward: send to mail/overflow if needed.
Never silently delete it.
```

---

## Player Uses Last Item in Stack

Rule:

```text
Reduce quantity by 1.
If quantity reaches 0, remove stack.
Hotbar slot should gray out or clear.
```

---

## Player Splits Stack

Rule:

```text
Server validates stack size.
New stack must fit in empty slot.
Cannot split locked or invalid stack if rules forbid it.
```

---

## Player Merges Stacks

Rule:

```text
Items must be stack-compatible.
Do not exceed max stack size.
Leave remainder in original stack if needed.
```

---

## Player Equips Bound-on-Equip Item

Rule:

```text
Show warning:
"Equipping this item will bind it to you."
If confirmed, bind item and equip it.
```

---

## Player Sells Rare Item by Accident

Rule:

```text
Rare+ items require confirmation or go to buyback.
Locked items cannot be sold.
```

---

## Player Deletes Quest Item

Rule:

```text
Usually block deletion.
If deletion allowed, warn heavily and provide reacquire method.
```

---

## Player Trades Item Then Disconnects

Rule:

```text
Trade cancels.
Items remain with original owners.
No partial trade can occur.
```

---

## Server Crashes During Trade

Rule:

```text
Atomic transaction prevents partial item movement.
On recovery, trade is either completed fully or rolled back fully.
```

---

## Player Learns Same Spell Twice

Rule:

```text
If already known, block use or convert scroll to currency/material.
Show message: "You already know this spell."
```

---

## Player Loses Required Spell Reagent

Rule:

```text
Spell cannot be cast.
Tooltip should say missing reagent.
```

---

## Spell Removed From Class

Rule:

```text
If game update removes spell, refund cost or replace with equivalent.
Do not leave broken hotbar slot without explanation.
```

---

## Equipment Swapped During Combat

Rule:

```text
Block gear changes during combat unless weapon swapping is a supported feature.
```

---

## Player Attempts to Use Item While Dead

Rule:

```text
Only resurrection-related items may be usable.
All other use actions fail.
```

---

## Item Expires While Equipped

Rule:

```text
Unequip item.
Remove or transform item.
Update player stats immediately.
Notify player.
```

---

## Bag Contains Items and Player Removes Bag

Rule:

```text
Do not allow removing a bag if it contains items unless items can be moved elsewhere.
```

---

## Bag Inside Bag Exploit

Rule:

```text
Prevent recursive storage.
A bag cannot contain itself or any parent bag.
```

---

## Duplicate Item Request

Rule:

```text
Use server-side transaction IDs.
Ignore duplicate client requests.
Prevent double-use and double-reward bugs.
```

---

# 65. Inventory UX Rules

Good inventory should be fast and readable.

The player should be able to:

```text
Sort inventory.
Search inventory.
Filter by item type.
Lock important items.
Compare equipment.
See what is new.
Sell junk quickly.
Deposit all materials.
Repair all equipment.
Open all containers.
Track quest items.
See item source.
See if item is used in crafting.
See if item is safe to sell.
```

Useful buttons:

```text
Sort
Search
Sell Junk
Deposit Materials
Repair All
Lock Item
Unlock Item
Show Usable
Show Upgrades
Show Quest Items
Show New Items
```

---

# 66. Inventory Assistance Rules

The system should help the player understand items.

Examples:

```text
"This item is better than your equipped sword."
"This item is used in Blacksmithing."
"This item is required for an active quest."
"This item can be safely sold."
"This item teaches a spell you do not know."
"You already know this spell."
"This item is stolen."
"This item will bind if equipped."
"This item expires soon."
```

---

# 67. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this into your MMO design prompt:

```text
Design a full inventory, item, equipment, and spell system for an MMO.

The system must support:
- Backpack inventory
- Equipment slots
- Spellbook
- Hotbar/action bar
- Quest item pouch
- Currency wallet
- Crafting material storage
- Keyring
- Bank
- Shared account storage
- Vendor buy/sell
- Trading
- Auction house
- Mail attachments
- Item locking
- Sorting, filtering, and searching

Every item must define:
- Item ID
- Name
- Category
- Subtype
- Quality
- Stackable status
- Max stack
- Required level
- Required class/faction/race, if any
- Binding rules
- Trade rules
- Sell value
- Durability
- Stats
- Use effects
- Equip effects
- Quest usage
- Expiration
- Stolen status, if applicable

Every item instance must track:
- Instance ID
- Owner ID
- Location
- Slot
- Quantity
- Durability
- Bound state
- Locked state
- Stolen state
- Enchantments
- Expiration
- Creator, if crafted

Spells should be stored in a Spellbook, not normal backpack inventory after learned.
Spells can be learned from scrolls, trainers, quests, bosses, books, runes, artifacts, or discovery.
Players can know many spells but may equip a limited number to active spell slots.
Spells must check level, class, resource cost, cooldown, target, range, line of sight, silence/stun/death state, reagents, and zone rules before casting.

Quest items should go into a separate Quest Item Pouch when possible.
Currencies should go into a wallet.
Crafting materials should go into material storage.
Keys should go into a keyring.
Mounts, pets, and cosmetics should unlock collections after use instead of permanently filling inventory.

The server must be authoritative.
The client can request inventory actions, but the server validates ownership, item state, stack count, currency, cooldowns, trade rules, vendor prices, quest item status, crafting materials, and spell knowledge.

All inventory operations must be atomic:
- Trading
- Crafting
- Buying
- Selling
- Mailing
- Looting
- Equipping
- Splitting stacks
- Merging stacks
- Learning spells
- Claiming quest rewards

The system must handle edge cases:
- Inventory full
- Quest item missing
- Item expires
- Item is sold by accident
- Player disconnects during trade
- Server crash during inventory transaction
- Duplicate client requests
- Player tries to use item while dead
- Player tries to equip invalid gear
- Player already knows spell
- Player tries to trade bound item
- Player tries to sell locked item
- Player tries to put a bag inside itself
- Player receives reward with full inventory
- Player abandons quest with quest items
- Player uses last item in stack
- Player attempts to exploit stack splitting or merging

The inventory UI must clearly show:
- Item icon
- Stack count
- Rarity
- Durability
- Bound status
- Locked status
- Stolen status
- Quest item marker
- Expiration timer
- Cooldown
- Usable/equippable state
- Tooltip with stats and requirements
- Comparison against equipped gear
- Search, sorting, and filtering options

The system should always help the player understand what an item is for, whether it is safe to sell, whether it teaches a spell, whether it is used in a quest, whether it is better than current gear, and whether using or equipping it has consequences.
```

---

# Final Inventory Rule

Every item, spell, currency, key, material, and reward should answer:

```text
Where is it stored?
What can the player do with it?
Can it stack?
Can it be used?
Can it be equipped?
Can it be traded?
Can it be sold?
Can it be destroyed?
Can it be lost?
Is it needed for a quest?
Is it safe to remove?
What happens if the player uses it?
What happens if the inventory is full?
```

If the system can answer those questions, the inventory will feel reliable instead of frustrating.

Below is a full **Quest / Mission Implementation System** for an MMO.

The goal is:

```text id="qv7t3n"
Players should always know:
- What quests they have
- Which quests are available nearby
- Where to go next
- What the next objective is
- How to complete the quest
- What progress they have made
- What rewards they will receive
- Whether the quest is blocked, failed, completed, or ready to turn in
```

The system should help players without making the world feel fake or overly hand-holdy.

---

# MMO Quest / Mission System Overview

Every quest should have:

```text id="t3f1f7"
Quest ID
Quest title
Quest type
Quest giver
Quest receiver
Quest location
Quest level
Quest difficulty
Quest chain
Quest objectives
Quest progress
Quest hints
Quest markers
Rewards
Failure conditions
Completion conditions
Quest status
Map and compass guidance
Journal entry
Dialogue states
Party sharing rules
Replay rules
Abandon/restart rules
```

---

# 1. Quest Design Rule

Every quest must answer:

```text id="jykz5g"
Who gives the quest?
Why does the quest exist?
What does the player need to do?
Where does the player need to go?
How does the player know they are making progress?
What happens when they complete it?
What happens if they fail, abandon, or leave the area?
What reward do they receive?
What changes in the world afterward?
```

Bad quest design:

```text id="s8oqha"
"Find the thing."

No location.
No clue.
No marker.
No progress tracker.
No turn-in target.
No explanation.
No fallback if the object does not spawn.
```

Good quest design:

```text id="q69qno"
"Find Mira's lost satchel near the old bridge east of town."

The map marks the search area.
The compass points east.
The quest journal explains the clue.
The satchel glows subtly when nearby.
Progress updates when collected.
The quest tells the player to return to Mira.
Mira has a turn-in icon above her head.
```

---

# 2. Quest Status System

Every quest should have a clear status.

## Quest Statuses

```text id="cyuw9t"
Unavailable
Available
Accepted
In Progress
Objective Complete
Ready to Turn In
Completed
Failed
Abandoned
Expired
Locked
Repeatable Available
Repeatable Completed
Paused
Blocked
```

---

## Status Meaning

### Unavailable

The quest exists but the player cannot see or accept it yet.

Reasons:

```text id="aoacq7"
Wrong level
Wrong faction
Wrong class
Wrong reputation
Previous quest not complete
World event not active
NPC dead or missing
Area not discovered
```

---

### Available

The player can accept the quest.

Visual indicators:

```text id="ozq9e9"
Quest giver has an icon above their head.
Quest appears on the nearby quest list.
Quest appears on the map if discovered.
Quest appears on town notice board if public.
```

---

### Accepted

The player has taken the quest.

System should immediately show:

```text id="wlkpqh"
Quest title
Current objective
Map marker
Compass direction
Journal entry
Reward preview
Optional hints
```

---

### In Progress

The player is actively working on the quest.

System should show:

```text id="zcdgu7"
Current step
Progress count
Target location
Distance to objective
Required items or enemies
Optional objectives
Failure risks
```

Example:

```text id="ts08lv"
Collect Wolf Pelts: 3 / 8
Search Area: North Pine Woods
Optional: Do not kill the white wolf
```

---

### Objective Complete

The current objective is complete, but the quest may have another step.

Example:

```text id="ca0pqe"
Objective Complete:
Collected 8 / 8 Wolf Pelts

Next Objective:
Return to Tanner Brigg in Harthmere.
```

---

### Ready to Turn In

The quest is complete and needs to be handed in.

System should show:

```text id="qk0g1g"
Turn-in NPC
Turn-in location
Map icon
Compass marker
Reward choices
```

---

### Completed

Quest has been finished and rewards have been given.

System should record:

```text id="s31p9l"
Completion date
Reward received
Choices made
NPC relationship changes
Reputation changes
World changes
Next quest unlocked
```

---

### Failed

Quest failed due to conditions.

Examples:

```text id="z8it0z"
Escort NPC died.
Timer expired.
Player killed protected target.
Player chose opposite faction.
Quest item was destroyed.
```

System should explain:

```text id="cyjqqm"
Why it failed
Whether it can be restarted
Who to talk to next
What changed because of failure
```

---

### Abandoned

Player manually dropped the quest.

Rules:

```text id="x3mazd"
Remove quest from active tracker.
Remove temporary quest items if required.
Reset objective progress if not persistent.
```
