```text id="x3mazd"
Allow restart if quest supports it.
Keep world consequences if already triggered.
```

---

### Blocked

Quest cannot currently progress.

Examples:

```text id="r9tmxr"
Target NPC is dead.
Required world event is inactive.
Dungeon is locked.
Party member owns required object.
Quest item missing due to bug.
Player is in wrong phase.
```

System should never leave the player confused.

Show:

```text id="d0f721"
"Quest currently blocked: The gate is locked until nightfall."
"Quest currently blocked: You must complete 'A Guard's Trust' first."
"Quest currently blocked: Your party leader is in a different quest phase."
```

---

# 3. Quest Discovery System

Players should be helped in finding quests.

## Ways Players Find Quests

```text id="p0aaih"
NPC quest icons
Town notice boards
Map markers
Rumors from NPCs
Exploration discoveries
Faction boards
Guild halls
Item-triggered quests
World events
Companion suggestions
Dynamic local alerts
Mail/messages
Road signs
Bards/news criers
Quest radar for nearby opportunities
```

---

## Nearby Quest Helper

The UI should include a nearby quest helper.

Example:

```text id="tks7nt"
Nearby Quests:
- Tanner Brigg needs help at the tannery, 42m west.
- Guard Captain Helra is recruiting at the barracks, 115m north.
- A notice board has new monster contracts, 30m east.
```

Rules:

```text id="xaq57d"
Show nearby quests within town or region.
Sort by distance, level, importance, and relevance.
Do not overload the player with too many markers.
Let players filter quest types.
```

---

## Quest Availability Icons

Use clear icons.

```text id="lg4stk"
Gold ! = Main story quest available
Blue ! = Side quest available
Green ! = Profession/crafting quest available
Red ! = Combat/bounty quest available
Purple ! = Faction quest available
Silver ! = Repeatable quest available
Gold ? = Main story quest ready to turn in
Blue ? = Side quest ready to turn in
Gray ! = Quest unavailable but visible
Clock icon = Timed quest
Group icon = Party quest
Skull icon = Dangerous quest
Dungeon door icon = Dungeon quest
```

---

# 4. Quest Journal System

The player should always have access to a quest journal.

## Quest Journal Sections

```text id="lzhk9n"
Tracked Quests
Main Story
Side Quests
Faction Quests
Daily / Weekly Quests
Profession Quests
Dungeon Quests
Raid Quests
PvP Quests
Completed Quests
Failed Quests
Nearby Available Quests
Recommended Next Quests
```

---

## Quest Journal Entry

Each quest entry should show:

```text id="fv3e1b"
Quest title
Quest type
Quest level
Difficulty
Current status
Current objective
Objective progress
Quest giver
Turn-in NPC
Location
Map button
Track/untrack button
Abandon button
Share button, if allowed
Reward preview
Story summary
Hints
Failure conditions
Party status
Time remaining, if timed
```

---

## Example Journal Entry

```text id="w0qlm8"
Quest: Wolves at the North Fence
Type: Side Quest
Level: 6
Status: In Progress

Summary:
Farmer Kell says wolves have been attacking livestock near the northern fence.

Current Objective:
Defeat wolves near the northern fence.

Progress:
Wolves defeated: 3 / 6

Location:
North Field, Harthmere

Next Step:
Follow the road north from the village gate. The wolves roam near the broken fence.

Rewards:
120 XP
35 gold
+50 Harthmere Likeability
Choice of leather gloves or farmer's cloak
```

---

# 5. Active Quest Tracker

The player should have a screen-side quest tracker.

## Tracker Rules

```text id="72bvx7"
Show 3 to 5 tracked quests by default.
Allow players to pin/unpin quests.
Auto-track newly accepted important quests.
Auto-switch tracking when entering a quest area.
Show progress updates immediately.
Do not cover too much screen space.
```

---

## Tracker Example

```text id="cmglhv"
Tracked Quests

Wolves at the North Fence
Defeat wolves: 3 / 6
Distance: 84m north

The Missing Satchel
Search near the old bridge
Distance: 210m east

A Blacksmith's Favor
Bring Iron Ore: 5 / 10
```

---

# 6. Map Guidance System

The map should guide players clearly.

## Quest Map Marker Types

```text id="vojv3w"
Exact location marker
Search area circle
Route marker
Turn-in marker
Quest giver marker
Objective item marker
Enemy spawn zone marker
Dungeon entrance marker
Escort route marker
Patrol zone marker
Hidden clue marker
```

---

## Exact Location Marker

Use when the objective has a precise location.

Example:

```text id="s0hpl8"
Talk to Guard Captain Helra.
Return to Mira.
Enter the Old Mill.
Open the sealed chest.
```

---

## Search Area Circle

Use when the player should explore.

Example:

```text id="z8d7yz"
Search the woods for tracks.
Find the missing child near the river.
Look for clues in the abandoned farm.
```

Rules:

```text id="7zqqh6"
The search circle should be large enough to preserve exploration.
The journal should include clues.
The object should become easier to notice when nearby.
```

---

## Route Marker

Use when the player needs a path.

Example:

```text id="kxmfp2"
Escort caravan along the western road.
Follow the guard patrol.
Deliver medicine to the mountain village.
```

Show:

```text id="h2agla"
Start point
Path direction
Major checkpoints
Destination
Danger areas
```

---

# 7. Compass and Direction System

The compass should help the player move toward objectives.

## Compass Rules

```text id="jtzcdt"
Show tracked quest objectives on compass.
Show distance to objective.
Use icon matching the quest type.
Fade markers when too far away if needed.
Show height difference if objective is above or below player.
```

Example:

```text id="ijzoc9"
Quest marker: 142m east
Turn-in marker: 86m south
Objective above player: upward arrow
Objective below player: downward arrow
```

---

# 8. Objective System

Every quest is made of objectives.

## Objective Types

```text id="ezzqmg"
Talk to NPC
Kill enemies
Collect items
Deliver item
Escort NPC
Defend location
Explore area
Discover landmark
Craft item
Use item
Read object
Solve puzzle
Follow tracks
Investigate clues
Gather resources
Survive attack
Capture target
Heal NPC
Repair object
Build structure
Sneak into area
Steal item
Persuade NPC
Choose dialogue outcome
Win PvP match
Complete dungeon
Defeat boss
```

---

## Objective Data

Each objective should define:

```text id="v1rtag"
Objective ID
Objective text
Objective type
Target entity or area
Required count
Current count
Location marker
Completion trigger
Optional status
Failure rules
Hint text
Party sharing rules
```

---

## Objective Example

```json id="sa5y8c"
{
  "id": "obj_collect_wolf_pelts",
  "type": "collect_item",
  "text": "Collect Wolf Pelts",
  "target_item": "wolf_pelt",
  "required_count": 8,
  "current_count": 3,
  "location": "north_pine_woods",
  "map_marker_type": "search_area",
  "hint": "Wolves roam near the broken fence north of Harthmere.",
  "party_shared": true
}
```

# 9. Multi-Step Quest Structure

Quests should support multiple stages.

Example:

```text id="k7241q"
Quest: The Missing Heirloom

Stage 1:
Talk to Elder Marrin.

Stage 2:
Search the abandoned farmhouse.

Stage 3:
Find clues near the broken window.

Stage 4:
Defeat the thieves in the cellar.

Stage 5:
Recover the heirloom.

Stage 6:
Return to Elder Marrin.

Stage 7:
Choose whether to reveal the thief was his nephew.
```

Each stage should update:

```text id="jgnvvg"
Journal
Tracker
Map marker
Compass marker
NPC dialogue
Available choices
Quest status
```

---

# 10. Quest Assistance System

The game should always assist players, but assistance can scale.

## Assistance Levels

```text id="q21tf9"
Minimal
Standard
Detailed
Full Guidance
```

### Minimal

```text id="il0ai1"
Only journal clues and rough map area.
Good for exploration-heavy players.
```

### Standard

```text id="3aai4z"
Map markers, compass, quest tracker, basic hints.
Recommended default.
```

### Detailed

```text id="v37z94"
Shows exact route, stronger hints, nearby objective highlighting.
Good for casual players.
```

### Full Guidance

```text id="mju95h"
Shows exact objective path, step-by-step instructions, item highlights, NPC directions.
Good for accessibility.
```

Players should be able to change this.

---

## Hint Escalation

If a player is stuck, the game should help more.

```text id="0sc00a"
After 2 minutes near wrong area: show subtle hint.
After 5 minutes with no progress: show clearer hint.
After 10 minutes: offer exact guidance.
After repeated failure: suggest party, better gear, or different quest.
```

Example:

```text id="ooex7t"
Subtle:
"The satchel should be somewhere near the old bridge."

Clear:
"Search beneath the broken cart near the east side of the bridge."

Exact:
"The satchel is under the broken cart 12 meters ahead."
```

---

# 11. Quest Recommendation System

The game should suggest what to do next.

## Recommended Quest Logic

Recommend based on:

```text id="vofsbj"
Player level
Current location
Quest chain progress
Main story progress
Faction reputation
Available rewards
Party size
Player class
Profession
Distance
Difficulty
Time remaining
Daily/weekly availability
```

---

## Recommendation Examples

```text id="8od4bz"
Recommended Next:
- Continue Main Story: Speak to Captain Helra at the barracks.
- Nearby Side Quest: Help Farmer Kell at the north field.
- Good Reward: Complete "Iron for the Forge" for new gloves.
- Too Difficult: "The Bone Pit" is 5 levels above you.
```

---

# 12. Quest Difficulty Rules

Quest difficulty should be clear.

## Difficulty Labels

```text id="mgwxds"
Trivial
Easy
Normal
Hard
Elite
Group
Dungeon
Raid
PvP
Timed
Stealth
Puzzle
```

## Difficulty Display

```text id="7byo0o"
Green = easy
White = normal
Orange = hard
Red = very hard
Purple = elite/group
Skull = deadly
```

The system should warn players.

Example:

```text id="m1vpuh"
Warning:
This is a group quest recommended for 3 players.
```

# 13. Quest Rewards

Every quest should clearly show rewards.

## Reward Types

```text id="c61n64"
XP
Gold
Items
Gear choice
Reputation
Likeability
Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction access
Title
Mount
Pet
Crafting recipe
Skill unlock
Area access
Story choice
Housing item
Cosmetic
Currency
Achievement
```

---

## Reward Preview Rule

Before accepting, show:

```text id="j0mpfy"
Base rewards
Optional rewards
Choice rewards
Reputation changes
Risk of legal or faction consequences
```

Example:

```text id="o9knd5"
Rewards:
+500 XP
+75 gold
+100 Harthmere Likeability
Choose one:
- Iron Dagger
- Leather Boots
- Healing Charm
```

---

# 14. Quest Turn-In System

When a quest is ready to turn in:

```text id="tyuk7x"
Show turn-in marker on map.
Show turn-in NPC on compass.
Update tracker to "Return to..."
NPC has question mark icon.
Dialogue opens to completion state.
Rewards are shown before final confirmation.
```

Example:

```text id="hp6ihn"
Ready to Turn In:
Return to Farmer Kell at the north field.
Distance: 92m south.
```

---

# 15. Quest Sharing and Party Rules

MMOs need quest sharing.

## Party Quest Rules

```text id="vye1zq"
Party members can share eligible quests.
Party members must meet level/faction requirements.
Progress may be shared for kills and events.
Collection items may be individual or shared depending on design.
Quest choices may be individual unless party leader controls instance.
```

---

## Shared Progress Rules

```text id="mnygt2"
Kill objective:
All nearby eligible party members get credit.

Collect objective:
Either each player must collect their own item or one item counts for all, depending on quest.

Escort objective:
All nearby members receive progress.

Dialogue objective:
Usually individual unless in shared instance.

Dungeon objective:
All members get credit if present and eligible.
```

---

## Party Edge Cases

```text id="n88ymy"
Player joins party after objective completion:
Do not give retroactive progress unless quest allows it.

Player is too far away:
No shared progress.

Player is dead:
May receive credit if contributed recently.

Player is in different phase:
Warn party and provide phase sync option.

Player has already completed quest:
Can help but receives no duplicate main reward unless repeatable.
```

---

# 16. Quest Phasing and World State

Quests may change the world for a player.

Examples:

```text id="dactyi"
Village before attack
Village under attack
Village after being saved
Village destroyed
Village rebuilt
```

Rules:

```text id="ckk39l"
Players in different quest phases should still be able to play together when possible.
Party phase sync should be offered.
Important NPCs should not disappear without explanation.
Map should show if objective is in a different phase.
```

---

# 17. Dynamic Quest System

Some quests can spawn based on events.

## Dynamic Quest Examples

```text id="41le8h"
Bandits attacking road
Merchant caravan needs escort
Monster nest discovered
Fire breaks out in town
Guard calls for help
Animal escapes stable
Public bounty appears
World boss awakens
```

Dynamic quests should show:

```text id="lq6e84"
Event marker
Time remaining
Recommended level
Number of players nearby
Objective
Reward
Failure condition
```

---

# 18. Daily, Weekly, and Repeatable Quests

Repeatable quests should be clear.

## Repeatable Rules

```text id="p3xqx8"
Show reset timer.
Show whether rewards are reduced after first completion.
Prevent farming exploits.
Vary objectives when possible.
Scale rewards to player level.
```

Example:

```text id="h0bp05"
Daily Quest:
Clear 5 corrupted roots.
Reset: 14h 22m
Reward: 300 XP, 25 gold, +20 Druid Circle reputation
```

---

# 19. Quest Chain System

Many quests should connect.

## Chain Rules

```text id="zw325k"
Completing one quest can unlock the next.
Quest journal should show chain progress.
Players should know when they reached the end of a chain.
Choices can branch future quests.
Failed quests can unlock alternate quests.
```

Example:

```text id="1je6yy"
Chain: Trouble at the Mill
1. Missing Grain
2. Tracks in the Mud
3. The Cellar Door
4. Bandits Below
5. Mercy or Justice
```

---

# 20. Quest Choice System

Players should have choices when appropriate.

## Choice Types

```text id="3jvoqy"
Spare or kill
Tell truth or lie
Return item or keep it
Help guards or thieves
Save one NPC or another
Take payment or refuse
Expose noble or protect secret
Use force, stealth, diplomacy, or bribery
```

Choices should affect:

```text id="y39sut"
Quest outcome
Rewards
NPC relationship
Likeability
Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction reputation
Future quests
World state
```

Example:

```text id="xeircr"
Player steals medicine from a noble to save villagers.

Villagers:
+Likeability

Nobles:
-Likeability

Legal System:
-Legal Standing

Notoriety:
Increases if public or rumored
```

---

# 21. Quest Failure System

Failure should be clear and fair.

## Failure Conditions

```text id="e4vxq7"
Timer expires
Escort NPC dies
Protected NPC dies
Player kills wrong target
Player chooses opposing faction
Player abandons required item
Event fails
Player leaves instance
Object is destroyed
Stealth quest alarm triggered
```

## Failure Feedback

```text id="2zkkoz"
Show failure message.
Explain why it failed.
Show whether it can be restarted.
Show consequences.
Remove or update map markers.
Update NPC dialogue.
```

Bad:

```text id="3wpdss"
Quest silently disappears.
```

Good:

```text id="yf3mx2"
Quest Failed:
The caravan leader was killed.

You may restart this quest tomorrow from the trade office.
Your Merchant Guild reputation decreased by 25.
```

---

# 22. Quest Abandon and Restart Rules

Players should be allowed to abandon most quests.

## Abandon Rules

```text id="6uoq0o"
Warn before abandoning.
Remove active markers.
Remove temporary quest items.
Stop timers.
Reset objective progress if appropriate.
Keep permanent consequences.
Allow reacquire from original source if allowed.
```

Warning example:

```text id="2pm1n9"
Abandon "The Missing Satchel"?
Your collected quest items will be removed.
You can restart this quest from Mira.
```

---

# 23. Quest Item Rules

Quest items need strict handling.

## Quest Item Rules

```text id="o1g905"
Quest items should be marked clearly.
Quest items should not be accidentally sold or destroyed unless allowed.
Quest items should say which quest they belong to.
If a required quest item is missing, provide recovery.
```

Examples:

```text id="4o7y47"
Quest item cannot be sold.
Quest item disappears when quest is abandoned.
Quest item can be reacquired from NPC.
Quest item has tooltip: "Used for: The Missing Satchel"
```

---

# 24. Quest Progress Tracking

Progress should be immediate and visible.

## Progress Updates

```text id="frth0j"
Show on-screen update when objective changes.
Play subtle sound.
Update journal.
Update tracker.
Update map marker.
Update NPC dialogue.
```

Example:

```text id="4whbwg"
Wolf Pelts: 4 / 8
Objective Complete: Find the old bridge.
New Objective: Search beneath the broken cart.
```

---

# 25. Objective Highlighting

Players should be assisted in seeing quest targets.

## Highlight Types

```text id="7boaoz"
Subtle glow on quest item
Nameplate icon over target enemy
Outline when nearby
Sparkle on interactable object
Footprint trail for tracking quest
Sound cue
Companion comment
Camera hint, used sparingly
```

Rules:

```text id="qck2u7"
Do not highlight everything at all times.
Increase highlighting if player is stuck.
Use accessibility settings for stronger highlights.
```

---

# 26. NPC Quest Dialogue States

Quest NPCs need different dialogue based on quest status.

## Dialogue States

```text id="qsr5oj"
Before available
Available
Accepted but not complete
Objective partially complete
Ready to turn in
Completed
Failed
Abandoned
Repeatable cooldown
Player not eligible
Player hostile/outlaw
```

Example:

```text id="6qx2ku"
Available:
"Please, traveler, my daughter is missing."

Accepted:
"Please search near the old mill. She was last seen by the river."

Partial:
"You found her scarf? Then she must be close."

Ready:
"You found her? Thank the gods!"

Completed:
"I will never forget what you did for us."

Failed:
"She is gone. Leave me."
```

---

# 27. Quest UI Warnings

The game should warn players before mistakes.

Warnings:

```text id="pkn3ie"
You are leaving the quest area.
You are entering a high-level quest zone.
This action will fail the quest.
This choice will affect your reputation.
Your party is in a different quest phase.
You do not have the required item.
This quest is timed.
This NPC is important to your active quest.
```

Example:

```text id="w1kry1"
Warning:
Attacking this NPC will fail "A Diplomatic Visit" and reduce your Legal Standing.
Continue?
```

```text id="yjq2ez"
By time remaining
```

## Search

```text id="hngzwv"
Quest title
NPC name

```

# 30. Quest Data Structure

A quest should be data-driven.

```json id="adweo2"
{
  "id": "quest_wolves_north_fence",
  "title": "Wolves at the North Fence",
  "type": "side",
  "level": 6,
  "difficulty": "normal",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "quest_giver": "npc_farmer_kell",
  "turn_in_npc": "npc_farmer_kell",
  "status": "in_progress",
  "summary": "Farmer Kell says wolves are attacking livestock near the northern fence.",
  "current_stage": 1,
  "stages": [
    {
      "stage": 1,
      "objective_text": "Defeat wolves near the northern fence.",
      "objectives": [
        {
          "id": "kill_wolves",
          "type": "kill",
          "target": "npc_forest_wolf",
          "required_count": 6,
          "current_count": 3,
          "map_marker_type": "search_area",
          "location": {
            "region": "harthmere",
            "area": "north_field",
            "x": 124.5,
            "y": 0,
            "z": -88.2,
            "radius": 45
          },
          "hint": "The wolves roam near the broken fence north of town."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "stage": 2,
      "objective_text": "Return to Farmer Kell.",
      "objectives": [
        {
          "id": "return_to_kell",
          "type": "talk",
          "target": "npc_farmer_kell",
          "required_count": 1,
          "current_count": 0,
          "map_marker_type": "exact",
          "hint": "Farmer Kell is waiting at the north field."
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "rewards": {
    "xp": 120,
    "gold": 35,
    "items": ["leather_gloves"],
    "reputation": {
      "harthmere_likeability": 50
    }
  },
  "failure_conditions": [],
  "shareable": true,
  "repeatable": false,
  "abandonable": true
}
```

---

# 31. Player Quest State Structure

The quest definition should be separate from each player’s progress.

```json id="w65opr"
{
  "player_id": "player_123",
  "quest_id": "quest_wolves_north_fence",
  "status": "in_progress",
  "accepted_at": "2026-05-12T14:32:00Z",
  "current_stage": 1,
  "tracked": true,
  "objective_progress": {
    "kill_wolves": 3
  },
  "completed_objectives": [],
  "temporary_items": [],
  "choices_made": [],
  "party_shared": true,
  "last_known_marker": {
    "region": "harthmere",
    "x": 124.5,
    "y": 0,
    "z": -88.2
  }
}
```

---

# 32. Quest Marker Data Structure

```json id="07bqgk"
{
  "quest_id": "quest_missing_sachel",
  "objective_id": "search_old_bridge",
  "marker_type": "search_area",
  "icon": "blue_question_area",
  "label": "Search near the old bridge",
  "region": "harthmere",
  "position": [210.4, 0, -42.8],
  "radius": 35,
  "visible_on_map": true,
  "visible_on_compass": true,
  "visible_in_world": false,
  "height_hint": "same_level",
  "requires_discovery": false
}
```

---

# 33. Backend / Server Rules

The server should own quest truth.

## Server Authoritative Rules

```text id="epfxbd"
Server validates quest acceptance.
Server validates objective progress.
Server validates kills, collections, dialogue, and turn-ins.
Server grants rewards.
Server prevents duplicate rewards.
Server checks eligibility.
Server stores completed quests.
Server handles party progress.
Server handles abandoned and failed quests.
```

The client can show UI, but the server decides whether progress is real.

---

# 34. Quest Completion Pipeline

When a player completes an objective:

```text id="c8jjgv"
1. Server receives event.
2. Server checks player has quest.
3. Server checks objective is active.
4. Server validates event target.
5. Server updates progress.
6. If objective complete, server advances stage.
7. If final stage complete, quest becomes ready to turn in or completed.
8. Server sends update to client.
9. Client updates tracker, map, compass, and journal.
```

---

# 35. Quest Turn-In Pipeline

```text id="lwlxrx"
1. Player talks to turn-in NPC.
2. Server checks quest status.
3. Server confirms all objectives complete.
4. Server shows reward options.
5. Player selects reward if needed.
6. Server grants rewards.
7. Server applies reputation/world changes.
8. Server marks quest completed.
9. Server unlocks follow-up quests.
10. Client updates journal and map.
```

---

# 36. Quest Edge Cases

## Player Kills Quest Target Before Accepting Quest

Options:

```text id="z1ijhg"
Do not count progress unless quest was active.
Count progress if target is unique and recently killed.
Allow retroactive credit for important world bosses or rare enemies.
```

Recommended:

```text id="pjohx0"
For normal quests, require quest active.
For rare/world targets, allow limited retroactive credit.
```

---

## Player Collects Quest Item Before Quest

Rule:

```text id="fh4nqf"
If item is a normal world item, allow it.
If item is quest-specific, only spawn or count after quest is accepted.
```

---

## Quest NPC Is Dead

Rule:

```text id="ze12ko"
If NPC is essential, enter wounded/unavailable state, not permanent death.
If NPC can die, show alternate turn-in or failure state.
If NPC respawns, map should show respawn status or replacement NPC.
```

Example:

```text id="auayis"
"Farmer Kell is unavailable. Return after the town attack ends."
```

---

## Player Leaves Quest Area

Rule:

```text id="f45s0v"
Warn player if leaving matters.
Do not fail casual quests for walking away.
Fail or pause only timed/escort/defense quests when appropriate.
```

---

## Player Disconnects During Quest

Rule:

```text id="k4xgxq"
Normal quests remain active.
Timed quests continue only if designed that way.
Escort quests may pause, fail, or continue based on rules.
Dungeon quests depend on instance persistence.
```

---

## Player Abandons Quest With Quest Item

Rule:

```text id="k3vusw"
Remove temporary quest items.
Do not remove normal items.
Warn player first.
```

---

## Player Has Full Inventory

Rule:

```text id="l6aokd"
If quest reward cannot fit:
Send to mail, overflow storage, or require inventory space before turn-in.
Never silently delete rewards.
```

---

## Multiple Players Need Same Quest Object

Options:

```text id="ei1gk7"
Object is personal per player.
Object respawns quickly.
Object can be shared by party.
Object is instanced.
```

Recommended for MMOs:

```text id="3bpqex"
Use personal or shared party quest objects to avoid griefing.
```

---

## Quest Enemy Does Not Spawn

Rule:

```text id="m2ht5d"
Quest area should monitor spawn availability.
If target population is too low, increase respawn rate.
If target is missing due to bug, show fallback marker or alternate objective.
```

---

## Player Is Too Low Level

Rule:

```text id="zn7bze"
Show warning.
Allow accepting only if design permits.
Do not hide all future quests without explanation.
```

Example:

```text id="m4x9g1"
"This quest is recommended for level 12. You are level 8."
```

---

## Player Is in Wrong Phase

Rule:

```text id="rr3szd"
Show phase warning.
Offer party sync if possible.
Show what quest step is required to enter the correct phase.
```

---

## Player Cannot Find Objective

Rule:

```text id="003mkg"
Escalate hints.
Show search area.
Highlight objective when nearby.
Allow asking NPC for directions again.
```

---

## Quest Choice Would Cause Major Consequence

Rule:

```text id="20v6xs"
Warn before irreversible choices.
```

Example:

```text id="10h9tw"
"This choice will permanently reduce your reputation with the Merchant Guild. Continue?"
```

---

# 37. Accessibility Rules

Quest guidance should support accessibility.

Include:

```text id="fmvxj3"
Text directions
Map markers
Compass markers
Colorblind-safe icons
Sound cues
Screen reader-friendly quest text
High-contrast objective outlines
Adjustable guidance level
Large text option
Simplified quest tracker
Auto-route option
```

Do not rely only on color.

Bad:

```text id="1k2q8b"
"Go to the red marker."
```

Good:

```text id="m67tqh"
"Go to the red sword marker labeled 'Bandit Camp' northwest of town."
```

---

# 38. Quest Guidance Without Ruining Exploration

A good MMO helps players without turning everything into a checklist.

Use three styles:

## Direct Guidance

For basic services and main quests.

```text id="7fg5k1"
Talk to Captain Helra at the barracks.
```

## Area Guidance

For exploration quests.

```text id="iqb5x3"
Search the old battlefield west of the river.
```

## Clue Guidance

For mystery quests.

```text id="v5c6wi"
The thief was seen near a place where water runs beneath the city.
```

Then offer optional hints if stuck.

---

# 39. Quest Types and Special Rules

## Fetch Quest

```text id="xyoh7o"
Show item count.
Show source area.
Show whether item drops from enemies, plants, chests, or shops.
```

## Kill Quest

```text id="6o5zlh"
Show enemy name.
Show spawn area.
Show progress count.
Share progress with nearby party members.
```

## Escort Quest

```text id="x8e59g"
Show escort NPC HP.
Show route.
Warn if player moves too far away.
Pause or fail if NPC dies.
NPC should not get stuck.
```

## Delivery Quest

```text id="gpetfh"
Show recipient.
Show destination.
Protect quest item from accidental deletion.
```

## Investigation Quest

```text id="oh7io3"
Show clue count.
Mark general search area.
Update journal with discovered clues.
Do not reveal final answer too early unless assistance level is high.
```

## Dungeon Quest

```text id="5kg027"
Show dungeon entrance.
Show recommended group size.
Track boss/objective progress.
Share with party.
Complete only when eligible boss or objective is finished.
```

## Timed Quest

```text id="ibfk58"
Show timer clearly.
Warn before accepting.
Show failure condition.
Allow abandoning if appropriate.
```

## Stealth Quest

```text id="l6hb33"
Show detection rules.
Warn if player is about to fail.
Show alternative path hints.
```

---

# 40. Quest Board System

Towns should have notice boards for easy quest discovery.

## Notice Board Features

```text id="k873pq"
Local bounties
Delivery jobs
Crafting requests
Missing people
Monster contracts
Wanted criminals
Public events
Daily quests
Faction requests
```

Notice boards should show:

```text id="ourv8m"
Quest level
Difficulty
Distance
Reward
Time limit
Recommended party size
Faction impact
```

---

# 41. Quest Status Notifications

The game should notify players clearly.

## Notification Types

```text id="cuqkmi"
Quest Accepted
Quest Updated
Objective Complete
New Objective
Quest Ready to Turn In
Quest Completed
Quest Failed
Quest Abandoned
Quest Reward Received
New Quest Available
Quest Chain Updated
```

Example:

```text id="3tmvdw"
Objective Complete:
Collect Wolf Pelts 8 / 8

New Objective:
Return to Tanner Brigg.
```

---

# 42. Quest Anti-Abuse Rules

Players will exploit quests if allowed.

## Anti-Abuse Rules

```text id="6lzq5z"
Prevent duplicate reward claims.
Prevent kill credit from inactive players.
Prevent farming the same quest beyond intended limits.
Prevent party members from sharing quests they are not eligible for.
Prevent abandoning/reaccepting to duplicate quest items.
Prevent using alts to farm reputation rewards.
Prevent objective progress from invalid targets.
```

---

# 43. Quest Analytics and Debugging

Track quest health.

Log:

```text id="73l7ti"
Quest accept rate
Completion rate
Failure rate
Abandon rate
Average completion time
Where players get stuck
Objectives with low completion
Bugged spawn reports
NPC unavailable reports
Phase mismatch reports
Reward claim errors
```

This helps improve the MMO over time.

---

# 44. Quest Implementation Checklist

Before adding any quest, check:

```text id="drhaf0"
Does the quest have a clear giver?
Does the quest have a clear reason?
Does the quest have a clear objective?
Does the player know where to go?
Does the map show the objective?
Does the compass show the direction?
Does the journal explain the quest?
Does the tracker show progress?
Can the quest be completed in a party?
Can the quest be abandoned?
Can it be restarted?
Are rewards shown clearly?
Are failure conditions clear?
Are quest items protected?
Are NPC dialogue states complete?
Are all map markers removed or updated correctly?
Are edge cases handled?
```

---

# 45. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into an AI/game design prompt:

```text id="jynqwv"
Design a complete quest and mission system for an MMO.

The system must always help players understand:
- What quests are available
- What quests they have accepted
- What each quest wants them to do
- Where to go next
- How to complete the current objective
- What progress they have made
- Which quests are ready to turn in
- Which quests are blocked, failed, abandoned, completed, or expired
- What rewards they will receive

Every quest must include:
- Quest ID
- Title
- Type
- Level
- Difficulty
- Quest giver
- Turn-in NPC
- Region/location
- Quest status
- Current objective
- Objective progress
- Map marker
- Compass marker
- Journal text
- Hints
- Rewards
- Failure conditions
- Abandon/restart rules
- Party sharing rules
- Completion rules

Quest statuses must include:
Unavailable, Available, Accepted, In Progress, Objective Complete, Ready to Turn In, Completed, Failed, Abandoned, Expired, Locked, Paused, and Blocked.

The player must have:
- Quest journal
- Active quest tracker
- Map markers
- Compass markers
- Nearby quest list
- Recommended next quest list
- Quest filters
- Search
- Objective progress updates
- Turn-in guidance
- Optional hint escalation if stuck

Map markers must support:
- Exact location
- Search area
- Route
- Turn-in marker
- Quest giver marker
- Enemy zone
- Collection zone
- Dungeon entrance
- Escort path
- Hidden clue area

NPC quest dialogue must change based on:
- Quest unavailable
- Quest available
- Quest accepted
- Objective partially complete
- Ready to turn in
- Completed
- Failed
- Abandoned
- Player reputation
- Player legal standing
- Player faction
- Player hostility

The system must support:
- Main quests
- Side quests
- Daily quests
- Weekly quests
- Faction quests
- Dungeon quests
- Raid quests
- PvP quests
- Profession quests
- Dynamic event quests
- Escort quests
- Fetch quests
- Kill quests
- Investigation quests
- Delivery quests
- Stealth quests
- Timed quests

The system must handle edge cases:
- Quest NPC is dead or unavailable
- Target dies before player reaches it
- Player leaves the quest area
- Player disconnects
- Player abandons quest
- Quest item is missing
- Player inventory is full
- Party members are on different quest phases
- Quest enemy does not spawn
- Player is too low level
- Player cannot find the objective
- Player makes irreversible choices
- Player tries to claim reward twice
- Player tries to exploit quest item duplication

The server must be authoritative:
- Server validates quest acceptance
- Server tracks progress
- Server validates objectives
- Server grants rewards
- Server prevents duplicate completion
- Server stores completed, failed, and abandoned quests
- Client only displays UI and sends interaction requests

The system should help players without removing exploration. Use exact markers for simple objectives, search areas for exploration objectives, and optional hint escalation when players are stuck.
```

---

# Final Quest System Rule

Every active quest should answer this at all times:

```text id="heol61"
What am I doing?
Why am I doing it?
Where do I go?
How do I make progress?
How much progress have I made?
What happens when I finish?
Who do I return to?
What reward do I get?
What is blocking me, if anything?
```

If the game can answer those questions clearly, players will not feel lost.


Below is a full **Town and City Design Rule System** for an MMO. This is meant to guide an AI so towns feel alive, readable, explorable, and physically believable.

The core rule:

```text
A town or city is not just buildings placed together. It must have roads, districts, entrances, landmarks, people, services, traffic flow, public spaces, private spaces, danger zones, economy, law enforcement, and reasons for the player to explore.
```

---

# MMO Town and City Design Guide

Every town or city should answer these questions:

```text
Why does this place exist?
Who lives here?
How do people make money?
Who controls it?
Where do people sleep, work, shop, pray, drink, trade, and gather?
How does the player enter, leave, explore, and understand the area?
Where are the important services?
Where are the hidden secrets?
What makes this town different from every other town?
```

---

# 1. City Layout Rules

## Every town needs a clear structure

A believable settlement should have:

```text
Entrances and exits
Main roads
Side roads
Buildings with accessible doors
Public gathering spaces
Shops and services
Residential areas
Work areas
Storage areas
Defensive or guard areas
Landmarks
NPC routes
Hidden or optional areas
```

Bad design:

```text
Buildings randomly scattered with no roads.
Doors blocked by props.
NPCs standing inside walls.
Shops impossible to identify.
No reason to explore.
Everything is the same size and shape.
No town center.
No signs, banners, lights, or landmarks.
```

Good design:

```text
Main road leads from gate to town square.
Shops are near the market.
Houses are grouped into neighborhoods.
Guards patrol gates and important streets.
Nobles live in cleaner, larger, guarded areas.
Poor areas are more crowded and worn down.
Hidden alleys contain thieves, secrets, and side quests.
Important buildings are marked clearly on the map.
```

---

# 2. Settlement Types

Different towns should have different layouts.

## Small Village

```text
Population: 20 to 100 NPCs
Layout: Simple roads, farms, wells, homes, small shrine, one or two shops
Main purpose: Farming, fishing, mining, hunting, trade stop
Important locations: Inn, general store, blacksmith, elder’s house, barn, well
Exploration: Nearby woods, caves, ruins, farms, hidden cellar, old shrine
```

## Market Town

```text
Population: 100 to 500 NPCs
Layout: Central market, several roads, shops, guild buildings, homes
Main purpose: Trade and services
Important locations: Market square, inn, stables, blacksmith, alchemist, guard post
Exploration: Back alleys, merchant disputes, hidden thieves, storage buildings
```

## Walled City

```text
Population: 500 to 5,000+ NPCs
Layout: Gates, walls, districts, plazas, major roads, side streets
Main purpose: Politics, trade, protection, military, crafting
Important locations: Castle, market, temple, guild halls, barracks, prison, docks
Exploration: Sewers, rooftops, noble district, slums, underground tunnels
```

## Port City

```text
Population: 500 to 3,000+ NPCs
Layout: Docks, warehouses, markets, taverns, shipyards, customs gates
Main purpose: Sea trade, fishing, smuggling, naval power
Important locations: Harbor master, fish market, taverns, warehouses, lighthouse
Exploration: Smuggler tunnels, ship interiors, dockside gangs, hidden cargo
```

## Military Fort

```text
Population: 50 to 500 NPCs
Layout: Defensive walls, barracks, training yard, armory, command hall
Main purpose: Defense, war, border control
Important locations: Gatehouse, barracks, armory, stables, watchtowers
Exploration: Restricted areas, prisoner cells, war rooms, supply stores
```

## Noble Capital

```text
Population: 2,000 to 20,000+ NPCs
Layout: Large districts, palace, walls, major roads, plazas, fountains
Main purpose: Government, wealth, religion, military, culture
Important locations: Palace, court, grand temple, guild halls, markets, noble estates
Exploration: Political secrets, hidden tunnels, elite shops, slums, rooftops
```

## Criminal / Outlaw Town

```text
Population: 50 to 500 NPCs
Layout: Hidden, chaotic, defensive, hard to reach
Main purpose: Smuggling, black market, thieves, fugitives
Important locations: Fence, black market, tavern, fighting pit, hidden boss
Exploration: Secret entrances, traps, informants, rival gangs
```

---

# 3. District Rules

Large towns and cities should be divided into districts.

## Common District Types

```text
Market District
Residential District
Crafting District
Noble District
Temple District
Guard/Military District
Dock District
Slums
Farm Outskirts
Guild District
Entertainment District
Magic District
Industrial District
Criminal Underground
Sewer Network
Old Ruins
```

Each district should have a purpose, visual style, NPC population, sounds, smells, colors, and gameplay function.

---

## Market District

Should include:

```text
Open stalls
Shopkeepers
Customers
Guards
Pickpockets
Street performers
Notice boards
Delivery workers
Crates and carts
Banners and signs
```

Design rules:

```text
Main paths must be wide enough for player traffic.
Stalls should not block all movement.
Important vendors should have visible signs.
Crowds should gather naturally near stalls, fountains, and boards.
```

---

## Residential District

Should include:

```text
Homes
Small gardens
Laundry lines
Wells
Children playing
Elders sitting
Dogs, cats, chickens
Private doors
Backyards
Small side quests
```

Design rules:

```text
Homes should have doors facing paths.
Homes should not be sealed behind walls unless private/gated.
NPCs should move between homes, wells, markets, and work.
```

---

## Noble District

Should include:

```text
Large estates
Clean roads
Guards
Servants
Carriages
Gardens
Fountains
Private gates
Expensive shops
Court buildings
```

Design rules:

```text
More open space.
Cleaner architecture.
Better lighting.
Restricted areas.
Guards should challenge suspicious or outlaw players.
```

---

## Slums

Should include:

```text
Tight alleys
Poor housing
Broken carts
Begging NPCs
Cheap taverns
Hidden thieves
Sick NPCs
Makeshift shelters
Trash piles
Dim lighting
```

Design rules:

```text
Crowded, but still navigable.
Lots of shortcuts.
More hidden quests.
More crime.
Lower guard presence or corrupt guards.
```

---

## Guard / Military District

Should include:

```text
Barracks
Training yard
Armory
Jail
Stables
Command room
Watchtowers
Patrol routes
Weapon racks
Target dummies
```

Design rules:

```text
Restricted areas must be clearly marked.
Guards should patrol.
Players with bad legal standing may be stopped or attacked.
```

---

## Temple District

Should include:

```text
Temple
Shrines
Priests
Healers
Pilgrims
Beggars
Candles
Offerings
Gardens
Graveyard, if appropriate
```

Design rules:

```text
Quiet, clean, spiritual atmosphere.
Healers and priests should be easy to find.
Crimes here should cause stronger reputation/legal penalties.
```

---

## Dock District

Should include:

```text
Ships
Fishermen
Warehouses
Crates
Ropes
Nets
Seagulls
Sailors
Taverns
Smugglers
Harbor guards
```

Design rules:

```text
Docks must connect logically to roads.
Water boundaries must be clear.
NPCs should not walk into water unless swimming or working near boats.
```

---

# 4. Road and Path Design Rules

Roads are how players understand a city.

## Road Hierarchy

Every town should have:

```text
Main roads
Secondary roads
Alleys
Private paths
Service paths
Hidden paths
```

## Main Roads

```text
Connect city gates to major landmarks.
Wide enough for players, NPCs, carts, mounts, and crowds.
Usually lead to market, castle, temple, or central square.
```

## Side Roads

```text
Connect neighborhoods.
Lead to homes, smaller shops, farms, docks, and crafting areas.
```

## Alleys

```text
Useful for exploration, shortcuts, thieves, hidden quests, and secrets.
Should be narrower but still walkable.
Should not accidentally trap players.
```

## Path Width Guidelines

```text
Tiny alley: 1.5 to 2 meters wide
Normal footpath: 2 to 3 meters wide
Small road: 3 to 5 meters wide
Main street: 6 to 12 meters wide
Market road: 8 to 15 meters wide
City gate road: 10+ meters wide
```

For MMOs, err on the wider side because players, pets, mounts, and NPCs need space.

---

# 5. Building Placement Rules

Buildings must be placed with physical logic.

## Building Rules

```text
Every important building must have at least one accessible entrance.
Doors must face a road, path, courtyard, alley, or public space.
Buildings cannot overlap roads unless intentionally built as arches or bridges.
Buildings cannot float, sink, or clip into terrain.
Stairs or ramps must exist if a door is above or below ground level.
Buildings must not trap NPCs or players.
Buildings should have enough space around them for movement.
```

Bad:

```text
Shop door opens into a wall.
House has no path to it.
Building sits halfway inside another building.
Castle gate is blocked by crates.
Important quest building is hidden with no clue.
NPC shopkeeper is inside a sealed room.
```

Good:

```text
Shop has sign, road access, door, counter, merchant, shelves, and map icon.
Guard barracks near gate has training yard and patrol routes.
Temple has steps, public entrance, shrine markers, and healers.
Noble estate has gate, guards, garden, servant entrance, and restricted interior.
```

---

# 6. Spacing Rules

Towns should feel full, but not impossible to move through.

## Spacing Guidelines

```text
Leave clear walkable paths between buildings.
Do not overfill every street with props.
Keep doorways clear.
Keep shop counters accessible.
Keep quest NPCs visible and reachable.
Keep stairs and bridges unobstructed.
Place clutter near walls, corners, stalls, storage zones, and edges.
Use open plazas to break up dense city areas.
```

## Minimum Clearance Rules

```text
Doorway clearance: at least 1.5 meters
Main shop entrance: at least 2 meters
Quest NPC interaction area: at least 2 meters around NPC
Market walking lane: at least 4 meters
Mounted traffic lane: at least 5 to 6 meters
Combat-safe plaza: at least 10 meters wide
```

## Crowd Spacing

```text
NPCs should not stand shoulder-to-shoulder unless it is a crowd event.
NPCs need personal space.
Crowds should form around markets, performers, boards, ceremonies, and emergencies.
NPCs should not block every route through a district.
```

---

# 7. Accessibility Rules

Players must be able to understand and use the town.

## Important Buildings Must Be Reachable

Important buildings include:

```text
Inn
Bank
Market
Blacksmith
General store
Quest hub
Guard barracks
Temple/healer
Crafting stations
Auction house
Stables
Guild halls
Fast travel point
Dungeon entrance
Main story location
```

Rules:

```text
Each important building needs a clear path from main roads.
Each should have signs, lighting, NPCs, or map symbols.
Important doors should not be hidden unless intentionally secret.
Players should not need to jump over props to reach basic services.
```

---

# 8. Map Symbol Rules

Stores and important locations should be highlighted on the map.

## Map Icon Categories

```text
General Store
Blacksmith
Weapon Shop
Armor Shop
Alchemist
Inn
Tavern
Bank
Auction House
Stable
Healer
Temple
Quest Giver
Main Story Quest
Side Quest
Guild Hall
Crafting Station
Guard Barracks
Prison
Town Hall
Market
Fast Travel
Dungeon Entrance
Raid Entrance
PvP Arena
Player Housing
Mailbox
Notice Board
Fence / Black Market
Hidden Location
Danger Zone
```

---

## Suggested Symbols

```text
General Store: Bag icon
Blacksmith: Anvil icon
Weapon Shop: Sword icon
Armor Shop: Shield icon
Alchemist: Potion bottle icon
Inn: Bed icon
Tavern: Mug icon
Bank: Coin chest icon
Auction House: Gavel icon
Stable: Horse icon
Healer: Cross or hand icon
Temple: Shrine icon
Quest Giver: Exclamation mark
Main Story Quest: Gold exclamation mark
Side Quest: Silver or blue exclamation mark
Completed Quest: Question mark
Guard Barracks: Helmet icon
Prison: Bars icon
Town Hall: Banner icon
Market: Stall icon
Fast Travel: Compass icon
Dungeon Entrance: Cave icon
Raid Entrance: Skull or portal icon
PvP Arena: Crossed swords icon
Crafting Station: Hammer icon
Mailbox: Envelope icon
Notice Board: Scroll icon
Black Market: Dagger or masked face icon
Hidden Location: Faded question mark, only after discovery
Danger Zone: Red warning icon
```

---

## Map Visibility Rules

```text
Basic services appear when entering town.
Major landmarks appear from far away.
Hidden locations appear only after discovery, rumor, quest, or high perception.
Criminal services appear only to trusted/outlaw players.
Restricted areas appear with warning markers.
Quest markers should not cover every mystery unless the game is designed that way.
```

---

# 9. Landmark Rules

Every town needs landmarks so players remember it.

## Landmark Types

```text
Large tree
Central fountain
Clock tower
Castle
Temple
Statue
Market square
Giant gate
Bridge
Harbor
Windmill
Ruined tower
Lighthouse
Arena
Mage tower
Dragon skeleton
Massive forge
Sacred shrine
Waterfall
Town bell
```

Rules:

```text
Use landmarks to help navigation.
Place landmarks near major routes.
Make each town visually distinct.
Use color, architecture, sound, and lighting to reinforce identity.
```

Bad:

```text
Every town looks like the same brown buildings.
```

Good:

```text
Mining town has smoke, stone, rails, carts, and a huge mine entrance.
Port city has gulls, ropes, blue banners, wet wood, ships, and warehouses.
Noble capital has marble, gold, gardens, fountains, and guarded gates.
Forest village has mossy roofs, tree platforms, green banners, and animal pens.
```

---

# 10. Exploration Rules

A good town should reward curiosity.

## Exploration Hooks

Add:

```text
Alleys
Rooftop paths
Locked doors
Hidden cellars
Sewers
Secret tunnels
Basements
Attics
Abandoned houses
Notice boards
Rumor NPCs
Rare merchants
Small shrines
Hidden chests
Environmental storytelling
Unmarked mini-events
NPC arguments
Crime scenes
Lost animals
Secret gardens
Hidden courtyards
```

## Exploration Balance

```text
Important services should be easy to find.
Secrets should require observation, rumors, quests, or exploration.
Do not hide basic shops in confusing locations.
Do not make every building enterable unless there is content inside.
Use locked/private doors to imply depth without wasting player time.
```

---

# 11. Town Life Rules

Towns should look alive, not frozen.

## Ambient Life Elements

```text
NPCs walking to work
Children playing
Merchants calling out goods
Guards patrolling
Animals moving
Smoke from chimneys
Flags moving
Lights turning on at night
Doors opening and closing
Bells ringing
Carts moving
Farmers working
Bards performing
People gathering around events
Workers carrying crates
Priests blessing citizens
Thieves watching from alleys
```

## Time-Based Changes

```text
Morning:
Shops open, workers move, markets set up.

Day:
Crowds gather, trade is active, guards patrol normally.

Evening:
Taverns fill, shops close, lamps are lit.

Night:
Most civilians sleep, guards increase patrols, thieves and criminals become active.

Rain:
Markets cover goods, NPCs seek shelter, roads become muddy.

Festival:
Decorations, music, crowd density, special vendors.

Attack:
Civilians flee, guards respond, shops close, alarms ring.
```

---

# 12. NPC Placement in Towns

NPCs should be placed based on role.

## NPC Placement Rules

```text
Merchants stand behind counters, at stalls, or near goods.
Guards stand at gates, patrol routes, barracks, prisons, and noble areas.
Nobles appear in estates, courts, gardens, carriages, or balconies.
Peasants appear in fields, homes, markets, wells, taverns, and workshops.
Thieves appear in alleys, rooftops, taverns, sewers, and crowds.
Priests appear near temples, shrines, graveyards, or sick NPCs.
Animals appear in pens, streets, farms, stables, forests, or docks.
Creatures appear outside town, in ruins, caves, sewers, or danger zones.
```

NPCs must not:

```text
Stand inside walls
Hover above roads
Spawn under terrain
Block doors unless intentionally guarding
Walk through closed gates
Stand in water unless swimming or working
Face walls forever
Cluster too tightly
Patrol through solid props
Ignore danger unrealistically
```

---

# 13. Storefront Design Rules

Stores should be visually understandable.

## Every Store Should Have

```text
Sign or symbol
Door or counter
Visible goods
Storekeeper
Lighting
Map icon
Interior or stall layout
Nearby storage
Customer space
```

Examples:

```text
Blacksmith:
Anvil sign, forge smoke, weapon racks, armor stands, hammering sound.

Alchemist:
Potion bottle sign, colorful bottles, herbs, glowing jars, strange smoke.

Inn:
Bed or mug sign, warm lights, tables, stable nearby, rooms upstairs.

Bank:
Stone building, guards, vault door, coin symbol, formal counter.

Stable:
Horse icon, hay, fences, saddles, animals, stable master.

Temple:
Shrine icon, candles, bells, priests, clean stone, healing area.
```

---

# 14. Public Space Rules

A town needs breathing room.

## Public Space Types

```text
Town square
Market plaza
Temple courtyard
Training yard
Fountain area
Dock loading area
Park or garden
Festival ground
Auction square
Execution square
Arena entrance
```

Public spaces should:

```text
Help navigation
Create social gathering points
Allow events
Give players room to move
Support NPC conversations
Host vendors, festivals, fights, speeches, or quests
```

---

# 15. Defensive Design Rules

Towns need believable defense depending on danger.

## Defensive Elements

```text
Walls
Gates
Watchtowers
Guard posts
Barracks
Moats
Drawbridges
Spiked barricades
Alarm bells
Torch towers
Patrol routes
Archery platforms
Checkpoint roads
```

Rules:

```text
A dangerous region needs stronger defenses.
A rich city has more guards and better walls.
A poor village may only have fences and militia.
Gates must connect to roads.
Watchtowers must face useful directions.
Guards must be near entry points and important areas.
```

---

# 16. Economy and Work Logic

A settlement should show how it survives.

Ask:

```text
Where does food come from?
Where does water come from?
Where do goods come from?
Where do people work?
Where are goods stored?
Who protects trade?
Who benefits from the economy?
Who is poor and who is rich?
```

## Economic Features

```text
Farms
Mines
Forests
Fishing docks
Trade roads
Warehouses
Mills
Workshops
Markets
Guild halls
Caravans
Stables
Water wells
Granaries
Tax offices
Banks
```

A mining town should have mines, carts, ore piles, blacksmiths, dusty workers, guarded storage, and trade wagons.

A fishing town should have docks, nets, fish markets, boats, gulls, salt barrels, and seafood vendors.

---

# 17. Building Variety Rules

Avoid copy-paste towns.

Vary:

```text
Building height
Roof shape
Materials
Wall color
Signs
Banners
Windows
Doors
Damage/wear
Cleanliness
Lighting
Props
Yard layout
NPC density
Street width
```

## Visual Class Differences

```text
Noble area:
Stone, marble, gold trim, gardens, guards, clean roads.

Merchant area:
Bright signs, stalls, awnings, crates, busy roads.

Peasant area:
Wood, clay, thatch, laundry, chickens, tools, mud.

Slums:
Broken walls, patched roofs, trash, dim lights, tight alleys.

Military area:
Stone, banners, racks, training dummies, watchtowers.

Magic district:
Glowing runes, crystals, floating lights, libraries, towers.
```

---

# 18. Player Guidance Rules

Players should not be lost in a bad way.

Use:

```text
Signs
Road shape
Lighting
Map icons
Landmarks
NPC directions
Banners
Color coding
Sound cues
Quest markers
Street names
Gate names
District names
```

Example:

```text
Blacksmith district uses red banners and forge smoke.
Temple district uses white stone and bells.
Market district uses colorful awnings and crowd noise.
Guard district uses blue banners and armor racks.
Thieves district has no official signs, but has graffiti and suspicious NPCs.
```

---

# 19. Hidden and Restricted Areas

Not every area should be open.

## Restricted Areas

```text
Noble estates
Guard barracks
Prisons
Treasuries
Private homes
Guild-only buildings
Temples’ inner sanctums
Magic laboratories
Black market
Faction headquarters
```

Rules:

```text
Restricted areas need visual warnings.
Guards should react to trespassing.
Stealth, disguise, reputation, bribes, or quests may allow access.
Locked doors should have reasons.
Alternate entrances may exist for exploration.
```

---

# 20. Town Event Rules

Towns should support events.

## Event Types

```text
Market day
Festival
Monster attack
Public trial
Execution
Wedding
Funeral
Political speech
Caravan arrival
Crime investigation
Fire
Plague outbreak
Guard raid
Thief chase
Religious ceremony
Arena tournament
Faction protest
```

Events should change:

```text
NPC positions
Dialogue
Shop availability
Guard patrols
Map markers
Crowd density
Quest options
Music and sound
Lighting and decorations
```

---

# 21. Creature and Danger Zone Placement

Not all town areas are safe.

## Safe Zones

```text
Town square
Temple
Inn
Bank
Major roads
Guard posts
```

## Medium-Risk Zones

```text
Alleys
Docks at night
Slums
Sewers
Abandoned buildings
Outer farms
Forest edge
```

## Dangerous Zones

```text
Bandit hideout
Monster cave
Cursed graveyard
Old ruins
Deep sewer
Warfront gate
Criminal district
Haunted district
```

Rules:

```text
Danger level should be communicated visually.
Do not place deadly monsters beside basic starter shops unless clearly intended.
Players should understand when they are leaving safety.
```

---

# 22. Town Map Design Rules

The map should make the town readable.

## Map Requirements

```text
Show player location.
Show district names.
Show important shops.
Show major services.
Show quest locations.
Show gates and exits.
Show fast travel points.
Show danger zones.
Show discovered hidden areas.
Allow filtering icons.
```

## Map Filters

```text
All
Quests
Shops
Crafting
Travel
Guilds
Combat
Services
Housing
Hidden/Discovered
Criminal
```

## Icon Rules

```text
Do not overcrowd the map with too many icons.
Group nearby vendor icons when zoomed out.
Reveal more detail when zoomed in.
Use different icon shapes for different services.
Use color carefully: gold for main quest, blue for side quest, red for danger, green for services.
```

---

# 23. Town Design Edge Cases

## Building Is Inaccessible

Rule:

```text
Every important building must have a walkable route to its entrance.
If decorative only, it should not have a misleading important map icon.
```

## Door Opens Into Wall

Rule:

```text
Door must open into valid interior, street, courtyard, or transition zone.
```

## NPC Inside Wall

Rule:

```text
NPC spawn must validate collision and navmesh.
If invalid, move to fallback anchor or disable spawn.
```

## Building Blocks Main Road

Rule:

```text
Main roads must remain clear unless building is intentionally an archway, gate, bridge, or checkpoint.
```

## City Feels Empty

Fix with:

```text
More NPC schedules
Ambient animals
Moving carts
Smoke, lights, banners
Sound design
Market stalls
Public events
Patrols
Idle animations
```

## City Feels Too Crowded

Fix with:

```text
Wider roads
More plazas
NPC density limits
Better crowd paths
Less clutter in walking lanes
District separation
```

## Important Shop Is Hard to Find

Fix with:

```text
Map icon
Sign
Lighting
Road access
NPC directions
Distinct building shape
Sound cue, such as hammering for blacksmith
```

## Player Gets Stuck

Rule:

```text
No narrow dead-end should trap the player.
Use collision checks.
Add unstuck logic.
Keep minimum movement clearance.
```

## Mounts Cannot Fit

Rule:

```text
Main roads and important service areas must support mounts if mounts are allowed in town.
Restrict mounts in tight interiors or mark dismount zones clearly.
```

## Too Many Identical Buildings

Rule:

```text
Vary materials, props, signs, rooflines, colors, damage, height, and purpose.
```

---

# 24. City Validation Checklist

Before finalizing a town, run this checklist:

```text
Are all important buildings reachable?
Do all doors face usable paths?
Are roads wide enough?
Are main services marked on the map?
Are stores visually identifiable?
Are NPCs placed on valid ground?
Are NPCs outside walls and props?
Do guards patrol logical areas?
Do merchants have goods and counters?
Do residential areas feel lived in?
Does the town have food, water, work, and trade logic?
Are there landmarks for navigation?
Are there hidden areas for exploration?
Are dangerous areas clearly communicated?
Are there public spaces for events?
Are there enough NPCs to feel alive?
Are there too many NPCs blocking paths?
Can players, NPCs, pets, and mounts move through the town?
Does the town look different from other towns?
Does the town change by time of day or events?
```

---

# 25. AI Prompt Version

You can paste this directly into your AI/game design prompt:

```text
When designing towns and cities for this MMO, follow these rules:

1. Every town must have a clear purpose.
   Define why the settlement exists: farming, mining, trade, fishing, politics, religion, military, magic, crime, or survival.

2. Every town must have a readable layout.
   Include entrances, exits, main roads, side roads, alleys, public spaces, shops, homes, work areas, guard areas, and landmarks.

3. Buildings must be physically believable.
   Buildings cannot float, overlap incorrectly, block main roads, or have unusable entrances.
   Every important building must have a clear accessible path and door.

4. NPCs must be physically valid.
   NPCs cannot spawn inside walls, hover above ground, sink into terrain, block doors, or patrol through props.
   NPCs must stand on valid ground, use navmesh, and be placed according to role.

5. Towns must feel alive.
   Add NPC schedules, merchants, guards, workers, animals, conversations, smoke, lights, sounds, crowds, patrols, carts, market activity, and time-of-day changes.

6. Important locations must be highlighted.
   Use map symbols for shops, inns, healers, banks, stables, quest givers, guild halls, guard barracks, crafting stations, fast travel, dungeons, and danger zones.

7. Storefronts must be readable.
   A blacksmith should have an anvil sign, forge smoke, weapon racks, armor, and hammering sounds.
   An alchemist should have potion signs, bottles, herbs, and colored smoke.
   An inn should have warm lights, a bed or mug sign, tables, and rooms.
   A stable should have horses, hay, fences, and a horse icon.

8. Roads must support movement.
   Main roads should be wide enough for players, NPCs, pets, mounts, and carts.
   Alleys can be narrow but must remain walkable.
   Important paths, doors, stairs, bridges, and quest areas must not be blocked.

9. Use districts for larger towns.
   Include market, residential, noble, slum, temple, guard, crafting, dock, guild, and criminal districts where appropriate.
   Each district should have its own architecture, NPC types, sounds, colors, and gameplay purpose.

10. Encourage exploration.
   Add alleys, rooftops, basements, sewers, hidden courtyards, secret doors, rumors, rare merchants, small quests, hidden chests, and environmental storytelling.

11. Make social class visible.
   Noble areas should be cleaner, larger, guarded, and more decorated.
   Peasant areas should be practical, worn, and work-focused.
   Slums should be cramped and poor but still navigable.
   Merchant areas should be colorful and busy.
   Guard areas should be ordered and defensive.

12. The town must support law and reputation.
   Guards should patrol gates, markets, noble areas, prisons, and crime-heavy districts.
   NPCs should react differently to beloved heroes, unknown players, outlaws, famous villains, and public enemies.

13. The town must support economy.
   Show where food, water, goods, work, storage, trade, and wealth come from.
   Add farms, wells, markets, workshops, warehouses, mines, docks, caravans, mills, and guilds as needed.

14. The town must support events.
   Design spaces for festivals, markets, speeches, monster attacks, trials, executions, religious ceremonies, and faction events.

15. Run a final town sanity check.
   Check for inaccessible buildings, blocked roads, floating objects, NPCs in walls, missing map icons, empty streets, overcrowded paths, unclear shops, missing landmarks, and lack of exploration rewards.
```

---

# Final Town Design Rule

Every town should answer this:

```text
Can the player understand it, move through it, find important services, meet believable NPCs, discover optional secrets, and feel like the place existed before they arrived?
```

That is the difference between a random cluster of buildings and a real MMO town.


Below is a full **MMO NPC System** covering placement, behavior, movement, merchants, guards, social class, conversations, quests, reputation reactions, animals, creatures, and edge cases.

The biggest rule:

```text
NPCs are not decorations. Every NPC must have:
- A valid physical position
- A reason to exist in that location
- A role in the world
- A daily behavior loop
- A response to the player
- A response to danger
- A relationship to nearby people, places, laws, factions, and economy
```

---

# MMO NPC System Overview

NPCs should make the world feel alive. They should not just stand randomly in rooms or roads.

Every NPC should have:

```text
Name
Type
Role
Faction
Social Class
Home Location
Work Location
Valid Spawn Point
Movement Rules
Conversation Rules
Combat Rules
Reputation Reaction Rules
Quest Rules
Inventory or Trade Rules, if applicable
Crime Response Rules
Daily Schedule
Emergency Behavior
```

NPCs can be:

```text
People
Animals
Creatures
Monsters
Guards
Merchants
Nobles
Peasants
Thieves
Quest-givers
Travelers
Children
Pets
Mounts
Companions
Bosses
Faction leaders
Servants
Craftsmen
Priests
Scholars
Bandits
Undead
Magical beings
```

---

# 1. NPC Placement Rules

NPCs must always be placed correctly in the environment.

## Core Placement Rule

```text
Every NPC must be standing, sitting, sleeping, working, flying, swimming, crawling, hanging, or otherwise positioned in a way that matches their body, animation, and environment.
```

Bad placement:

```text
NPC floating above the floor
NPC half inside a wall
NPC standing on a table unless intended
NPC sitting with no chair
NPC sleeping on the floor when a bed is nearby, unless intentional
NPC behind a shop counter but clipping through it
NPC spawned inside another NPC
NPC walking through closed doors
Animal standing on a roof for no reason
Fish swimming in air
Bird walking underwater
```

Good placement:

```text
Merchant stands behind a counter
Guard stands at a gate, patrol route, tower, barracks, or checkpoint
Noble sits at a table, throne, carriage, balcony, or court chamber
Peasant works in a field, market, kitchen, stable, or workshop
Thief waits in alleys, rooftops, taverns, sewers, or shadowed corners
Wolf patrols forest paths or dens
Rat moves near trash, sewers, barns, or food storage
Dragon rests in a cave, ruin, mountain nest, or boss arena
```

---

# 2. Physical Positioning System

Every NPC placement should pass a **physical validation check**.

## Spawn Validation Checklist

Before spawning an NPC, check:

```text
1. Is there solid ground beneath the NPC?
2. Is the NPC’s feet position touching the ground or valid surface?
3. Is the NPC capsule not inside a wall, prop, another NPC, or terrain?
4. Does the NPC have enough headroom?
5. Is the NPC on a valid navigation mesh?
6. Can the NPC move at least a short distance from this position?
7. Is the NPC facing a believable direction?
8. Is the NPC appropriate for this location?
9. Is the NPC’s animation compatible with the location?
10. Is the NPC blocking an important gameplay path?
```

---

## Ground Snapping Rule

When placing an NPC:

```text
Cast downward from the intended spawn point.
Find the first valid floor, ground, platform, chair, bed, perch, or water surface.
Place the NPC so their feet, body, or animation anchor aligns to that surface.
```

Examples:

```text
Standing NPC: feet snap to floor.
Sitting NPC: hips align to chair seat marker.
Sleeping NPC: body aligns to bed marker.
Bird: feet align to perch, branch, roof beam, or ground.
Fish: body aligns within water volume.
Flying creature: position must be inside valid flying zone.
Spider: can align to wall or ceiling if species allows it.
```

---

## Collision Rule

NPCs must have collision capsules or bounds.

```text
If NPC collision overlaps wall, furniture, terrain, door, another NPC, or player spawn point, the placement is invalid.
```

The system should try nearby alternate points.

```text
Try original point.
If invalid, search within 1 meter.
If invalid, search within 3 meters.
If invalid, search within 8 meters.
If still invalid, do not spawn the NPC and log the error.
```

---

## Navigation Mesh Rule

Most ground NPCs must be on the navmesh.

```text
If an NPC is expected to walk, patrol, flee, chase, or wander, it must spawn on a valid navigation mesh.
```

Exceptions:

```text
Sitting NPCs
Sleeping NPCs
Prisoners in cages
Mounted NPCs
Flying NPCs
Swimming NPCs
Static magical NPCs
Scripted scene NPCs
Dead bodies
```

Even then, they need valid animation anchors.

---

# 3. NPC Anchor System

Do not place NPCs randomly. Use **anchors**.

An anchor is a marked position in the world that tells an NPC where and how to exist.

## Anchor Types

```text
Standing Anchor
Sitting Anchor
Sleeping Anchor
Work Anchor
Shop Counter Anchor
Guard Post Anchor
Patrol Point
Conversation Spot
Eating Spot
Drinking Spot
Market Stall Spot
Prayer Spot
Training Spot
Animal Grazing Spot
Creature Den Spot
Perch Spot
Water Swim Zone
Flying Zone
Ambush Spot
Hidden Thief Spot
Quest Interaction Spot
```

---

## Anchor Data Example

```json
{
  "id": "blacksmith_work_anchor_01",
  "type": "work",
  "position": [42.5, 0.0, 18.2],
  "rotation": [0, 180, 0],
  "required_animation": "hammer_anvil",
  "allowed_npc_roles": ["blacksmith", "apprentice"],
  "max_users": 1,
  "requires_navmesh": true,
  "nearby_props": ["anvil", "forge", "tool_rack"],
  "blocked_if_combat": true
}
```

---

# 4. Environmental Believability Rules

Every NPC must match the space around them.

## Room Role Matching

```text
Tavern:
Bartender, servers, patrons, musicians, gamblers, drunkards, travelers, guards, thieves.

Blacksmith:
Blacksmith, apprentice, customer, delivery worker, guard if city-owned.

Temple:
Priest, healer, monk, worshippers, beggars, sick people, guards, pilgrims.

Market:
Merchants, customers, guards, pickpockets, animals, porters, children, nobles with escorts.

Noble Court:
Nobles, servants, guards, scribes, advisors, musicians, messengers.

Peasant Village:
Farmers, children, elders, animals, traders, militia, cooks, woodcutters.

Thieves Den:
Thieves, smugglers, fences, lookouts, informants, assassins, gamblers.

Forest:
Wolves, deer, bears, birds, bandits, hunters, hermits, spirits.

Dungeon:
Monsters, prisoners, cultists, guards, bosses, traps, scavengers.
```

---

## Surface Logic

NPCs must use the correct surface.

```text
Humans walk on floors, roads, stairs, bridges, docks, fields, and platforms.
Fish swim in water.
Birds perch on beams, trees, rooftops, cages, or fly in air.
Bats hang from ceilings or fly in caves.
Spiders can walk on floors, walls, and ceilings if designed.
Snakes crawl on ground, rocks, sand, or jungle floors.
Ghosts may hover if explicitly marked as supernatural.
Magical creatures may float only if their species supports it.
```

---

# 5. NPC Types

## Main NPC Categories

```text
Civilian NPCs
Merchant NPCs
Guard NPCs
Noble NPCs
Peasant NPCs
Thief NPCs
Criminal NPCs
Crafting NPCs
Quest NPCs
Faction NPCs
Companion NPCs
Animal NPCs
Creature NPCs
Monster NPCs
Boss NPCs
Ambient NPCs
Traveling NPCs
Service NPCs
```

Each category should have different placement, behavior, conversations, combat, and reactions.

---

# 6. Civilian NPCs

Civilian NPCs make towns feel alive.

Examples:

```text
Villagers
Children
Elders
Travelers
Laborers
Servants
Farmers
Cooks
Stable hands
Dock workers
Miners
Inn guests
Street performers
```

## Civilian Behavior

```text
Follow daily routines.
Avoid danger.
Talk to nearby NPCs.
React to weather.
React to crime.
React to player reputation.
Buy goods, eat, sleep, work, rest.
Call guards when threatened.
Flee from monsters or criminals.
```

## Civilian Combat

Most civilians should not fight well.

```text
Low HP
Low Attack Points
Low armor
High fear response
May throw objects or use basic tools
May call guards
May hide indoors
```

Example:

```text
Common Villager
HP: 120
Attack Points: 10
Defense: 5
Behavior: flee, call guards, remember attacker
```

---

# 7. Merchant and Storekeeper System

Storekeepers must have items to sell and rules for buying items from players.

## Merchant Core Rules

A merchant has:

```text
Shop Type
Inventory
Gold Supply
Buy Price Modifier
Sell Price Modifier
Restock Schedule
Reputation Modifier
Regional Economy Modifier
Illegal Goods Rules
Faction Restrictions
Dialogue Options
Security Response
```

---

## Merchant Types

```text
General Goods Merchant
Blacksmith
Armorer
Weapon Seller
Alchemist
Food Vendor
Innkeeper
Stable Master
Tailor
Jeweler
Book Seller
Magic Item Vendor
Fence / Black Market Dealer
Auctioneer
Guild Supplier
Traveling Merchant
Luxury Merchant
Poor Village Trader
Faction Quartermaster
```

---

## Selling and Buying Rules

Storekeepers sell items at a higher price than they buy them.

```text
Merchant Sell Price = Base Item Value × Sell Modifier
Merchant Buy Price = Base Item Value × Buy Modifier
```

Recommended:

```text
Normal merchant sells at 120% of base value.
Normal merchant buys at 50% of base value.
Friendly merchant buys at 60% to 75%.
Disliked merchant buys at 25% to 40%.
Black market fence buys stolen goods at 20% to 60%.
Luxury merchant sells at 150% to 300%.
Guild quartermaster sells at discounted faction prices.
```

Example:

```text
Iron Sword Base Value: 100 gold

Normal Merchant:
Sells for 120 gold
Buys for 50 gold

Friendly Merchant:
Sells for 105 gold
Buys for 70 gold

Disliked Merchant:
Sells for 150 gold
Buys for 30 gold

Fence:
Buys stolen sword for 35 gold
```

---

## Merchant Inventory Rules

Merchant inventory should match role and location.

```text
Blacksmith:
Weapons, armor, tools, ingots, repair service.

Alchemist:
Potions, herbs, antidotes, reagents, poison if shady.

Innkeeper:
Food, drinks, rooms, rumors, local quests.

Stable Master:
Mounts, feed, saddles, travel service.

Temple Healer:
Healing, cures, blessings, holy items.

Thief Fence:
Stolen goods, lockpicks, disguises, illegal contracts.

Noble Tailor:
Expensive clothing, dyes, jewelry, formal outfits.

Rural Trader:
Basic food, rope, torches, simple tools.
```

---

## Merchant Reaction to Player Standing

Merchant behavior should use:

```text
Player Likeability
Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction Reputation
Personal Relationship
Recent Actions
```

### Merchant Response Examples

```text
Beloved player:
Discounts, rare goods, warm greetings, rumors.

Known outlaw:
Refuses service unless criminal-friendly.
Calls guards if lawful merchant.
Sells secretly if black market merchant.

High notoriety hero:
Special inventory unlocks.
Merchant asks for endorsement.

Hated player:
Higher prices.
Limited dialogue.
May refuse to buy items.

Thief reputation:
Merchant watches closely.
May hide expensive items.
May accuse player if theft happens nearby.
```

---

## Stolen Goods Rules

Merchants should detect stolen goods based on type.

```text
Normal merchants refuse stolen goods if detected.
Fences buy stolen goods at reduced prices.
Guards may inspect suspicious players.
Rare stolen items may trigger alerts.
```

Detection depends on:

```text
Merchant skill
Item fame
Player disguise
Player legal standing
Location
Witnesses
Notoriety
```

---

# 8. Guard NPC System

Guards enforce the law and respond to crime.

## Guard Types

```text
Town Guard
Gate Guard
Patrol Guard
Elite Guard
Noble Guard
Temple Guard
Prison Guard
Road Warden
Bounty Hunter
Military Soldier
Captain of the Guard
Royal Guard
Corrupt Guard
Faction Enforcer
```

---

## Guard Placement

Guards should be placed at logical points.

```text
City gates
Market squares
Noble districts
Prisons
Banks
Temples
Barracks
Patrol routes
Road checkpoints
Docks
Taverns during busy hours
Festival areas
High-crime alleys
Near important NPCs
```

Bad guard placement:

```text
Guard standing alone in random bedroom
Guard facing a wall forever
Guard blocking a shop entrance
Guard floating on stairs
Guard patrolling through locked doors
Guard ignoring murder in front of them
```

---

## Guard Patrol Rules

Guards should patrol with purpose.

```text
Patrol Route:
Point A: Gate
Point B: Market
Point C: Alley
Point D: Fountain
Point E: Barracks
Return to Point A
```

Each patrol point can have behavior:

```text
Stand watch
Look around
Talk to citizen
Inspect suspicious NPC
Check door
Pause at torch
Turn around
Observe crowd
```

---

## Guard Crime Detection

Guards look for:

```text
Assault
Murder
Theft
Pickpocketing
Trespassing
Breaking locks
Smuggling
Arson
Weapon drawn in restricted zone
Attacking civilians
Attacking guards
Illegal magic
Public intoxication, if law exists
Forbidden faction symbols
Bounty targets
```

---

## Guard Detection Rules

Crime detection should depend on:

```text
Line of sight
Distance
Lighting
Noise
Witnesses
Player stealth
Player disguise
Guard alertness
Crowd density
Player notoriety
Legal standing
Crime severity
```

Example:

```text
Stealing bread in a dark alley:
Low chance of guard detection unless witnessed.

Murdering a noble in the market:
Very high detection chance.

Casting forbidden magic in a temple:
Immediate detection if guards or priests are nearby.
```

---

## Guard Response Levels

Guards should not always instantly attack.

```text
Level 1: Warning
Level 2: Questioning
Level 3: Fine
Level 4: Confiscation
Level 5: Arrest Attempt
Level 6: Combat
Level 7: Reinforcements
Level 8: City Lockdown
```

---

## Guard Response by Legal Score

```text
High Legal Standing:
Guards trust player.
Minor crimes may get warnings.

Neutral:
Normal law enforcement.

Suspicious:
Guards inspect player more often.

Troublemaker:
Guards stop player at gates.
Higher fines.

Outlaw:
Arrest on sight.

Public Enemy:
Attack/capture on sight.
Elite guards and bounty hunters called.
```

---

## Guard Combat Rules

Guards should have:

```text
High HP
High Attack Points
Stun ability
Net/root ability
Whistle/call reinforcements
Shield block
Arrest ability
Non-lethal takedown option
High detection of stealth
```

Example:

```text
Town Guard
HP: 800
Attack Points: 160
Abilities:
- Sword Strike
- Shield Bash
- Arrest Command
- Call Reinforcements
- Detect Suspicious Behavior
```

---

# 9. Social Class System

NPCs should behave differently based on class and position.

## Social Classes

```text
Royalty
High Nobility
Low Nobility
Wealthy Merchants
Scholars
Priests
Military Officers
Guild Masters
Craftsmen
Common Merchants
Peasants
Servants
Laborers
Travelers
Criminals
Thieves
Outcasts
Slaves or indentured workers, if your world includes them carefully
Hermits
Nomads
Foreigners
Cultists
```

---

## Class Behavior Examples

### Nobles

Nobles are wealthy, protected, and status-driven.

```text
Prefer formal speech.
Expect respect.
May refuse to speak to dirty, poor, outlaw, or low-status players.
Have guards or servants nearby.
Offer political quests.
Reward with influence, titles, land, or access.
May look down on peasants.
May care about reputation, fashion, lineage, and alliances.
```

Noble reaction:

```text
High Notoriety Hero:
Invited to court.

High Likeability Commoner:
Treated politely but still socially below them.

Outlaw:
Guards called.

Low class but rich player:
May be tolerated but mocked.

Player wearing noble clothes:
Gets better initial treatment if disguise works.
```

---

### Peasants

Peasants are practical, local, and survival-focused.

```text
Care about food, safety, taxes, crops, sickness, monsters, and family.
React strongly to kindness or cruelty.
May help hide beloved outlaws.
May fear nobles and guards.
Offer small quests.
Have limited money.
Pay in food, rumors, shelter, or local trust.
```

Peasant quests:

```text
Find lost goat.
Repair fence.
Drive wolves from field.
Deliver medicine.
Recover stolen tools.
Escort child home.
Bring firewood.
Help during harvest.
```

---

### Merchants

Merchants care about profit, safety, reputation, and trade.

```text
Adjust prices based on standing.
Remember theft.
Reward protection.
Hire escorts.
Offer delivery quests.
Trade rumors.
Refuse dangerous customers.
```

---

### Guards and Soldiers

Guards care about law, order, rank, and duty.

```text
Respect lawful players.
Distrust criminals.
Follow orders.
Patrol areas.
Respond to crime.
May be corrupt depending on personality.
```

---

### Thieves

Thieves care about secrecy, opportunity, and risk.

```text
Avoid guards.
Operate at night.
Use alleys, rooftops, sewers, taverns, and markets.
React positively to outlaw players.
Distrust highly lawful players.
Sell illegal services.
Offer stealth quests.
May rob careless players.
```

Thief services:

```text
Fence stolen goods
Sell lockpicks
Sell disguises
Provide rumors
Offer smuggling jobs
Reveal secret entrances
Teach stealth skills
Arrange bribes
```

---

### Priests and Healers

```text
Heal players.
Bless players.
Condemn murder or dark magic.
Help the poor.
Offer moral quests.
May refuse evil players.
May shelter redeemed criminals.
```
