
---

### Scholars and Mages

```text
Offer lore.
Sell books and spells.
Ask for rare materials.
Study ruins.
React to magical crimes.
May ignore social class but care about knowledge.
```

---

# 10. NPC Personality System

Class should not make all NPCs identical. Give each NPC a personality.

## Personality Traits

```text
Kind
Greedy
Cowardly
Brave
Proud
Suspicious
Corrupt
Honest
Lazy
Hardworking
Religious
Cruel
Cheerful
Angry
Curious
Secretive
Romantic
Bitter
Loyal
Ambitious
Paranoid
Naive
Wise
Aggressive
Peaceful
```

NPC behavior should be influenced by:

```text
Class + Role + Personality + Faction + Recent Events + Player Standing
```

Example:

```text
A kind noble may help peasants.
A cruel noble may exploit them.
A corrupt guard may accept bribes.
An honest guard will arrest criminals.
A cowardly bandit may flee when injured.
A brave peasant may defend their family.
```

---

# 11. Player Standing Response System

NPCs should respond to the player based on prior systems:

```text
Likeability
Good Samaritan / Outlaw Legal Standing
Notoriety
Faction Reputation
Personal Relationship
Recent Crime
Disguise
Social Class
Quest History
```

---

## General NPC Reaction Matrix

| Player Standing                   | NPC Reaction                                     |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| High Likeability                  | Friendly, helpful, discounts, extra dialogue     |
| Low Likeability                   | Cold, hostile, refuses help                      |
| High Legal Standing               | Guards trust player, lawful NPCs approve         |
| Low Legal Standing                | Guards suspicious, lawful NPCs fearful           |
| High Notoriety                    | NPCs recognize player, gossip spreads            |
| Outlaw + High Likeability         | Commoners may hide player from guards            |
| Outlaw + Low Likeability          | NPCs report player immediately                   |
| High Notoriety + Low Likeability  | Fear, avoidance, possible assassination attempts |
| High Notoriety + High Likeability | Cheers, gifts, unique quests                     |
| Thief Reputation                  | Criminals offer jobs, merchants watch player     |
| Noble Reputation                  | Court access, peasant resentment possible        |

---

## NPC Response Levels

Each NPC should choose a response level.

```text
Level 1: Ignore
Level 2: Greet
Level 3: Friendly conversation
Level 4: Offer help
Level 5: Offer quest
Level 6: Offer discount/service
Level 7: Avoid player
Level 8: Refuse service
Level 9: Call guards
Level 10: Flee
Level 11: Threaten player
Level 12: Attack player
```

---

## When NPCs Attack the Player

NPCs may attack if:

```text
Player attacks them first.
Player attacks their ally.
Player is a known enemy faction.
Player is a public enemy.
Player enters forbidden territory.
Player steals from them.
Player threatens their family.
Player is a monster/creature target.
Player has an active bounty.
Player is disguised but discovered.
Player breaks sacred law.
Player harms animals protected by the NPC.
```

NPCs should not attack randomly unless their type supports it.

```text
Bandits may ambush.
Wolves may attack when hungry.
Guards attack criminals.
Cultists attack outsiders.
Territorial creatures attack intruders.
Nobles usually order guards instead of fighting directly.
Peasants usually flee or defend weakly.
```

---

# 12. Conversation System

NPCs should have multiple responses and real conversations.

## Conversation Requirements

Each NPC should support:

```text
Greeting
Small talk
Role-specific dialogue
Reputation response
Quest dialogue
Trade dialogue, if merchant
Crime response
Faction response
Rumor response
Goodbye
Repeated interaction response
Emergency dialogue
```

---

## Dialogue Should Vary

Bad:

```text
NPC says the same line forever.
```

Good:

```text
NPC has several greetings based on time, weather, reputation, and events.
```

Example:

```text
Morning:
"Early start, traveler?"

Rain:
"Best get inside before the roads turn to mud."

High Likeability:
"Good to see you again. The town still talks about what you did."

Low Legal Standing:
"Keep your hands where I can see them."

High Notoriety:
"Wait... are you the one who slew the Ashen Drake?"

Outlaw:
"I do not want trouble. Please leave."
```

---

## Conversation Memory

NPCs should remember major interactions.

```text
Player helped NPC.
Player insulted NPC.
Player stole from NPC.
Player saved NPC’s family.
Player killed NPC’s friend.
Player completed quest.
Player failed quest.
Player lied.
Player paid debt.
Player attacked town.
```

Memory can be:

```text
Short-term memory: minutes or hours
Daily memory: current in-game day
Long-term memory: permanent important events
Regional rumor memory: spreads to other NPCs
Faction memory: shared among related NPCs
```

---

## Dialogue Branching

NPC conversations should branch by:

```text
Player reputation
Quest status
Class
Faction
Race/species
Time of day
Current event
Player equipment
Player criminal status
NPC mood
NPC fear level
NPC trust level
```

---

## Conversation Example

```text
Player speaks to farmer.

Farmer, neutral:
"Need something? I have work to finish."

Farmer, high Likeability:
"Ah, friend! If you have a moment, I could use your help again."

Farmer, outlaw but liked:
"I shouldn't be seen speaking with you... but you helped my son. What do you need?"

Farmer, hated:
"Get away from my field."

Farmer, public enemy:
"Guards! Guards!"
```

---

# 13. Small Quest Core Loops

NPCs should have small quests that support daily life.

These are not always epic quests. They make the MMO world feel alive.

## Small Quest Types

```text
Fetch Quest
Delivery Quest
Escort Quest
Repair Quest
Gathering Quest
Protection Quest
Lost Item Quest
Animal Rescue Quest
Debt Collection Quest
Message Delivery Quest
Crafting Request
Investigation Quest
Clean-up Quest
Monster Removal Quest
Social Mediation Quest
Trade Route Quest
Rumor Confirmation Quest
Hidden Object Quest
```

---

## Small Quest Examples

### Peasant Quests

```text
Find missing goat.
Bring medicine to sick child.
Collect firewood.
Fix broken fence.
Clear rats from barn.
Deliver grain to mill.
Protect crops from wolves.
Recover stolen tools.
Help during harvest.
```

### Merchant Quests

```text
Deliver package.
Escort caravan.
Recover stolen goods.
Find rare ingredient.
Negotiate with supplier.
Expose fake coins.
Protect shop during festival.
```

### Guard Quests

```text
Report suspicious activity.
Find stolen weapon.
Patrol with guard.
Capture thief alive.
Investigate alley murder.
Escort prisoner.
Watch city gate during festival.
```

### Noble Quests

```text
Deliver sealed letter.
Investigate rival house.
Recover family heirloom.
Attend court event.
Protect noble carriage.
Expose scandal.
```

### Thief Quests

```text
Pickpocket target.
Smuggle item.
Distract guard.
Retrieve stolen goods.
Break into warehouse.
Plant evidence.
Hide fugitive.
```

### Animal/Critter Quests

```text
Return lost dog.
Heal wounded horse.
Calm aggressive bull.
Track missing hawk.
Relocate dangerous bear.
Feed starving animals.
```

---

## Quest Reward Rules

Rewards should match NPC class.

```text
Poor NPC:
Food, small coins, local reputation, shelter, rumors.

Merchant:
Gold, discount, trade access, rare item.

Guard:
Legal standing, bounty access, weapon, city access.

Noble:
Large gold, title, political favor, estate access.

Thief:
Illegal goods, secret routes, lockpicks, black market access.

Priest:
Blessing, healing, moral reputation.

Scholar:
Lore, spells, maps, ancient knowledge.
```

---

## Small Quest Cooldowns

To prevent farming:

```text
Minor daily quests reset once per in-game day.
Repeated quests give reduced reputation.
Important personal quests happen once.
Emergency quests spawn dynamically.
```

---

# 14. NPC Movement and Life Simulation

NPCs should move like they live in the world.

## Daily Schedule System

Each NPC should have a schedule.

Example:

```text
6:00 AM - Wake up
7:00 AM - Eat breakfast
8:00 AM - Walk to work
9:00 AM - Work
12:00 PM - Eat lunch
1:00 PM - Resume work
5:00 PM - Visit market or tavern
8:00 PM - Return home
10:00 PM - Sleep
```

---

## Schedule Examples by NPC Type

### Merchant

```text
Morning: Opens shop.
Day: Sells goods.
Evening: Counts money, closes shop.
Night: Goes home or sleeps behind shop.
Emergency: Locks door and calls guards.
```

### Guard

```text
Morning: Reports to barracks.
Day: Patrols route.
Evening: Gate duty.
Night: Torch patrol.
Emergency: Runs to crime location.
```

### Noble

```text
Morning: Breakfast with servants.
Day: Court, meetings, hunting, politics.
Evening: Feast, music, private chambers.
Night: Sleeps under guard protection.
```

### Peasant

```text
Morning: Works field.
Day: Carries goods, repairs, harvests.
Evening: Eats with family.
Night: Sleeps.
Rain: Moves indoors or works in barn.
```

### Thief

```text
Day: Pretends to be commoner or hides.
Evening: Watches targets.
Night: Steals, smuggles, meets contacts.
Danger: Escapes through alleys or sewers.
```

### Animal

```text
Morning: Grazes or hunts.
Day: Wanders territory.
Evening: Returns to den/stable.
Night: Sleeps or hunts depending on species.
Danger: Flees or attacks.
```

### Creature

```text
Patrols territory.
Guards nest.
Sleeps in lair.
Hunts when hungry.
Attacks intruders.
Flees if wounded unless aggressive.
```

---

# 15. Movement Types

NPCs should not all move the same way.

```text
Idle Standing
Wandering
Patrolling
Working
Following
Escorting
Fleeing
Chasing
Searching
Hiding
Sitting
Sleeping
Flying
Swimming
Climbing
Crawling
Teleporting, if magical
Mounted movement
Carriage movement
Group movement
```

---

## Wander Movement

Used by civilians and animals.

Rules:

```text
NPC chooses random valid point within allowed area.
NPC walks there.
NPC pauses.
NPC performs idle animation.
NPC chooses next point.
```

Limits:

```text
Do not wander into walls.
Do not wander into restricted zones.
Do not block doors.
Do not wander outside assigned district unless schedule says so.
```

---

## Patrol Movement

Used by guards, creatures, and military NPCs.

Rules:

```text
NPC follows predefined route.
NPC pauses at patrol points.
NPC scans area.
NPC reacts to suspicious activity.
NPC may vary timing slightly to avoid robotic behavior.
```

---

## Flee Movement

Used by civilians and animals.

Rules:

```text
NPC identifies threat.
NPC chooses safe destination.
NPC avoids running toward threat.
NPC avoids blocked paths.
NPC calls for help if intelligent.
NPC hides if possible.
```

Safe destinations:

```text
Home
Guard post
Temple
Barracks
Cave
Den
Crowd
Locked room
Behind stronger ally
```

---

## Chase Movement

Used by hostile NPCs.

Rules:

```text
NPC follows target using navmesh.
NPC checks leash radius.
NPC uses abilities if in range.
NPC gives up if target unreachable for too long.
NPC returns home if target escapes.
```

---

# 16. Lifelike AI Behavior

NPCs should have small behaviors that make them feel alive.

## Idle Behaviors

```text
Look around
Stretch
Scratch head
Talk to nearby NPC
Eat
Drink
Sweep
Hammer
Read
Pray
Count coins
Feed animals
Sharpen weapon
Lean on wall
Sit down
Warm hands by fire
Check inventory
Wave to friend
Complain about weather
React to player bumping into them
```

---

## Social Behaviors

```text
NPCs greet each other.
Friends talk longer.
Enemies avoid each other.
Nobles ignore peasants unless needed.
Guards question suspicious people.
Thieves watch guards.
Children run from monsters.
Merchants advertise goods.
Priests bless followers.
Bards sing about current events.
```

---

## Weather Reactions

```text
Rain:
NPCs seek cover, use cloaks, close stalls.

Snow:
NPCs move slower, gather near fires.

Storm:
Animals hide, townspeople go indoors.

Heat:
NPCs rest in shade, drink more water.

Fog:
Guards become more alert, thieves more active.
```

---

## Time of Day Reactions

```text
Morning:
Workers go to jobs.

Day:
Markets busy.

Evening:
Taverns fill.

Night:
Guards patrol more.
Thieves become active.
Shops close.
Most civilians sleep.
Dangerous creatures roam.
```

---

# 17. Animal NPC System

Animals are NPCs too, but they should behave differently from people.

## Animal Types

```text
Domestic animals
Wild animals
Predators
Prey animals
Mounts
Pets
Livestock
Birds
Fish
Insects
Magical beasts
```

---

## Animal Behavior Examples

### Cow

```text
Grazes in field.
Returns to barn at night.
Flees from wolves.
Can be milked if owned.
May trigger legal penalty if killed.
```

### Dog

```text
Follows owner.
Barks at thieves.
May help track scents.
Can become friendly with player.
May attack if owner is attacked.
```

### Wolf

```text
Packs with other wolves.
Hunts deer or players.
Avoids large towns.
May flee from fire.
Attacks weaker targets.
```

### Horse

```text
Stays at stable.
Can be mounted if owned.
Panics during combat unless trained.
Can be stolen, triggering crime.
```

### Bird

```text
Perches on roofs, trees, fences.
Flies away when approached.
Returns after danger passes.
```

### Fish

```text
Must remain inside water volume.
Swims in schools.
Flees from players.
Can be caught by fishing.
```

---

# 18. Creature and Monster NPC System

Creatures should have ecology and territory.

## Creature Behavior

```text
Guard territory.
Hunt prey.
Sleep in dens.
React to noise.
Protect nests.
Avoid stronger predators.
Attack enemies.
Flee when injured, depending on species.
Call pack members.
```

---

## Creature Placement

```text
Wolves near forests, dens, prey routes.
Bears near caves, rivers, berry bushes.
Undead near graveyards, ruins, cursed lands.
Goblins near camps, caves, roads, stolen goods.
Dragons in mountains, caves, volcanic zones.
Spiders in caves, forests, abandoned buildings.
Water monsters in lakes, rivers, swamps.
```

---

# 19. NPC Faction System

Every NPC should belong to a faction or group.

Examples:

```text
Town Civilians
City Guard
Royal Court
Merchant Guild
Thieves Guild
Temple
Farmers
Bandits
Forest Wildlife
Undead
Cultists
Mage Council
Noble House
Rebel Faction
```

Faction affects:

```text
Who they help
Who they attack
Who they trade with
Who they fear
What laws they follow
What quests they offer
What rumors they spread
How they react to the player
```

---

# 20. Emergency Behavior System

NPCs need emergency reactions.

## Emergency Types

```text
Crime nearby
Monster attack
Fire
War invasion
Player attacks NPC
Public enemy enters town
Weather disaster
Boss event
Plague
Riot
Building collapse
Raid
```

---

## Emergency Responses

```text
Civilian:
Flee, hide, call guards.

Merchant:
Close shop, lock chest, call guards.

Guard:
Move to threat, warn, arrest, fight, call backup.

Noble:
Retreat to protected area, order guards.

Thief:
Hide, loot, escape, exploit chaos.

Animal:
Flee, panic, hide, stampede.

Monster:
Attack, roar, call pack, defend territory.
```

---

# 21. NPC Memory and Rumor System

NPCs should not forget everything instantly.

## Memory Types

```text
Personal Memory
Family Memory
Faction Memory
Town Memory
Regional Rumor
World News
```

Example:

```text
Player saves a farmer:
Farmer remembers permanently.
Farmer’s family likes player.
Village rumor spreads.
Nearby merchants offer small discount.

Player murders a shopkeeper:
Witnesses remember.
Guards create bounty.
Shopkeeper’s family hates player.
Town becomes fearful.
Other merchants lock doors.
```

---

# 22. NPC Conversation With Memory

NPC dialogue should change after events.

Example:

```text
Before quest:
"My daughter went missing near the old mill."

After accepting:
"Please hurry. I fear the wolves will find her."

After success:
"You saved her. I will never forget this."

Weeks later:
"There will always be a warm meal for you here."

If player failed:
"She is gone because you were too late."

If player caused harm:
"Do not speak to me. Guards!"
```

---

# 23. NPC AI Decision Loop

Each NPC should run a decision loop.

## Basic Loop

```text
1. Am I alive?
2. Am I in a valid position?
3. Am I in danger?
4. Am I currently assigned to a script, quest, or conversation?
5. What is my schedule right now?
6. Is the player interacting with me?
7. Are there nearby crimes, threats, friends, enemies, or opportunities?
8. Choose action.
9. Validate movement/action.
10. Execute action.
11. Update memory and state.
```

---

## Priority Order

Emergency should override normal life.

```text
Highest Priority:
Death
Combat
Fire/danger
Crime response
Scripted quest event
Conversation with player
Current job task
Schedule movement
Idle behavior
```

Bad:

```text
NPC continues sweeping while dragon attacks town.
```

Good:

```text
NPC drops broom, screams, runs indoors, and locks door.
```

---

# 24. NPC Data Structure

Example NPC data:

```json
{
  "id": "npc_blacksmith_elda",
  "name": "Elda Ironhand",
  "type": "person",
  "role": "blacksmith",
  "social_class": "craftsman",
  "faction": "town_civilians",
  "level": 12,
  "hp": 420,
  "max_hp": 420,
  "attack_points": 65,
  "defense": 80,
  "personality": ["hardworking", "honest", "stern"],
  "home_location": "elda_house",
  "work_location": "blacksmith_shop",
  "valid_anchors": ["forge_anchor", "counter_anchor", "storage_anchor"],
  "shop": {
    "sells": ["iron_sword", "iron_axe", "repair_kit", "chainmail"],
    "buys_categories": ["weapons", "armor", "ore", "tools"],
    "sell_modifier": 1.2,
    "buy_modifier": 0.5,
    "gold_supply": 1500,
    "restock_hours": 24
  },
  "conversation": {
    "greeting_neutral": "Need a blade repaired?",
    "greeting_liked": "Good to see you. Your steel is always welcome here.",
    "greeting_outlaw": "I do not want trouble in my shop.",
    "greeting_hated": "Leave before I call the guards."
  },
  "schedule": [
    {
      "time": "06:00",
      "action": "wake_up",
      "location": "elda_house"
    },
    {
      "time": "08:00",
      "action": "work_forge",
      "location": "blacksmith_shop"
    },
    {
      "time": "18:00",
      "action": "close_shop",
      "location": "blacksmith_shop"
    },
    {
      "time": "21:00",
      "action": "sleep",
      "location": "elda_house"
    }
  ],
  "quest_pool": ["repair_bellows", "deliver_sword", "recover_stolen_tools"],
  "crime_response": "call_guards",
  "combat_behavior": "defensive"
}
```

---

# 25. NPC Placement Data Structure

```json
{
  "spawn_id": "merchant_spawn_01",
  "npc_id": "npc_blacksmith_elda",
  "anchor_id": "blacksmith_counter_anchor",
  "required_surface": "floor",
  "requires_navmesh": true,
  "collision_radius": 0.45,
  "height_required": 1.9,
  "allowed_states": ["standing", "working", "talking"],
  "invalid_if_blocking": ["doorway", "stairs", "quest_object", "player_spawn"],
  "fallback_radius": 5.0
}
```

---

# 26. NPC Edge Cases

## NPC Spawns Inside Wall

Rule:

```text
Do not spawn.
Search nearby valid anchor.
If none exists, disable NPC and log placement error.
```

---

## NPC Floats Above Ground

Rule:

```text
Snap to valid ground.
If no ground exists within allowed distance, placement is invalid.
```

---

## NPC Sinks Into Floor

Rule:

```text
Use character capsule height and foot offset.
Place feet on surface, not body center.
```

---

## NPC Blocks Door

Rule:

```text
Doorways are no-standing zones unless NPC is intentionally guarding the door.
If guarding, leave enough space or use interaction to pass.
```

---

## NPC Walks Through Furniture

Rule:

```text
Furniture must block navmesh.
NPC pathfinding must route around it.
```

---

## NPC Gets Stuck

Rule:

```text
Try path recalculation.
Try local avoidance.
Move to nearest valid navmesh point.
If still stuck, teleport to assigned anchor only if player is not watching.
Log the issue.
```

---

## NPC Walks Through Closed Door

Rule:

```text
NPC must open door, wait for door, or choose another route.
Locked doors require key/permission.
```

---

## NPC Stands on Chair Instead of Sitting

Rule:

```text
Chair must use sitting anchor.
Sitting animation must align hips to chair seat.
NPC cannot use standing anchor on chair seat unless intended.
```

---

## Too Many NPCs in One Area

Rule:

```text
Use density limits.
NPCs must avoid overlapping.
Crowds need crowd navigation.
Important paths must remain clear.
```

---

## NPC Talks While Dead

Rule:

```text
Dead state disables normal conversation.
Only corpse inspection, resurrection, or ghost dialogue may remain.
```

---

## NPC Continues Shop During Combat

Rule:

```text
Combat or emergency state disables trade.
Merchant flees, hides, locks shop, or calls guards.
```

---

## NPC Forgets Player Murdered Them

Rule:

```text
If NPC survives, personal memory records attack.
If NPC dies and respawns as same character, world memory or faction memory should preserve major crime.
```

---

## Player Attacks Quest NPC

Rule options:

```text
Essential NPC cannot die, only becomes wounded.
NPC can die, quest fails or changes.
NPC death creates alternate quest path.
Guards respond.
Reputation changes.
```

For MMOs, avoid letting players permanently break shared world quests unless the world is designed for it.

---

# 27. NPC Relationship System

NPCs should have relationships with each other.

Relationship types:

```text
Family
Friend
Enemy
Employer
Employee
Guarded Person
Business Partner
Guild Member
Romantic Partner
Rival
Debt Owed
Faction Ally
Faction Enemy
Pet Owner
Master/Servant
Teacher/Student
```

This affects reactions.

Example:

```text
Player attacks shopkeeper.
Shopkeeper’s spouse hates player.
Shopkeeper’s apprentice refuses service.
Town guards respond.
Merchant guild raises prices.
Thieves guild may approve if shopkeeper was corrupt.
```

---

# 28. NPC World Population Rules

A town should have a believable NPC mix.

Example medieval town:

```text
5% nobles/officials
10% guards/soldiers
15% merchants/craftsmen
40% peasants/laborers
10% servants
5% priests/scholars
5% travelers
5% criminals/thieves
5% children/elders/special NPCs
```

Adjust based on location.

A military fort has more soldiers.
A farming village has more peasants.
A port has more sailors and merchants.
A slum has more laborers, beggars, thieves, and guards.
A noble district has more servants, guards, and wealthy NPCs.

---

# 29. NPC Interaction With Player Reputation

Tie NPC behavior directly to the ranking systems.

## Likeability

```text
High Likeability:
NPCs smile, help, offer rumors, discounts, small gifts.

Low Likeability:
NPCs insult, avoid, refuse service, report player.
```

## Legal Standing

```text
High Legal Standing:
Guards trust player, lawful NPCs cooperate.

Low Legal Standing:
Guards question player, lawful NPCs fear player, criminals may approach.
```

## Notoriety

```text
High Notoriety:
NPCs recognize player.
Dialogue changes.
More quests unlock.
Enemies may target player.
Fans may follow player.
```

---

# 30. NPC Reaction Examples

## Beloved Hero Enters Town

```text
Children follow.
Merchants offer discounts.
Guards salute.
Peasants ask for help.
Nobles request meeting.
Bards sing about player.
```

## Hated Outlaw Enters Town

```text
Civilians flee.
Guards approach.
Merchants close shops.
Bounty hunters track player.
Thieves may offer secret help if player has criminal reputation.
```

## Unknown Player Enters Town

```text
Most NPCs ignore player.
Merchants offer normal prices.
Guards watch normally.
Small quests available.
```

## Famous Villain Enters Village

```text
Villagers hide indoors.
Guards call reinforcements.
Some NPCs beg for mercy.
Dark cultists may worship player.
Bounty event may trigger.
```

---

# 31. AI Prompt Version

You can give this directly to an AI building/designing NPCs:

```text
Design all NPCs as living entities, not decorations.

Every NPC must have:
- A valid physical position
- A valid floor, surface, perch, chair, bed, water volume, or flying zone
- Collision that does not overlap walls, terrain, props, other NPCs, or player spawn points
- A navigation mesh position if the NPC needs to move
- A role in the world
- A faction
- A social class or creature category
- HP and attack points
- A daily schedule
- A behavior loop
- A conversation system
- A reaction to crime and danger
- A reaction to the player's Likeability, Legal Standing, Notoriety, faction reputation, and personal history
- Optional small quests that match the NPC's role

NPCs must never float, clip into walls, sink into the ground, spawn inside props, block important paths, or use animations that do not match their environment.

Use anchors for placement:
- Standing anchors
- Sitting anchors
- Sleeping anchors
- Work anchors
- Shop counter anchors
- Guard post anchors
- Patrol points
- Animal grazing anchors
- Creature den anchors
- Perch anchors
- Water swim zones
- Flying zones

Merchants must have:
- Inventory
- Items they sell
- Item categories they buy
- Sell price modifiers
- Buy price modifiers
- Gold supply
- Restock schedule
- Reputation-based prices
- Refusal rules for criminals, hated players, or stolen goods

Guards must:
- Patrol believable routes
- Watch for crime
- Respond based on severity
- Warn, question, fine, arrest, attack, or call reinforcements
- Treat players differently based on legal standing
- Protect civilians and important locations

Social classes must affect behavior:
- Nobles expect respect and have guards
- Peasants care about survival and local safety
- Merchants care about profit and trust
- Guards care about law and order
- Thieves care about secrecy and opportunity
- Priests care about morality and healing
- Scholars care about knowledge
- Criminals care about risk, money, and power

NPCs must support multiple conversation responses based on:
- Time of day
- Weather
- Player reputation
- Quest status
- Player crime status
- Faction relationship
- Personal memory
- Recent world events

NPCs should offer small quests:
- Fetch
- Delivery
- Escort
- Repair
- Protection
- Lost item
- Animal rescue
- Gathering
- Investigation
- Crime report
- Crafting request
- Monster removal

NPC movement should feel lifelike:
- Work during the day
- Eat and rest
- Sleep at night
- Visit markets, taverns, temples, homes, or workplaces
- Patrol if guard
- Hide if thief
- Graze or hunt if animal
- Guard territory if creature
- Flee from danger unless brave, aggressive, or duty-bound

NPCs can be people, animals, or creatures. Each must behave according to its body, intelligence, habitat, faction, and survival instincts.
```

---

# Final NPC Design Rule

Every NPC should answer these questions:

```text
Where do they belong physically?
What are they standing, sitting, sleeping, swimming, flying, or working on?
Why are they here?
What do they do every day?
What do they want?
What do they fear?
Who do they serve or belong to?
How do they react to the player?
How do they react to danger?
What can the player do for them?
What can they do to the player?
```

That rule keeps NPCs from becoming random bodies placed in a map and makes them feel like part of a real MMO world.


Below is a full **MMO combat, attack, and hit system** you can give to an AI or use as a design foundation.

The system assumes:

```text
Every player has HP, attack stats, defensive stats, accuracy, evasion, critical chance, and combat state.

Every NPC also has HP and attack points.

Every attack must go through a clear combat pipeline:
Target Check → Range Check → Hit Check → Defense Check → Damage Calculation → Effects → Death/Reward Handling.
```

---

# MMO Combat System Overview

Combat should be **server-authoritative**.

That means the client can show animations and previews, but the server decides:

```text
Did the attack happen?
Was the target valid?
Was the attacker close enough?
Did the attack hit?
How much damage was dealt?
Did the target die?
Who gets credit?
What loot, XP, reputation, or threat changes happen?
```

This prevents cheating, desyncs, fake hits, speed hacks, range hacks, and client-side damage manipulation.

---

# 1. Core Combat Stats

Every combat entity should have these stats:

```text
HP
Max HP
Attack Points
Defense
Accuracy
Evasion
Critical Chance
Critical Damage
Armor
Magic Resistance
Attack Speed
Attack Range
Movement Speed
Level
Threat Value
Faction
Combat State
```

This applies to:

```text
Players
Hostile NPCs
Friendly NPCs
Neutral NPCs
Bosses
Guards
Merchants
Quest NPCs
Pets
Summons
Mounts, if attackable
Training dummies
```

Even peaceful NPCs should have HP and attack points, even if their attack points are very low or unused unless provoked.

---

# 2. Main Stat Definitions

## HP

HP is the entity’s health.

```text
Current HP cannot go below 0.
Current HP cannot exceed Max HP unless temporary shields or overheal systems exist.
```

Example:

```text
Village Child: 50 HP
Common Villager: 120 HP
Guard: 800 HP
Wolf: 250 HP
Bandit: 400 HP
Elite Knight: 2,500 HP
Dungeon Boss: 50,000 HP
World Boss: 5,000,000 HP
```

---

## Attack Points

Attack Points determine base outgoing damage.

```text
Attack Points = the entity’s raw offensive power.
```

Example:

```text
Chicken: 1 Attack Point
Rat: 5 Attack Points
Villager: 10 Attack Points
Wolf: 35 Attack Points
Bandit: 80 Attack Points
Guard: 160 Attack Points
Elite Monster: 500 Attack Points
Boss: 2,000+ Attack Points
```

Attack Points should not directly equal final damage. They are the starting value.

---

## Defense

Defense reduces incoming physical damage.

```text
Higher Defense = less physical damage taken.
```

Defense should have diminishing returns so players cannot become completely immune.

Example formula:

```text
Physical Damage Reduction = Defense / (Defense + AttackerLevel * 100)
```

Example:

```text
Attacker Level: 10
Defender Defense: 500

Reduction = 500 / (500 + 1000)
Reduction = 33.3%
```

---

## Armor

Armor is a type of Defense specifically against physical hits.

Use Armor for:

```text
Sword attacks
Axe attacks
Arrows
Bites
Claws
Blunt weapons
Physical monster attacks
```

---

## Magic Resistance

Magic Resistance reduces magical damage.

Use Magic Resistance for:

```text
Fire
Ice
Lightning
Poison magic
Dark magic
Holy magic
Arcane spells
```

Optional: split resistances by element.

```text
Fire Resistance
Ice Resistance
Poison Resistance
Lightning Resistance
Holy Resistance
Dark Resistance
Arcane Resistance
```

---

## Accuracy

Accuracy determines how likely an attack is to hit.

```text
Higher Accuracy = less chance to miss.
```

---

## Evasion

Evasion determines how likely the defender is to dodge.

```text
Higher Evasion = higher chance to avoid attacks.
```

---

## Critical Chance

Critical Chance is the chance to deal extra damage.

Example:

```text
Critical Chance: 5%
Critical Damage: 150%
```

If the attack normally deals 100 damage, a critical hit deals 150 damage.

---

## Attack Speed

Attack Speed controls how often an entity can attack.

Example:

```text
1.0 attack speed = 1 attack per second
0.5 attack speed = 1 attack every 2 seconds
2.0 attack speed = 2 attacks per second
```

For MMO balance, avoid making attack speed too high. Very fast attacks create server load and balance problems.

---

## Attack Range

Attack Range determines how close the attacker must be.

Example:

```text
Fists: 1.5 meters
Sword: 2.2 meters
Spear: 3.5 meters
Bow: 25 meters
Fireball: 30 meters
Boss stomp: 8 meters
Dragon breath: 20 meters cone
```

---

# 3. Combat Pipeline

Every attack should follow this order:

```text
1. Can the attacker act?
2. Is the target valid?
3. Is the target alive?
4. Is the target attackable?
5. Is the target in range?
6. Is the target in line of sight?
7. Did the attack hit?
8. Was it dodged, blocked, parried, resisted, or absorbed?
9. Calculate damage.
10. Apply damage.
11. Apply secondary effects.
12. Update threat/aggro.
13. Check death.
14. Distribute XP, loot, quest credit, and reputation.
```

This prevents many edge-case bugs.

---

# 4. Attack Validation Rules

Before an attack can happen, validate the following.

## Attacker Must Be Able to Act

The attacker cannot attack if they are:

```text
Dead
Stunned
Frozen
Asleep
Feared
Silenced, for spells
Disarmed, for weapon attacks
Mounted, if mounted combat is not allowed
In a cutscene
Teleporting
Loading
Logging out
In a safe zone
Under spawn protection
Already casting another non-interruptible ability
```

---

## Target Must Be Valid

The target must:

```text
Exist
Be alive
Be attackable
Be within range
Be visible or detectable
Not be immune
Not be in a protected state
Not be inside a safe zone unless rules allow it
Not be on the same faction unless friendly fire is enabled
```

---

## Range Check

The server checks distance, not the client.

```text
If distance between attacker and target > attack range + latency tolerance, attack fails.
```

Example:

```text
Sword range: 2.2 meters
Latency tolerance: 0.3 meters
Allowed range: 2.5 meters
```

Do not give too much tolerance, or players will feel like they are getting hit from too far away.

---

## Line of Sight Check

Ranged attacks and spells should usually require line of sight.

Blocked by:

```text
Walls
Closed doors
Terrain
Large objects
Shields or barriers
Certain magical fields
```

May not be blocked by:

```text
Grass
Small bushes
Decorative props
Transparent windows, if the design allows it
Low fences, depending on projectile type
```

---

# 5. Hit Chance System

An attack should not always hit unless the game is designed that way.

Base formula:

```text
Hit Chance = Base Hit Chance + Attacker Accuracy Modifier - Defender Evasion Modifier
```

Recommended:

```text
Minimum Hit Chance: 5%
Maximum Hit Chance: 95%
```

This prevents guaranteed misses or guaranteed hits in normal combat.

Example:

```text
Base Hit Chance: 80%
Attacker Accuracy Bonus: +10%
Defender Evasion Bonus: -15%

Final Hit Chance = 75%
```

---

## Hit Result Types

Each attack can result in:

```text
Miss
Dodge
Parry
Block
Resist
Absorb
Normal Hit
Critical Hit
Glancing Hit
Crushing Hit
Immune
Evade
```

Each means something different.

---

## Miss

The attacker fails to connect.

```text
No damage.
No effect.
Small threat may still be generated.
Durability loss may still happen depending on design.
```

---

## Dodge

The defender avoids the attack.

```text
No damage.
Some dodge-based abilities may trigger.
Mostly used against melee and projectile attacks.
```

---

## Parry

The defender deflects the attack with a weapon.

```text
No damage or reduced damage.
May allow counterattack.
Usually requires defender to face attacker.
Mostly used by humanoids, guards, duelists, bosses.
```

---

## Block

The defender blocks with a shield, armor, or ability.

```text
Damage reduced by block value.
Some attacks can still partially hit.
```

Example:

```text
Incoming Damage: 300
Block Reduction: 120
Final Damage: 180
```

---

## Resist

The defender resists a spell or effect.

```text
Damage reduced or effect duration shortened.
```

Example:

```text
Fireball damage reduced by Fire Resistance.
Poison duration reduced by Poison Resistance.
Stun resisted entirely by Control Resistance.
```

---

## Absorb

A shield or barrier absorbs damage before HP is affected.

Example:

```text
Incoming Damage: 500
Magic Shield: 300
HP Damage: 200
Shield Remaining: 0
```

---

## Normal Hit

Attack hits and deals normal damage.

---

## Critical Hit

Attack hits and deals bonus damage.

Example:

```text
Normal Damage: 200
Critical Damage: 150%
Final Damage: 300
```

---

## Glancing Hit

A weak hit, usually caused by level difference or poor accuracy.

```text
Deals 50% to 75% normal damage.
```

---

## Crushing Hit

A very strong hit, usually caused by a high-level attacker hitting a low-level defender.

```text
Deals 125% to 200% normal damage.
```

Be careful with this in PvP. It can feel unfair.

---

## Immune

Target cannot be affected by that attack.

Example:

```text
Fire elemental is immune to fire.
Ghost is immune to normal weapons.
Boss is immune to stun.
Player has temporary invulnerability.
```

---

## Evade

Used when the target or attacker is in an invalid combat state.

Example:

```text
NPC cannot reach the player.
Player is on unreachable terrain.
Target is outside leash range.
Pathfinding failed for too long.
```

When this happens:

```text
NPC stops combat.
NPC returns home.
NPC resets HP if appropriate.
Player gets no XP or loot.
```

---

# 6. Damage Formula

Use a simple formula that can scale.

```text
Base Damage = Attack Points × Ability Multiplier
```

Then apply modifiers:

```text
Modified Damage =
Base Damage
× Level Modifier
× Random Damage Variance
× Critical Modifier
× Damage Type Modifier
× Buff/Debuff Modifier
```

Then reduce by defense:

```text
Final Damage =
Modified Damage × (1 - Damage Reduction)
```

Minimum damage rule:

```text
If an attack successfully hits, it should usually deal at least 1 damage unless immune or fully absorbed.
```

---

## Example Damage Calculation

Player attacks wolf with sword.

```text
Player Attack Points: 100
Sword Slash Ability Multiplier: 1.2
Base Damage: 120

Random Variance: 0.95
Critical: No
Wolf Physical Damage Reduction: 25%

Modified Damage = 120 × 0.95 = 114
Final Damage = 114 × 0.75 = 85.5

Final Rounded Damage = 86
```

Wolf loses 86 HP.

---

# 7. Damage Variance

Damage should not be exactly the same every hit.

Recommended:

```text
Normal attacks: 90% to 110% damage
Heavy attacks: 85% to 120% damage
Magic attacks: 95% to 105% damage
Boss attacks: 90% to 115% damage
```

Avoid huge randomness unless the game is intentionally chaotic.

---

# 8. Damage Types

Every attack should have a damage type.

```text
Physical
Piercing
Slashing
Blunt
Fire
Ice
Lightning
Poison
Bleed
Holy
Dark
Arcane
True Damage
Siege Damage
Environmental Damage
```

## Physical Damage

Reduced by Armor/Defense.

## Magical Damage

Reduced by Magic Resistance or element resistance.

## Poison Damage

May ignore some armor, but can be resisted.

## Bleed Damage

Usually physical damage over time. May not affect undead, constructs, or elementals.

## True Damage

Ignores armor and resistance.

Use very carefully. Too much true damage ruins tanks and defensive builds.

## Siege Damage

Used against:

```text
Gates
Walls
Buildings
War machines
Large bosses
```

---

# 9. HP Rules

## HP Cannot Go Below 0

```text
If damage exceeds current HP, set HP to 0.
```

## Overkill

Track overkill for stats, but do not let it create negative HP.

```text
Current HP: 50
Incoming Damage: 200
HP after hit: 0
Overkill: 150
```

Overkill may matter for:

```text
Achievements
Execution abilities
Boss mechanics
Combat logs
PvP stats
```

---

## Healing

Healing restores HP.

```text
New HP = Current HP + Healing Amount
```

But:

```text
Current HP cannot exceed Max HP unless overheal shields exist.
```

---

## Regeneration

Out of combat regeneration can be faster than in-combat regeneration.

Example:

```text
In combat: 1% Max HP every 5 seconds
Out of combat: 5% Max HP every 5 seconds
Resting: 10% Max HP every 5 seconds
Food buff: additional 2% every 5 seconds
```

---

# 10. NPC HP and Attack Point System

Every NPC must have HP and Attack Points.

Even non-combat NPCs need these values so the world behaves consistently.

## NPC Categories

```text
Passive NPC
Civilian NPC
Merchant NPC
Guard NPC
Hostile Creature
Bandit/Enemy Humanoid
Elite NPC
Mini-Boss
Dungeon Boss
World Boss
Quest NPC
Faction Leader
Training Dummy
Pet/Summon
```

---

## NPC Stat Table

| NPC Type      |        HP | Attack Points | Behavior                 |
| ------------- | --------: | ------------: | ------------------------ |
| Chicken       |        10 |             1 | Flees                    |
| Rat           |        25 |             5 | Attacks weak targets     |
| Deer          |        80 |        0 to 5 | Flees                    |
| Villager      |       120 |            10 | Flees or calls guards    |
| Merchant      |       180 |            15 | Calls guards             |
| Farmer        |       160 |            20 | Defends self weakly      |
| Town Guard    |       800 |           160 | Attacks criminals        |
| Elite Guard   |     2,500 |           350 | Protects important areas |
| Wolf          |       250 |            35 | Aggressive predator      |
| Bear          |       900 |           120 | High damage creature     |
| Bandit        |       400 |            80 | Attacks players/NPCs     |
| Bandit Leader |     1,500 |           220 | Uses abilities           |
| Dungeon Elite |     3,000 |           450 | Group challenge          |
| Dungeon Boss  |    50,000 |         2,000 | Mechanics-based fight    |
| World Boss    | 5,000,000 |       10,000+ | Server/group event       |

---

## NPC Level Scaling

Basic NPC formula:

```text
NPC Max HP = Base HP × Level HP Multiplier × Rank Multiplier
NPC Attack Points = Base Attack × Level Attack Multiplier × Rank Multiplier
```

Example rank multipliers:

```text
Civilian: x0.5
Normal: x1.0
Strong: x1.5
Elite: x3.0
Mini-Boss: x8.0
Dungeon Boss: x30.0
World Boss: x500.0+
```

---

## NPC Level Formula Example

```text
Level 10 Wolf

Base HP: 100
Level HP Multiplier: 1 + (Level × 0.15)
Rank Multiplier: 1.0

Max HP = 100 × 2.5 × 1.0
Max HP = 250

Base Attack: 14
Level Attack Multiplier: 1 + (Level × 0.15)

Attack Points = 14 × 2.5
Attack Points = 35
```

---

# 11. NPC Combat Behavior

NPCs should not all fight the same way.

## Passive NPCs

Examples:

```text
Deer
Rabbit
Chicken
Civilian
Child
Unarmed villager
```

Behavior:

```text
Avoid combat
Flee when attacked
Call for help if intelligent
May hide indoors
May alert guards
May remember attacker
May affect Likeability and Legal Score
```

---

## Defensive NPCs

Examples:

```text
Merchant
Farmer
Craftsman
Temple healer
Stable master
```

Behavior:

```text
Do not attack first
Defend themselves if attacked
Call guards
May flee at low HP
May refuse future service
```

---

## Aggressive NPCs

Examples:

```text
Wolf
Bandit
Undead
Monster
Hostile faction soldier
```

Behavior:

```text
Attack on sight
Chase within leash range
Use abilities
Switch targets based on threat
Flee if morale breaks, depending on creature type
```

---

## Guard NPCs

Guard behavior depends on legal score.

```text
If player is lawful: ignore or assist.
If player is suspicious: watch closely.
If player is outlaw: attempt arrest.
If player attacks civilians: intervene.
If player attacks guards: call reinforcements.
If player is public enemy: attack on sight.
```

Guards should have:

```text
High HP
High Attack Points
Stun or root ability
Call-for-help ability
Detection against stealth
Authority to arrest
Faction-wide memory
```

---

## Boss NPCs

Bosses need mechanics, not just high HP.

Bosses should have:

```text
Large HP pool
Phases
Telegraphed attacks
Enrage timer
Area attacks
Summons
Target switching
Threat mechanics
Resistance rules
Anti-exploit leash
Reward lockout
Contribution tracking
```

---

# 12. Aggro and Threat System

NPCs need a way to decide who to attack.

Each hostile NPC has a threat table.

```text
Threat Table:
Player A: 1,200 threat
Player B: 800 threat
Player C: 2,500 threat
```

NPC attacks the highest threat target unless mechanics say otherwise.

---

## Threat Sources

| Action                 |                Threat Generated |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------: |
| Deal 1 damage          |                       +1 threat |
| Heal someone in combat |       +0.5 threat per HP healed |
| Taunt                  |   Forces top threat temporarily |
| Buff ally              |                    Small threat |
| Debuff enemy           |                   Medium threat |
| Crowd control          |                     High threat |
| Standing too close     | Small threat for some creatures |
| Attacking NPC’s ally   |                    Bonus threat |

---

## Tank Rules

Tank abilities should manipulate threat.

```text
Taunt: forces NPC to attack tank for 3 to 6 seconds
Threat stance: increases threat generation by 300%
Shield slam: damage + bonus threat
Guard ally: redirects some damage or threat
```

---

# 13. Attack Types

## Basic Attack

Default attack.

```text
Uses weapon or natural attack.
Costs no resource.
Moderate damage.
Uses attack speed.
```

---

## Heavy Attack

Slow but strong.

```text
Longer wind-up.
Higher damage.
Can be interrupted.
May break block.
Uses stamina or energy.
```

---

## Quick Attack

Fast but weak.

```text
Lower damage.
Short cooldown.
Good for interrupts or finishing low-HP targets.
```

---

## Charged Attack

Power increases while charging.

```text
Minimum charge: weak hit
Medium charge: normal hit
Full charge: strong hit or bonus effect
```

Edge rule:

```text
If interrupted before minimum charge, attack fails.
```

---

## Area Attack

Hits multiple targets.

Rules:

```text
Must check each target individually.
Each target gets its own hit/dodge/block/resist calculation.
Damage may fall off by distance.
Targets behind walls should not be hit unless attack ignores walls.
```

---

## Damage Over Time

Examples:

```text
Bleed
Burn
Poison
Curse
Disease
```

Rules:

```text
Ticks every X seconds.
Cannot stack infinitely unless designed.
Source player gets credit.
Damage stops if cleansed, expires, or target becomes immune.
```

---

## Crowd Control

Examples:

```text
Stun
Root
Slow
Silence
Fear
Knockback
Sleep
Charm
Taunt
Blind
Disarm
```

Rules:

```text
Bosses should resist or reduce most hard crowd control.
Players should have diminishing returns in PvP.
Repeated crowd control should become less effective.
```

---

# 14. Crowd Control Diminishing Returns

To prevent stun-locking:

```text
First stun: 100% duration
Second stun within 15 seconds: 50% duration
Third stun within 15 seconds: 25% duration
Fourth stun within 15 seconds: immune for short time
```

Apply similar rules for:

```text
Fear
Root
Silence
Sleep
Charm
Knockback
```

Do not put every crowd control type in the same category. Group them.

Example categories:

```text
Hard Control: stun, sleep, charm
Movement Control: root, slow
Caster Control: silence, interrupt
Displacement: knockback, pull
Forced Targeting: taunt
```

---

# 15. PvE Rules

PvE combat should prioritize readability and fairness.

## PvE Combat Rules

```text
Enemies should telegraph major attacks.
Boss attacks should be avoidable or defensible.
Trash mobs can have simpler behavior.
NPCs should not hit through walls unless intended.
NPCs should not chase forever.
NPCs should not instantly reset unless exploiting is detected.
Players should receive clear combat feedback.
```

---

## NPC Leash System

NPCs should not chase players across the entire world.

```text
Each NPC has a home position.
Each NPC has a leash radius.
If pulled beyond leash radius, NPC enters Evade state.
```

Example:

```text
Wolf leash: 40 meters
Bandit leash: 60 meters
Guard leash: city district
Boss leash: boss arena
World boss leash: event zone
```

When evading:

```text
NPC stops attacking.
NPC becomes temporarily immune.
NPC returns to spawn/home.
NPC resets HP if fully evaded.
Threat table clears.
Players receive no reward if they were exploiting pathing.
```

---

# 16. PvP Rules

PvP needs stricter rules because players will abuse everything.

## PvP Attack Rules

```text
Player must be flagged for PvP, in a PvP zone, dueling, at war, or criminally attackable.
Safe zones prevent PvP damage.
Spawn protection prevents immediate killing.
Level difference may reduce rewards.
Repeated kills against same target give reduced rewards.
Crowd control has diminishing returns.
Damage burst should be capped where appropriate.
```

---

## PvP Flag Types

```text
Unflagged
Duel Flagged
Faction War Flagged
Guild War Flagged
Criminal Flagged
Bounty Target
Arena Flagged
Open World PvP Flagged
```

---

## PvP Protection Rules

```text
No attacking players during loading.
No attacking players during teleport.
No attacking players during login grace period.
No attacking players in safe zones.
No attacking players under spawn protection unless they attack first.
No rewards for killing the same player repeatedly.
No rewards for killing much lower-level players.
```

---

# 17. Death System

When HP reaches 0, the entity dies or enters a downed state.

## Player Death Options

Choose based on your MMO design:

```text
Instant death
Downed state
Knockout
Respawn at graveyard
Respawn at bind point
Revive by healer
Corpse run
Item durability loss
Temporary weakness debuff
Partial gold loss
PvP loot drop, if hardcore
```

---

## Recommended MMO Death Rules

For a broad MMO:

```text
Player reaches 0 HP.
Player enters Downed state for 30 seconds.
Allies can revive them.
Enemies can finish them in PvP if rules allow.
If not revived, player respawns at nearest safe point.
Equipment loses durability.
Temporary resurrection sickness may apply.
```

---

## NPC Death Rules

When NPC reaches 0 HP:

```text
Stop AI actions.
Stop movement.
Play death animation.
Remove from threat table.
Distribute credit.
Drop loot if eligible.
Update quests.
Update reputation.
Start respawn timer if not unique.
```

---

# 18. Kill Credit System

Do not give credit only to the last hit. That causes toxic gameplay.

Use contribution.

```text
Contribution Score =
Damage Dealt
+ Healing Done to Participants
+ Tanking/Threat Contribution
+ Objective Contribution
+ Crowd Control Contribution
```

---

## Kill Credit Rules

```text
Player must contribute at least 3% to 5% of total meaningful contribution.
Party members share credit if nearby and eligible.
Raid members share credit by raid rules.
Players outside level range may receive reduced rewards.
Players who tag and run away may lose credit.
Players dead too long before kill may receive reduced credit.
```

---

## Anti-Kill-Stealing Rules

```text
First tag alone should not own the mob forever.
Meaningful contribution matters.
Party and public event rules should be clear.
If a player does 90% of the damage, they should not lose credit to a last hit.
```

---

# 19. Loot Rules

Loot can be based on:

```text
Individual loot
Shared loot
Need/greed
Master loot
Public event reward
Contribution-based reward
Faction reward
Quest-specific reward
```

For modern MMOs, individual loot prevents many player conflicts.

---

# 20. Combat States

Each entity should have a combat state.

```text
Idle
Alert
In Combat
Casting
Stunned
Rooted
Fleeing
Evading
Dead
Respawning
Invulnerable
Leashed
Returning Home
```

---

## State Rules

An entity cannot be in incompatible states.

Bad:

```text
Dead and attacking
Stunned and casting
Evading and taking damage
Respawning and targetable
Teleporting and taking PvP damage
```

Good:

```text
If Dead, cancel all attacks and casts.
If Stunned, cancel movement and most actions.
If Evading, clear threat and ignore damage.
If Invulnerable, show immune feedback.
```

---

# 21. Buffs and Debuffs

Buffs and debuffs modify combat.

## Buff Examples

```text
Increase Attack Points
Increase Defense
Increase Accuracy
Increase Evasion
Increase Attack Speed
Add shield
Increase healing received
Add elemental damage
Grant temporary immunity
```

## Debuff Examples

```text
Poison
Bleed
Burn
Slow
Armor reduction
Healing reduction
Blind
Silence
Weakness
Vulnerability
Marked target
```

---

## Buff Stacking Rules

Define stacking clearly.

```text
Same buff from same source refreshes duration.
Same buff from different sources may stack up to a cap.
Stronger buff may override weaker buff.
Some buffs are exclusive.
Some buffs stack additively.
Some buffs stack multiplicatively.
```

Avoid unlimited stacking. It breaks MMOs.

---

# 22. Hitbox and Collision Rules

Combat should use clear hitboxes.

## Melee Hitbox

```text
Check attacker range.
Check target collision capsule.
Check facing direction if required.
Allow small latency tolerance.
Do not allow attacks through walls.
```

## Projectile Hitbox

```text
Projectile spawns from attacker or weapon.
Projectile travels through world.
Server checks collision.
Projectile can miss if target moves.
Projectile can be blocked by walls.
Projectile may have travel time.
```

## Area Hitbox

Types:

```text
Circle
Cone
Line
Rectangle
Sphere
Donut
Ground zone
Chain bounce
```

Each target inside the area is checked separately.

---

# 23. Facing Rules

Some attacks require facing.

```text
Melee attacks require attacker to face target within an angle.
Backstab requires attacker behind target.
Shield block requires defender facing attacker.
Parry requires defender facing attacker.
Cone attacks use attacker direction.
```

Example:

```text
Normal melee facing cone: 120 degrees
Backstab rear cone: 60 degrees behind target
Shield block cone: 140 degrees in front of defender
```

---

# 24. Combat Logging

Every combat event should be logged.

Example:

```text
Timestamp
Attacker ID
Target ID
Ability ID
Attack Type
Hit Result
Raw Damage
Mitigated Damage
Final Damage
Critical
Blocked
Dodged
Resisted
Absorbed
Target HP Before
Target HP After
Position
Zone
Source
```

This is necessary for:

```text
Debugging
Anti-cheat
Balancing
Player combat logs
Dispute review
Bug reports
Boss tuning
PvP moderation
```

---

# 25. Edge Cases to Handle

These are the things that break combat systems if you do not design for them.

---

## Edge Case: Target Dies Before Attack Lands

Example:

```text
Player casts fireball.
Another player kills the target before the fireball arrives.
```

Rule:

```text
When projectile lands, check if target is still alive.
If target is dead, projectile fizzles or hits corpse with no effect.
No resource refund unless design allows it.
```

---

## Edge Case: Attacker Dies Before Attack Lands

Rule:

```text
Instant attacks still apply if completed before death.
Projectiles already fired may still land.
Channeled attacks stop immediately.
Damage-over-time effects may continue if already applied.
```

---

## Edge Case: Simultaneous Death

Rule:

```text
If two entities kill each other in the same server tick, both deaths are valid.
Both receive appropriate credit.
PvP draw rules may apply.
```

---

## Edge Case: Healing After Fatal Damage

Rule:

```text
Server processes events in timestamp/tick order.
If damage reduces HP to 0 before heal resolves, death/downed state triggers.
If heal resolves first, target may survive.
```

Do not let the client decide this.

---

## Edge Case: Disconnect During Combat

Rule:

```text
Character remains in world for a short combat logout timer.
If killed during that timer, death is valid.
If disconnected due to server crash, apply safe recovery rules.
```

Example:

```text
Normal logout: 10 seconds
Combat logout: 30 seconds
PvP combat logout: 60 seconds
```

---

## Edge Case: Player Logs Out to Avoid Death

Rule:

```text
If in combat, logout does not instantly remove character.
Character remains attackable until timer ends.
Repeated combat logging may apply penalties.
```

---

## Edge Case: Safe Zone Abuse

Example:

```text
Player attacks, then runs into a safe zone.
```

Rule:

```text
Recently aggressive players cannot gain safe-zone protection for X seconds.
NPC guards may still respond.
PvP damage may stop at boundary, but DoTs may either continue or pause based on design.
```

Recommended:

```text
Aggression timer: 15 to 60 seconds
```

---

## Edge Case: Attacking From Safe Zone

Rule:

```text
Players inside safe zones cannot damage players or NPCs outside the safe zone.
Projectiles cannot be fired from protected areas into combat areas.
```

---

## Edge Case: Pulling Boss Out of Arena

Rule:

```text
Boss enters Evade state.
Boss becomes immune.
Boss returns to arena.
Boss resets if players caused exploit movement.
```

---

## Edge Case: Pathfinding Exploit

Example:

```text
Player stands on a rock where melee enemies cannot reach them.
```

Rule:

```text
If NPC cannot reach target for X seconds:
- Try ranged ability if available.
- Try repositioning.
- Call for help if intelligent.
- Enter Evade if unreachable.
```

---

## Edge Case: NPC Stuck in Wall

Rule:

```text
If NPC is stuck:
- Recalculate path.
- Teleport short distance to last valid navmesh point.
- If still stuck, return home.
- Log bug event.
```

---

## Edge Case: Player Attacks Through Wall

Rule:

```text
Server performs line-of-sight check.
Attack fails with “No line of sight.”
```

---

## Edge Case: AoE Hits Too Many Targets

Rule:

```text
AoE has a target cap.
Targets beyond cap receive reduced damage or are ignored.
Priority may be nearest targets, highest threat targets, or random valid targets.
```

Example:

```text
Full damage to first 5 targets.
50% damage to next 5.
No damage after 10.
```

---

## Edge Case: Infinite Healing Loop

Rule:

```text
Healing generates threat.
Healing can be reduced by dampening in long fights.
Mana/resource limits prevent infinite healing.
Some bosses apply healing reduction mechanics.
```

---

## Edge Case: Buff Stacking Exploit

Rule:

```text
Set maximum stack count.
Prevent duplicate stacking from same source unless intended.
Use categories so similar buffs do not multiply infinitely.
```

---

## Edge Case: Damage Reflection Loop

Example:

```text
Player reflects damage.
Enemy reflects reflected damage.
Loop repeats forever.
```

Rule:

```text
Reflected damage cannot trigger another reflect.
```

---

## Edge Case: Pet/Summon Kill Credit

Rule:

```text
Pet damage counts toward owner contribution.
If owner is too far away or inactive, contribution may be reduced.
Summons despawn when owner dies, logs out, or leaves zone unless designed otherwise.
```

---

## Edge Case: Neutral NPCs

Rule:

```text
Neutral NPCs do not attack first.
If attacked, they become hostile to attacker.
Nearby allied NPCs may assist.
Reputation/legal penalties may apply.
```

---

## Edge Case: Friendly Fire

Decide globally.

Options:

```text
No friendly fire.
Friendly fire only in hardcore zones.
Friendly fire only for certain AoE abilities.
Friendly fire enabled during guild wars.
Friendly fire disabled in raids.
```

If friendly fire exists, show warnings clearly.

---

## Edge Case: Low-Level Griefing

Rule:

```text
High-level players receive no reward for killing much lower-level players.
May receive Likeability or Legal penalties.
Guards or bounty systems may respond.
Victim may get temporary protection after respawn.
```

---

## Edge Case: Spawn Camping

Rule:

```text
Respawned players receive temporary protection.
Protection ends if they attack.
Repeated kills near spawn give no rewards and may trigger penalties.
```

Example:

```text
Spawn protection: 10 to 30 seconds
```

---

## Edge Case: Overpowered Guards

Guards should be strong but not absurdly game-breaking.

Rule:

```text
Normal guards should defeat criminals.
Elite guards should stop high-level criminals.
Capital guards should be extremely dangerous.
But players should still have outlaw gameplay options through stealth, bribery, disguises, or escape routes.
```

---

## Edge Case: Boss One-Shot Mechanics

One-shot mechanics must be clear.

Rule:

```text
If an attack can instantly kill players, it must be heavily telegraphed.
Players need a fair counter: dodge, block, interrupt, hide, split damage, cleanse, or use shield.
```

---

## Edge Case: Damage During Loading Screen

Rule:

```text
Players cannot take damage while loading into a zone.
After load completes, give short protection.
Protection ends when player moves, attacks, or uses ability.
```

---

## Edge Case: Server Lag

Rule:

```text
Server is source of truth.
Do not process impossible attack speeds.
Do not allow queued attacks to burst all at once after lag spike.
Clamp action rate.
Validate cooldowns by server time.
```

---

# 26. Combat Formulas

Here is a clean baseline formula set.

## Hit Chance

```text
Hit Chance = 80% + AccuracyBonus - EvasionBonus
Minimum Hit Chance = 5%
Maximum Hit Chance = 95%
```

## Critical Chance

```text
Final Crit Chance = Base Crit Chance + Crit Rating Bonus - Target Crit Resistance
Minimum Crit Chance = 0%
Maximum Crit Chance = 75%
```

## Physical Damage Reduction

```text
Physical Reduction = Armor / (Armor + AttackerLevel × 100)
Maximum Reduction = 75%
```

## Magic Damage Reduction

```text
Magic Reduction = MagicResistance / (MagicResistance + AttackerLevel × 100)
Maximum Reduction = 75%
```

## Base Damage

```text
Base Damage = Attack Points × Ability Multiplier
```

## Final Damage

```text
Final Damage =
Base Damage
× Random Variance
× Critical Modifier
× Buff Modifier
× Debuff Modifier
× Damage Type Modifier
× (1 - Damage Reduction)
```

## Minimum Damage

```text
If attack hits and target is not immune:
Final Damage must be at least 1.
```

---

# 27. Example Player vs NPC Combat

Player attacks a bandit.

```text
Player Level: 10
Player Attack Points: 120
Ability: Sword Slash
Ability Multiplier: 1.1
Player Accuracy: +8%
Bandit Evasion: +5%
Bandit Armor Reduction: 20%
Player Crit Chance: 10%
Crit Damage: 150%
```

Hit chance:

```text
80% + 8% - 5% = 83%
```

Damage:

```text
Base Damage = 120 × 1.1 = 132
Random Variance = 1.05
No Crit

Modified Damage = 132 × 1.05 = 138.6
Final Damage = 138.6 × 0.80 = 110.88
Rounded = 111
```

Bandit HP:

```text
Bandit HP Before: 400
Damage: 111
Bandit HP After: 289
```

---

# 28. Example NPC vs Player Combat

Wolf attacks player.

```text
Wolf Attack Points: 35
Bite Multiplier: 1.0
Player Armor Reduction: 30%
Wolf Accuracy: +0%
Player Evasion: +10%
```

Hit chance:

```text
80% + 0% - 10% = 70%
```

Damage if hit:

```text
Base Damage = 35
Variance = 1.08

Modified Damage = 37.8
Final Damage = 37.8 × 0.70 = 26.46
Rounded = 26
```

Player loses 26 HP.

---

# 29. Example Guard Response

Player attacks merchant.

```text
Merchant HP: 180
Merchant Attack Points: 15
Merchant Behavior: Calls guards, flees
Nearby Guard HP: 800
Nearby Guard Attack Points: 160
```

Combat sequence:

```text
Player attacks merchant.
Merchant becomes hostile to player.
Merchant calls for guards.
Player receives Legal Score penalty.
Player receives Likeability penalty if witnessed.
Guard enters combat with player.
Guard attempts arrest or attacks depending on severity.
If player kills merchant, murder penalty applies.
If player flees, bounty may be created.
```

---

# 30. Recommended Combat Data Structure

Each entity should have combat data like this:

```json
{
  "id": "npc_wolf_001",
  "name": "Forest Wolf",
  "type": "npc",
  "level": 10,
  "faction": "wildlife",
  "hp": 250,
  "max_hp": 250,
  "attack_points": 35,
  "defense": 120,
  "armor": 100,
  "magic_resistance": 30,
  "accuracy": 0,
  "evasion": 5,
  "critical_chance": 0.05,
  "critical_damage": 1.5,
  "attack_speed": 1.0,
  "attack_range": 1.8,
  "movement_speed": 4.0,
  "aggro_range": 12,
  "leash_range": 40,
  "combat_state": "idle",
  "abilities": ["bite", "howl", "lunge"]
}
```

---

# 31. Ability Data Structure

Each ability should be defined clearly.

```json
{
  "id": "sword_slash",
  "name": "Sword Slash",
  "type": "melee",
  "damage_type": "slashing",
  "ability_multiplier": 1.2,
  "range": 2.2,
  "cooldown_seconds": 1.5,
  "resource_cost": 0,
  "requires_line_of_sight": true,
  "requires_facing": true,
  "can_crit": true,
  "can_be_blocked": true,
  "can_be_parried": true,
  "can_be_dodged": true,
  "threat_multiplier": 1.0
}
```

---

```json
{
  "id": "dragon_breath_fire",
  "name": "Dragon Fire Breath",
  "type": "cone_aoe",
  "damage_type": "fire",
  "ability_multiplier": 3.5,
  "range": 22,
  "cone_angle": 70,
  "cooldown_seconds": 18,
  "cast_time_seconds": 3,
  "requires_line_of_sight": true,
  "can_crit": false,
  "can_be_blocked": false,
```
