```json
  "status_effects": ["burning"],
  "target_cap": 20
}
```

---

# 33. AI Prompt Version

You can give this directly to an AI that is designing MMO combat:

```text
When designing combat for this MMO, every player, NPC, monster, guard, merchant, civilian, boss, pet, and summon must have HP and Attack Points.

Every attack must follow this pipeline:
1. Check whether the attacker can act.
2. Check whether the target exists and is alive.
3. Check whether the target is attackable.
4. Check range.
5. Check line of sight.
6. Check facing requirements.
7. Roll hit chance.
8. Resolve miss, dodge, parry, block, resist, absorb, normal hit, critical hit, glancing hit, crushing hit, immune, or evade.
9. Calculate damage using Attack Points, ability multiplier, variance, critical modifier, buffs, debuffs, and defense/resistance.
10. Apply damage to HP.
11. Apply secondary effects.
12. Update threat and aggro.
13. Check death or downed state.
14. Distribute XP, loot, quest credit, reputation, and legal consequences.

The system must be server-authoritative. The client may request attacks and show animations, but the server decides hit validation, damage, death, rewards, and state changes.

All NPCs must have:
- HP
- Max HP
- Attack Points
- Defense
- Accuracy
- Evasion
- Attack Range
- Aggro Range
- Leash Range
- Faction
- Combat Behavior
- Death behavior
- Respawn behavior

Peaceful NPCs should still have HP and Attack Points, but they may flee, call guards, or defend weakly instead of fighting aggressively.

Design edge cases for:
- Target dies before attack lands.
- Attacker dies before projectile lands.
- Simultaneous death.
- Healing and damage resolving in the same server tick.
- Disconnecting during combat.
- Safe zone abuse.
- Spawn camping.
- Attacking through walls.
- NPC pathfinding exploits.
- Boss leash abuse.
- Damage reflection loops.
- Infinite healing loops.
- Buff stacking exploits.
- AoE target caps.
- Kill stealing.
- PvP griefing.
- Low-level player protection.
- Pet and summon contribution.
- Legal and reputation penalties for attacking civilians or guards.

Every combat action should produce clear feedback:
- Miss
- Dodge
- Block
- Parry
- Resist
- Absorb
- Immune
- Evade
- Critical Hit
- Normal Hit
- Death
- Interrupted
- Out of range
- No line of sight
- Invalid target

The combat system should be readable, fair, scalable, hard to exploit, and suitable for both PvE and PvP.
```

---

# Final Combat Design Rule

Every attack should answer these questions:

```text
Can the attacker legally and physically attack?
Can the target legally and physically be hit?
Did the attack reach the target?
Did the target avoid, block, resist, or absorb it?
How much damage was dealt?
Did the target die?
Who gets credit?
What consequences happen afterward?
```

That rule will prevent most obvious MMO combat mistakes.


Below is a complex MMO-ready ranking system with **three separate but connected scores**:

1. **Likeability** — how people personally feel about you.
2. **Good Samaritan / Outlaw** — how the law and authorities view you.
3. **Notoriety** — how well-known you are.

The key is this: **these should not all mean the same thing.**

A player can be:

* **Liked but legally wanted** — a Robin Hood type.
* **Law-abiding but disliked** — a cruel noble who follows the law.
* **Famous but hated** — a feared villain.
* **Unknown but good** — a helpful low-level citizen.
* **Notorious and respected** — a legendary warrior.

---

# MMO Ranking System Overview

Each player has three major public scores:

```text
Likeability: -10,000 to +10,000
Good Samaritan / Outlaw: -10,000 to +10,000
Notoriety: 0 to 100,000+
```

## Score Meaning

```text
Likeability
Measures emotional/social reputation.
Affected by kindness, cruelty, generosity, betrayal, social behavior, and how NPCs/players feel about you.

Good Samaritan / Outlaw
Measures legal reputation.
Affected by crimes, arrests, bounty status, paying fines, helping guards, reporting criminals, and obeying local laws.

Notoriety
Measures fame and recognition.
Affected by major quests, boss kills, PvP victories, public crimes, discoveries, titles, faction wars, and large-scale events.
Notoriety never goes below 0.
```

---

# Important MMO Design Rule

These scores should exist at **three levels**:

```text
Global Score
Your overall reputation across the world.

Regional Score
How a specific kingdom, city, village, guild, or faction views you.

Personal NPC Score
How a specific NPC personally feels about you.
```

Example:

```text
Global Likeability: +2,400
Capital City Likeability: +4,800
Bandit Camp Likeability: -3,200
Guard Faction Legal Score: +1,500
Thieves Guild Legal Score: -6,000
Notoriety: 18,000
```

This allows more interesting gameplay.

You may be loved by villagers but hated by nobles.
You may be wanted in one kingdom but welcomed in another.
You may be famous everywhere, but not equally respected everywhere.

---

# 1. Likeability System

## Purpose

Likeability measures how much people personally like, trust, respect, fear, admire, or dislike the player.

This is **not the same as lawfulness**.

A player can break the law and still be liked if people think their actions were justified.
A player can obey the law and still be disliked if they are selfish, rude, greedy, or cruel.

---

## Likeability Score Range

```text
-10,000 to +10,000
```

## Likeability Tiers

|       Score Range | Rank         | How People Treat You                                                |
| ----------------: | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| +8,000 to +10,000 | Beloved Hero | NPCs cheer, discounts, gifts, marriage options, followers volunteer |
|  +5,000 to +7,999 | Admired      | Friendly greetings, better quest rewards, easier persuasion         |
|  +2,000 to +4,999 | Well-Liked   | Minor discounts, NPCs trust you, more rumors and help               |
|    +500 to +1,999 | Friendly     | People are polite and slightly helpful                              |
|      -499 to +499 | Neutral      | Normal treatment                                                    |
|    -500 to -1,999 | Unpleasant   | Colder dialogue, worse prices, less help                            |
|  -2,000 to -4,999 | Disliked     | NPCs avoid you, insults, higher prices                              |
|  -5,000 to -7,999 | Hated        | Shops may refuse service, NPCs report you, companions leave         |
| -8,000 to -10,000 | Despised     | Crowds fear/hate you, mobs may form, bounty tips spread             |

---

## Likeability Point Rules

### Helpful Actions

| Action                                                            |                            Likeability Change |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------: |
| Help an NPC with a small task                                     |                                    +10 to +50 |
| Complete a village/local quest                                    |                                   +75 to +200 |
| Complete a major quest that helps civilians                       |                                  +300 to +800 |
| Rescue a kidnapped NPC                                            |                                  +250 to +700 |
| Heal or revive a non-party player                                 |                                   +25 to +100 |
| Give food, medicine, or supplies to poor NPCs                     |                                   +20 to +150 |
| Donate gold to a town, temple, orphanage, guild, or relief effort | +1 per meaningful donation unit, capped daily |
| Defend a settlement from monsters                                 |                                +200 to +1,000 |
| Help rebuild a damaged town                                       |                                  +100 to +600 |
| Save a merchant caravan                                           |                                  +150 to +500 |
| Defend a lower-level player from hostile mobs                     |                                   +50 to +200 |
| Return a lost item instead of keeping it                          |                                   +50 to +250 |
| Spare an enemy who surrenders                                     |                                   +30 to +200 |
| Give a compliment through dialogue to an NPC                      |                                     +2 to +10 |
| Successfully mediate a dispute peacefully                         |                                  +100 to +400 |
| Publicly expose corruption with proof                             |                                +300 to +1,000 |
| Choose a selfless quest ending                                    |                                  +200 to +800 |

---

### Negative Social Actions

| Action                                               | Likeability Change |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | -----------------: |
| Insult an NPC                                        |          -5 to -50 |
| Threaten an NPC                                      |        -25 to -150 |
| Refuse to help in an emergency                       |        -50 to -300 |
| Demand extra payment from a poor NPC                 |        -50 to -250 |
| Betray an NPC after accepting their quest            |       -200 to -800 |
| Kill a friendly NPC                                  |     -500 to -2,000 |
| Kill a beloved town NPC                              |   -1,500 to -5,000 |
| Attack civilians                                     |     -300 to -1,500 |
| Steal from poor/common NPCs                          |       -100 to -500 |
| Steal from a temple, healer, school, or relief group |     -300 to -1,000 |
| Abandon a companion in danger                        |     -200 to -1,000 |
| Sacrifice innocents for power                        |   -1,000 to -5,000 |
| Lie during a major moral quest                       |       -100 to -700 |
| Intentionally lead monsters into town                |     -500 to -3,000 |
| Destroy public property                              |       -100 to -600 |
| Scam NPCs using quest choices                        |       -150 to -800 |
| Repeated griefing of low-level players               |     -500 to -2,000 |
| Betray a player group during a public event          |     -300 to -1,500 |

---

## Likeability Context Multipliers

Not every action should change reputation the same way. Use context.

```text
Final Likeability Change =
Base Points × Witness Multiplier × Victim Multiplier × Reputation Multiplier × Repetition Modifier
```

### Witness Multiplier

| Situation                             | Multiplier |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------: |
| No witnesses and no evidence          |       x0.1 |
| One private witness                   |       x0.3 |
| Several witnesses                     |      x0.75 |
| Public place                          |       x1.0 |
| Town square, tavern, market, or court |      x1.25 |
| Seen by a respected NPC               |       x1.5 |
| Broadcast by rumor/news system        |       x2.0 |

### Victim/Target Multiplier

| Target                   |             Multiplier |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------: |
| Unknown traveler         |                  x0.75 |
| Common citizen           |                   x1.0 |
| Poor/vulnerable NPC      |                   x1.5 |
| Child/family/elder NPC   |                   x2.0 |
| Beloved town leader      |                   x2.5 |
| Corrupt noble            |                   x0.5 |
| Hated criminal           |         x0.25 to x0.75 |
| Monster threatening town | Positive modifier x1.5 |

### Repetition Modifier

To prevent farming:

```text
First time: 100% value
Second time same day: 50% value
Third time same day: 25% value
Further repeats: 5% value
```

Example:

Helping the same farmer 20 times should not make you a saint.

---

## Likeability Treatment Rules

### Positive Likeability Effects

At high Likeability, NPCs may:

```text
Offer discounts
Give free food, lodging, or repairs
Reveal hidden rumors
Warn you about ambushes
Offer rare quests
Vote for you in town politics
Defend you in legal trials
Offer companionship or romance
Give better quest rewards
Allow access to private homes
Invite you to festivals or councils
Lower suspicion when minor crimes happen nearby
```

### Negative Likeability Effects

At low Likeability, NPCs may:

```text
Refuse service
Charge higher prices
Spread rumors about you
Hide valuables when you enter
Call guards faster
Refuse quests
Lock doors
Deny lodging
Throw insults
Organize mobs
Sabotage your reputation
Refuse to revive or heal you
Leave your party
Demand payment upfront
```

---

# 2. Good Samaritan / Outlaw System

## Purpose

This measures how the **legal system** views the player.

This is different from Likeability.

A player can be legally clean but disliked.
A player can be legally wanted but loved by commoners.

---

## Legal Score Range

```text
-10,000 to +10,000
```

Positive score means lawful/helpful citizen.
Negative score means criminal/outlaw.

---

## Legal Tiers

|       Score Range | Rank                | Law Response                                                        |
| ----------------: | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| +8,000 to +10,000 | Champion of the Law | Guards salute, legal immunity on minor issues, access to law quests |
|  +5,000 to +7,999 | Trusted Citizen     | Guards help you, reduced fines, court favors you                    |
|  +2,000 to +4,999 | Good Samaritan      | Guards are friendly, may ask for help                               |
|    +500 to +1,999 | Law-Abiding         | Normal positive treatment                                           |
|      -499 to +499 | Unremarkable        | Standard legal treatment                                            |
|    -500 to -1,999 | Suspicious          | Guards watch you, more inspections                                  |
|  -2,000 to -4,999 | Troublemaker        | Higher fines, guards question you, restricted areas blocked         |
|  -5,000 to -7,999 | Outlaw              | Wanted posters, arrest on sight in some areas                       |
| -8,000 to -10,000 | Public Enemy        | Kill/capture orders, bounty hunters, city lockdown response         |

---

## Legal Point Rules

### Lawful Actions

| Action                                              | Legal Score Change |
| --------------------------------------------------- | -----------------: |
| Pay taxes, tariffs, or legal fees                   |          +5 to +50 |
| Report a crime with evidence                        |        +50 to +250 |
| Help guards stop a crime                            |       +100 to +500 |
| Capture a wanted criminal alive                     |       +200 to +800 |
| Kill a dangerous wanted criminal with legal warrant |       +100 to +500 |
| Return stolen property                              |        +50 to +300 |
| Testify truthfully in court                         |       +100 to +400 |
| Refuse a bribe                                      |        +50 to +250 |
| Help defend a city from attack                      |     +300 to +1,500 |
| Complete official guard/military quest              |       +150 to +800 |
| Pay off your fines voluntarily                      |        +25 to +200 |
| Serve jail/community service sentence               |        +50 to +500 |
| Turn yourself in peacefully                         |       +100 to +400 |
| Prevent a public riot                               |     +250 to +1,000 |

---

### Criminal Actions

| Action                                         | Legal Score Change |
| ---------------------------------------------- | -----------------: |
| Trespassing                                    |        -25 to -150 |
| Pickpocketing                                  |        -50 to -300 |
| Theft from citizen                             |       -100 to -500 |
| Theft from noble/guard/government              |       -200 to -800 |
| Lockpicking private property                   |        -50 to -250 |
| Assault                                        |       -200 to -800 |
| Assaulting a guard                             |     -500 to -1,500 |
| Murder                                         |   -1,000 to -4,000 |
| Murder of guard or official                    |   -2,000 to -6,000 |
| Arson                                          |   -1,000 to -4,000 |
| Smuggling illegal goods                        |     -300 to -1,500 |
| Selling illegal items                          |     -300 to -2,000 |
| Bribery                                        |       -100 to -800 |
| Jailbreak                                      |   -1,000 to -4,000 |
| Helping a wanted criminal escape               |     -500 to -2,500 |
| Attacking a settlement                         |   -2,000 to -8,000 |
| Treason                                        |  -5,000 to -10,000 |
| Killing players in protected zones             |   -1,000 to -5,000 |
| Repeated griefing in lawful territory          |     -500 to -3,000 |
| Refusing arrest and fleeing                    |     -300 to -1,500 |
| Killing a bounty hunter legally sent after you |     -250 to -1,000 |

---

## Severity Rules

Crimes should scale based on severity.

```text
Minor Crime: -25 to -250
Moderate Crime: -250 to -1,000
Major Crime: -1,000 to -4,000
Extreme Crime: -4,000 to -10,000
```

### Crime Severity Factors

Increase penalty if:

```text
The victim was innocent
The victim was low-level
The crime happened in public
The crime happened in a sacred/legal area
The player resisted arrest
The crime was repeated
The crime harmed many NPCs/players
The crime disrupted a town economy
The crime happened during a festival, war, or emergency
```

Reduce penalty if:

```text
The target was a criminal
The player had a legal bounty contract
The act was self-defense
The victim attacked first
The action happened in an outlaw zone
The player surrendered peacefully
The player paid restitution
The player had proof of corruption
```

---

## Legal Jurisdiction Rules

The legal system should be regional.

Example:

```text
Kingdom of Aurelian: +5,200
Free City of Marrowgate: -2,800
Bandit Coast: -6,500
Mage Council: +900
Temple Law: +3,200
```

This creates better gameplay.

A player may be a hero in one kingdom and a criminal in another.

---

## Guard Behavior by Legal Score

### +8,000 to +10,000 — Champion of the Law

```text
Guards salute you
Minor crimes may receive warnings instead of fines
You may enter restricted guard areas
You can request backup during public threats
You can take elite bounty contracts
NPCs assume you are trustworthy in legal disputes
Some guards may protect you from angry mobs
```

### +5,000 to +7,999 — Trusted Citizen

```text
Guards greet you warmly
Fines reduced by 25%
Arrest checks are less aggressive
You may be asked to help with investigations
You can vouch for lower-reputation players
```

### +2,000 to +4,999 — Good Samaritan

```text
Guards are polite
Minor legal mistakes receive one warning
You get access to lawful side quests
Witnesses are more likely to believe you
```

### -500 to -1,999 — Suspicious

```text
Guards follow you in cities
Random inspections may happen
Restricted areas become harder to access
Shopkeepers may warn guards if you linger
Persuasion checks with officials become harder
```

### -2,000 to -4,999 — Troublemaker

```text
Higher fines
Guards stop you at gates
Weapon checks in cities
Some legal quests locked
Bounty hunters may track you after repeated offenses
```

### -5,000 to -7,999 — Outlaw

```text
Wanted posters appear
Guards attempt arrest on sight in lawful areas
Merchants may refuse service
Fast travel through lawful cities may be blocked
Housing access in lawful towns may be suspended
Bounty hunters actively pursue you
```

### -8,000 to -10,000 — Public Enemy

```text
Elite guards attack or capture on sight
City gates may close when you approach
NPCs flee or report your location
Bounty value increases
Some factions refuse diplomacy
Special prison, execution, exile, or redemption questline may trigger
```

---

## Arrest, Fines, Jail, and Redemption

A legal system needs consequences beyond “guards attack.”

### When Caught

The player may be offered:

```text
Pay fine
Return stolen goods
Serve jail time
Fight/escape
Bribe guard
Use legal writ/pardon
Call witness
Demand trial
```

### Fine Calculation

```text
Fine = Crime Value + Damage Value + Victim Status Modifier + Repeat Offender Modifier
```

Example:

```text
Stealing bread: 50 gold
Stealing royal artifact: 5,000 gold
Assaulting guard: 2,000 gold
Murder: 10,000+ gold or prison/bounty
```

### Jail Effects

Jail should hurt, but not ruin the game.

```text
Temporary skill debuff
Lost time
Confiscated stolen items
Reduced legal penalty after sentence
Possible prison quests
Possible escape route
Possible reputation loss with lawful factions
Possible reputation gain with criminals
```

### Redemption

Players should be able to recover.

```text
Pay restitution
Complete public service quests
Capture criminals
Defend towns
Donate to victims
Serve prison sentence
Receive royal pardon
Complete redemption storyline
Join city guard temporarily
Recover stolen goods
```

---

# 3. Notoriety System

## Purpose

Notoriety measures how well-known the player is.

It does **not** mean good or bad.

A famous hero, feared murderer, rich merchant, legendary crafter, arena champion, and world-first raider can all have high Notoriety.

---

## Notoriety Range

```text
0 to 100,000+
```

Notoriety cannot go negative.

It may decay slowly at low/mid levels, but major historical achievements should leave permanent marks.

---

## Notoriety Tiers

|      Score Range | Rank             | World Response                                                    |
| ---------------: | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
|         0 to 499 | Unknown          | Nobody recognizes you                                             |
|     500 to 1,999 | Local Name       | Some locals recognize you                                         |
|   2,000 to 4,999 | Known Adventurer | Regional NPCs know of you                                         |
|   5,000 to 9,999 | Famous           | Merchants, guards, and quest-givers recognize you                 |
| 10,000 to 24,999 | Renowned         | Bards mention you, factions recruit you                           |
| 25,000 to 49,999 | Legendary        | Statues, songs, elite quests, political influence                 |
| 50,000 to 99,999 | Mythic Figure    | World leaders know you, enemies plan around you                   |
|         100,000+ | Living Legend    | Server-wide recognition, history books, world events react to you |

---

## Notoriety Point Rules

### PvE and Quest Notoriety

| Action                                      |    Notoriety Gain |
| ------------------------------------------- | ----------------: |
| Complete first major questline in a village |      +100 to +300 |
| Complete regional story arc                 |    +500 to +1,500 |
| Complete main campaign chapter              |  +1,000 to +3,000 |
| Defeat dangerous elite monster              |      +100 to +500 |
| Defeat dungeon boss                         |    +250 to +1,000 |
| Defeat raid boss                            |  +1,000 to +5,000 |
| Server-first raid boss kill                 | +5,000 to +15,000 |
| Discover hidden region                      |    +500 to +2,000 |
| Discover world secret first                 | +2,000 to +10,000 |
| Complete legendary weapon quest             | +2,500 to +10,000 |
| Survive impossible event                    |  +1,000 to +5,000 |
| Win seasonal world event                    | +2,000 to +10,000 |

---

### PvP Notoriety

| Action                             |                                      Notoriety Gain |
| ---------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------: |
| Win fair duel                      |                                          +10 to +50 |
| Win arena match                    |                                         +25 to +150 |
| Win ranked match                   |                                         +50 to +300 |
| Defeat a high-ranked player        |                                        +100 to +750 |
| Defeat a famous player             |                                      +250 to +1,500 |
| Win tournament                     |                                    +1,000 to +5,000 |
| Lead faction victory               |                                   +2,000 to +10,000 |
| Capture enemy fortress             |                                    +1,000 to +4,000 |
| Kill a player far below your level |    +0 Notoriety, negative Likeability/Legal instead |
| Repeated grief kills               | +0 or very low Notoriety, heavy penalties elsewhere |

Important: do **not** reward griefing with fame unless your MMO intentionally supports villain play. Even then, separate “Infamy” from “Notoriety.”

---

### Criminal Notoriety

Bad actions can make someone known.

| Action                               |    Notoriety Gain |
| ------------------------------------ | ----------------: |
| Escape jail                          |    +250 to +1,000 |
| Rob famous noble                     |    +500 to +2,000 |
| Kill important official              |  +1,000 to +5,000 |
| Successfully raid caravan            |    +300 to +1,500 |
| Become wanted in a region            |    +500 to +2,000 |
| Reach Public Enemy legal tier        | +3,000 to +10,000 |
| Lead outlaw faction attack           |  +2,000 to +8,000 |
| Massive betrayal in public questline |  +1,000 to +5,000 |

This increases fame, but it should usually damage Likeability and Legal Score.

Example:

```text
Player assassinates a corrupt duke in public.

Notoriety: +3,000
Legal Score: -4,000
Commoner Likeability: +1,500
Noble Likeability: -3,000
Guard Faction Legal Score: -5,000
Rebel Faction Likeability: +2,500
```

That is the kind of complexity an MMO needs.

---

## Notoriety Treatment Rules

High Notoriety should make the world react.

### Positive Effects

```text
NPCs recognize you by name
Bards sing about you
Quest-givers seek you out
Rare merchants approach you
Guilds invite you
Leaders request meetings
Fans ask for help
Special titles unlock
Statues, plaques, or murals may appear
Discounts from admirers
Unique dialogue options
Access to elite quests
Ability to recruit followers
```

### Negative Effects

High Notoriety also has downsides.

```text
Enemies recognize you
Assassins target you
Rivals challenge you
Criminals may seek revenge
Higher prices from fearful merchants
More guard attention
Harder to travel anonymously
Political factions try to use you
Failure becomes more public
Rumors spread faster
Disguises become valuable
```

---

# Combined Reputation Logic

The best version of this system uses all three scores together.

## Example Character Types

### Beloved Lawful Hero

```text
Likeability: +8,500
Legal Score: +7,000
Notoriety: 35,000
```

Treatment:

```text
Crowds cheer
Guards salute
Villagers give gifts
Elite quests unlock
Enemies fear you
Nobles invite you to councils
```

---

### Famous Outlaw Hero

```text
Likeability: +6,500 among commoners
Legal Score: -7,500 in royal cities
Notoriety: 42,000
```

Treatment:

```text
Commoners hide you from guards
Guards arrest on sight
Bards sing rebel songs
Black market merchants help you
Nobles hate you
Rebels recruit you
```

---

### Lawful but Hated Noble

```text
Likeability: -5,000
Legal Score: +5,500
Notoriety: 12,000
```

Treatment:

```text
Guards protect you
Citizens insult you quietly
Merchants overcharge when possible
Poor NPCs refuse help
Nobles respect you
Rebels target you
```

---

### Unknown Criminal

```text
Likeability: -500
Legal Score: -2,500
Notoriety: 200
```

Treatment:

```text
Guards are suspicious
Most people do not know you
No major bounty hunters yet
Local shops may distrust you
```

---

### Mythic Villain

```text
Likeability: -9,000
Legal Score: -10,000
Notoriety: 85,000
```

Treatment:

```text
Civilians flee
Guards attack on sight
Bounty hunters pursue constantly
Dark factions offer alliances
Cities lock gates
World leaders discuss you
Special villain storyline unlocks
```

---

# Reaction Matrix

NPC behavior should depend on the combination of scores.

| Likeability | Legal Score | Notoriety | NPC Reaction                             |
| ----------- | ----------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| High        | High        | Low       | “Helpful citizen”                        |
| High        | High        | High      | “Famous hero”                            |
| High        | Low         | High      | “Beloved outlaw”                         |
| Low         | High        | High      | “Powerful but disliked authority figure” |
| Low         | Low         | High      | “Feared villain”                         |
| Low         | Low         | Low       | “Petty criminal”                         |
| Neutral     | High        | High      | “Respected official/adventurer”          |
| Neutral     | Low         | High      | “Dangerous unknown quantity”             |
| High        | Neutral     | High      | “Popular celebrity”                      |
| Low         | Neutral     | High      | “Infamous troublemaker”                  |

---

# Score Change Rules

## 1. Witnesses Matter

Actions should not fully affect reputation unless someone knows about them.

```text
No witnesses: 10% reputation impact
Witnessed privately: 30% impact
Witnessed by group: 75% impact
Public event: 100% impact
Recorded by magical/legal system: 125% impact
Spread through news/rumors: 150% to 200% impact
```

## 2. Reputation Spreads Slowly

A crime in one village should not instantly affect the entire world unless it is huge.

```text
Small action: affects personal NPCs only
Medium action: affects town/region
Large action: affects kingdom/faction
Legendary action: affects world/server
```

## 3. Repeated Actions Give Less Reward

Players will farm anything. Stop that.

```text
Same action repeated against same target:
1st time: 100%
2nd time: 50%
3rd time: 25%
4th+ time: 5%
```

## 4. Bigger Risk Means Bigger Reputation Change

Helping someone when it costs nothing should give small points.
Helping someone when you risk death, money, or status should give more.

```text
Low risk: x0.5
Normal risk: x1.0
High risk: x1.5
Extreme risk: x2.5
Server-wide risk: x5.0
```

## 5. Power Difference Matters

A max-level player killing a low-level player should not gain fame.

```text
Helping weaker player: positive modifier
Killing much weaker player: no Notoriety gain, heavy Likeability/Legal penalty
Defeating stronger enemy fairly: increased Notoriety
Defeating equal opponent fairly: normal reward
```

---

# Score Decay Rules

## Likeability Decay

Likeability should slowly move toward neutral unless reinforced.

```text
Above +5,000: decay 1% per week toward +5,000
Between +500 and +5,000: decay 0.5% per week
Between -500 and +500: no decay
Between -500 and -5,000: recover 0.5% per week toward neutral
Below -5,000: recover 0.25% per week unless crimes continue
```

Reason: people forget minor things, but not major heroism or villainy.

---

## Legal Score Decay

Legal score should not simply vanish. Crimes need systems.

```text
Minor crimes decay after several in-game days
Moderate crimes decay after fines, jail, or restitution
Major crimes require pardon, sentence, or questline
Extreme crimes never fully decay without major story resolution
```

Example:

```text
Stealing bread: fades over time
Murdering a king: permanent until major legal/political resolution
```

---

## Notoriety Decay

Notoriety should decay slowly only for ordinary fame.

```text
Minor fame: decays 1% per week
Regional fame: decays 0.5% per week
Major achievements: permanent floor
Server-first achievements: permanent
Legendary crimes: permanent historical mark
```

Use a **Permanent Notoriety Floor**.

Example:

```text
Current Notoriety: 25,000
Permanent Notoriety Floor: 12,000
```

The player can fade from current fame, but never become completely unknown after major accomplishments.

---

# Anti-Abuse Rules

MMO players will exploit reputation systems unless you prevent it.

## Do Not Allow Farming

```text
Repeatedly healing the same friend should give reduced points.
Repeatedly donating tiny amounts should hit a daily cap.
Repeatedly killing staged criminals should stop rewarding points.
Repeatedly reporting fake crimes should cause penalties.
```

## Account and Guild Abuse Protection

```text
No full reputation gain from alternate accounts on same IP/device/payment cluster.
Guild members cannot endlessly farm each other for reputation.
Killing the same player repeatedly gives reduced or zero Notoriety.
Trading items back and forth does not count as charity.
Player endorsements have daily/weekly limits.
```

## Grief Prevention

```text
Killing low-level players gives no Notoriety.
Killing low-level players gives negative Likeability.
Killing players in protected zones gives major Legal penalty.
Camping spawn points gives escalating penalties.
Dragging monsters into towns gives major penalties.
```

---

# Player-Facing UI

Players should understand the system clearly.

## Reputation Panel

```text
Likeability: Admired (+6,240)
Legal Standing: Troublemaker (-3,150)
Notoriety: Renowned (18,500)
```

## Regional Breakdown

```text
Capital City
Likeability: +4,500
Legal Standing: -2,000
Notoriety: 12,000

Northern Villages
Likeability: +7,800
Legal Standing: +1,000
Notoriety: 9,500

Thieves Guild
Likeability: +3,200
Legal Standing: Not Applicable
Notoriety: 6,000
```

## Recent Reputation Events

```text
+250 Likeability: Rescued villagers from wolves.
-500 Legal Standing: Trespassed inside royal archive.
+1,000 Notoriety: Defeated the Ashen Drake.
-300 Likeability: Threatened shopkeeper during argument.
```

This helps players understand cause and effect.

---

# Titles and Public Labels

Players should earn visible labels based on score combinations.

## Likeability Titles

```text
Beloved
Trusted Friend
Village Favorite
Kind Soul
Cold-Hearted
Despised
Cruel One
Oathbreaker
```

## Legal Titles

```text
Good Samaritan
Trusted Citizen
Deputized Protector
Champion of the Law
Suspicious Character
Troublemaker
Outlaw
Public Enemy
```

## Notoriety Titles

```text
Unknown
Local Name
Known Adventurer
Famous
Renowned
Legendary
Mythic
Living Legend
```

## Combined Titles

```text
Beloved Hero
Famous Outlaw
Feared Criminal
Lawful Tyrant
People’s Champion
Royal Hound
Shadow Celebrity
Living Legend
The Butcher of [Town]
Savior of [Region]
Enemy of the Crown
Friend of the Commonfolk
```

---

# Final Design Rule

Every major action should answer these questions:

```text
Did people like what you did?
This changes Likeability.

Was it legal or illegal?
This changes Good Samaritan / Outlaw.

Did enough people hear about it?
This changes Notoriety.
```

Example:

```text
Player steals medicine from a noble and gives it to plague victims.

Likeability:
+800 among poor/commoners
-500 among nobles
+400 among healers

Legal Score:
-1,200 in the kingdom
-300 with city guard
+200 with rebel faction

Notoriety:
+1,500 if witnessed or spread through rumor
```

That is the kind of layered system that makes an MMO feel alive.
